# Two Disjoint Loops: A K-Uniform Bound on the Renormalised Pair Expectation (27 October 2026)

{#3} Derived, for review, given the single-loop inputs listed under *Inherited* and except the R-step inputs (R-eval), (R-round), and (R-att-ext) below, which are derived and not reviewed, (R-member), derived and reviewed given (R-eval) (its rounds P1 to P1-4), and (R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed given (R-eval) (rounds P1–P10), (R-eval) given the readings of its #17 and, with (R-eval-cr), with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed, (6B)'s *15.5* derived through #3.3, not reviewed, with its part (R-eval-old) derived and reviewed, given LF (#5); the single-loop (6.3) needs (R-eval), (R-round), and (R-att-ext) as well: an exact identity for the pair ((8.1)) and a bound on it uniform in K ((8.2)). Setting: two vertex-disjoint squares C₁, C₂ in planes parallel to that of e₁, e₂, with characters χ₁, χ₂. The renormalised pair expectation is W^×_ren(ε) := E_ε[W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)]/(κ^{(K′)}_{C₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂}). Here κ^{(K′)}_{C_i} is the single-loop normalisation of C_i with the character χ_i at the common stopping step. This extends (6.3) of `WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md` (the continuum note) from one loop to two. Its first step is V4 Section 10 (`WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex`): Theorem thm:two-loop-theorem, and Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated for the retreated carrier. Map: `WILSON_IT1_D2_STEP_MAP_2026_10_22.md`, 1614-line version.

The tags {#N} are the line numbers of `WILSON_IT1_TWO_LOOPS_UNIFORM_BOUND_PRETAG_7e6f58f2bea8.md`, so "l.N" of this note in earlier reviews and notes is #N. {#3.2} **Derived and reviewed: the frame of the N-step chain on the deep layers ((R-eval) (iii), #110–#123, #144, #150, #6.2, #6.13).** At a point of layer n, inside a stage-m block of layer J, ũ_m changes between U and 𝐔 by at most O(B̄_G)L^Jη times the stage's ε's (#3.2.2). Every stage block has J ≤ k and J − n ≤ N (#3.2.1). With Bałaban's N taken as N_k of #3.2.1, L^{N_k} is a power of log g_k^{−2} up to constants. So #116.1's bound, and with it #116, #119, #121–#123, #6.2 and #6.13, hold with one extra factor L^N. L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k is still g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}, so the induction and the landing close for g small (#3.2.3).
{#3.2.1} *The stage blocks and the choice of N (quoted and checked).* The stages' determining sets are LFR I (1.15)–(1.16) (p.180 l.6–27), with h = k − N ((1.11), p.179 l.27). 𝔹_k^{(j−h)} keeps 𝔹_k on Ω_k ∪ Z^c and on Ω_h^c ∩ Z. It keeps Γ_{k−1}, …, Γ_{j+1}, covers (Ω_{j+1}^c∖Z″_j) ∩ Z by layer-j blocks, and puts Γ″_{j−1}, …, Γ″_{h+1}, Γ_h ∩ Z″_{h+1} below, for j = h + 1, …, k. So a point of layer n ≥ h lies in a stage block of layer J with n ≤ J ≤ k, and a point of layer n < h lies in one of layer n. Hence J − n ≤ k − h = N. LFR I does not fix N: "Conditions on N will be formulated in constructions of this section" (p.177 l.31–32). LFR II takes N = R_j in its large-field induction (p.384 l.17), which #3.2.1.2 replaces by N_k. The conditions are:
  {#3.2.1.1} (i) N > N₀ (p.179 l.10). (ii) N ≤ O(1)(log g_k^{−2})^ν with ½ν ≤ p₀ − p₁ − 1, and N so large that L^{−N}N^{1/2}O(1)B₃³B₅M⁶ ≤ ½α ((1.94), p.198 l.54–56). (iii) L^{−N} small against powers of log g_k^{−2}: "L^{−N} ≤ (log g_k^{−2})^{−ν} for sufficiently large ν, or N ≥ (ν/log L) log log g_k^{−2}", with ν ≥ 2p₀ + dr₀ (LFR II p.363 l.39–40), and the same "usual conditions" at p.364 l.15, p.368 l.31, and p.382 l.23. (iv) N ≤ R_k (LFR II p.361 l.12–14), with Bałaban's R_k, the smallest power of L with R_k ≥ (log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.36–39), not the R_k of (R-member). Each is stated at the step k, in g_k. At the R-step k take N := N_k := max(N₀ + 1, N′_k), with N′_k the least integer such that L^{−N′_k} ≤ (log g_k^{−2})^{−ν_L}, and ν_L ≥ max(2p₀ + dr₀, r(1 + β₀) + p₀ − p₁ + 1) fixed so large that (ii)'s lower bound, (iii), and #3.2.1.2's p.382 condition hold. Then (i) holds. L^{N_k} ≤ L²(L+1)(N₀+1)^{β₀}R_k + L(log g_k^{−2})^{ν_L}, by L^{N₀−1} = R_{k−N₀+1} ≤ (L+1)(N₀+1)^{β₀}R_k (p.192 l.18–19). So N_k = O(log log g_k^{−2}), and (ii)'s upper bound and (iv) hold for g_k small.
  {#3.2.1.2} *Bałaban's N = R_j and the choice N_k (derived).* LFR II states its large-field induction (1.80) for the least K such that S^K(Z) satisfies (i), (ii) "with N = R_j" (p.384 l.14–17). LFR I p.198 l.57 says (1.94)'s two conditions "can be satisfied by N to the positive power of log g_k^{−2}". With N = R_j, L^N is not a power of log g_k^{−2}, so #3.2.3 needs N_k in its place. Every use of N in LFR II is of one of three kinds. (a) Upper bounds: N ≤ R_k (p.361 l.14); R_{j−N+1} ≤ (L+1)N^{β₀}R_j and N^{β₀} ≤ R_j^{β₀} (p.382 l.4, l.16, l.21; p.383 l.18, l.24); j − N ≤ m (p.381 l.21). (b) Step counts: K ≤ n₀ − j + R_j (p.385 l.4), K = R_{j+1} for a small Z (p.386 l.3), and K ≤ K₂ + n₁ + R_{j+1} (p.387 l.15). They count the steps a domain needs before it satisfies (i)–(ii), which are at most N, and (1.80)'s right side increases with K. (c) Lower bounds through L^{−N}: p.363 l.39–40, p.364 l.15, p.368 l.23–31, and p.382 l.10–23, with LFR I (1.94). With N = N_j ≤ R_j ((iv)), the bounds (a) hold as printed. Each K is at most its value at N = R_j, so the cases of (1.80)'s proof (pp.385–387) go through wherever R_j counts steps, given N_{j+K} ≤ R_j (#3.2.1.2.1). The cube sizes MR_j do not involve N. The bounds (c) are #3.2.1.1's (ii) and (iii), together with p.382 l.21–23: there O(1)(A₀/A₁)B₃²B₅M⁷R_j^{1+β₀}(p₀(g_j)/p₁(g_j))L^{−N} must be below ¼. With R_j ≤ L(log g_j^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)) and p₀(g)/p₁(g) = A₀(log g^{−2})^{p₀−p₁} (CRE (2.4), p.255 l.33; LFR I p.183 l.2), this holds for g small once ν_L > r(1 + β₀) + p₀ − p₁. So N := N_k meets every condition LFR I–II place on N.
    {#3.2.1.2.1} *The remaining uses (derived).* (a) also contains N^{β₀} ≤ R_k^{β₀} (p.362 l.5), L^{(N−1)(d−2)} (p.362 l.35), O(N) ≤ O(R_k) and N^{2+4β₀} (p.366 l.5, l.9), and N(100MN^{β₀}R_j)^d (p.382 l.40). Each increases with N and holds for N_k ≤ R_k ((iv)). N also enters definitions, the boundary layer of width 5M₁L^{−N+1} (p.363 l.9), the N new boundary terms (p.369 l.22), and the space (1.64)–(1.69) [IV] at n = N (p.375 l.24), which hold at every N. The R-operation that removes S^K(Z) runs at step j + K, with N_{j+K}. By CRE (2.7) (p.255 l.53–59), (log g_{j+K}^{−2})^{ν_L} ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_j^{−2})^{ν_L}, so L^{N_{j+K}} ≤ L²(L+1)(N₀+1)^{β₀}R_{j+K} + L(1+β₀)(log g_j^{−2})^{ν_L} (#3.2.1.1). Hence N_{j+K} = O(log log g_j^{−2}) ≤ R_j for g small, since R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)) and R_{j+K} ≤ (1+β₀)R_j·L by (2.7). LFR I also uses N in the proof of (1.89), 22d²max{1, L^{−2N}N^{1/2}O(1)B₃B₅M⁵(1+β₀)}ε_h + 2δ′_k ≤ 22d²ε_h + 2(1+β₀)N^{1/2}(A₁/A₀)(p₁(g_h)/p₀(g_h))ε_h < 23d²ε_h (LFR I p.199 l.30–31). This needs L^{−2N}N^{1/2}O(1)B₃B₅M⁵(1+β₀) ≤ 1 and N^{1/2}p₁(g_h)/p₀(g_h) small, at h = k − N. The same proof needs B₃(1+β₀)(1+(k−j)^{1/2})(A₁/A₀)(p₁(g_j)/p₀(g_j)) < α for h ≤ j ≤ k (p.199 l.47–50), with k − j ≤ N. N_k meets all three for g small. L^{−2N_k} ≤ (log g_k^{−2})^{−2ν_L} and N_k = O(log log g_k^{−2}). p₁/p₀ = A₀^{−1}(log g^{−2})^{p₁−p₀} with p₀ − p₁ > 0 (#3.2.1.2), and the first line of CRE (2.7) with n = k > m = j gives (log g_j^{−2})^{p₁−p₀} ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₁−p₀} for h ≤ j < k, with equality at j = k. So both products are O((log log g_k^{−2})^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{p₁−p₀}), which tends to 0. With p.179 l.10 and (1.94), these are all of LFR I's conditions on N (pp.175–202).
{#3.2.2} *The stage transformations at every point (derived, as #106–#108).* Let x be a point of layer n, and B ∋ x its stage-m block, of layer J. (1) The u-free step is LFR I's stage, U(𝔅_k^{(m)}, ·) at the step's data d_m ((1.18), (1.20), (1.44); #104). It is the axial-gauge minimiser relative to its own base 𝕌, not relative to U₀ (#3.2.2.1). So in 𝕌^{(m)} = 𝕎_m^{G_m} (#104), G_m is u_{L,m} followed by w_m, and w_m takes the axial element relative to U₀ to the one relative to 𝕌. ũ_m is made of w_m^{−1}, u_{L,m}^{−1}, and G, with G = u_Lw step m's frame (#110). At U, G = 1, w_m = 1, and u_{L,m}(U) is real (#115, #3.2.2.1). (2) u_{L,m} is REG's u = u′u₁ from the axial element to 𝕎_m = e^{iη𝓗(d_m)}U₀ (p.89 l.18). As at #106, REG's reasoning (1.70)–(1.72), applied to u′u₁ itself (p.89 l.35), gives u_{L,m}(x) through the logarithms of 𝕎_m's own averaged transports, along paths of at most dL^{i+1} bonds inside B, i < J (p.88 l.36–61). These logarithms are Lipschitz in 𝕎_m's bond variables near 1, with constant 1 + O(ε) (BCH), and averaging does not increase differences. So log u_{L,m}(x) changes between U and 𝐔 by at most 4dL^{J+1}η sup_B|Δ𝓗(d_m)|. Here Δ𝓗(d_m) = ΔA_L + η^{−1}ΔF(d_{m+1}, s̃), with |A_L| ≤ O(C_GS_L)ε_{m+1} (#120), and ΔF is bounded by step m's (a)–(b) (#118–#119), whose (b) carries O(c_χB̄_GL^N + c′_CC_G)S_Lδ′_j. (3) #108 bounds G at every point of B in stage m's blocking: |λ̂(x)| ≤ B_GL^Jηε_{m+1}. (4) So, up to a BCH remainder of second order in (2), (3), and #3.2.2.1's bound on w_m, ũ_m changes at x by at most O(B̄_G)L^Jη(ε_{m+1} + O(L^Nδ′_j)Σ_{m′≥m+1}(ε_{m′} + ε_{m′+1})), with B̄_G := B_G + C_uLC_GS_L and C_u depending on d, so large that C_uLC_GS_L covers (2)'s ΔA_L part and w_m's 4dC_G, since L, S_L ≥ 1. The O(L^Nδ′_j) hides the constant 4dL·O(c_χB̄_G + c′_CC_G)S_L of (2)'s ΔF part, since L^N ≥ 1. That constant depends on d, L, r_B, S_L, c_χ, c′_C, c_C, c_D, C_G, and B̄_G (r_B through #98), and g is taken small after these. This holds at every point. That includes the block points of the later steps' collars, where #116 conjugates the sources, and every plaquette of #6.2. Since L^Jη ≤ L^NL^nη (#3.2.1), it is #116.1's bound with the factor L^N.
  {#3.2.2.1} *The factor w_m (derived).* (1.44) is VAR Proposition 9 (p.309) at the base 𝕌, VAR's U_k(V′V₀) with U_k(V₀) = 𝕌, VAR taking "U_0=U_k(V_0)" for this pair (p.305 l.36). Its Landau form is exp iηℍ^{(m)}𝕌, and the transformation "back to the axial gauge" gives "a gauge field configuration in the axial gauge, which we denote also by U_k(V′V₀)" (VAR p.307 l.2–4), axial in Ax_k(𝔅_k^{(m)}, 𝕌) (VAR p.280 l.44). LFR I writes (1.34) "in the standard way, as in (1.30)", the same representation, and bounds its u_j through (106)–(108) of [12] (p.184 l.36, l.55–58), and CMP98 (106) (p.33 l.44) is that transformation, given through the axial contours. U_k(d_m) = 𝕎_m^{u_{L,m}} is the axial element relative to U₀ (#103). Both have the plain data d_m, so w_m lies in VAR's group (4), and w_m(y) = 1 at the stage-m block points (#103). Let x ∈ B, with x = x_0, x_1, …, x_J = y and x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}) (REG p.79 l.4). By (1.20), the product in REG (1.19) telescopes to V̄^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n})(V̄_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}))^{−1}, with V₀ the base. So a configuration is axial relative to a base exactly when its layer-n averaged transports along the axial contours equal the base's. The averages are covariant, \overline{V^v}^n_b = v(b_−)V̄^n_bv^{−1}(b_+) (CMP98 (11), p.19 l.13; (70), p.29 l.36), also at G^c-valued configurations (p.27 l.2–3). For 𝕌^{(m)} = U_k(d_m)^{w_m} this gives w_m(x_n) = 𝕌̄^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n})^{−1}w_m(x_{n+1})Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}). Write 𝕌̄^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = e^{iτ_n}Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}). Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n} has at most dL layer-n bonds, and 𝕌 = e^{iηA_Φ}U₀, so |τ_n| ≤ dLC_GL^nηε_{m+1}(1 + O(ε_{m+1})), as for the block data (#97). Ū_0^n is real, so conjugation by it preserves the norm. Starting from w_m(y) = 1, |log w_m(x)| ≤ Σ_{n<J}|τ_n|(1 + O(ε_{m+1})) ≤ 4dC_GL^Jηε_{m+1} for L ≥ 2. At U, 𝕌 = U₀ (#115), so every τ_n vanishes and w_m(U) = 1. So w_m changes between U and 𝐔 by at most 4dC_GL^Jηε_{m+1} at every point of B, a bound of #106's form. It uses no gauge condition on U₀: U₀ is a real minimiser with its own data (#103).
{#3.2.3} *The consequences (derived).* With the factor L^N: #116's conjugation part is at most O(B̄_G)L^NL^jηΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1})|s|. #119 then reads O(c_χB̄_GL^N + c′_CC_G)S_Lδ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), and #121 reads dev_n ≤ C_*L^NS_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}). #122's induction closes, since L^N(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j ≤ L^N(N + 2)(N + 1)(1+β₀)²N^{β₀}δ′_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2} (#3.2.1). *The sum* (#123) is at most C_*O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²λα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond, which is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} for g small. #116.2's size of s̃, the gradient chain (#144), and the current chain (#150) take the same factor through (b), and close in the same way. #6.2's bound on a layer-n block becomes O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}. #6.13 needs it below ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)}, which holds once O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k}) ≤ β/8, since L ≥ 2. That is a smallness of g.
{#3.3} **Derived, not reviewed: the carried insertions of every species on the value space (1.65) ((6B), *15.5*, #37, #613).** (6B) bounds the parts with 𝒦 ≠ ∅ of the carried insertions of every species (cross, dev, and attached; #37, #613) through D2.7 15.5(i) and R-far″(α′) of D2.7 15.5(ii), derived, not reviewed (D2.7 at c384e6a20d7a), in the form |D^{𝒦}| ≤ ‖D‖(C_αe^{δMt_k})^{|𝒦|+1}e^{−δℓ(D,𝒦)} (#613; δ := δ_α/M, #728.1, not #46's δ). (α′) places its s-points within the slack that this note's (R-eval) points leave (#75, #76, #166, #6.13), and those rest on the deep-layer bound of #3.2 (its frame factor L^N derived and reviewed, the rest of the chain derived and reviewed in packet C1, #90–#128, except the gradient and the current on the newer layers, derived, not reviewed, #140, #145).

{#5} **(R-eval-old): the mixed term on the old part of Λ (derived; its own steps reviewed, #5.1–#5.11 with #5.10.1–#5.10.6 in packet PV (PV12, PV17), the passage in packet PL, and the contacts draft in packet CT; as a whole it rests on LF's (KW) (#5.4, #5.8 (1)–(2), #5.10.5, #5.11) and LF's block size (#5.8 (a), (c); #5.10.5 (i)–(ii)), derived, not reviewed in LF).** On Λ^{old} := Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, Bałaban's U_k^0 takes the real integration variables V_h of T′_k(X) (LFR II (1.50), p.370 l.4; p.378 l.31), which only χ_{h,1/2} restricts, through the curvature of (1, V_h) (LFR I (1.88), p.197 l.46–48), together with T_h. The post-R configuration takes its own averages there.
{#5.1} What is needed is a bound on the part T₂^{old} of the response's mixed term (*The mixed term* in (R-eval)) that is sourced by this real data difference. Either of two inputs would close it: a G-valued gauge u, trivial off Λ, with sup_{Λ^{old}}|log V_h(M^h(U)^u)^{−1}| → 0 as g → 0 on the support of the R-step's indicators; or a reformulation of the evaluation points that keeps V_h out of the comparison. A bound on how the minimiser's linear response depends on real data of size O(1) gives Λ-decay but leaves the term proportional to λ, so it does not close the gap.
{#5.2} Bałaban's own chain does not supply either input. It cuts off the dependence on V″⌈_{(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c} only on Z^c (LFR II p.370 l.14–15; U_{1,2} of (1.53) equals U_1 on Z^c, p.371 l.26–27), and on Λ^∼ his extended U_k^0 has "the properties of the configuration U_k″" (p.375 l.13–14). His (1.65) lands in the spaces of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69), whose phase radius on an old layer m exceeds the top layer's by the factor L^{k−m}α_{1,m}/α_{1,k} (LFR I p.190 l.27). 𝒟^λ_k caps this factor at λ, so (1.65) does not give the landing in 𝒟^λ_k on Λ^{old}.
{#5.3} A third input would close it on the layers where λ^{(k)}_n = λ. At the points where the next T-step evaluates, every datum has phase per fine bond at most λ(1+β₀)L^{−1}α_{1,k}L^{−k} plus the 𝐇-part (#54). So a bound C_{resp,∞} on the sup-norm response of U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·) at any real base (VAR (190) summed over the sources, in fine-phase units), with C_{resp,∞}(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/4, would bound U_k^0(M(𝐔))'s complex part there directly, with no comparison of bases, once λ also absorbs C_{resp,∞} times the Λ-data's M⁵ amplification. VAR's O(1) depends on d and L (p.299 l.26), so the print does not give this. Against the weighted room of #52.1 this input holds at every site of Λ, the contacts included. Every site uses the passage at U₀ ≠ 1 (#5.8 (a); the passage draft's §9, reviewed and cleared). The input against the weighted room is derived and reviewed (the contacts draft, packet CT, cleared), given LF's (KW), derived, not reviewed in LF. On the layers k₀ < n ≤ k, T₂^{old}'s phase is bounded through its decay away from Λ^{old} (#5.8 (c); derived and reviewed, packet CT).
{#5.4} The large-field note (WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md) bounds the response of the same minimisers (U_k^0 is among its members) in a weighted norm, not in the supremum. It bounds the finer averages of the linearised member map, abs(Q_{i′}HB) ≤ C_HL^{−(i−i′)}‖B‖ in the units of the data, "in a norm that every step of the chain preserves", and states that "the supremum does not serve" (its #0.1.10). The norm is ‖f‖_W := sup_xabs(f(x))/W(x), with W(x) := (1 + log L)^p(L/t(x))^κ(L/t₃(x))^{1−κ} and κ := max(1 − α/2, 1 − ν) < 1. Here t is the distance to the faces, edges, and vertices of Λ, and t₃ the distance to the contacts between distinct local components of Λ^c, with ρ₀ := C(d)log L ≤ t ≤ t₃ ≤ L (its #0.1.18). In VAR's problem these are the contacts of codimension ≥ 2 (its #0.1.29). Its bound is ‖u‖_W ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^pC_H^{reg}θ_B for the response u to data B, gradient part included, with C_H^{reg} ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{p′} "with constants of d only and no dependence on M or β₀" (its #0.1.27, (KW), with (K0)(f), #K0.f; derived, not reviewed). It is taken at the representative A_N := U′_ax + ∂ζ^N (its #0.1.4). With C_H := C(d)(1 + log L)^pC_H^{reg} (its #0.1.10, #KW), C_H ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{p+p′}, the form that the response needs (table #T11.17), derived, not reviewed. Leaving the weighted norm costs C(d)(1 + log L)^pL once (its #0.1.10).
{#5.11} *Away from the contacts (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* Since t ≥ ρ₀, W ≤ (1 + log L)^pL^κ(L/t₃)^{1−κ}. So where t₃ ≥ L^{1/2}, W ≤ (1 + log L)^pL^{(1+κ)/2}, and the sup-norm response there is C_{resp,∞} ≤ (1 + log L)^pL^{(1+κ)/2}C_H = o(L), since a datum on an i-block has L^{−i} times its phase per fine bond. At the contacts the response on the contact bond is L times the datum, and the weight L/t₃ is not removable by a gauge (its #0.1.10, #0.1.28). So C_{resp,∞}(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/4 fails there against the rooms of #49–#50, and the third input does not close the gap at the sites with t₃ < L^{1/2} in those rooms. Against the weighted room of #52.1 it holds there at the flat base (#5.8 (1); derived and reviewed, packet CT), with the data bounded by #47 where w = 1 (#5.8 (b)). At a real base U₀ ≠ 1 it holds through the passage at U₀ (#5.8 (a); derived and reviewed, packet PL).
{#5.5} At the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1} with j ≥ 1 its chain uses the representative A_♮ := A_N + ∂ω_♮ of (K0)(g)(6), ω_♮ ∈ N(Q′), and A_♮ = A_N "at the points of R₀ outside the blocks of Λ₁ that meet R₁" (its #K0.g6 (vi)), which include Λ₀/Λ₁ (its #0.1.30). Its former open item S5 (WILSON_IT1_LF_TRIAGE_R38_2026_11_03.md l.47), the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1} with j ≥ 1, where a coarser block at multiscale distance O(1) carried L^{d/2−1} times the local-unit sources, is now (K0)(g), also derived, not reviewed: the bound holds there in the units of L^j, and the chain's norm is ‖·‖_{W_ms} with W_ms := max_jW_j.
{#5.6} The large-field note's bound is at a real base that is regular in VAR's sense. U_k^0(M(U)) is regular on all of Λ^{old}. LFR I (1.88) splits each component of (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Ω_h by the set P of squares where the curvature of (1, V_h) is at least ½ε_h(L^{k−h}η)² (p.197 l.44–51). The components with P ≠ ∅ carry "new large fields" and are excluded from Z, and on the remaining components, which form the new Z, the factor is χ_{h,1/2} of the whole domain (p.198 l.2). Λ ⊂ Z (p.192 l.43), so P = ∅ on Λ^{old}, the restriction by χ_{h,1/2} stated above. There Bałaban's estimates (1.91) (p.198) and (1.95) (p.199) bound the curvature of U″_{k,Z} by (½ + 2α + O(αε_h))ε_h(L^{k−h}η)², with α the small absolute constant of (1.90) and (1.94) (p.198 l.27). By the properties quoted above, U_k^0 inherits this regularity.
{#5.7} So at the sites with t₃ ≥ L^{1/2}, given the passage from A_N to V̂ (#5.10), C_{resp,∞}(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/4 holds for L large given d. The Λ-data's M⁵ part does not pass through C_{resp,∞}: it is T₁'s data, whose response is at most C_ΛM⁵e^{−δ dist(b,Λ)}s_Z by (190) (#46, #164), and λ absorbs C_ΛM⁵ by #72's choice (#73, #166; C_Λ's frame bound derived, not reviewed, #41.1–#41.2), and Λ lies on the layers n ≤ k₀, where λ^{(k)}_n = λ since c′^{N₀} ≥ λ_w ≥ λ for g small (*Where it lands*, #52.1).
{#5.8} So T₂^{old} comes down to the items (a)–(c) below: (a), (1), (b), and (c) are derived and reviewed (packets PL and CT, cleared), given LF's (KW) and block size, derived, not reviewed in LF (LF #0.1.27, LF #4.21). (1) At the sites with t₃ < L^{1/2}, near the contacts, the third input of #5.3 holds against the room enlarged at the free bonds by the weight w of #52.1. There the response is ≤ ⅛(1−β)λw at the flat base, and at a real base U₀ ≠ 1 given (a). The data carry no weight: they lie in Z, on the layer k of their own post-R layering, where w = 1, and #47 bounds them, at every block ((b) below). The consumers take λ_w = λL (WILSON_IT1_CONTACTS_FREE_BOND_WEIGHT_DRAFT_2026_11_04.md, §§2–4; derived and reviewed, packet CT). (2) At the other sites, the passage of the large-field note's bound from A_N to VAR's representative V̂ = u_R, that is, a sup bound |∂ω| = o(L) times the datum for the block-anchored gauge ω (#5.10). The passage is derived and reviewed at one scale and the flat base (#5.10.1–#5.10.6; packet PV, cleared). The o(L) bound rests on #5.10.6 near the contacts of W on ∂Λ, at the sites of #5.7 included: there a block within O(1) block distances contains a contact, and without the block averages the bound is only sup W·‖A_N‖_W. The faces and contacts of W lie on ∂Λ, the interface with Λ₀, which the one-scale model does not contain. So #5.10.6 holds there with the operator bounds of (i) below. At Λ₀/Λ₁, where it holds through the weighted count of the passage draft's §3 (b₀), the result lands in the room λw of #52.1 (the contacts draft, §3). Of the passage, the layered geometry and a real base U₀ ≠ 1 are derived and reviewed (#5.10.5 (i)–(ii); the passage draft's §§1–9, packet PL, cleared). Derived and reviewed: (a) the passage at U₀ ≠ 1, uniformly in n = L^j (the passage draft's §9): at a regularity ε₁′ → 0 as g → 0, near each site z the base is replaced by a cut-off base Ũ₀, which equals U₀ near z and is regular in one global gauge, built from the regular gauge of VAR Theorem 1 on a cube of VAR's classes about z; §2's comparison, with Lemma A′ and Lemma R at U₀ (Kato's inequality for the zero order, c split at a point with no Calderón–Zygmund logarithm, and a supremum weighted by the distance to the boundary), passes from U₀ to the two-layer problem at Ũ₀; and an energy estimate with Lemma A′ and Lemma R at Ũ₀ on the fine minimisers, which uses only the local operators, compares that with the flat two-layer problem within C(d, L, M, R)ε_Q times the data; for g small given L, M, R, and M_{CRE} (this note's M) and RM ≥ C(d)R₁M₁L, which on the problem's layering, R = LK_{CRE} − 1 and the passage's M = M′ (LF #4.22–#4.24, derived, not reviewed in LF, LF #4.21; #99's K_{CRE} and M′), read g small given L, K_{CRE}, M′, and this note's M, which enters the base's regularity ε₁′ through B₅M⁶ (LFR II p.378 l.14; #6.16), together with K_{CRE} ≥ C(d) + 1 and M′ ≥ L, lower bounds on K_{CRE} and M′ (LF #4.22.1; the passage draft's §9, *Result of (ii)*); (b) the supremum of the data on every block that the T-step reads, the blocks that the large-field chain reads in supremum only included (LF #A.11.42's exception): the data are the post-R point's, Λ ⊂ Z lies on the layer k of their layering (LFR II (1.33), p.365 l.2–7), where w = 1, and #47 bounds them (the contacts draft, §2); (c) T₂^{old}'s decay away from Λ^{old}: its phase on the layers k₀ < n ≤ k and its gradient and current where μ^{(k)}_n < μ are at most (1−β)λ^{(k)}_n/64 times the units, through PBG's random walk expansion of H₁, whose terms depend on the base only near their walks (Corollary 3.8, p.410 l.33–34; p.427 l.14–21), the two bases' nearness to pure gauges on the walks' cubes (VAR Theorem 1, p.279 l.17–27), and the layer distance (n − k₀)M/M₁ to Λ^{old} (#166), for M large given d, L, LF's block size M′ (LF #4.22–#4.24, derived, not reviewed in LF, LF #4.21), and M₁, and g small given λ (the contacts draft, §4a). T₂^{old}'s gradient and current (D*D) are bounded through VAR (190)'s gradient and D*D entries, which carry the constant K fixed after L, and its current is proportional to λ through the phase room α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k}, so against the unweighted rooms they carry the factors KL and KLλ_R. They land in the gradient and current rooms weighted by μ^{(k)}_n on every layer, against the supremum of the data θ_B ≤ 2λ, which uses only #49, where μ^{(k)}_n = μ (#52.1, #166; the contacts draft, §4a, from VAR (190), p.308 l.37–47; derived and reviewed, packet CT), and, where μ^{(k)}_n < μ, by (c).
{#5.9} In REG's terms, C_{resp,∞} is the norm B₀ of H(U₀) in |A|_{(−1)} ≤ B₀(|J|_{(−3)} + |B₁|) (REG p.86 l.52–53), restricted to the data. REG prints B₀ as depending "on d and L only" (p.87 l.16), and it enters REG's own bounds as B₁ = 5dLB₀ ((1.62), (1.75)–(1.76)).
{#5.10} The large-field note's bound covers the whole response at A_N, its gradient part included: it writes u = v_{x̄} + w_{x̄} + ∂₁λ on each cube Q_{6L}(x̄), with v_{x̄} taken locally (its #0.1.25–#0.1.27). Bałaban's representative u_R (VAR (152)–(153)) is u_R = A_N + ∂ω, with ω block-anchored and abs(Im ηω) ≤ C(L, M)ε₁ pointwise (its #0.1.3, #0.1.4, #0.1.14). The landing here is read at VAR's Landau representative V̂ ((iii), *The Landau representatives*), which satisfies REG's Landau condition R(U₀)D*a = 0. V̂ is u_R: both are the configuration U₁ = U′^{u^{−1}} of REG's Theorem 2 (p.83) for the pair (U′U₀, U₀), with U₀ the real base, u the unique transformation with the normalisation (1.29) for which U₁ obeys (1.36)–(1.39), among them the Landau condition R(U₀)D*_{U₀}A = 0 ((1.38), p.82). REG states it "for arbitrary U₀, U′U₀" satisfying (1.33)–(1.35), and its Theorem 4 (p.88), from which #102 takes V̂, implies Theorem 2 (p.88 l.8). VAR (152)–(153) (p.301 l.50–61) is the case U₀ = 1 of the same theorem ("Theorem 2 of the paper [6]", [6] being REG, VAR p.309 l.26), and the large-field note's u_R is taken relative to U_k (its #A.9). So the passage from A_N to V̂ is the block-anchored gauge ω = ζ^R − ζ^N, with ω = 0 on Λ₀ and Q′ω = KU′_ax (the large-field note's #0.1.4, #A.9). The missing input is a sup bound |∂ω| = o(L) times the datum's phase per fine bond: that note bounds ω through u_R itself, whose complex part it takes from VAR (190) with a constant C(d, L, M) (its #A.9), which does not give o(L). #5.10.1–#5.10.6 supply this bound at one scale and the flat base; the bound o(L) near the contacts of W, at the sites of #5.7 included, rests on #5.10.6, which reads the faces and contacts of W on ∂Λ and so depends on part (i) of #5.10.5 (derived and reviewed, packet PL). The layered geometry and the real base are stated at #5.10.5.
{#5.10.1} *The closed form of ω (derived and reviewed, packet PV; one block scale n := L^j, flat base, linear order, d = 4).* The computation is formal on ℤ^d, where Δ^{−2} does not exist (its symbol abs(θ)^{−4} is not integrable at 0) and τ below diverges for h that does not decay. It is made exact as on PRT2's torus, with μ orthogonal to the constants (p.241 l.2–7): H′ is the operator of the symbol (2.132) (#5.10.2.1), whose kernel decays exponentially, and Th := Σ_yh_yK(·; y) with the kernels K(b; y) of #5.10.3, built from τ_y := Δ^{−1}Eδ_y, which exists for d ≥ 3, and summable in y. Q′ is the average over the blocks B(y) of side n, E the block-constant extension, and Δ = ∂*∂ the scalar Laplacian. REG's R(U₀) is the orthogonal projection onto ΔN(Q′) (REG p.80, before (1.27)), so the Landau condition R∂*u_R = 0 says ⟨∂*(A_N + ∂ω), Δν⟩ = 0 for every ν ∈ N(Q′). This is the Euler–Lagrange equation of the least ‖∂*A_N + Δω‖ over ω with Q′ω = μ, μ := KU′_ax (#5.10). By the large-field note's #0.1.4, ∂*A_N = Eh with h one number per block. Put τ := Δ^{−1}Eh. Then ‖∂*A_N + Δω‖ = ‖Δ(τ + ω)‖, and τ + ω is the least ‖Δλ‖ with Q′λ = μ + Q′τ, that is, H′(μ + Q′τ) with H′ := Δ^{−2}Q′*(Q′Δ^{−2}Q′*)^{−1} (PRT2 (2.101)–(2.102), p.241). So ω = H′μ − (I − P)τ with P := H′Q′, and ∂ω = ∂H′μ − Th with T := ∂(I − P)Δ^{−1}E.
{#5.10.2} *The operators (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* (a) H′ and its derivatives up to third order are bounded and decay exponentially, with constants and a rate of d only, in the units of the block: derived from the momentum form (2.132) at #5.10.2.1 (PRT2 states the constants of d only for H′ itself, p.241 l.14–15, and the rate of d only for its derivatives, p.246 l.28–29). In fine units, with δ = δ(d), abs(P(x, z)) ≤ C(d)n^{−d}e^{−δ|x−z|/n}, abs(∂_xP(x, z)) ≤ C(d)n^{−d−1}e^{−δ|x−z|/n}, and abs(∂H′μ(x)) ≤ C(d)n^{−1}Σ_ye^{−δ|x−y|/n}abs(μ(y)). (b) P reproduces the polynomials of degree ≤ 4. With e_θ(x) := e^{iθ·x}, u(θ) := Π_μn^{−1}Σ_{k=0}^{n−1}e^{iθ_μk} and S₂(θ) := Σ_l abs(u(θ + l))²Δ(θ + l)^{−2}, l over (2π/n){0, …, n−1}^d, the form (2.132) gives Pe_θ = Σ_lm_l(θ)e_{θ+l} with m_l(θ) := u(θ)ū(θ + l)Δ(θ + l)^{−2}/S₂(θ). For l ≠ 0 some l_μ ≠ 0, so u(θ + l) vanishes linearly at θ = 0, while Δ(θ + l) ≥ c(n) > 0 near θ = 0. Hence Δ(θ)²S₂(θ) = abs(u(θ))² + O(abs(θ)⁶), m_0 = 1 + O(abs(θ)⁶), and m_l = O(abs(θ)⁵) for l ≠ 0, all analytic near θ = 0. P's kernel decays exponentially, so x^α = (−i∂_θ)^αe_θ at θ = 0 may be differentiated under P, and Px^α = x^α for abs(α) ≤ 4. (c) For a block y, τ_y := Δ^{−1}Eδ_y is harmonic off B(y). By Lawler–Limic Theorem 4.3.1 (p.81–82) and Corollary 4.3.3 (p.84), abs(G(z)) ≤ C(d)max(abs(z), 1)^{2−d} and abs(∇G(z)) ≤ C(d)max(abs(z), 1)^{1−d}. So abs(τ_y) ≤ C(d)n² and abs(∇τ_y) ≤ C(d)n everywhere, and abs(∇τ_y(x)) ≤ C(d)n^dD^{1−d} at the distance D from x to B(y). Lawler–Limic Theorem 6.3.8 (p.131), applied to the harmonic function ∇τ_y on balls of radius D/4, gives abs(∇³τ_y) ≤ C(d)n^dD^{−1−d} within the distance D/4 of x, once D ≥ D₁n with D₁ = D₁(d).
{#5.10.2.1} *The bounds of (a) from (2.132) (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* In the notation of (b), (2.132) reads (H′μ)^(θ + l) = ū(θ + l)Δ(θ + l)^{−2}S₂(θ)^{−1}μ̂(θ), θ in the dual of the block lattice. So H′(x, y) = (2π)^{−d}∫dq e^{−iq·y}Σ_ke^{i(q+2πk)·x/n}h_k(q) for a fine site x and a block y (block-lattice coordinates), with θ = q/n, l = 2πk/n, q ∈ [−π, π)^d, k ∈ (−n/2, n/2]^d, and h_k := ū(θ + l)Δ(θ)²/(Δ(θ + l)²Δ(θ)²S₂(θ)). Continue in q to abs(Im q_μ) ≤ δ, with u(−θ − l) in place of ū(θ + l). (1) For k_μ ≠ 0, (q_μ + 2πk_μ)/n lies at distance ≥ π abs(k_μ)/n from 2πℤ, so the μ-factor (e^{iq_μ} − 1)/(n(e^{i(q_μ+2πk_μ)/n} − 1)) of u(θ + l) is ≤ C/abs(k_μ), and Re Δ(θ + l) ≥ c abs(k)²/n² − Cδ²/n² ≥ c′abs(k)²/n² for k ≠ 0. (2) For k_μ = 0 the μ-factor is analytic with derivatives bounded uniformly in n, and ≥ 2/π at real q. (3) abs(Δ(θ)) ≤ C/n². So in Δ(θ)²S₂(θ) = u(θ)u(−θ) + Σ_{k≠0}u(θ + l)u(−θ − l)Δ(θ)²/Δ(θ + l)², the term k ≠ 0 and its q-derivatives are ≤ C(d)abs(k)^{−4}Π_{μ:k_μ≠0}abs(k_μ)^{−2}, and Σ_{k≠0}abs(k)^{−a}Π_{μ:k_μ≠0}abs(k_μ)^{−1} ≤ C(d, a) for a > 0, since Σ_{abs(k)≤R}Π_{μ:k_μ≠0}abs(k_μ)^{−1} ≤ (3 + 2 log R)^d. At real q, Δ(θ)²S₂(θ) ≥ abs(u(θ))² ≥ (2/π)^{2d}, so for δ = δ(d) small it stays ≥ ½(2/π)^{2d} on the strip. Hence h_k is analytic on the strip, abs(h_0) ≤ C(d), and abs(h_k) ≤ C(d)abs(k)^{−4}Π_{μ:k_μ≠0}abs(k_μ)^{−1} for k ≠ 0, uniformly in n. A fine difference multiplies the term k by e^{i(q_ν+2πk_ν)/n} − 1, of size ≤ C(1 + abs(k))/n, so a differences of order a ≤ 3 cost ≤ C(1 + abs(k))^a/n^a, and Σ_k(1 + abs(k))^3abs(h_k) ≤ C(d). Shifting Im q_μ to δ sign(x_μ/n − y_μ) gives abs(∂^aH′(x, y)) ≤ C(d)n^{−a}e^{−δΣ_μabs(x_μ/n − y_μ)}, a ≤ 3, with C(d) and δ(d) free of n and L; P = H′Q′ adds the factor n^{−d} of Q′.
{#5.10.3} *The bound on T (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* Let K(b; y) := ∂(I − P)τ_y(b) for a fine bond b at x, and r := D/n. If D < D₁n: P reproduces constants, so K(b; y) = ∂τ_y(b) − ∂_bP(τ_y − τ_y(x)), and abs(τ_y(z) − τ_y(x)) ≤ C(d)n abs(z − x), so abs(K(b; y)) ≤ C(d)n + C(d)n^{−d−1}Σ_ze^{−δ|z−x|/n}n abs(z − x) ≤ C(d)n. If D ≥ D₁n: let q be the Newton (forward-difference) polynomial of τ_y at x of degree 2. Then (I − P)q = 0 by #5.10.2 (b), so K(b; y) = ∂ρ(b) − ∂_bPρ with ρ := τ_y − q. By the discrete Taylor formula and #5.10.2 (c), abs(ρ(x + w)) ≤ C(d)abs(w)³n^dD^{−1−d} for abs(w) ≤ D/4, and abs(∂ρ(b)) ≤ C(d)n^dD^{−1−d}: ∂ρ vanishes on the forward bonds at x and is a third difference on the backward ones. For abs(w) > D/4, abs(ρ(x + w)) ≤ C(d)n²(1 + abs(w)/D)², against e^{−δ|w|/n}. So abs(K(b; y)) ≤ C(d)(n^{d+2}D^{−1−d} + n^dD^{−1−d} + n(1 + r)^{d−1}e^{−δr/4}) ≤ C(d)n(1 + r)^{−1−d}. With a bump φ_y supported in B(y), equal to 1 on its middle half, with abs(∂φ_y) ≤ C(d)/n, h_yΣφ_y = ⟨∂*A_N, φ_y⟩ = ⟨A_N, ∂φ_y⟩, so n abs(h_y) ≤ C(d)avg_{B(y)}abs(A_N). Hence abs(Th(b)) ≤ C(d)Σ_y(1 + r_y)^{−1−d}avg_{B(y)}abs(A_N), with r_y the distance from x to B(y) in the units of n.
{#5.10.4} *The bound on μ (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* μ(y) = KU′_ax(y) = KA_N(y) − K∂ζ^N(y), since U′_ax = A_N − ∂ζ^N (the large-field note's #0.1.4). K is the composite over the levels n′ < j of RG I's average over the shortest contours 𝐆 (RG I (0.4), p.252 l.28–32, p.253; the large-field note's #A.8: "the composites over the scales keep this form"). So KA(y) is the average, over the chains y = x_j, x_{j−1}, …, x_0 with x_{n′} uniform in B(x_{n′+1}), of the sums over n′ of the level-n′ averaged line integrals of A along 𝐆(x_{n′+1}, x_{n′}), the level-n′ bond variables being Bałaban's full averages, the composites of the one-step averages up to the level n′ (REG's Ũ′ⁿ, (1.20), p.79 l.12). Such a contour has at most dL level-n′ bonds. The bound uses the masses of the linear functionals, not the lengths of the fine contours. The fine contours of the composite of (0.4), level by level, have lengths up to (d(L − 1) + L)^{n′}. The masses are bounded through a cancellation. A level-n′ averaged bond variable is Q̃_{n′}A, with Q̃_{n′} = Q_{n′} − D̄K_{n′} (the large-field note's #A.8), Q_{n′} the plain average of the line integrals over the block's parallel lines of length L^{n′}, of mass L^{n′}. Composing one step gives Q̃_{n′+1} = Q^{(1)}Q̃_{n′} − D̄K^{(1)}Q̃_{n′}, with K^{(1)} the one-step contour average, and since Q^{(1)}Q_{n′} = Q_{n′+1} and Q^{(1)}D̄ = D̄Q′, K_{n′+1} = Q′K_{n′} + K^{(1)}Q̃_{n′}. K^{(1)}D̄f = Q′f − f at the block point, the contours from the centre telescoping, so K_{n′+1} = K_{n′} + K^{(1)}Q_{n′} and K_{n′}A(y) = Σ_{m<n′}K^{(1)}(Q_mA)(y). The contours of K^{(1)} at the level m have on average at most dL/4 bonds of the level m, each carrying Q_mA, of mass L^m, so mass(K_{n′}) ≤ (d/4)L^{n′}·L/(L − 1) ≤ (d/2)L^{n′} and mass(Q̃_{n′}) ≤ (1 + d)L^{n′}. So abs(KA_N(y)) ≤ C(d)Σ_{n′<j}L^{n′+1}sup_{B̃(y)}abs(A_N) ≤ C(d)n sup_{B̃(y)}abs(A_N), with B̃(y) the union of B(y) and its neighbouring blocks. Linearised, the one-step average maps ∂ζ on a coarse bond c to ζ(c_+) − ζ(c_−), the values at the coarse points themselves: the contours from the two ends cancel against the transported bond (RG I (0.4), p.252 l.28–32). So the full average at the level n′ maps ∂ζ to the gradient of ζ restricted to the level-n′ points, as REG's covariance (1.30) (p.81 l.27) states for its averages, with the transformation u taken at the end points. Hence the level-n′ term of K∂ζ^N is ζ^N(x_{n′}) − ζ^N(x_{n′+1}), and the terms telescope. REG's axial condition (1.19) (p.79 l.4–8), a condition on the full averages (1.20), linearised at U₀ = 1, says that the level-n′ averaged line integral of U′_ax along REG's contour Γ_{x_{n′+1},x_{n′}} vanishes, that is, ζ^N(x_{n′}) − ζ^N(x_{n′+1}) is the level-n′ averaged line integral of A_N along Γ_{x_{n′+1},x_{n′}}, a contour of at most dL level-n′ bonds. So abs(K∂ζ^N(y)) ≤ C(d)n sup_{B̃(y)}abs(A_N), and abs(μ(y)) ≤ C(d)n sup_{B̃(y)}abs(A_N). More precisely, abs(μ(y)) ≤ C(d)n𝒜_y, with 𝒜_y the average of abs(A_N) against the absolute weights of these functionals, taken in the cancelled form above (the plain averages Q_m along the contours of K^{(1)}, and Q_{n′} with D̄K_{n′} for the axial terms), normalised by their total mass. μ(y) is the average of the level-wise differences between the 𝐆 line integrals and the axial line integrals of A_N between the same points, that is, of averaged loop integrals, which are gauge invariant. So the bound does not depend on the gauge in which A_N is written.
{#5.10.5} *The passage at one scale (derived and reviewed, packet PV), and what remains.* By #5.10.1–#5.10.4, at one scale and the flat base, for a fine bond b at x, abs(∂ω(b)) ≤ C(d)Σ_y[e^{−δr_y}𝒜_y + (1 + r_y)^{−1−d}avg_{B(y)}abs(A_N)] ≤ C(d)[sup_y𝒜_y + sup_yavg_{B(y)}abs(A_N)], since Σ_ye^{−δr_y} and Σ_y(1 + r_y)^{−1−d} are ≤ C(d), with C(d) and δ(d) free of n, L, and j. In particular sup abs(∂ω) ≤ C(d)sup abs(A_N): the passage from A_N to V̂ costs a factor of d only, and it reads abs(A_N) only through averages over blocks and along contours. With #5.10.6 and the large-field note's bound (#5.4; LF's (KW), derived, not reviewed in LF), abs(∂ω) ≤ C(d, κ)(1 + log L)^p‖A_N‖_W ≤ C(d, κ)(1 + log L)^{2p}C_H^{reg}θ_B = o(L)θ_B at every site, the contacts included. Two parts of the passage are in WILSON_IT1_PASSAGE_LAYERED_DRAFT_2026_11_04.md: (i) and (ii) are derived and reviewed (packet PL, cleared; #5.8 (a)). (i) The layered geometry, with ω = 0 on Λ₀ and blocks of side L^j on the layer j, which changes H′ near the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1} (PBG's layered constants depend on d and L, p.397 l.37): there abs(∂ω(b)) ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^qL^{1−α(d)/2}[sup_y𝒜_y + sup_yavg_{B(y)}abs(A_N)], with q = q(d) and α(d) > 0, for M ≥ L and RM ≥ L² (the draft's §§1–8), which on the problem's layering, R = LK_{CRE} − 1 and the draft's M = M′ (LF #4.22–#4.24, derived, not reviewed in LF, LF #4.21; #99), hold for M′ ≥ L (LF #4.22.1; the draft's §9, *Result of (ii)*). With #5.10.6 this is ≤ C(d, κ)(1 + log L)^{q+2p}L^{1−α/2}C_H^{reg}θ_B, still o(L)θ_B, except for the τ-part at Λ₀/Λ₁ (the draft's §3 (b₀)), which lands in the weighted room of #5.8, with the data bounded by #47 (#5.8 (b)). (ii) A real base U₀ ≠ 1: the same bounds hold with C(d) doubled, uniformly in n, for g small given L, M, R, and M_{CRE} (this note's M) and RM ≥ C(d)R₁M₁L, that is, g small given L, K_{CRE}, M′, and this note's M (through ε₁′'s B₅M⁶) and K_{CRE} ≥ C(d) + 1 (LF #4.22.1), through a cut-off base Ũ₀ near each site, regular in one global gauge built from VAR Theorem 1's regular gauge, §2's comparison at U₀ with Lemma A′ and Lemma R at U₀, and an energy estimate on the fine minimisers at Ũ₀ against the flat ones, which uses only the local operators (the draft's §9; derived and reviewed; #5.8 (a)).
{#5.10.6} *The contacts in the passage (derived and reviewed, packet PV).* At the contacts abs(A_N) is L times the datum (#5.11), but the passage reads abs(A_N) only through block averages (#5.10.3) and the contour averages 𝒜_y (#5.10.4). With abs(A_N) ≤ W‖A_N‖_W, both are ≤ C(d, κ)(1 + log L)^p‖A_N‖_W, with no factor L, as follows. The block averages of W are ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^p (the large-field note's #0.1.29 and #A.11.42). 𝒜_y is the average of W against the absolute weights of #5.10.4's cancelled form, normalised by their total mass, so it is a convex combination, with the pieces' masses as weights, of the averages of W over the following pieces, each normalised by its own mass: (1) for KA_N(y) = Σ_{m<j}K^{(1)}(Q_mA_N)(y), the level-m contours of K^{(1)} from y, each level-m bond carrying Q_m; (2) for the axial terms, the level-n′ bonds of Γ_{x_{n′+1},x_{n′}}, each carrying Q_{n′}; (3) for their D̄K_{n′} parts, K_{n′}A_N(z) = Σ_{m<n′}K^{(1)}(Q_mA_N)(z) at the two level-n′ end points z of each such bond, that is, the level-m contours of K^{(1)} from z, each level-m bond carrying Q_m. In each piece the contours start at a point c (y, x_{n′+1}, or a level-n′ point z) that is the centre of the block of side L^{m+1} on whose level-m points they end, m the level of the piece. Averaged over the end point, uniform in that block, and, for 𝐆, over the orders of the coordinates (REG's axial contour has one order), the k-th leg sweeps a k-dimensional coordinate slab of level-m bonds through c: a line for k = 1, the whole block for k = d. The leg at the distance s from c along its direction is swept by the end points whose coordinate in that direction is at the distance ≥ s, so the swept measure has a density ≤ C(d) relative to the uniform measure on the slab. For m ≥ 1, Q_m spreads each level-m bond uniformly over the parallel fine lines of its level-m block, so the piece has a density ≤ C(d) relative to the uniform measure on a union of level-m blocks, which are unions of blocks of side L, and its average of W is ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^p by the block averages. For m = 0 the slab is fine, and for odd L its centre c is the centre of a block of side L (for even L, the nearest lattice planes are taken, as there), so the slab is a union of the sections of blocks of side L by a coordinate plane through their centres, and on each such section the average of W is ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^p by Hölder (the large-field note's #0.1.19: (L/t)^{qκ} with qκ < 1 and (L/t₃)^{(1−κ)q/(q−1)} have bounded averages there, since the faces meet the plane transversally or at distance ≥ L/2 − ½, and the contacts meet it in codimension ≥ 2 or at distance ≥ L/2 − ½). Hence 𝒜_y ≤ C(d, κ)(1 + log L)^p‖A_N‖_W, and the contacts do not enter the passage's bound beyond that factor. The faces and contacts here are those of W (#5.4), while the operator bounds are the one-scale ones of #5.10.2–#5.10.4. In the layered geometry this step holds as written at the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1}, j ≥ 1, with the bounds of (i) of #5.10.5, since their blocks have side ≥ L. At Λ₀/Λ₁ the blocks of Λ₀ are single sites, and the passage's τ-part reads ∂*A_N on them pointwise, where W ≥ (1 + log L)^p(L/ρ₀)^κ. That part is bounded by a count weighted with W, and it lands in the room enlarged by the contact and stratum weights (the passage draft's §3 (b₀), #5.8) (derived and reviewed, at the flat base; the data through #47, #5.8 (b)).
{#6} **The G^c transformations (derived, not reviewed). The conjugation factors of the covariant members at part (b)'s pairs, the curvature conditions, and the outer fields' domain under the G^c transformations, CRE's B-terms' argument R(u_L)A at (P(𝐔), 𝐉) included, are derived, not reviewed (#6.4, #6.7–#6.17, #130.15.1–#130.15.2, #153), at 𝕌̃ with T₂^{old} in #6.2's conjugation derived and reviewed, given LF ((R-eval-old), #5), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ there derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2, #123).** The class's bounds at an evaluation point are taken at the reduced-frame representative 𝕌̃ ((iii), *The reduced frame*), which has to lie in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟♮_k (#6.12). 𝒟_k contains CRE (2.34)–(2.37) at the configuration itself (CRE p.261 l.5–24), which 𝒟^λ_k's phase, gradient, and current conditions do not give. Bałaban's chain gives (2.34)–(2.37) at the u-free representative 𝕌^{(0)} (LFR I p.191 l.39–44, read with p.191 l.33–34's "coincides", although (1.68) prints the factor c = 3 on Ω_k, p.191 l.13–14: the reading *R-coincides*, #17, with *R-chain-start*, #86, *R-chain-norm* for the normalised stages, #88.1, and p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, #156, given R-far″(β), #87), with no margin. But 𝕌̃ = (𝕌^{(0)})^{ũ} with a G^c-valued ũ, conjugation by ũ can enlarge |∂𝕌 − 1| and |J^h| by e^{2|Im log ũ|}, and 𝒟_k is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (CRE p.261 l.42–43). A reduction of (2.34) at 𝕌̃ to the real base through 𝒟^λ_k's gradient condition does not work: CRE's constants have α_{1,j} = λ_Rα_{0,j} with λ_R > 1 (table #T2.24; CRE p.262 l.43–46 imports RG1's restrictions on α_{0,j} and α_{1,j}, among them B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁, RG1 p.277 l.54), so that condition allows a term 2η²|∇A′| of up to 2λ_R times (2.34)'s room.
{#6.1} *The reduction to 𝕌^{(0)} (derived; #6.2's bound with C_Λ's frame bound derived, not reviewed, #41.1–#41.2).* At each plaquette ∂𝕌̃ = Ad(ũ(x))∂𝕌^{(0)}, with x the plaquette's base point, and J^h(𝕌̃) = Ad(ũ)J^h(𝕌^{(0)}) (RG1 (1.10)). M(𝕌̃) is M(𝕌^{(0)}) transformed by ũ at the block points, and U_{p,X}(V^v) = U_{p,X}(V)^{v̄}, with v̄ constant on blocks and equal to v at their centres, as RG1 uses at complex u_j in (3.38) (p.278 l.3–12).
{#6.2} So each quantity in (2.34)–(2.37) at 𝕌̃ is the same quantity at 𝕌^{(0)} conjugated by ũ or by its block-constant form. ũ is real at U (*The reduced frame*), and on a layer-n block |log(ũũ(U)^{−1})| ≤ O(B̄_G)L^NL^nηΣ_m(ε_m + ε_{m+1}) ≤ O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)} (#3.2.2), with λ_w := λL of #52.1, up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), and with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2, #116, #123). This follows from *The sources* and *The bound on G*, both of whose terms carry L^iη (#106, #108). Since Ad of a real element preserves the norm, conjugation multiplies each quantity on layer n by at most 1 + O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}. That is below ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)} for g small, uniformly in n, since L ≥ 2 and (N+1)L^Nα_{1,k} is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2} (#3.2.1).
{#6.3} So (2.34)–(2.37) hold at 𝕌̃ once they hold at 𝕌^{(0)} with each coefficient f_n lowered by ½β2^{−(k+1−n)}.
{#6.4} *Where 𝒟_k itself is needed (derived, not reviewed).* Bałaban's chain gives the coefficients f_n themselves at 𝕌^{(0)}, with no margin. The class's bounds at 𝕌̃ need only the members' own spaces, which carry slack (#6.12), so they need no margin at 𝕌^{(0)} (#6.13). 𝒟_k itself, with CRE's radii, is needed where a point enters LFR II (1.65), at its Landau representative (#6.10). At the T-step's points the margin is Bałaban's own device on old layers (CRE p.277 l.2–7): the next step's domain has coefficient f_n − β2^{−(k+1−n)} on layer n. The T-step's deformations use at most an eighth of this for g small: the map fits them into all of it with its α₀-entry constant C′ (#MD2.2.4.80, #MD2.2.6.142–#MD2.2.6.143, #MD2.4.13.19), the curvature change is linear in 𝐇 up to a quadratic term, and T_Y's α₀-entry here has C_K′ ≥ 8C′ ((iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*). At s ≠ 1, P of the point also carries the response 2K sup|𝐇| (#137), which is not shown to decay with the layer; its share of this margin is below a quarter on every layer (#6.9). At a localized background, P of the point at every s, s = 1 included, differs from 𝐕 by 𝓗 with |𝓗| ≤ (1 + O(α_{1,k}))K sup|𝐇| (#130.3–#130.4), which takes the place of both the deformation and the response; #6.9's bound with K sup|𝐇| in place of 2K sup|𝐇| puts its share below an eighth. #6.14 assembles this.
{#6.5} So 𝒟_k itself is needed at the Landau representatives of the points that enter (1.65). At the T-step's points, at every s and at both kinds of background, it is derived (#6.14), and so it is along the segments V̂_t of the reduced frame's continuation at those points (#113, #6.14). SW (6b)'s radius R′ is covered by the map's own choice of c₀ (#6.15). The bound of (iii), *On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k* (#92), needs no scaled points (#6.7), and the extension-domain points of D2.7 15.6(c)–(f) ((iv), #167) lie in 𝒟_k itself at V̂ (#6.16).
{#6.6} CRE (2.39) at the axial form P(𝐔) is not needed: the old insertions' extension is taken at (V̂, J^h(V̂)) (#6.10). At P(𝐔) it would not follow from V̂'s membership, since u_L changes the complex part additively, by D(Im λ̂) at first order, and on Ω_k the rooms of 𝒟^λ_k and 𝒟_k coincide (#52–#53).
{#6.7} *No scaled points (derived, not reviewed).* #92 bounds the deformations' complex parts on the layers newer than k₀ by *The sum* (#123), at VAR's representatives, as on the older layers. It uses no Schwarz step in the background (#16) and no bound at scaled points, and it does not need the stages to lie in Bałaban's spaces (#88.1).
{#6.8} *The conjugation factors (derived, not reviewed).* A G-covariant member's bound at a point does not follow from its bound at a G^c gauge transform of the point without a factor from the conjugation at its endpoints. This transfer is needed for the covariant members (D2.6 closure 5.4's transports, and every member with Cov(k)'s endpoint factors) at the T-step's re-chosen pairs (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω) of (iii), *The bounds*, part (b). There it enlarges their values at the T-step's points over their bounds at the pairs by at most e^{c_π(|Im λ_ĥ(x)|+|Im λ_ĥ(y)|)} ≤ e^{2c_πa_c}, with |λ_ĥ| ≤ a_c = O(α_{1,k}) by REG (1.70)–(1.72) at steps k and k+1. CRE's B-terms are scalars, invariant under G-valued gauge transformations of (𝐔, 𝐉, A), with 𝐉 and A in the adjoint (CRE (2.41)(iii), p.261 l.60–62). So, as for the words (#6.10), their extension is taken at the G^c transform (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉, R(u_L)A), with no conjugation factor (#153). Their outer-field argument R(u_L)A, and R(ĥ)ω at part (b)'s pairs, lie in the outer fields' domain by #6.17 (derived, not reviewed). These factors must not compound over the steps, since Σ_kα_{1,k} is not bounded uniformly in K, and they do not: part (b) normalises to the member's own determining set at every step, and 𝒟^λ_k ⊆ 𝒟^λ_j for k ≥ j, so a carried member's bound on the family at every later step is its production bound, with no factor, and its value at the T-step's points carries one factor e^{2c_πa_c}, at that step only, which the step's shares absorb (#130.15.1–#130.15.2, derived, not reviewed).
{#6.9} *The response's share (derived, not reviewed).* T_Y's α₀-entry gives sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ 4min(β,1)α_{0,k}/C_K + O(ε_k), with C_K = 128K/(1−β)² (#138), so 2K sup|𝐇| ≤ β(1−β)²α_{0,k}/16 + O(Kε_k) for β ≤ 1. At s ≠ 1, P of the point differs from the s = 1 point by at most 2K sup|𝐇| in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights (#137), and the localized minimisers U_{p,X} obey the same bound (#130.1). With m := k − n, on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} the difference δ of the complex parts then has (L^nξ)²|∇δ| ≤ 2K sup|𝐇|L^{−m}, up to the relative error O(sup|𝐇|/r_B), and its current part obeys the same bound in the weight (L^nξ)³. A plaquette's curvature changes by at most 2ξ²|∇δ|, up to terms with an extra factor O(λα_{1,k}). The margin of #6.4 on that layer is β2^{−(m+1)} times the rooms α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2} of (2.34)–(2.35) and α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} of (2.36)–(2.37), and α_{0,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²max(1, √m)α_{0,n} (#53). So the response takes at most (1 + o(1))(1−β)²(1+β₀)²max(1, √m)(2/L)^m/4 + O(Kε_k/α_{0,k}) of the margin. At m = 0 this is (1−β)²/4 + o(1). For m ≥ 1 and L ≥ 8 it is at most (1+β₀)²/16 + o(1) ≤ 9/64 + o(1), since √m 4^{−m} ≤ 1/4 and β₀ ≤ 1/2. Kε_k/α_{0,k} = λ_RKε_k/α_{1,k} → 0 under q₁ > p₀ (#138). So for g_K small the response takes less than a quarter of the margin on every layer. With the deformations' eighth, more than half of the margin remains, which #6.14 uses. At a localized background, 𝓗 of #130.4 replaces both, with K sup|𝐇| in place of 2K sup|𝐇|, so its share is below an eighth (#6.4).
{#6.10} *The Landau frame (derived, not reviewed).* The old insertions' post-R function is a word W(M) of the averages (#127). It is invariant under G-valued block-constant gauge transformations of M, since the words are gauge invariant. For M within VAR's radius, v ↦ W(M^v) is analytic on a connected neighbourhood of the G-valued block transformations in the G^c-valued ones, and G is totally real of maximal dimension in G^c, so W(M^v) = W(M) there. u_L has |λ̂| ≤ B_Gλ_wα_{1,k}L^{−m} on layer m (#106, #108), so M(V̂) = M(P(𝐔))^{ū_L} lies in that neighbourhood for g small. The extension is therefore taken as Bałaban's at (V̂, J^h(V̂)), where (1.65) applies, read as an inclusion of spaces, once V̂ lies in 𝒟_k itself (#156). It is analytic in 𝐔 ((ii), under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β), #88, #156), since u_L is (VAR p.307), and it is the real post-R function on 𝓜, where u_L is G-valued. Neither (1.65) at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) nor CRE (2.39) at the axial form is used (#6.6).
{#6.11} *The conjugation to V̂.* The step-k Landau representative of a T-step point built on the datum's step-(k+1) Landau representative is that point transformed by (u_L^{(k+1)})^{−1}u_L^{(k)}, whose factors are bounded per layer as in #106 and #108. The T-step's words are M-functions (*The M-factored domain*), so by #6.10 at step k+1 the point may be built on that representative. Conjugation multiplies each quantity of (2.34)–(2.37) on layer n by at most 1 + O(B_Gλ_wα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}, which is below 1 + ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)} for g small, as at #6.2. Here the factors are the Landau transformations of the datum and of the T-step's point, bounded by those configurations' own phase, |λ̂| ≤ B_Gλ_wα_{1,k}L^{−m} (#6.10; #106, #108), so ε_Φ, and with it T₂^{old} and C_Λ, does not enter.
{#6.12} *The members' slack (quoted and derived, not reviewed).* Let 𝒟♮_k be 𝒟_k with each coefficient f_n of (2.34)–(2.37) replaced by f♮_n := f_n(1 + ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)}), with (2.38)–(2.39) kept. Bałaban's members are analytic, with their bounds, on 𝒟♮_k.
  {#6.12.1} (a) The boundary terms 𝐁^{(j)}, j ≤ k − 1, are analytic on Ũ_j^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) with the bound (2.42) (CRE (2.41)(ii)–(iv), p.261 l.57–64), whose coefficient on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} is f^{(j)}_n = 1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}). For n < j, f^{(j)}_n − f_n = β(2^{−(j−n)} − 2^{−(k−n)}) ≥ β2^{−(k−n)} ≥ ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)}f_n. At n = j < k, f♮_j ≤ (1 − β/2)(1 + β/16) < 1, the step-j coefficient on Ω_j. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} with n > j, 𝒟♮_k's curvature room is at most (1 + β/8)(1+β₀)²√(n−j)L^{−2(n−j)} times the step-j room (α_{0,n} ≤ (1+β₀)²√(n−j)α_{0,j}, #53), which is below 1 for L ≥ 2; the current gains L^{−3(n−j)} in (2.36) and L^{−2(n−j)} in (2.37) (CRE p.261 l.19–21), and (2.39) gains ((1+β₀)/L)^{n−j} (map #MD2.2.4.72). Ũ_j^c asks (2.35) and (2.37) for p ≤ j only (the map's reading R-p; CRE p.261 prints no range for p). This is CRE p.277 l.2–7's "the analyticity domains become smaller after each step".
  {#6.12.2} (b) The newest boundary terms, made by the T-step into step k, are analytic with their bounds on Ũ_k^c(Y, (1+β)α̃₀, (1+β)α̃₁) (CRE (3.43), p.276 l.25–31), and f♮_n ≤ (1 + β/8)f_n.
  {#6.12.3} (c) The E- and R-terms take RG I's spaces, unions of G^c orbits (RG1 p.262 l.11–13). The newest are on U^c_k(Y, (1+β)α₀, (1+β)α₁, α₀) (RG2 (1.34), p.9, the (I.6.34) of CRE p.276 l.28). The older ones are evaluated at the derived configurations (U_n(M·), J_n(M·)) (RG1 p.262 l.61–p.263 l.2; CRE p.259 l.40–45), and they are G^c-invariant ((1.19)), so one point of the orbit settles their values and their domain.
  {#6.12.4} (d) The transports and Cov(k)'s endpoint factors are entire in the configuration and are bounded through A_k at the point itself (#78), with no curvature condition, on 𝒟^λ_k, where A_k's bound holds (#78, #171). The T-step-made members of the class are functions of the step-k data through the T-step's points, which lie in 𝒟_{k−1} itself when the data lie in 𝒟^λ_k (#6.14 one step down). So (d)'s members are bounded on 𝒟^λ_k, not on all of 𝒟♮_k, and that is where #6.13 uses them.
{#6.13} *The R-step lands in 𝒟♮_k (derived, not reviewed).* Bałaban's chain gives (2.34)–(2.37) at 𝕌^{(0)} with the coefficients f_n (*R-coincides*, *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β); #86, #87, #88.1, #156), once its input lies in 𝒟_k itself, which #6.14 gives at the T-step's points. By #6.1–#6.2, conjugation by ũ multiplies each quantity on layer n by at most 1 + O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}, below 1 + ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)} once O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k}) ≤ β/8, since L ≥ 2, a smallness of g (#3.2.3), up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), #6.2), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2). So 𝕌̃ obeys (2.34)–(2.37) with the coefficients f♮_n, and with its derived phase, gradient, and current conditions it lies in the family 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟♮_k (#48). By #6.12 the pre-R members are analytic and bounded there. No margin at 𝕌^{(0)} is used.
{#6.14} *The T-step's points lie in 𝒟_k itself (derived, not reviewed).* With data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} ⊂ 𝒟♮_{k+1}, the datum's step-(k+1) Landau representative has the coefficients f♮^{(k+1)}_n = (f_n − β2^{−(k+1−n)})(1 + ¼β2^{−(k+2−n)}) ≤ f_n − (7/8)β2^{−(k+1−n)} on layer n ≤ k, for the step-k configurations that CRE (2.35) and (2.37) derive from it. At a localized background these are 𝐕's own conditions, 𝐕 being the datum's localized minimiser (#130.3), whose conditions at step k+1 are CRE (2.35)'s. The deformations take ⅛β2^{−(k+1−n)} (#6.4), the response less than a quarter (#6.9; at a localized background 𝓗 less than an eighth in all, #6.4), and the conjugation to V̂ less than a quarter (#6.11). So more than a quarter of the margin is left, and V̂ ∈ 𝒟_k, given map #MD2.2.6.142–#MD2.2.6.143 (R-dec; R-mJ for (2.35) and (2.37), derived, not reviewed). Along the segment of #113, V̂_t's complex part is t times V̂'s up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B) (#125), with |B| ≤ C_dλα_{1,k} (#96), and the quarter of the margin left absorbs it on every layer for g small (#6.14.1), so the identity theorem of #113 runs there.
{#6.14.1} *The segment's error on every layer.* The absorption is uniform in the layer, as at #6.9. Put m := k − n. The gradient is not covariant under the conjugation to V̂ (#6.6), so δ's gradient is bounded at the Landau representative itself. Along the segment the Landau complex part is 𝓗^{post}(tB), with B = Δd the complex part of the data (#96, #125, #128), so δ = 𝓗^{post}(tB) − t𝓗^{post}(B). (190)'s phase, gradient, and D*D entries hold uniformly on the ball of radius r_B, at most K := O(C_dS_L) times 𝐔's rooms (#94, #128). The Cauchy estimate in t on that ball then gives (L^nξ)²|∇δ| ≤ O(K|B|/r_B)μλα_{1,k}L^{−m} on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (𝐔's gradient room, #50, with λ^{(k)}_n ≤ λ and #52.1's factor μ^{(k)}_n ≤ μ). The current's error is bounded in #6.14.2. δ changes a plaquette's linearised curvature by at most 2ξ²|∇δ| (#6.9). The curvature's nonlinear part, quadratic in a_t := 𝓗^{post}(tB) and its first differences as 𝒬 is in #147, has no factor |B|/r_B; (190) is applied to Δd, which is bounded by 𝐔's phase alone (#96, #128), so a_t's phase and gradient are at most K times 𝐔's phase room in both weights, that is, Kλ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond and Kλ_wα_{1,k}L^{n−k} in the weight (L^nξ)², it is at most O(K²λ_w²α_{1,k}²)L^{−m} in the weight (L^nξ)²ξ^{−2}, which gives the second term of the share below, as in #6.14.2. Since α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k} (#6) and α_{0,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²max(1, √m)α_{0,n} (#53), δ takes at most [O(K|B|/r_B)μλλ_R + O(K²λ_w²λ_Rα_{1,k})](1+β₀)²max(1, √m)L^{−m} of the layer-n rooms of (2.34)–(2.37), the second term from #6.14.2, with K, μ, λ_w, and λ_R fixed before g, and |B|, α_{1,k} → 0 with g. Since max(1, √m)(2/L)^m ≤ 1 for L ≥ 8, this is below the quarter β2^{−(m+3)} of #6.4's margin that is left (#6.14), on every layer, for g small.
{#6.14.2} *The current's segment error.* By #147, J^h(V̂_t) − J(P(U)) = D*Da_t + 𝒬(a_t) with a_t := 𝓗^{post}(tB). (190)'s D*D entry holds on the ball (#94) and is at most K times the phase room (#128), so the Cauchy estimate in t bounds D*D(a_t − ta_1) by O(K|B|/r_B)λ_wλ_Rα_{0,k}L^{−m} in the weight (L^nξ)³, with λ_w := λL where #52.1's free-bond weight applies and λ elsewhere. This is within O(|B|/r_B)μλα_{0,k}L^{−m}, since μ ≥ 32KC(d)Lλ_R/(1−β) (#52.1). The nonlinear part 𝒬(a_t) − t𝒬(a_1) has no factor |B|/r_B: by #147's (L^iη)²|a||∇a| on layer i, with a_t's phase and gradient at most K times 𝐔's phase room in both weights ((190) on the ball applied to Δd, which is bounded by the phase alone, #94, #96, #128), that is, Kλ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond and Kλ_wα_{1,k}L^{n−k} in the weight (L^nξ)², it is at most O(K²λ_w²α_{1,k}²)L^{−m} in the same weight. 𝒬's terms with the real curvature as a coefficient (#147) are linear in a_t to leading order, with the coefficient bounded by (2.34)'s room, so the Cauchy estimate in t gives them a factor O(|B|/r_B) and they lie within the D*D part's bound; their higher-order part, such as ξ²[a_t,[a_t,F_{P(U)}]] with F_{P(U)} the real curvature, is at most α_{0,n}K²λ_w²α_{1,k}²L^{−m−k} and lies in the second term. Against (2.36)'s room α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3}, with α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k} (#6) and #53's ratio, this is O(K²λ_w²λ_Rα_{1,k})(1+β₀)²max(1, √m)L^{−m}, the second term of #6.14.1's share.
{#6.15} *SW (6b)'s radius R′ (derived, not reviewed).* SW (6b)'s points are e^{izξa″}e^{izξA} on 𝒬^{∼2} in the gauge where U_{k+1} = e^{iξA} (SW l.93), with real part 1, so (2.34)'s real part and (2.38) hold trivially. For |z| < R′ their field has phase and layer-n gradient below c₀ᾱ_{k+1}L^{n−k−1} in the weights (L^nξ) and (L^nξ)² (#57–#58), with ᾱ = α₀ (#14). This is the map's own placement (#MD2.4.4.16): c₀ depends on d and L only and is taken so that D(k+1)(iii)'s domain at the reference background 1 contains (2.34)–(2.35) for e^{izηA} and its averages, through |∂e^{iξA} − 1| ≤ C(ξ²|∇A| + ξ²|A|²), RG1 (1.17) (p.263), and R-av (l.168) with C_av = 2B₃, and (2.37) for the averaged 𝐉. SW (6b) uses the same c₀, and on an older layer the curvature room exceeds the top-layer one by α_{0,n}L^{2(k+1−n)}/α_{0,k+1} ≥ L^{2m′}/((1+β₀)²√m′), m′ := k + 1 − n. So (2.34)–(2.37) hold at (6b)'s points, and #58 needs no margin.
{#6.16} *The extension-domain points lie in 𝒟_k itself (derived, not reviewed).* At a point of (iv) the real base is U_k(V_k⁰), with V_k⁰ on the support of the R-step's indicators, and V̂ is the Landau representative relative to it (#167). The real base's own conditions leave almost all the room. Off Z, on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, the data are ε_n-regular at the scale n, up to the factor 2L₀^{2N₀} < 2L(L+1)N₀^{−1}R_n of the L₀-weighted functions: LFR I p.193 l.21–23 prints this at step k, for V_k on Ω_k″ with R_k, and on an older layer n it is the same print at step n, for the step-n variables V_n on Ω_n″, which the later steps keep as the data there off Z. On Z the data are V_k = V_k′V_Λ, with |V_k′ − 1| < M₀ε_k by χ (LFR II p.378 l.12–14, M₀ = O(1)B₅M⁶), |∂V_k − 1| < L²R_kε_k on Z ∩ Λ^c (LFR I p.193 l.27), and V_Λ obeying (1.78), |V_Λ(∂p′) − 1| < B₅M⁵ε with that ε (LFR I p.194 l.38–44). This holds on components of both classes, since the second class keeps χ_{k,Λ_i}χ_i (LFR II p.378 l.21–22). So on every layer the data's curvature is at most O(1)B₅M⁶L²R_nε_n at its scale. By VAR Theorem 1 (p.279), as at #173 with ε-regular data in place of α-regular data, the real minimiser's curvature, those of its derived configurations, and its current are at most B₃ times that in the weights of (2.34)–(2.37). With ε_n = g_np₀(g_n), p₀(g) = A₀(log g^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4), p.255 l.33), R_n ≤ L(log g_n^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.36–38), and α_{0,n} = g_nC₀(log g_n^{−2})^{q₀}, the ratio to the room is O(1)B₃B₅M⁶L³(A₀/C₀)(log g_n^{−2})^{r+p₀−q₀}, which tends to 0 as g → 0 uniformly in n ≤ k, since q₀ > p₀ + r, which the project's choice q₀ > p₀ + 3r gives (the large-field note's #0.10; table #T1.2), and g_n ≤ (1+β₀)g_k (#130.12).
  {#6.16.1} V̂'s complex part is at most 2Kr^{ext}_k ≤ ((1+β₀)/L)α_{0,k} in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights, for phase, gradient, and D*D (#167). On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, m := k − n, a plaquette's curvature then changes by at most 2ξ²|∇δ|, up to second-order terms, as at #6.9. That is at most 2(1+β₀)³max(1, √m)L^{−m−1}/f_n of (2.34)–(2.35)'s room, by α_{0,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²max(1, √m)α_{0,n} (#53). The current changes by at most (1+β₀)³max(1, √m)L^{−m−1}/f_n of (2.36)–(2.37)'s room. With f_n ≥ 1 − β ≥ ¾, β₀ = 1/7, and max(1, √m)L^{−m} ≤ 1, the curvature's change is at most 8(8/7)³/(3L) and the current's at most 4(8/7)³/(3L), both < ½ for L ≥ 8, which table #T1.3 gives. The derived configurations of (2.35) and (2.37) change by the same bound, by VAR Proposition 9 and (190) at their own determining sets, which is map #MD2.2.6.142–#MD2.2.6.143's R-mJ, as at #6.14. (2.39) follows from the phase and gradient conditions (#53), and (2.38) is the real base's (#212).
  {#6.16.2} So V̂ ∈ 𝒟_k with more than a third of each room left, for g small. Along the segment V̂_t of #113 the complex part is t times V̂'s up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B) (#125), so the segment stays in 𝒟_k. Hence (1.65) applies at (V̂, J^h(V̂)) (#6.10), the words' equality runs along V̂_t (#113), and 𝕌̃ lies in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13), up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), #6.2), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2).
{#6.17} *The outer fields' domain (derived, not reviewed).* The outer fields ω_j live on Ω_{j+1} ∩ Λ_{j+1}ᶜ, and Λ_k ⊆ Ω_k ⊆ Λ_{k−1} (map #MD2.2.2.16, CRE pp.264–265). So Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1}, and ω_j's bonds lie on the layer Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} at every step k ≥ j+1. These are the inclusions of the current admissible sequence: an R-operation integrates out the variables localized in Z_{j+1}∖Z″_{j+1} (LFR I p.178 l.55–57, p.179 l.53–55, (1.15)–(1.16) on p.180), and the later steps' variables sit on the layers Γ″_j = (Ω″_j∖Ω″_{j+1})^{(j)} of the new admissible sequence ((1.12)–(1.13), p.179), so no bond keeps an older layer. At a point of layer n, REG (1.70)–(1.71) bound each normalised transformation by 16dL^nφ, with φ = O(λ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k}) the phase per fine bond (#130.11). So at ω_j's bonds |λ_ĥ| (ĥ = c = v_kv_{k+1}^{−1}, #130.7) and |λ_{u_L}| are at most C_odλ_wα_{1,k}L^{1−m}, with m := k − j ≥ 1 and C_o absolute.
  {#6.17.1} For j ≥ 1, the map's ω-domain at step k is the polydisc of radius r^{(k)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−m}), with r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2}, around a real support point (map #MD2.0.3.61, #MD2.2.6.168); j = 0 is #6.17.3. For g ∈ G, R(e^{iX}g) maps the polydisc of radius r around a real support point x into the polydisc of radius e^{2|X|}r + 2|X||x|(1 + O(|X|)) around R(g)x, which is a real support point (map #MD2.2.6.71). On the support g_j|x| ≤ O(1)ε_j + g_j^{3/2} (map #MD2.2.6.168). So the step-(k+1) radius maps into the step-k radius if (e^{2|X|} − 1)(1/2 + 2^{−m−1}) + 2|X||x|g_j^{−1/2}(1 + O(|X|)) ≤ 2^{−m−1}. With |X| ≤ C_odλ_wα_{1,k}L^{1−m}, the left side times 2^{m+1} is at most 8C_odλ_wL(2/L)^mα_{1,k}(1 + O(1)p₀(g_j)g_j^{−1/2}), with p₀(g) = A₀(log g^{−2})^{p₀} and ε_j = g_jp₀(g_j) (CRE (2.4), p.255 l.33). By CRE (2.6) (p.255 l.44–45), g_j^{−1/2} ≤ (1+β₀)^{1/2}m^{1/4}g_k^{−1/2}, and by CRE (2.7) (p.255 l.56–57), (log g_j^{−2})^{p₀} ≤ (1+β₀)m^{β₀}(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}. So α_{1,k}p₀(g_j)g_j^{−1/2} ≤ O(1)(1+β₀)^{3/2}m^{1/4+β₀}g_k^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀+p₀}, and m^{1/4+β₀}(2/L)^m ≤ m^{3/4}2^{−m} < 1 for L ≥ 4 and β₀ ≤ ½. So the condition holds for g small, uniformly in k and j. Each step's enlargement fits that step's own slack 2^{−m−1} of the radius schedule, so nothing compounds over the steps.
  {#6.17.2} The B-terms take the outer fields through the configuration's shift, on R-m's domain 𝒫_j^m = {g_j|B| < r_{α,j}/C₁^{RG2}}, and on the ω-polydiscs g_j sup|B| ≤ 2c₃ε_j (map #MD2.2.6.170). R(u_L) at most doubles this, and 4c₃ε_j < r_{α,j}/C₁^{RG2} for g small, since ε_j/α_{1,j} → 0 (q₀ > p₀). So R(u_L)A lies in their domain, and (2.42) holds at (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉, R(u_L)A) (#153). The outer fields' domain under ĥ and u_L is therefore derived, and #6.8 treats the conjugation factors of the covariant members.
  {#6.17.3} *The case j = 0 (derived, not reviewed).* ω₀ lives on Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁ᶜ, the layer 1, with r_{ω,0} = 1 in V3's B-units (map #MD2.2.6.168) and the radius r^{(k)}_{ω,0} = 1/2 + 2^{−k}. The bounds at complex ω₀ hold, for the t > 0 species, on the sub-polydisc of radius ρ_ω ≪ r_{ω,0} around the term's step-1 exterior data (map #MD2.0.3.61). With the family's phase λ^{(k)}_nwα_{1,k} per fine bond on the layer n (#71, #52.1), and λ^{(k)}_nw ≤ λ̄^{(k)} := min(λ_w, c′^k), since λ^{(k)}_nw ≤ λ_w ≤ c′^{k−n} where w > 1 (#52.1) and λ^{(k)}_n ≤ min(λ, c′^{k−n}) elsewhere, #6.17's bound gives |X| ≤ C_odλ̄^{(k)}α_{1,k}L^{1−k} for X = λ_ĥ and X = λ_{u_L} at ω₀'s bonds. (1) The radius: the real inputs satisfy |B(b)| ≤ 2r (map #MD2.0.3.61), so #6.17.1's condition becomes (e^{2|X|} − 1)(1/2 + 2^{−k−1}) + 2|X|·2r(1 + O(|X|)) ≤ 2^{−k−1}. Its left side times 2^{k+1} is at most 8C_od(1 + 2r)λ_wL(2/L)^kα_{1,k}, by λ̄^{(k)} ≤ λ_w, and α_{1,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²k^{1/2}α_{1,1} (#71), so it holds for g small given λ_w, uniformly in k. (2) The sub-polydisc: each of these rotations is #71's c₀-rotation charge at j = 0, λC_{c₀}α_{1,k}L^{−k}, with C_{c₀} replaced by C_odL. #71's sum, with λ̄^{(k)} ≤ c′^k, then gives Σ_k2·2C_odLλ̄^{(k)}α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ 8C_odL(1+β₀)²α_{1,1}. So D2.6 §5.0's ρ_ω (and the O4 note's, §4(f), l.74) is taken with 4C_{c₀} + 8C_odL in place of 4C_{c₀}, and the O4 note's C_ω with C_w + 3d + 4C_{c₀} + 8C_odL. C_o is absolute and L is fixed before the coupling, so ρ_ω ≪ r_{ω,0} and the O4 note's tilt absorption stand. Hence R(ĥ)ω₀ and R(u_L)ω₀ lie in the outer fields' domain, and #6.8 and #130.15 hold at j = 0.
{#7} **(R-eval), derived, given the readings of #17, with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed, (R-eval-old) above included (reviewed, given LF, #5), not reviewed apart from (R-eval-old), the transfer to step k+1 ((iii), *The bounds*, parts (a) and (b), #130–#136, packet TB, with the curvature conditions at the T-step's points (#6.14), #130.15's outer fields' domain and B-terms (#6.17, #153), the covariant members' conjugation factors (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2), and #130.17's radius R′ (#6.15), derived, not reviewed) and the real base current (#173.1–#173.7, packet JB; at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause, derived, not reviewed, #130.12), with the cross insertions' weights in (R-eval-cr) below: the class's bounds at the R-steps' evaluation points, with an α₀-entry in T_Y for the current part of K (*The constants*).**
- {#8} How it holds: the bounds below hold on the complexified post-R minimiser manifold 𝓜^c ((iii), *The scope*). The old insertions' post-R function is extended as a function of M(𝐔) alone, so its value at 𝐔 is its value at P(𝐔) := U^{post}(M(𝐔)) ∈ 𝓜^c. This extension is the note's own: Bałaban's takes (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way" (LFR II p.374 l.38, p.375 l.11–12), and the class's members built on the note's extension agree with Bałaban's as far as (V̂, J^h(V̂)) lies in Ũ_k^c(Y₄), the extension being taken there (#6.10, #127); at the T-step's points and the extension-domain points this is derived, not reviewed (#6.14, #6.16, #156). Every member of the class is taken composed with P, so the class's domain is M-factored ((iii), *The M-factored domain*, derived). The T-step's factorisation at s = 0 needs each member to be local. P is local, since U^{post} = U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·) is supported in Y₁, and each member takes the P of its own domain: a member that is a function of 𝐔 takes the localized projection P_Ω onto 𝐁_j″(Ω) (CRE (2.14)–(2.15)), with j its production step, kept at every later step, as Bałaban uses local minimisers there (RG1 (3.23)). P_Ω is the identity on the minimisers ((iii), *The bounds*, derived). The transfer to step k+1 is derived ((iii), *The bounds*). In part (a), at a localized background, P of the T-step's point is compared with the background itself, which each member's P fixes, and the re-projection costs 𝓗 at every s, s = 1 included (#130.3–#130.4), which replaces both the deformation and the response, with its share of the curvature margin below an eighth, derived, not reviewed (#6.4, #6.14). In part (b), REG's normalisation for each member's own determining set, fixed at production, is met exactly by evaluating at another pair of the same G^c-orbit, chosen from the background alone through REG's normalised transformations at steps k and k+1, and equal at real parameters by the members' G-invariance; for the covariant members this holds up to one conjugation factor at the current step, read from the member's production bound, derived, not reviewed (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2), for the outer fields by #6.17 (derived, not reviewed), and for the base current's frame term by #173's bound on B_J (reviewed, packet JB; at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause, derived, not reviewed, #130.12). Its bounds there are those on 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k. The T-step's points land in it with no condition on L involving K: SW (b3)'s, and #6.9's L ≥ 8 for the curvature conditions (table #T1.3). The factor K := O(C_dS_L) of VAR Proposition 9 and (190) costs only a factor 4K/(1−β) in T_Y and, for the current, an α₀-entry with constant C_K′ in T_Y, which D2.4 absorbs for g_K small (*The constants*). The other parts:
  - {#9} the current of the deformations is tracked covariantly at the configurations, J^h(V^G) = Ad_GJ^h(V), so the gauge transformation enters only through |λ̂|, and no Hessian of λ̂, 𝓗, or A_L enters ((iii), *The current of the deformations*);
  - {#10} the Landau representatives are VAR's, generated from the axial-gauge element as in REG, so 𝓗's argument is the configuration's own plain data, and the one gauge transformation G is bounded through REG (1.70)–(1.71) ((iii), *The Landau representatives*, *The bound on G*, *The reduced frame*);
  - {#11} the data's 𝐉: the domain is a set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉), and the pair's 𝐉, in the Landau frame, obeys 𝒟^λ_k's current condition and (2.36) about 0 by definition, with no transport ((iii), *The 𝐉 coordinate*);
  - {#12} (2.36) about 0 and the complex curvature conditions (2.34), (2.35), and (2.37) at the evaluation points are quoted from LFR II (1.65) and LFR I's chain statement (p.191 l.39–44), applied at the Landau representative (V̂, J^h(V̂)) (#6.10), which lies in 𝒟_k = Ũ_k^c at the T-step's points (#6.14) and at the extension-domain points (#6.16) ((iii), *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*). They are quoted at the u-free representative, under the readings *R-chain-start*, *R-coincides*, *R-chain-norm*, and p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β) (#17; #86, #87, #88.1, #156, #158). At the reduced representative, where the class's bounds are taken, they hold with the coefficients f♮_n, up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), #6.2), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2), and the members are bounded there: Bałaban's terms on 𝒟♮_k, the transports and the T-step-made members on 𝒟^λ_k (#6.12–#6.13), the latter's covariant constituents up to one conjugation factor at part (b)'s pairs (#6.8, #130.15–#130.15.2). All of this is derived, not reviewed, and the Schwarz step uses no scaled points (#6.7).
- {#13} *The constants.*
  - {#14} CRE's C₀ and C₁. CRE (2.28) asks only that C₀ and C₁ be "sufficiently large" (p.259 l.50), but CRE p.262 l.43–46 imports the restrictions of RG1 on α_{0,j} and α_{1,j}, among them B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (RG1 p.277 l.54, the deformation 𝐇_j of (3.37) placed in the complex part). So C₁ = λ_RC₀ with λ_R = K_RLM + 1 (table #T2.24), α_{1,j} = λ_Rα_{0,j} under q₀ = q₁, and ᾱ = min(α₀, α₁) = α₀. No choice C₀ ≥ C₁ is available. The current part of K: T_Y carries an α₀-entry with constant C_K′ := max(8C′, C_K) ≥ C_K := 128K/(1−β)², fixed after L and M ((iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*; the map's α₀-entry, #MD2.2.6.143 and #MD2.4.11.5, with C′ ≥ 16; the factor 8 is #6.4's). On the polydisc it gives sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ 4min(β,1)α_{0,k}/C_K + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k. So the current change at s ≠ 1, at most 2K sup|𝐇| in the phase room's units, is at most min(β,1)(1−β)²α_{0,k}/16 + O(Kε_k) ≤ (1−β)²α_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k), since β ≤ 1/4 (CRE p.261 l.42; LFR I p.191 l.35), below (1−β)f_nα_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k) and so below ⅛(1−β)f_nα_{0,k} for g small (q₀ > p₀), and so is the 𝐇-part's own current at s = 1. C_K enters C_T, which is fixed after L and M, and 1/T_Y ≤ C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k with b_k/α_{0,k} ≤ C(A₀/C₀)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q₀} → 0, so g_K small absorbs it, as it absorbs the factor 4K/(1−β). The other places where C₀/C₁ enters take C₁/C₀ = λ_R, fixed after L and M: the att constant of the reclassified dev insertions, Cρ_k·max(1, α_{0,k}/α_{1,k}) = Cρ_k (map #MD2.4.6.7; D2.6 closure §3, l.70–73), and their constant's factor 1 + 6S₃₀C₁/C₀ = 1 + 6S₃₀λ_R (D2.6 closure §3, l.73), λ times that on the family (#55); the new groups' complex-data clause, (1 + 2C₀/C₁)C_χ(C₁/min(C₀, C₁))(Σ_tw_t)ρ_{k+1} ≤ 3λ_RC_χ(Σ_tw_t)ρ_{k+1} on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} (SW §6(6b)) and ≤ 3λλ_RC_χ(Σ_tw_t)ρ_{k+1} on the family (#57–#58), where (6b)'s radius R′ keeps (2.34)–(2.37) (#6.15, derived, not reviewed; #58); (b2)'s 3λS₃₀λ_Rρ_k (#55); the dev term's complex-data size Cρ_kα_{1,k}/ᾱ_k = Cρ_kλ_R (map #MD2.4.5.3, #MD2.4.11.5); and the covariance note's c_nc and m₀. T₁'s current at the start of the chain is not such a source term: it is absorbed by λ's factor λ_R (#72) on the layers n ≤ k₀, and by M large on the layers k₀ < n ≤ k ((iii), *The starting values*), with C_Λ′'s frame bound derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2, #164). Each is a source term, not compounded from step to step, and multiplies an amplitude of order ρ ∝ g²·polylog, so g_K small absorbs it. Three further places take λ_R in fractions of a room, not in source terms: the base current's frame term 3C_fdλ_wλ_RB_J/(1−β) (#130.11, #154), the transfer's O(λ_Rh_k) of the current room (#136), and T₂'s D*D, O(λ_RK²(…)ε_k/r_B) (#165). Each is absorbed by g small, since B_J (#173; at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause, derived, not reviewed, #130.12), h_k, and ε_k tend to 0 uniformly in k and K, and λ_R is fixed after L and M. Two more take λ_R in smallness conditions on g: the complex part |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε₁ of VAR (172), whose ε₁ ≥ 3α_{1,n}/C₁^{VAR} = 3λ_Rα_{0,n}/C₁^{VAR} must be ≤ a₁ (map #MD2.2.6.142, #MD2.2.6.186), and the slot-value move across the gauge frames, |Δσ_j| ≤ 10S_H/g_j ≤ r_{ω,j}/4 with S_H = C_H(1 + λ_R)α_{0,j+1}e^{−R_j}, that is 40C_H(1 + λ_R)α_{0,j+1}g_j^{−3/2}e^{−R_j} ≤ 1 (map #MD2.2.6.185, #MD2.2.6.193, #MD2.2.6.196). Both hold for g small, λ_R being fixed after L and M. The covariance note's former condition M ≥ 2C_avC₁/C₀ cannot hold, since λ_R grows with M; its disc is reduced instead (WILSON_IT1_COMPLEX_COVARIANCE_2026_10_29.md §3(b)). RG2 p.20 bounds (LM)⁴α₀ and (LM)⁴α₁ separately (map #MD2.4.1.18), which g small gives.
  - {#15} The phase and gradient part of K is derived on the M-factored domain ((iii), *The M-factored domain*), which agrees with Bałaban's T-step inside □₀ (RG1 p.275 l.2–5) and is the note's own extension outside it. Bałaban's generalized axial gauge (RG1 (3.26)–(3.29), p.275–276) keeps the phase away from the minimiser by a different device, which the M-factored domain does not need.
  - {#16} Where this sits: at complex external data the R-step's integrand evaluates the insertions at the old configuration U_k^{(0)}: Bałaban's U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} (LFR II (1.51), p.370 l.11, with U_k^0 from (1.50)), further deformed by the N preliminary steps of LFR I (p.181 (1.21), p.191 l.39–41). D(k)(iii)'s bounds are sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub}_k (SW closure (b2), (b6); D2.6 closure 5.0 *Amendment*, 5.4), and the class's are sup bounds on 𝒟^λ_k ((R-eval-cr)), whose room on the older layers is at most λ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (#52.1, #171). There the Schwarz lemma in the background bounds the deformations' complex parts only up to a factor L^{k−m}, with k − m up to N (LFR II's N = R_j, replaced by N_k, #3.2.1.2). VAR Proposition 9's data derivatives instead bound them in proportion to the data's complex part, with coefficient O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k), which is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2} and whose L^N is the stages' frame factor ((iii) below, #123; #3.2, derived and reviewed): for the phase in the top-layer weight, and for the gradient and the current in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights.
