- {#35} Where it enters: every bound of an R-step at complex external data.
  - {#36} For one loop: D2.7 item 5(b)'s uniform bound sup‖w_X‖ ≤ e^{2ΣA}, 15.3's activities, 15.6's |Ψ(S)| and |Ψ_g|, 15.6(c)'s B(g) through e^{2ΣA} and e^{A_k}, the Cauchy step of 15.6(d) on the extension domain of radius r^{ext}_k, 15.6(e)'s att charge, 15.6(f)'s members, 15.7, 15.8(b)'s member masses, and item 7 with 15.8(c)'s mass e^{3+2ΣA}. 15.1, at the flat reference, uses real data only, and with them the base-point conditions (*The remaining parts*, *The real base points*). So the continuum note's (4.5), (5.6), (6.3), and (8.1)–(8.3) need (R-eval) as well.
  - {#37} For the pair, in addition: in (6B), the cross members' factors (*15.5*, *15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas*' ‖D_g^×‖ ≤ B(g), *The payment*'s member part of Ψ_g, *Second case: g has a carried cross member*, *The rooted sum*, *15.8*), and the parts with 𝒦 ≠ ∅ of the dev members and of the attached insertions (D2.7 15.5(i)); in (6C), the constant C_Ψ″ and E(Y_i) with item 5(b)'s mass (*Second case*), and the member sum x_g (*The single loop's R-made groups*). So the R-step part of the cross carry lemma, and with it (8.2), is derived only up to this input.
- {#38} The derivation: the mechanism of LFR I's proof of its (1.89) (p.197–200), which LFR II p.375 l.24–27 names as the proof of (1.65). There the M⁵ response is beaten by the age of Λ and by its distance from the newer layers. For the class it has three parts. The insertions are evaluated at a transport form Φ of U_k^{(0)}, whose complex part is the data's own plus a response sourced on Λ. The response lands on layers at least N₀ old, where dilated sub-domains have room for it. The class's bounds are carried on the dilated sub-domains. The bullets below are derived or quoted, except for T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), with the conjugation factors of the covariant members at part (b)'s pairs, the outer fields' domain there, and the B-terms' outer-field argument at (P(𝐔), 𝐉) derived, not reviewed (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2, #6.17, #153). The bound B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, for the real base current in part (b)'s frame term, B_J the sup of (L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, is derived at #173 and reviewed (packet JB), at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause (derived, not reviewed, #130.12). Their bounds hold on 𝓜^c ((iii), *The scope*), hence on the M-factored domain ((iii), *The M-factored domain*).
  - {#39} *The transport form (derived).* Let U^{post}(V) := U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), V) be the post-R minimiser map, with Bałaban's localized determining set 𝐁_k″(Y₁) = 𝐁(Y₁) ∪ 𝐁_k″ (LFR II p.373 l.15–17; the joining is CRE (2.14), p.257), so that U = U^{post}(M(U)) at every real post-R minimiser U (by nesting and LFR II (1.61); *The M-factored domain*, *At s = 1*). M is the averaging of 𝐁_k″(Y₁), which is finer than the post-R 𝐁_k on Z; U_k^0 ∘ M needs these finer averages, since (1.50) takes data on Z at the scales of 𝐁_k″. Let E(𝐔) := U_k^0(M(𝐔))·U^{post}(M(𝐔))^{−1} and Φ(𝐔) := E(𝐔)·𝐔, bondwise in G^c. Φ is analytic wherever U_k^0 ∘ M ((1.65)) and U^{post} ∘ M are. Its identity with Bałaban's configuration holds through P := U^{post} ∘ M: Φ(P(U)) = U_k^0(M(U)) at every real configuration U, since M(P(U)) = M(U) and U^{post}(M(P(U))) = P(U) (*The M-factored domain*, *At s = 1*). Off the real minimisers 𝓜 := {U real : U = P(U)}, Φ(U) itself need not equal U_k^0(M(U)). This includes the gauge transforms U = V^g of V ∈ 𝓜 with g not the extension g̃ that U^{post}'s gauge fixing gives to g's values at the block points: there P(U) = V^{g̃}, so Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U))·(V^{g̃})^{−1}V^g bondwise. The class's bounds are therefore taken at Φ ∘ P. So the R-step's integrand at real data is unchanged when the insertions are evaluated at Φ ∘ P instead of at Bałaban's U_k^0 ∘ M, with 𝐇_k″(g_kB) and the N preliminary deformations applied as left factors, as in (1.51) and LFR I p.191 l.39–41 (the left factor exp iη𝐇^{(n)}_k at l.40), and with the later sources conjugated by the accumulated gauge transformation ((iii), *The reduced frame*). The gauge transformation u_k″ of (1.51) is dropped. Every word is the trace of an ordered product of insertions evaluated at one configuration, so it does not change, and each insertion changes by a real conjugation, which preserves its norm. At 𝐔 = e^{iηA′}U the base point of Φ(𝐔) is Φ(U) = E(U)U, which is U_k^0(M(U)) for a real base U ∈ 𝓜, as at Φ ∘ P (#159). Its complex part is Ad(E(U))A′ plus the complex part of E(𝐔)E(U)^{−1}, up to second-order terms. No re-minimisation of the data enters off Λ.
  - {#40} *The response is sourced on Λ (derived).* Off Λ the determining set and the field of U_k^0 are those of the new configuration ((1.50), p.370 l.3–8). So U_k^0(M(𝐔)) and U^{post}(M(𝐔)) differ only through their data on Λ. The first takes the averages of U_0(𝐔) := U_0^{(AL)} at M^k(𝐔) on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} and the real V″ on the rest of Λ, Λ^{old}, where V″ are T′_k(X)'s integration variables V_h (p.370 l.4, p.378 l.31); the second takes 𝐔's own field. Here s_Z(𝐔) is the sup of 𝐔's phase per fine bond on Z, and L^kη = 1. The bound has four steps.
    - {#41} The scale-k response. U_0(𝐔) = U_0^{(AL)}(𝐔) is U^{(L)}_{k,Z}(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}, V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c})) with V_k replaced by M^k(𝐔) (LFR II p.366 l.23–25). Its axial form is (1.43)'s U_0^{ax}(𝐔) := U_{k,Z}(M^k(𝐔), V_Λ(M^k(𝐔))), "in the axial gauge" (p.367 l.27–30), and U_0(𝐔) = U_0^{ax}(𝐔)^{u_0(𝐔)}, with u_0 the transformation of LFR I (1.86) to the Landau gauge around the configuration 1, so that U_0(𝐔) = exp iη𝔸₀ inside Λ (LFR I p.197 l.18–29). (1.43) supplies the data. U_0^{ax}(𝐔) has its data at the scale k on all of Z, since V_Λ is defined on Z^{(k)} (p.365 l.13). By (1.38) and (1.42) its data differ from those of U_0^{ax}(U) by B̃′ = Q̃_k(ηA′) off Λ and by B′_Λ(B̃′) on Λ (p.366 l.28–32, p.367 l.19–23). B̃′ is an average of sums of L^k fine phases, so |B̃′| ≤ O(1)L^ks_Z. And |B′_Λ| ≤ 4d(100M)⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃²|B̃′| by (1.41) (p.367 l.15–17).
      {#41.1} *The frame (derived, not reviewed).* VAR (190) bounds the Landau form of U_0^{ax}(𝐔)U_0^{ax}(U)^{−1} relative to U_0^{ax}(U): U_0^{ax}(𝐔)^w = e^{iηℋ}U_0^{ax}(U), with w the G^c-valued transformation to VAR's representative, w = 1 at 𝐔 = U (VAR Proposition 9, p.309 l.10–11; p.305 l.36–39). With U^u(b) = u(b₋)U(b)u(b₊)^{−1}, (U^a)^b = U^{ba} and (e^{iηℋ}X)^v = e^{iηAd(v)ℋ}X^v, with Ad(v) taken at b₋. So U_0(𝐔) = (e^{iηℋ′}U_0(U))^g, with ℋ′ := Ad(u_0(U))ℋ and g := u_0(𝐔)w^{−1}u_0(U)^{−1}. u_0(U) is G-valued, so ℋ′ has ℋ's bounds, and g = 1 at 𝐔 = U. The averages are covariant, M^j(X^g) = M^j(X)^{ḡ} with ḡ the values of g at the block points (as at #6.1), so the data of U_k^0 on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} are those of e^{iηℋ′}U_0(U) transformed by ḡ.
      {#41.2} *The bound on g (derived, not reviewed).* U_0^{ax} depends on 𝐔 only through B̃′ ((1.38)–(1.43)), so follow the data tB̃′, t ∈ ℂ, on the disc |t| ≤ T := cα_{1,k}/|B̃′|, on which Bałaban's (1.38)–(1.45) hold (|B̃′| < O(1)α_{1,k}, p.366 l.28); T ≥ 2 at the evaluation points, since |B̃′| ≤ O(1)L^ks_Z and s_Z ≤ ½α_{1,k}L^{−k} (#92). Put λ := (1/i)log g, analytic in t, with λ = 0 at t = 0. On the disc |λ| is small at the block points (|u′u₁ − 1| < 16dB₁(α₀ + α₁), REG p.89 l.35, for u_0; #106 for w), so, as in #107, η|Dλ| ≤ 2(η|𝔸₀(t)| + η|ℋ′(t)| + η|𝔸₀(U)|) while |λ| and η|Dλ| stay below #107's c₀. Here 𝔸₀(t) = ℍ_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M^·(U_0^{ax}(t))), whose averages are O(B₃B₅M⁶ε_k) at t = 0 ((1.84)) and change by O(B₃B₅M⁵)|t||B̃′| ((1.44)–(1.45)), so η|𝔸₀(t)| ≤ O(1)L^{−k}B₃²B₅(M⁶ε_k + M⁵|t||B̃′|), as (1.87) follows from (1.84); η|ℋ′(t)| ≤ O(1)L^{−k}(1 + C⁰_{rsp}M⁵)|t||B̃′| on Λ (#42); and η|𝔸₀(U)| ≤ O(1)L^{−k}B₃²B₅M⁶ε_k ((1.87)). The Schwarz lemma at t = 1 then gives η|Dλ| ≤ O(1)L^{−k}(1 + (C⁰_{rsp} + B₃²B₅)M⁵ + B₃²B₅M⁶ε_k/α_{1,k})|B̃′| ≤ O(1)(1 + (C⁰_{rsp} + B₃²B₅)M⁵ + o(1))s_Z, since ε_k/α_{1,k} → 0 under q₁ > p₀. Adjacent j-block points are joined by at most dL^j bonds, so ḡ changes a datum on a j-bond of Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} by at most O(d)L^j(1 + (C⁰_{rsp} + B₃²B₅)M⁵)s_Z, up to a small multiple of the datum's own phase.
    - {#42} The fine field at the scale k. VAR (190) (p.308 l.37–44) bounds the derivative of the minimiser's field in the data by O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8}, for the target in layer j and the data at y′ in layer j′, with PRT2 (2.46)'s distance (p.231 l.11; VAR p.288 l.45 takes d(y, y′) from its [3], which is PRT2, VAR p.309 l.23), which measures each layer's part in that layer's block units. With j = j′ = k and the mean value theorem along the data, the fine phase of ℋ at x (#41.1) splits into the response to B̃′ and the response to B′_Λ. The first is at most O(1)L^{−k}sup|B̃′| ≤ O(1)s_Z, since the sum over the source blocks of e^{−δ₀d/8} is O(1). The second is at most C⁰_{rsp}M⁵s_Z e^{−δ₀d(x,Λ)/16}, with C⁰_{rsp} := O(1)4d·100⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃², and below C_{rsp} := O(d)(C⁰_{rsp} + B₃²B₅) ≥ C⁰_{rsp} also covers the frame (#41.2); the sum over the source blocks y′ ⊂ Λ of e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/16} being O(1).

A j-average's phase is at most O(1)L^j times the sup of the fine phase on its block, and ḡ adds at most the bound of #41.2. So the data of U_k^0 and U^{post} differ on a j-block y′ of Λ by a phase at most O(1)L^j(C_{rsp}M⁵ + 1)s_Z e^{−δ₀d(y′,Λ)/16}. The +1 bounds, up to O(1), the response to B̃′ (previous step) and 𝐔's own phase; the latter is also the difference on the rest of Λ, where U_k^0 takes the real V″.
    - {#44} Across the layers of 𝐁″_k(Y₁). VAR (190) again, now with the data on Λ's layers. A source of j′-block phase S at y′ gives at a target x of layer i a fine phase at most O(1)L^{−i}S e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/8}. For i ≥ j′ this is at most O(1)L^{−j′}S e^{−δ₀d/8}. For i < j′ a contour from y′ to x crosses the layers i+1, …, j′−1 entirely. Consecutive layers are separated by at least one layer of M-cubes of their own scale: {Ω″_j} is an admissible sequence (LFR I p.179 l.40), and admissibility based on partitions into M-cubes in the corresponding scales means this separation ("Thus … separated by one layer of M-cubes", p.179 l.20–22). This covers the layers in Z and outside it. VAR's d is the distance (2.46) of its [3] (VAR p.288 l.45; PRT2 p.231 l.10–12), which counts a contour in blocks of each layer's own scale. So each fully crossed layer adds at least M to d, and L^{j′−i}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ L(Le^{−δ₀M/16})^{j′−i−1} ≤ L once Le^{−δ₀M/16} ≤ 1, which M large given L gives.

At the real base U the two maps' real data on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} differ by δ^{re}, with |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd²)ε_k per block of 𝐁_k″(Y₁) and M₀ = O(1)B₅M⁶ (*The two minimisers*, (iii); LFR II p.356 l.37 and p.378 l.13–14), plus O(KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k at the T-step's real base U_{k+1} ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*). On Λ^{old} = Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} they differ by T′_k(X)'s integration variables V_h against U's averages, which is not bounded. Its part T₂^{old} of the term is (R-eval-old) (top of the note), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5, #5.8). So the shared complex change Δd of the data passes through two minimisers with different real Λ-data. In the Landau representative relative to U, the part sourced on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} is T₂ := ∫₀¹[κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)]Δd dt with κ := δ𝓗/δB, as in (iii). Δd has phase at most O(1)L^{j′}λα_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ O(1)λα_{1,k} on a j′-block, since a j′-average's phase is at most O(1)L^{j′} times the fine phase (previous steps) and 𝐔 obeys 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition. By the Cauchy estimate in w, |κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)| ≤ (4|δ^{re}|/r_B) times (190)'s bound. A source y′ of layer n′ carries at most O(1)L^{n′}λ^{(k)}_{n′}α_{1,k}L^{−k} of Δd. At a target x of layer i, (190) gives at most O(1)L^{n′−i}λ^{(k)}_{n′}α_{1,k}L^{−k}e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/8}. For n′ > i the previous step gives L^{n′−i}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ L, and λ^{(k)}_{n′} ≤ λ^{(k)}_i. For n′ < i, L^{n′−i} ≤ 1, and λ^{(k)}_{n′} ≤ c′^{i−n′}λ^{(k)}_i, while the i − n′ − 1 fully crossed layers give c′^{i−n′}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ c′(c′e^{−δ₀M/16})^{i−n′−1} ≤ L, since c′ < L. So T₂'s phase at a bond b of layer i is at most C_{mx}ε_kλ^{(k)}_iα_{1,k}L^{−k}, with C_{mx} := O(1)L(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})/r_B, which depends on d, L, and M and is fixed before g. The same uniformity of (190) on the ball makes the mean-value steps above hold at complex intermediate data.

In particular it does not depend on CRE's C₀ or C₁. Bałaban's (1.45) (p.367 l.40–45) bounds the same averages uniformly in j, which per fine bond would lose L^{k−j}.   - {#47} *Where it lands (quoted, with the bound on s_Z derived).* Before R, Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Ω^c_{k₀+1} (LFR I p.192 l.43), with k₀ = k − N₀ (p.181 l.40), so Λ is at least N₀ layers old. On Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1}, k₀ < m < k, the exponential is at most exp(−4δ(m − k₀)M) (p.200 l.12–19), and Bałaban makes the M⁵ term small there "for M large enough" (p.200 l.20). N₀ is fixed by L^{−N₀+1}R_{k−N₀+1} = 1 (p.200 l.30–31), so N₀ → ∞ as g → 0. After R all of Z lies in the new Ω_k, the components of both classes: the new determining set has Γ_k = Γ″_k ∪ Z^{(k)} ((1.33), p.365 l.2–7), and Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)} (CRE (2.2), p.255 l.15–16). A second-class component carries the scale-k large-field function χ^c_k(Y_i^{∼−6}) inside the new Ω_k ((1.72), p.379 l.17), not an older layer. So at the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause, s_Z ≤ ((1−β)/2 + h_k/α_{1,k})α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ α_{1,k}L^{−k}, by the family's bound on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} at step k+1 (next bullet) and (b3)'s 𝐇-part.
  - {#48} *The dilated sub-domains (derived).* For λ ≥ 1, let 𝒟^λ_k be the set of (A′, 𝐉) in 𝒟♮_k (#6.12) that obey three conditions on every Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n ≤ k. These weights are (190)'s: its gradient entry carries one power of (L^iη)^{−1} more than its first entry, and its D*D entry two more.

Since λ^{(k)}_nw ≤ λ_w ≤ c′^{k−n} where w > 1, the nesting of #130.15.2 (1) across steps keeps the ratio (1−β)/2 for L ≥ L₀(d) (that draft, §4). The consumers of the phase room take it with λ_w := λL in place of λ, and those of the gradient and current rooms with λ_cur := μλ, both fixed after L and M (that draft, §4 and §4a).
