Its source term grows on the family by at most λ², through the dev sups' λ ((b2)) and the transports' conditions above, so the att production constants are multiplied by λ², source terms fixed after L and M. The old masses do not compound across the steps. Map #MD2.4.12.50's recursion C^{(k+1)} ≤ 2C_winC_M + θ_kC^{(k)} closes uniformly in k only for the real-point part (map #MD2.4.12.49). The complex-data part is bounded from the production constants, Σ_{j≤k}E_j ≤ Ĉ·max_{j≤k}N^{(j)}ρ_{k+1}pl_{k+1} (covariance note §4, l.141), and the recursion used is C^{(k+1)} ≤ (1+Ĉ)max_{j≤k+1}N^{(j)} + L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15}C^{(k)}, which gives C^{(k)} ≤ C_S′ := max(2(1+Ĉ)C^{(1)}, 8(1+Ĉ)C_winC_M) once (1+Ĉ)s̄ ≤ 1/4 (covariance note l.149–157; map #MD2.4.12.51). On the family:
          - {#62} The production constant N^{(j)} ≤ 2C_winC_M + s_{j−1}C^{(j−1)} (j ≥ 2; covariance note l.131, map #MD2.4.12.50) gains λ² in its source term and at most λ⁴ in s_{j−1}, through the (β) term (λ²) and the quadratic term, which carries C^{(j−1)} again (λ⁴).
          - {#63} The carry term L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15} is unchanged, since a carried insertion's sup bound does not grow on the family ((R-eval-cr), *The carry*), and the conjugation factors of its covariant members at part (b)'s pairs do not enter it (#6.8, #130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #68).
          - {#64} Ĉ is unchanged: its inputs are the covariance note's top-layer bounds, which the family leaves as they are ((6c)'s inputs below).
          - {#65} C^{(1)} gains at most λ², as a production constant.
          - {#66} So the recursion closes with C^{(k)} ≤ λ²C_S′ for all k ≤ K, once (1+Ĉ)λ⁴s̄ ≤ 1/4. The term j = 1 of the max is (1+Ĉ)N^{(1)}, with N^{(1)} = C^{(1)} at most λ² times its 𝒟^{sub} value (previous sub-bullet), so it is at most λ²C_S′/2. For j ≥ 2, with C^{(j−1)} ≤ λ²C_S′ by induction: (1+Ĉ)2λ²C_winC_M ≤ λ²C_S′/4, (1+Ĉ)λ⁴s̄·λ²C_S′ ≤ λ²C_S′/4, and L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15}λ²C_S′ ≤ λ²C_S′/8. In either case the total is at most 5λ²C_S′/8. s̄ → 0 for g_K small ((R-m′), map #MD2.4.12.49), so the condition holds for g_K small given λ. C^{(k)} stays at most λ² times its 𝒟^{sub} bound C_S′, uniformly in k, and the λ⁴ is not compounded.
          - {#67} These bounds use the polymer conditions with the masses read on the family. The old masses grow by at most λ² there: the dev sups by λ ((b2)), and the att masses by the λ² of C^{(k)}. So (S-w)'s condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map #MD2.4.12.41), (12)(e)'s window counts, and D2.6 §5.2–5.3's (T0) condition (163 + C_win)M(ε^{new} + ε^{old} + ε^{ref}) ≤ ν_r/8 (D2.6 l.89) are needed with λ²ε^{old} in place of ε^{old}. D2.6 l.89 reads (T0) as a smallness of g under (R-m′), and the map's #MD2.4.12.41 condition is one as well, since ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ 2L⁴C(M/ν_e²)C^{(k)}ρ_k·pl_k (map #MD2.4.12.49) vanishes with g. The factor λ² is fixed before g, so all three hold for g_K small given λ.
        - {#68} The carry, D2.6 §5.4. §5.4 compares the transports of an insertion made at step j with their production value, over the carry length k + 1 − j (D2.6 l.96). On a layer n the family's phase room per fine bond is λ^{(k+1)}_nα_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1)} at step k + 1 and λ^{(j)}_nα_{1,j}L^{−j} at production. Since min(λ, c′^{k+1−n}) ≤ c′^{k+1−j}min(λ, c′^{j−n}) and α_{1,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)^{k+1−j}α_{1,j} (map #MD2.2.4.72), their ratio is at most (c′(1+β₀)/L)^{k+1−j} = ((1−β)/2)^{k+1−j} ≤ 1, on every layer, the layers older than j included. So the transports do not grow, and §5.4's condition reads 4c_πMλα_{1,k+1} ≤ 1, which holds for g small given λ. This bounds the transports at points of the family. Where the T-step evaluates them at part (b)'s pairs, their values at the T-step's points differ from those at the pairs by the conjugation factor of #6.8, paid once at that step and not compounded over the carry, since this ratio bounds the family values against production directly (#130.15.1–#130.15.2, derived, not reviewed).
        - {#69} D2.6 §5.1 uses 𝒟^{sub} as the clause's domain and the chain condition, which the transfer above gives on the family.
      - {#70} (6c)'s inputs from the covariance note (SW l.103). That note reads a carried dev insertion W only on X_W ⊂ (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} (covariance note §0, l.20), and its data bounds there, a, the per-step change, and |𝐉| < r_J^{(k+1)} (l.26–29), are top-layer bounds. On the top layer λ^{(k+1)}_{k+1} = min(λ, 1) = 1, so the family agrees with 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} there, and these bounds, the disc radii R_A, R_J (l.87), R-av in its top-layer uniform form (l.13), and c_πα_{1,j}·4096M ≤ ν (l.110) are used as the note states them, with no factor λ.
      - {#71} The c₀-rotation charge (map #MD2.4.11.5, *The charge*). The map bounds the imaginary part of 𝐔_pd per fine bond by α_{1,k} in η_k-units. On the family it is at most λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k} on the layer n: min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 from 𝐇(s, tB′), and λ^{(k+1)}_nα_{1,k+1}/L ≤ ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k} from a″ (the transfer bullet). Since λ^{(k)}_n ≤ λ, the telescoping gives |log c₀^{(k)}| ≤ λC_{c₀}α_{1,k}L^{j−k}(1 + o(1)) on the layer j, and the same for c₀^{(k+1)}(𝐔) at 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. The map's condition becomes 32c_GC_{c₀}λ(2/L)^{k−j}α_{1,k}(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 1. By #MD2.2.6.177's estimate its left side is at most λ times a quantity that is small uniformly in k, K, and j for L ≥ 4 and g_K small, so it holds for g_K small given λ. At j = 0, λ^{(k)}_n ≤ λ^{(k)}_0 = min(λ, c′^k) on every layer, since λ^{(k)}_n does not increase with n. With c′/L = (1−β)/(2(1+β₀)) ≤ 1/2 and α_{1,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²k^{1/2}α_{1,1}, this gives Σ_k2C_{c₀}λ^{(k)}_0α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ 2C_{c₀}(1+β₀)²α_{1,1}Σ_kk^{1/2}2^{−k} ≤ 4C_{c₀}(1+β₀)²α_{1,1}, because Σ_kk^{1/2}2^{−k} < 1.35. So on the family, D2.6 §5.0's ρ_ω (and the O4 note §4(f)'s, l.74) is taken with 4C_{c₀} in place of 2C_{c₀}, and the O4 note's C_ω with C_w + 3d + 4C_{c₀} in place of C_w + 3d, since its tilt entries are bounded through ρ_ω. C_{c₀} = 2d + 24d(d+1)B₀e^{16κ_s} is free of λ and fixed before the coupling, so ρ_ω ≪ r_{ω,0} and the O4 note's tilt absorption stand as before.
  - {#72} *The R-step lands in the family (derived from the bullets above, with T₂^{old} derived and reviewed, given LF: (R-eval-old), #5).* Take λ := max(8max(C_Λ, C_Λ′λ_R)M⁵/(1−β), C_QL), with C_Λ′ of (iii), *The starting values*, λ_R = C₁/C₀ (*The constants*), since T₁'s D*D is measured against the current room α_{0,k} = α_{1,k}/λ_R, and C_Q = C_Q(d) the O(1) of #45, so that a block datum of the data is at most 2λ(1+β₀)L^{−1} in units of α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (the contacts draft, §2). λ is fixed after L and M.
    - {#73} On a layer n ≤ k₀ the response, apart from T₂^{old}, is at most (C_ΛM⁵ + C_{mx}ε_kλ)α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}, once c′^{N₀} ≥ λ and C_{mx}ε_k ≤ ⅛(1−β) (g small given M).
    - {#74} On k₀ < n ≤ k it is, apart from T₂^{old}, at most (C_ΛM⁵e^{−4δM} + C_{mx}ε_kλ^{(k)}_n)α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}, once C_ΛM⁵e^{−4δM} ≤ ⅛(1−β), which M large given L gives (δ depends on d and L only), and C_{mx}ε_k ≤ ⅛(1−β).
    - {#75} The transported part lies in the post-R family with (b3)'s margin. Before R a bond's layer is no newer than after R, since R moves bonds only to more recent layers: Γ_j = Γ″_j ∩ Z^c for j < k and Γ_k = Γ″_k ∪ Z^{(k)} ((1.33), p.365 l.2–7). LFR I's preliminary integrations, from step k's original layering, in which Λ's age, p.200's decay, and 𝒟_k are stated, to 𝐁″_k, move bonds the same way: each replaces a part of Γ_j by its version at the scale j + 1 (LFR I (1.12)–(1.16), p.179 l.32–p.180 l.25). So the original layering, 𝐁″_k, and 𝐁_k are ordered from older to newer at every bond. So λ^{(k)}_n is no smaller before R. The factor f_n is no larger, since it decreases as k − n grows, but f_n ≥ 1 − β. The transported part is the data's, at most ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} (the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}), plus (b3)'s 𝐇-part, at most h_kL^{−k} with h_k ≤ min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2 (SW (b3); smaller on the older layers). With the response (at most ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} by the two bullets above) and the deformations (at most ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k}, (iii)), the pre-R phase is at most ((7/8)(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n + β/2)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. This is within the pre-R room f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} once β/2 ≤ ⅛(1−β), that is, β ≤ 1/5, the worst case being λ^{(k)}_n = 1. The project's β is 1/8 (the table's κ row; LF *The choice β = 1/8*); CRE's example is β = 1/4 (p.261 l.42).
    - {#76} Hence, up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), Φ(𝐔) obeys 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition (pre-R) at every point of 𝓜^c where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause. At the other points the bound goes through P ((iii), *The M-factored domain*). For the gradient and current conditions, the starting values are derived ((iii), *The starting values*), and the deformations' gradient and current are derived ((iii), *The current of the deformations*). Off Λ's decay range the response, apart from T₂^{old}, is at most C_{mx}ε_k times the room, which vanishes with g, so h_k's condition is (b3)'s and T_Y is unchanged by the Λ-response for g small (it is reduced by 4K for the phase and gradient part of K, (iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*). The same holds for U^{𝒦}, with U^{𝒦}(M(𝐔)) in place of U_k^0(M(𝐔)): its data differ from the post-R data only on the Λ's of the components that keep their old data.
  - {#77} *The remaining parts.*
    - {#78} (i) The class's other bounds at the points Φ(𝐔) (derived on 𝓜^c for the phase condition, except for T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)); the gradient and current conditions as in (iii); the conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative with the coefficients f♮_n, and the words' equality along V̂_t on which the bounds rest, derived, not reviewed (#6.10–#6.14, #113)). These are the cross members' weights ((R-eval-cr) below), the attached masses inside e^{2ΣA}, and the leading word's transports. Each is asserted on the family 𝒟^λ_k itself, the carried transports up to one conjugation factor at part (b)'s pairs (#6.8, #130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #68), and the points Φ(𝐔) lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*) up to T₂^{old}, so none of them needs a growth factor there. The masses and weights are the sub-bullet *The masses and weights* below. The transports are bounded through A_k, a sup over the evaluation points with A_k(S) ≤ c_πλ_wα_{1,k}ℓ_k(S) at every point of 𝒟^λ_k (λ_w := λL, #52.1, #171) (*A_k* below), so they are bounded at Φ(𝐔) as at the post-R points. What remains is the size of A_k where the R-step changes the data. Per fine bond, Φ(𝐔)'s phase differs from the transported part by the deformations and by the Λ-response ((iii); *The response is sourced on Λ*). The deformations are at most ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} on every layer ((iii)), and the pre-R budget of *The R-step lands in the family* counts them. The part T₂ of the Λ-response's mixed term, sourced on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, is at most C_{mx}ε_kλα_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} everywhere, for g small given λ (*The mixed term*); its part T₂^{old} is (R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5). Its other part exceeds ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} only where e^{−δ dist(b,Λ)} > (1−β)/(8C_ΛM⁵). So the Λ-response, apart from T₂^{old}, exceeds ¼(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} only where dist(b, Λ) < δ^{−1}log(8C_ΛM⁵/(1−β)). This lies inside the component's neighbourhood X^∼, which lies in a cube of side 100MR_k + O(1).
      - {#79} *The transports.* The loop's two squares meet that cube in O(d) segments of length at most its side, so the leading word's transport exponent there is at most O(d)·100MR_k·λα_{1,k}. With α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} (CRE (2.28)), this is g_k times a polylog, and it is below 1 for g small.
      - {#80} *The masses and weights.* The bounds that hold on the family 𝒟^λ_k itself need no growth factor at the points Φ(𝐔), which lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*) up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), with the reduced representative in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13). The bounds reach the members' values through the words' equality at that representative, which runs along V̂_t (#113, #6.10, #6.14), all derived, not reviewed.
        - {#81} The dev clause is asserted on 𝒟^λ_k (*The dilated sub-domains*), with the factor λ in a* and j* absorbed for g_K small.
        - {#82} D2.6's att bounds, and with them the attached masses inside e^{2ΣA}, hold on the family: D2.6 §5.4's condition there is 4c_πMλα_{1,k} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*), up to the transports' conjugation factor at part (b)'s pairs, paid once (#6.8, #130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #68).
        - {#83} The cross insertions' weights hold on the family: the cross clause (ii^cr), its production in (6), and the cross carry lemma are stated with sup bounds on 𝒟^λ (at step 1 on 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟_1, #227), and after an R-step on 𝒟^{ev} ((R-eval-cr) below, #222), the carry lemma's 5.4 step up to the transports' conjugation factor at part (b)'s pairs, paid once (#6.8, #130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #228).
      - {#84} *The payment.* The transports' factor is paid by the margin of (1.89) for a first-class component. For a second-class component Y_i it joins E(Y_i) in the quarter of exp(−p₀(g_k)) ((6C), *The payment of Π_iE(Y_i)*), since 3 + p₀(g_k)/16 + 1 + 7.3's costs at Y_i ≤ ¼p₀(g_k) for g small.
    - {#85} (ii) Analyticity on the whole post-R 𝒟_k (derived, given the readings already used at U_k^0). It is taken at Bałaban's configuration, not at Φ.
      - {#86} By (1.65), U_k^0 maps the post-R space into Ũ^{(N)c}, the space (1.64)–(1.69) [IV] for n = N (LFR II p.375 l.24). (1.60) evaluates the old terms at exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0 (p.372 l.23–25). It is printed for the terms with X not contained in □^{∼2} and for the second group's boundary terms (p.372 l.21–22), while the terms with X ⊂ Z^c and X ⊂ □^{∼2} (p.372 l.2–4) take (1.59). Bałaban says only that the analyticity domain "is restricted on the cube □₀, or on the domain X, by the analyticity properties of the original term" (p.372 l.34–35). The chain starts at U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} ((1.51), p.370 l.11), Bałaban's stage on 𝐁_k″ with its normalisation u_k″, since the first preliminary step reads its source at that stage's averages (#88.1). (1.60)'s 𝐄- and 𝐑-terms take the same value at exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0, being gauge invariant and analytic on RG I's G^c-invariant U^c_j (#87). The second group's boundary terms, CRE's 𝐁-terms, are invariant only when A is transformed as well (CRE p.261 l.60–62), and u_k″ is G^c-valued at complex data, so the note takes them at U_k″, as (1.49) evaluates them (p.369 l.14), not at the un-normalised point. That U_k″ lies in Ũ^{(N)c}, where the chain starts, is therefore a reading (the third reading of #17, *R-chain-start*).
      - {#87} If 𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n+1)c}, then exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n)c}, with no gauge transformation (LFR I p.191 l.39–41). For n = 0 the space is Ũ_k^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.191 l.33–34), CRE's space for the 𝐁-terms ((2.41)(ii), CRE p.261 l.59–60). The old terms' space is RG I's U^c_j (CRE p.259 l.41–42), which is G^c-invariant. With p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces and R-far″(β), it lies in 𝒟_k ((6C), *The insertions*).
      - {#88} So Bałaban's chain, his stages (1.44) continued in the data, applied to U_k″(𝐔) = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0(𝐔))^{u_k″^{−1}} (#86) lies in 𝒟_k for every 𝐔 in the post-R 𝒟_k, under the readings *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides* (#86, #88.1, #157), with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces and given R-far″(β) (#87), and every insertion and word is analytic there. The same holds for U^{𝒦}, component by component, since (1.65) is proved for each component separately.
        {#88.1} Bałaban's stages are the minimisers U(𝔹_k^{(n)}, ·) of their data, which (1.44) represents as (exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌)^{u^{−1}}, with the normalisation u (p.186 l.2–5). The inclusion of p.191 l.39–42 is printed for the step exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌 before u, as an example of the statements "connected with the expressions we have to consider in the n+1st step" (l.37–39), with a proof "almost identical" to that of (1.44)–(1.52), which carry u. The spaces are invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (CRE p.261 l.42–43), and u is G^c-valued at complex data, so there the printed step and the stage are different points. Iterated, the two chains differ even at real data and fixed real integration variables: each later source is read at the stage's own average (p.188 l.53), which u rotates, so the un-normalised chain is a gauge transform of Bałaban's only after the later variables are rotated by R(u) (#116's covariance). The function that the R-step integrates is Bałaban's, the normalised chain, and this note's u-free chain is that chain (#91, #95, #104, #110). That its stages, continued in the data, lie in Ũ^{(n)c} on the whole post-R 𝒟_k is the reading *R-chain-norm* of p.191 l.37–44 (#17): the inclusion holds for the stages (1.44) with their normalisation, in which Bałaban's integrand is written. It is used for the domain of the analytic extension ((ii), #113, #870), and for the complex curvature conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the final stage 𝕌^{(0)}, which #6.1–#6.2 and #6.13 pass to the reduced representative 𝕌̃ with the coefficients f♮_n, so that 𝕌̃ ∈ 𝒟♮_k (#6, #12, #158). The stages' complex parts, and with them 𝒟^λ_k's phase, gradient, and current conditions at 𝕌̃, are bounded at VAR's representatives without it (#92–#123, #140–#150).
      - {#89} At a real post-R minimiser this point equals the transport-form evaluation point, since Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U)) on 𝓜 (*The transport form*). The real minimisers are a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of 𝓜^c, not of the T-step's open set, so the two functions agree on 𝓜^c only ((iii), *The scope*). The bounds are taken at Φ on 𝓜^c and reach the other points through P. The analyticity is taken at Bałaban's configuration on the whole domain.
    - {#90} (iii) The complex parts of the N preliminary deformations and of 𝐇_k″(g_kB) as left factors: the phase, the gradient, and the current derived on every layer, on 𝓜^c, up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)) and given C_Λ's frame bound (#41.1–#41.2, derived, not reviewed) wherever the smallness of ε comes from ε_Φ (from *One step* on).
      - {#91} *Their sources are real.* The source of the n-th factor is the fluctuation V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} of (1.44), with |V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| < 2δ′_j on the * bonds by χ′_j (LFR I p.182 l.53), ≤ c_χδ′_j on all bonds, the b_0(c) included (#197), and δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) (p.183 l.2). The source of 𝐇_k″ is g_kB, with g_k|B| < δ′_k, localized in Λ (LFR II p.360 l.41–43, p.372 l.14–15). So the factors are real at real data, and their complex parts come from the complex background only. (1.45), sup_{B^i(y)} L^iη|ℍ^{(n)}|, sup_{B^i(y)} (L^iη)²|∇_{U_k^{(n+1)}}ℍ^{(n)}| ≤ 4B₃δ′_j exp(−δd(y, Ω^c_{j+1}∖Z″_{j+1})), is printed at the real U_{k,Z}^{(n+1)} (LFR I p.186 l.5–11). The u-free chain's source is s_n = (1/i)log V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1}, relative to V^{(j)} (p.188 l.53), with |s_n| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j (*χ′_j and χ′^{(j)}*), so (1.45) reads O(c_χ)B₃δ′_j for it at real data. At complex data the factors are bounded through VAR's data derivatives instead (#93–#123), not through (1.45). The gauge transformations u of (1.44) are kept: each step is followed by the inverse of its normalisation, so that the stage keeps its data (#95, #104).
      - {#92} *On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k (derived on 𝓜^c).* At the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause, the data's complex part is s_Z ≤ ((1−β)/2 + h_k/α_{1,k})α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ½α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (*Where it lands*, with h_k ≤ min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2). The deformations' complex parts are bounded on these layers as on Z's older layers, at VAR's representatives of the u-free chain's stages (#93–#123): no step of #93–#123 restricts the layer of the point, and *The sum* (#123) bounds them by C_*O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²λα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond on every layer, below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} for g small, inside the room that 𝒟^λ_k leaves after the transported part and the response. This uses neither (1.45) on Bałaban's complex spaces nor the stages' membership in those spaces (#88.1).
      - {#93} *On Z's layers m ≤ k₀: the phase, the gradient, and the current (derived on 𝓜^c; the phase on the newer layers by #92, and the gradient and the current there by #140–#150, on every layer).* (1.45)'s bound O(c_χ)B₃δ′_jL^{−m}e^{−δd} on |ηℍ^{(n)}| per fine bond, summed over the steps, carries the factor L^{k−m} there, and k − m runs up to N, LFR II's N = R_j (p.384 l.17) replaced by N_k (#3.2.1.2). The deformations are bounded instead through their dependence on the external data, whose derivatives VAR Proposition 9 bounds. Here i is the layer of the point x, y ∈ Λ_i the block with x ∈ Δ(y), and j the layer of step n's collar.
        - {#94} *The printed inputs.* VAR Theorem 1 (p.279) asks (7), |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁ on 𝔅_k with one ε₁ for all layers, and ε₁ ≤ a₁, with a₀, a₁, B₃ depending on d and L only; uniqueness needs B₃ε₁ ≤ a₀. So ε₁ is a fixed constant with M′ε₁ ≤ a₁ (p.305 l.6), and the real stage data meet (7) with room for g small. By Proposition 9 (p.309) and (172) (p.305), U_k(V′V_0) extends analytically to V′ = e^{iB} on |B| < r_B := C₁^{VAR}ε₁/2, a radius independent of g. Here C₁^{VAR} is the C₁ of VAR (172), VAR's absolute constant (p.305 l.44), not CRE's C₁; in the rest of this bullet C₁ is C₁^{VAR}. (172) assumes |V′ − 1| < C₁ε₁ and infers |B′| < 2C₁ε₁ (p.305 l.31–33), so the ball of B is the one inside the assumption: for |B| < C₁ε₁/2 ≤ log 2 (ε₁ small, as (172) takes it, l.36), |e^{iB} − 1| ≤ |B|e^{|B|} < C₁ε₁. On this ball U_k(V′V_0)U_k(V_0)^{−1} in the Landau gauge is exp iη𝓗(B), with 𝓗(0) = 0. B is dimensionless: H₀B minimises on L^{j(·)}ηQA′ = B (p.297 (129)). On the ball, (190) bounds δ𝓗_μ(B, x)/δB_ν(y′) by O(1)(L^iη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−δ₀d(y, y′)/8), y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, its x-gradient by the same with (L^iη)^{−2}, and D^{η*}_{U_k}D^η_{U_k} and Δ^η_{U_k} of it by the same with (L^iη)^{−3} (p.308 l.39–42). Sums over y′ carry the weights (L^{j′}η)^d ((130)). (190) follows from (189), which Bałaban states as "a final result only", the calculation not performed (p.308 l.28–30), with (188), Lemma 2.1, Proposition 2, and (181) (l.36); it is accepted from Bałaban.
        - {#95} *The stages are VAR minimisers.* LFR I represents its stage configurations by VAR's 𝓗 on their determining sets: (1.30), (1.34) ("in the standard way"), (1.44)–(1.45), with B₃ of VAR Theorem 1 (p.191 l.28). This application is accepted from Bałaban. The u-free chain's step n is exp iηℍ^{(n)}(s_n)𝕌 (p.191 l.39–41 states its landing), followed by the inverse of its normalisation, so that the stage keeps its data ((1.44); #104), with the source s_n = g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j) of the integration variable B_j (p.191 l.39–41), real at real U. Its complex part comes only from the background 𝕌: through 𝓗's argument, and on the bonds b_0(c) through C, h, and D̃, which depend on the stage's average V^{(j)} (RG I p.267 l.22–26, p.268 l.2–3; #197). On the * bonds s_n = g_jB_j is real and independent of the background (#196).
        - {#96} *The data.* Fix a real base point U of 𝐔 = e^{iηA′}U. The R-step's integration variables are real ((6C), *The integration variables are real*), so the stage data at 𝐔 and at U differ by Δd, the complex part of the external data. The phase per fine bond is at most λα_{1,k}L^{−k}, and a layer-j′ block bond averages over L^{j′} fine bonds, so |Δd(y′)| ≤ C_dλα_{1,k}L^{j′−k}, with C_d depending on d. Every stage is a function of the same Δd.
        - {#97} *One step.* Let U₀ be the real stage-(n+1) configuration at U, and write the u-free chain's stage-(n+1) configuration at 𝐔 as 𝕌 = e^{iηA_Φ}U₀, bondwise. The norms are PBG's (3.41), |A|_{(α)} := sup_i sup_{Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}}(L^iη)^{−α}|A|. ε_{n+1} := sup|A_Φ|_{(0)} is the stage-(n+1) deviation from the real chain, the sup taken over the segment 𝐔_t := U^{post}(e^{itB}M(U)), t ∈ [0, 1], with B := (1/i)log M(𝐔)M(U)^{−1}, of #125, which runs from P(U) to P(𝐔) in 𝓜^c, where #97.1's identity holds; the chain depends on 𝐔 only through M(𝐔), and M(P(U)) = M(U), so its stage-(n+1) configuration at t = 0 is U₀ (the bounds at 𝐔 are those at P(𝐔), #127; *The sum* bounds it there). Let d_{n+1} = (Δd_L, B*) be 𝕌's plain block data on 𝔹_k^{(n)} relative to those of U₀, with Δd_L on the external blocks and B* on step n's collar, whose blocks are of layer j, and 𝕎 := e^{iη𝓗(d_{n+1})}U₀ its VAR representative (*The Landau representatives*, below). A layer-j′ block variable is a product of at most O(1)L^{j′} fine bond variables along a path, each with phase at most η|A_Φ|, so |d_{n+1}(y′)| ≤ C_GL^{j′}ηε_{n+1} on y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, with C_G = O(1) depending on d, far below r_B once ε_{n+1} ≤ 2ε_Φ (*The induction*). With the stage-n 𝓗 at the base U₀, put F(d, s) := η𝓗(Δd_L, s ⊕ B*) − η𝓗(Δd_L, B*), where e^{i(s⊕B*)} = e^{is}e^{iB*}. F(·, 0) = 0 by definition. At s = 0, 𝓗(Δd_L, B*) =: A_L gives 𝕌's representative 𝕎 in stage n's Landau gauge (#104), since 𝕌 is the minimiser with its own stage-n data (#97.1). A_L is not 𝓗_{n+1} at 𝕌's stage-(n+1) data on 𝔹_k^{(n+1)}, the representative relative to stage (n+1)'s set, because REG's Landau gauge depends on the set through R(U₀) (p.80 l.11–14). The two differ by a gauge transformation, which G of #103–#104 absorbs. F is analytic for |s| < r_B/2, and its d-derivative obeys (190) uniformly there.
          {#97.1} *The minimiser at s = 0 (derived).* 𝔹_k^{(n)} refines 𝔹_k^{(n+1)}: the step replaces the layer-(j+1) blocks of step n's collar by the layer-j blocks they average, with the constraint V̄_j = V_{j+1} (LFR I p.180 l.3–5, l.15–19, (1.15)–(1.16)). So a configuration with 𝕌's stage-n data (Δd_L, B*) has 𝕌's stage-(n+1) data: stage n's constraint set 𝔙_k(𝔹_k^{(n)}, (Δd_L, B*)) (VAR (3), p.278 l.11–13) is a submanifold, through 𝕌, of stage (n+1)'s. 𝕌 is critical for the action (5) on stage (n+1)'s space (6), so it is critical on that submanifold. It also lies in stage n's space 𝔘_k({Ω_j^{(n)}}, ε₀) (VAR (2), p.278 l.2–8): stage n's layering puts step n's collar in layer j, where (2)'s bounds ε₀L^{−2j} are weaker than stage (n+1)'s ε₀L^{−2(j+1)}, and on the other layers the two layerings agree. So 𝕌 is a critical configuration in stage n's space (6). At real data that space has a unique critical orbit (VAR Theorem 1, p.279 l.15), so 𝕌 lies on the orbit of 𝕎 = e^{iηA_L}U₀, and on 𝓜^c by *The scope*.
        - {#98} *The Schwarz bound.* At real integration variables |s| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j (*χ′_j and χ′^{(j)}*). The Schwarz lemma in s gives |∂_dF| ≤ (12c_χδ′_j/r_B)·O(1)L^{−i}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8} for |s| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j. So |F(d_{n+1}, s) − F(0, s)| ≤ O(δ′_j/r_B)L^{−i}Σ_{y′}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8}|d_{n+1}(y′)| ≤ O(δ′_j)C_GS_L·ηε_{n+1} per fine bond at x, with S_L := sup_y Σ_{y′}L^{j′−i}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/16} ≥ 1.
        - {#99} *S_L is finite.* VAR's d is the distance (2.46) of its [3] (VAR p.288 l.45), PRT2 p.231 l.10–12: the length of a contour counted in blocks of each layer's own scale. A fully crossed layer is at least one layer of M-cubes of its own scale wide (*Across the layers of 𝐁″_k(Y₁)* in packet A), so it adds at least M ≥ M/M₁ to d. The newer layers' factor L^{j′−i} and the older layers' block count L^{d(i−j′)} are therefore beaten once δ₀M/(16M₁) ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1. This is a condition on M given d, L, and M₁, of the kind of LFR I's δM/M₁ ≥ 2 (p.186). A localized set 𝐁(Y) ∪ 𝐁_k″ (CRE (2.14)) adds the strip Y∖Y⁰ of width at most 2M₁ (LFR II p.373 l.26–27), whose layers satisfy dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ L^nξM₁ (CRE p.256 l.61). A fully crossed strip layer is therefore at least M₁ blocks of its own scale wide, adds at least M₁ to d, and its factor L·e^{−δ₀M₁/16} is beaten as above once δ₀M₁/16 ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1. This holds, since M₁ = K_{CRE}M′, with CRE's integer K_{CRE} ≥ 12d + 1 (not VAR's factor K below), and δ₀M′ ≥ 64 log L (table #T2.5 and #T2.15), so δ₀M₁/16 ≥ 4(12d + 1)log L.
        - {#100} *The gauge representatives (derived).* The words do not depend on the representative, but the bounds do. Membership in Bałaban's spaces, and in 𝒟^λ_k, is a condition on a representative 𝐔 = U′U (CRE p.261, (i)–(iii) and l.60–61). The transported part and the response are bounded in Φ's frame, and F at VAR's representatives. One G^c gauge transformation G relates a stage configuration to its representative. The bullets below bound G by O(1)L^iη times ε_{n+1}, run the chain in a frame where only G enters a step (*The reduced frame*), and run the induction over the steps.
          - {#101} *The base.* U₀ obeys VAR (9)–(10) (p.279), which are PBG (3.35)–(3.36) with α₀ = B₃ε₁, and M′B₃ε₁ ≤ a₅ (VAR p.305). VAR applies PBG at its minimisers in the same way (pp.285, 295, 297). So PBG's bounds hold at U₀ for γ ∈ [−4, 4]: |G′f|_{(γ+2)}, |∇G′f|_{(γ+1)}, |G′∇*f|_{(γ+1)} ≤ B₀|f|_{(γ)} ((3.47)), the kernel bound (3.48) for C = (Q′G′²Q′*)^{−1}, and (3.49) for P.
          - {#102} *The Landau representatives (derived).* A configuration V near U₀ is in the Landau gauge relative to U₀ if R(U₀)D*_{U₀}(1/iη)log(VU₀^{−1}) = 0 (REG (1.27)). REG's representative is not normalised from an arbitrary element of the orbit. The normalisation \overline{R_0u}^j = 1 ((1.29)) does not respect the group structure, so the set {V^{u^{−1}}} "depends on an element U chosen to generate" it (p.80 l.28–30). REG generates it from the element in the axial gauge Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀) ((1.28), p.81), "for deeper reasons connected with properties of the averaging operations" (p.82 l.25–26). The transformation u from the axial element is unique (Theorem 4, p.88). It is u = u′u₁ (p.89 l.18), with u₁ the transformation of the inductive hypothesis (p.88 l.18), which "is determined uniquely in terms of U₁" (p.88 l.36). With this generator the representative's double-bar averages are the data: \bar{\bar U}_1^j = e^{iB} with B given by the plain data V through (1.31) (p.82). This is VAR's constraint (20), and VAR Proposition 2 (p.281) takes these representatives.
            - {#103} The representatives used here are VAR's. The base U₀ is the real stage of #115, a real minimiser with its own data V₀ (#97.1), and U₀ = U_k(V₀): U′ = 1 satisfies REG (1.19), so U₀ lies in Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀) (VAR p.280 l.44). For a stage configuration 𝕌 with plain data V, relative to this base, let 𝕎 := e^{iη𝓗(B)}U₀, with B given by V. By VAR Proposition 9 (p.309) 𝕎 is, by definition, the Landau-gauge form of the axial-gauge minimiser U_k(V), with REG's normalisation from the axial element. At real data 𝕌 is a critical configuration with the data V ((iii), *The stages are VAR minimisers*). The critical orbit is unique (VAR Theorem 1, p.279), and the axial gauge meets the orbit at U_k(V). So 𝕌 = 𝕎^G for a gauge transformation G, which is u_L, taking 𝕎 to U_k(V), followed by w, taking U_k(V) to 𝕌. VAR's orbits are those of the group (4) of gauge transformations with u(y) = 1 on 𝔅_k (VAR p.278 l.16–19, l.32–33), and 𝕌 and U_k(V) lie on the same orbit, so w can be taken in this group: w(y) = 1 at the block points y ∈ 𝔅_k.
            - {#104} So 𝓗's argument is 𝕌's own plain data, with no conjugation by G. At step n, 𝕎 = e^{iη𝓗(d_{n+1})}U₀ =: e^{iηA_L}U₀ and 𝕎_n := e^{iη𝓗(d_n)}U₀, where d_{n+1} = (Δd_L, B*) is the plain data of 𝕌 relative to U₀'s (*One step*), and d_n = (Δd_L, s̃ ⊕ B*) is the data of the step, with the source s̃ of the reduced frame (#116). The Landau step exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌 relative to 𝕌 (VAR Proposition 9) does not keep the plain data ("The set Σ_k is not contained in 𝔙_k(𝔅_k, V)", REG p.81 l.23–24). LFR I's stage is that step transformed by the inverse of its normalisation u, (exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌)^{u^{−1}} ((1.44), p.186 l.5; (1.34), p.184 l.39–41), and it satisfies the constraints M^j(U_k^{(n)}) = V_j (p.183 l.38–39). So 𝕌^{(n)}'s plain data are d_n exactly, and 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} with G_n = u_{L,n} then w_n, as for 𝕌 (#103). Here w_n takes the axial element relative to U₀ to LFR I's stage, which is axial relative to its own base 𝕌 (#3.2.2.1). u_L, w, 𝕎, and 𝕎_n are explicit analytic functions of the configurations, and the identities 𝕌 = 𝕎^G and 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} hold at real post-R minimisers, so they hold on 𝓜^c (*The scope*). At a real non-minimiser the stages of Φ's chain are not minimisers, and the identities fail there ((iii), *The scope*).
          - {#105} *The bound on G (derived).* Write G = e^{iλ̂}. (190) gives |A_L|_{(0)}, |∇A_L|_{(−1)} ≤ O(C_GS_L)ε_{n+1}.
            - {#106} At the block points. At a block point y of a layer-i block, w(y) = 1, so G(y) = u_L(y), REG's transformation u = u′u₁ from the axial element U_k(V) to 𝕎 (p.89 l.18). REG bounds it by |u′u₁ − 1| < 16dB₁(α₀ + α₁), "reasoning as in (1.70)–(1.72)" (p.89 l.35). There (1.70)–(1.71) (p.88) bound the transformation at x ∈ B^j(Λ_j) by 2Σ_n max|log(\bar R\bar{\bar U}_1^n)(Γ)|, and these logarithms by the averages |Q_n(U₀, ηA)| over paths of at most dL^{n+1} bonds. With A_L in place of REG's A this gives |λ̂(y)| ≤ 16dL^iη|A_L|_{(0)}.
            - {#107} The gradient. Bondwise, 𝕎 = 𝕌^{G^{−1}} reads A_L = N(λ̂) + D₀λ̂, with N(λ̂) := e^{−i ad λ̂}A_Φ + (Φ(i ad λ̂) − 1)D₀λ̂ + R₂/η, Φ(z) := (1 − e^{−z})/z, and R₂ the BCH remainder, at most O(η|A_Φ|·η|D₀λ̂| + |λ̂|(η|D₀λ̂|)²). While |λ̂| and η|D₀λ̂| are at most a small constant c₀, this gives |D₀λ̂|_{(0)} ≤ 2(|A_L|_{(0)} + |A_Φ|_{(0)}) ≤ C_DS_Lε_{n+1}, with C_D = O(C_G).
            - {#108} Off the block points. Every point x of a layer-i block is joined to its block point by a path of at most dL^i bonds inside the block. Along a bond b, |λ̂(b₋)| and |λ̂(b₊)| differ by at most 2η|D₀λ̂(b)|, since the real U₀ transports by a norm-preserving Ad. So |λ̂(x)| ≤ |λ̂(y)| + 2dL^iη sup|D₀λ̂|, and |λ̂|_{(1)} ≤ B_Gε_{n+1} with B_G = O(d)(C_G + C_D)S_L.
            - {#109} Continuity. Along 𝐔_t, λ̂ is continuous (u_L is analytic in the configuration, VAR p.307 l.2–4, and w is by its construction, #104), and λ̂ = 0 at t = 0, where 𝕌 = U₀. The bounds make |λ̂| and η|D₀λ̂| at most O(S_Lε_{n+1}) < c₀/2 for g small, with ε_{n+1} ≤ 2ε_Φ (*The induction*), so they hold at t = 1. G is not determined by a fixed-point equation, and no bound on G′, R, or C at the complex point enters.
          - {#110} *The reduced frame (derived and reviewed: #116.1's bound on ũ_m carries the factor L^N on the deep layers, #3.2).* Let 𝕌^{(n)} := (exp iηℍ^{(n)}(s̃)𝕌)^{u^{−1}} be the u-free step from 𝕌, LFR I's stage (1.44) with its normalisation u (#104), with the source s̃ of this frame (below), and 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} as in *The Landau representatives*. Put 𝕌̃^{(n)} := 𝕎_n^G with this step's G, which is 𝕌^{(n)} transformed by a G^c gauge transformation ũ_n made of G_n^{−1} and G.
            - {#111} The chain is run on 𝕌̃^{(n)}, with the later sources conjugated by the accumulated ũ at the collars' block points. Its words equal those of the u-free chain, and the class's bounds are taken at this representative. The proof uses no continuation in the gauge variable, because 𝒟_k, like CRE's Ũ^c, is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (CRE p.261 l.42–43). It is the identity theorem in 𝐔 on 𝓜^c, as for the identities of *The Landau representatives*.
              - {#112} The base U of *The data*, and with it each U₀, is held fixed, so the reduced chain is a function of 𝐔 alone. At a real post-R minimiser, ũ is then G-valued: 𝕌, 𝕎, 𝕌^{(n)}, and 𝕎_n are real, so G and G_n are (at U itself G = 1, below). The u-free chain's steps are covariant under G-valued gauge transformations: they are built from gauge-covariant operators on Bałaban's spaces, which are G-invariant (LFR I p.191 l.32–33). So the reduced chain is the u-free chain transformed by the real ũ, and the words, which are G-invariant, take the same value on 𝓜.
              - {#113} Both words are analytic functions of 𝐔. The u-free chain's words are analytic on the post-R 𝒟_k ((ii), under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β), #88), at Bałaban's extension; taken at the Landau representative (V̂_t, J^h(V̂_t)) of P(𝐔_t) (#6.10, derived, not reviewed), they agree with it as far as V̂_t lies in Ũ_k^c(Y₄), which holds along the segments at the T-step's points (#6.14) and at the extension-domain points (#6.16), derived, not reviewed (#127, #156). The reduced chain's words are analytic wherever its data stay in VAR's ball |B| < r_B and its final stage lies in 𝒟♮_k (#6.12, derived, not reviewed), since G, u_L, w, 𝕎, and 𝕎_n are analytic in the configuration (*The Landau representatives*). Along each path P(𝐔_t), t ∈ [0, 1], the data are at most C_GL^{j′}ηε_{n+1}, far below r_B (*One step*, with the induction below run over the segment 𝐔_t), and the final stage lands in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟♮_k (*The sum*; *The R-step lands in the family*; #6.13, derived, not reviewed, its curvature conditions under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, #86, #88.1, #157, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, #156, given R-far″(β), #87), both up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)).
              - {#114} Each such path in 𝓜^c starts at the real minimiser P(U) ∈ 𝓜. 𝓜 is totally real of maximal dimension in 𝓜^c (*The scope*), so two functions analytic near P(U) that agree on 𝓜 agree on a neighbourhood of P(U) in 𝓜^c. By uniqueness of analytic continuation along the path, they agree at P(𝐔).
            - {#115} At U the same construction gives the chain of the real stages' VAR representatives, with G = 1 and ũ_n(U) = G_n(U)^{−1} real. This is a real gauge transform of the real chain, which preserves every norm. U₀ and ε_{n+1} of *One step* refer to this chain and to the reduced chain at 𝐔, and the next step starts from 𝕌̃^{(n)} with its own plain data and its own G.
            - {#116} The sources. With s := s_n(U), the reduced real source is s^{re} := Ad_{ũ(U)}s, with ũ(U) the accumulated real ũ, so |s^{re}| = |s| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j. At 𝐔, s̃ := Ad_ũs_n(𝐔), with s_n(𝐔) the u-free source at the u-free stage's average. A gauge transformation v of V induces B′ → R(v)B′, and the expressions are invariant under it (RG I p.265 l.34–35). So Q̃ and C̃ are covariant, and with them h and D̃ (built from Q̃ and C̃, p.267 l.21–26) and C. For G-valued v this gives C_{V^v} = R(v)C_VR(v)^{−1}, and likewise for h and D̃. So s̃ = g_jC(V_{red})Ad_ũB_j − hD̃(g_jC(V_{red})Ad_ũB_j), with V_{red} the reduced stage's average, at the real minimisers, where ũ is G-valued (#112), and on 𝓜^c by the identity theorem, as in #113–#114. s^{re} is the same expression at U. ũ_n is formed after step n's source is used, so s̃ carries only the ũ_m with m ≥ n + 1. So s̃ − s^{re} has two parts on step n's collar. The conjugation part is at most O(B̄_G)L^NL^jηΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1})|s|, by the bound on ũ_m below (#3.2.2). The average part: by #115 the reduced stage at 𝐔 deviates from the real chain by ε_{n+1}, so V_{red}(𝐔) = e^{iX}V_{red}(U) with |X| ≤ C_GL^jηε_{n+1} on the layer-j blocks B(c_−) ∪ B(c_+) that C reads (#197), as for the block data (#97), and C uses B_j only on the * bonds, where |g_jB_j| < δ′_j (#196). So c′_C, c_C, and c_D (#197) bound this part by O(c′_C + c_Dc_C²δ′_j)C_GL^jηε_{n+1}δ′_j(1 + O(δ′_j)), the last factor from hD̃'s B-derivative, which is O(|B|). This is at most O(c′_CC_G)L^jηε_{n+1}δ′_j for g small, since c′_C ≥ 1. So |s̃ − s^{re}| ≤ O(B̄_G)L^NL^jηΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1})|s| + O(c′_CC_G)L^jηε_{n+1}δ′_j.
              {#116.1} The bound on ũ_m (derived and reviewed, with the factor L^N of #3.2.2). ũ_m is made of w_m^{−1}, u_{L,m}^{−1}, and G (#3.2.2 (1)). Its value at U is real, and real factors conjugate by norm-preserving Ads, so the logarithm of ũ_m(𝐔)ũ_m(U)^{−1} is at most the sum of the three factors' changes, up to a BCH remainder of second order in them. At a point of a stage-m block of layer J, G changes by at most B_GL^Jηε_{m+1} (*The bound on G*, #108 in stage m's blocking), and w_m by at most 4dC_GL^Jηε_{m+1} (#3.2.2.1). u_{L,m} is REG's transformation from the axial element to 𝕎_m = e^{iη𝓗(d_m)}U₀ at the fixed real U₀ (#112), analytic in the configuration (VAR p.307 l.2–4), with 𝓗(d_m) = A_L + η^{−1}F(d_{m+1}, s̃) by #97's definition of F. As at #106, REG's reasoning (1.70)–(1.72) for u = u′u₁ (p.89 l.35) gives it through 𝕎_m's own averaged transports inside the stage-m block, within REG's conditions (1.69) (p.88 l.28–29). So its change is at most 4dL^{J+1}η sup_B|Δ𝓗(d_m)|, and L^Jη ≤ L^NL^jη (#3.2.2 (2), #3.2.1). ΔF is the change of step m's field F(d_{m+1}, s̃) between U and 𝐔: (a) and (b) of step m (#118–#119) bound it by O(c_χB̄_GL^N + c′_CC_G + C_G)S_Lδ′_jΣ_{m′≥m+1}(ε_{m′} + ε_{m′+1}) per unit of η, and (190)'s gradient bound (#94) bounds its gradient by the same, in the units of |∇·|_{(−1)}. Step m's (a)–(b) involve only the ũ_{m′} with m′ ≥ m + 1, so these bounds follow by induction along the chain from m = N down, with no circularity. Together, at every point of a layer-j block of step n, ũ_m differs from its value at U by at most O(B̄_G)L^NL^jηε_{m+1} plus O(L^Nδ′_j)L^NL^jηΣ_{m′≥m+1}(ε_{m′} + ε_{m′+1}) (#3.2.2), with a constant depending on d, L, r_B, S_L, c_χ, c′_C, c_C, c_D, C_G, and B̄_G, as in #3.2.2 (4). The second part's L^N is (b)'s. Its sum carries only ε's, so the factor does not compound along the chain. Summed over m ≥ n + 1, the second parts add at most O((N+1)L^Nδ′_j) times the main sum, which tends to 0 with g (#3.2.1). This gives the conjugation part above.
              {#116.2} The size of s̃. With the induction's ε ≤ 2ε_Φ ≤ 4λα_{1,k}, up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)) and given C_Λ's frame bound (#41.1–#41.2, derived, not reviewed), this is at most O((N+1)L^NB̄_Gλ_wα_{1,k})|s| + O(c′_CC_Gλα_{1,k})δ′_j ≤ ¼|s| + ½c_χδ′_j for g small (L^jη ≤ 1), since (N+1)L^Nα_{1,k} is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. So |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, inside *The Schwarz bound*.
          - {#117} *One step's deviation.* The factor of step n in the reduced frame is f := 𝕌̃^{(n)}𝕌^{−1}, and bondwise f_b = (𝕎_n^G)_b(𝕎^G)_b^{−1} = Ad_{G(b₋)}(e^{iηX_b}) exactly, with X := (1/iη)log(𝕎_n𝕎^{−1}). 𝕎_n𝕎^{−1} = e^{iη𝓗(d_n)}e^{−iηA_L}, so X = 𝓗(d_n) − A_L + β = F(d_{n+1}, s̃)/η + β, with the BCH remainder β at most η|𝓗(d_n) − A_L||A_L|. By (190) and |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, |X| ≤ O(c_χS_L)δ′_j(L^iη)^{−1}. At U, G = 1 and f(U) = e^{iF(0, s^{re})}, since F carries η. So the step's deviation dev_n := sup_b η^{−1}|log f_b − log f(U)_b| is at most the sum of three terms.
            - {#118} (a) η^{−1}|F(d_{n+1}, s̃) − F(0, s̃)| ≤ O(δ′_j)C_GS_Lε_{n+1}, by the Schwarz bound.
            - {#119} (b) η^{−1}|F(0, s̃) − F(0, s^{re})| ≤ O(c_χB̄_GL^N + c′_CC_G)S_Lδ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), by (190) in s and the bound on |s̃ − s^{re}| (its source term carries ε_{n+1}, the sum's first term).
            - {#120} (c′) |β| + (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|X| ≤ O(c_χ)(C_GS_L + B_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}, since |Ad_GX − X| ≤ (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|X|, |λ̂| ≤ B_GL^iηε_{n+1}, and |A_L| ≤ O(C_GS_L)ε_{n+1}.
            - {#121} Hence dev_n ≤ C_*L^NS_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), which involves only the stages before n, with C_* depending on d, L, B₃, c_χ, c′_C, c_D (#197), and r_B. G enters through |λ̂|, and the accumulated ũ_m with m ≥ n + 1 enter through (b), by #116.1.
          - {#122} *The induction.* 𝕌̃^{(n)} = f e^{iηA_Φ}U₀ and the reduced real chain's U₀^{(n)} = f(U)U₀, so 𝕌̃^{(n)}(U₀^{(n)})^{−1} = (f f(U)^{−1})·Ad_{f(U)}(e^{iηA_Φ}). Ad_{f(U)} is a real conjugation, which preserves |A_Φ|, so ε_n ≤ ε_{n+1} + dev_n(1 + O(δ′_j)). Starting from Φ's deviation ε_Φ, over 𝐇_k″(g_kB) and the N deformations, suppose ε_m ≤ 2ε_Φ for every stage before n. Then ε_n ≤ ε_Φ + 2C_*L^NS_L²(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j·4ε_Φ ≤ 2ε_Φ for g small, since Σ_nδ′_j ≤ (N + 1)(1+β₀)²N^{β₀}δ′_k (p.192 l.2–3) and L^N(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2} (N = N_k, #3.2.1). This keeps ε small at every stage, as the bullets above assume.
        - {#123} *The sum.* The steps add at most Σ_n dev_n ≤ C_*L^NS_L²(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j·4ε_Φ to the deviation. ε_Φ ≤ (λ + C_ΛM⁵)α_{1,k}(1 + o(1)) ≤ 2λα_{1,k}, from the transported part and the response (*The R-step lands in the family*), up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)). This bound uses only the phase of A′, which along 𝐔_t is t times that at 𝐔 up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B) (*The scope*), so it holds on the whole segment. So per fine bond the sum is at most C_*O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²λα_{1,k}L^{−k}. With N = N_k of #3.2.1 (within LFR I's conditions, p.198 l.54–56), L^{N_k} ≤ L²(L+1)(N₀+1)^{β₀}R_k + L(log g_k^{−2})^{ν_L} (Bałaban's R_k, #3.2.1.1), and δ′_k = g_kA₁p₁(g_k), the factor L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. So for g small the sum is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. 𝐇_k″(g_kB) is the step at the stage of U_k^0 with source g_kB, g_k|B| < δ′_k on Λ (LFR II p.360 l.42, by LFR I (1.82)), and is one step of the induction. The BCH terms between factors are inside the induction, since ε_n is the deviation of the composed configuration.
        - {#124} *The scope (derived).* Let 𝓜 := {U^{post}(V): V real} be the real post-R minimisers, and 𝓜^c := {U^{post}(V): V complex, within VAR's radius} its complexification. V ↦ U^{post}(V) is analytic and injective (M(U^{post}(V)) = V), so 𝓜 is a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of 𝓜^c. An identity between analytic functions that holds on 𝓜 therefore holds on 𝓜^c.
          - {#125} Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U)) and the identities of *The Landau representatives* hold on 𝓜, hence on 𝓜^c. On 𝓜^c the segment 𝐔_t of *One step* is taken as U^{post}(e^{itB}M(U)), with B := (1/i)log M(𝐔)M(U)^{−1}, from P(U) to P(𝐔), which stays in 𝓜^c. Since 𝓗(0) = 0 and 𝓗 is analytic on the ball of radius r_B, the complex part along it is t times that at t = 1, up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B). So *The sum*'s bound holds along it.
          - {#126} The T-step's points e^{iη𝐇_𝐕(s,tB′)}𝐕, 𝐕 ∈ 𝓜^c_{k+1} (*The M-factored domain*, *The bounds*), include real non-minimisers: real 𝐕 and B′ with s ∈ (0, 1). There Φ(U) ≠ U_k^0(M(U)), and the stages of Φ's chain are not minimisers.
          - {#127} The old insertions' post-R function is taken as the word at the u-free chain applied to U_k″(M(𝐔)) = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0(M(𝐔)))^{u_k″^{−1}} (#86), a function of M(𝐔) alone. This extension is the note's own. Bałaban introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way into the localized function 𝐇_k … hence into u_{k,Λ}", replaces "M·(U_k), V″ by the corresponding M·(𝐔), and V_Λ^{(A)} by the function V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔))" (LFR II p.374 l.37–40), and introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) into the other expressions "in the usual way" (p.375 l.11–12). So his extension depends on 𝐉, and on 𝐔 beyond M(𝐔) through 𝐇_k and u_{k,Λ}. At a real minimiser U ∈ 𝓜 with its current, (U, J(U)), both are the real post-R function, since P(U) = U and the old terms take the configuration's current (RG1 p.262 l.61–p.263 l.2). Both are analytic ((ii), under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β), #88), and {(V, J(V)) : V ∈ 𝓜} is a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of the graph {(𝐕, J^h(𝐕)) : 𝐕 ∈ 𝓜^c}, so the two agree on that graph, as far as it lies in the domain Ũ_k^c(Y₄) of Bałaban's extension (LFR II p.375 l.17–20). The two are compared at the Landau representative (V̂, J^h(V̂)), where the words take the same values (#6.10), and which lies in Ũ_k^c(Y₄) at the T-step's points (#6.14) and at the extension-domain points (#6.16), derived, not reviewed. (1.50)'s determining set and field off Λ are those of the new configuration (p.370 l.4–8), and the regularity off Λ^∼ is "almost the same as for the configuration U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), M·(𝐔))" (p.375 l.12–13). The value of the note's extension at 𝐔 is its value at P(𝐔) := U^{post}(M(𝐔)) ∈ 𝓜^c, and its bounds at 𝐔 are those at P(𝐔).
          - {#128} By Proposition 9, P(𝐔)'s complex part in the Landau gauge relative to U^{post}(M(U)) is 𝓗^{post}(Δd). Here Δd is M(𝐔)'s complex part, with |Δd(y′)| ≤ C_dL^{j′}η·sup|A′|. By (190) its phase and gradient are at most K := O(C_dS_L) times 𝐔's room, and its D*D is at most K times the phase room. K's printed constants depend on d and L: B₃ (VAR Theorem 1, p.279 l.28), δ₀ and B₀ (the common rate and constant of PBG Theorem 3.12, "depending on d and L only", PBG p.423 l.39–p.424 l.3, which VAR uses through "Theorem 3.12 from [5]", p.285 l.25, its [5] being PBG, p.309 l.25), and (190)'s O(1), and the multi-layer free constants depend on L through γ₀′ = π²/L² (PRT II p.242 l.42–43). So K is fixed after L and M, and is never compared with L.
        - {#129} *The M-factored domain (derived).* Let 𝒟^{λ,M}_k be the set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) with M(𝐔) within VAR's radius such that, for the localized projection P of each member (*The bounds*), with V̂ = P(𝐔)^{u_L} the VAR representative of P(𝐔) and u_L the G^c gauge transformation from P(𝐔) to it, both (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) and (V̂, J^h(V̂)) lie in 𝒟^λ_k ((𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉), RG1 (1.10)). P acts on 𝐔 alone, and 𝐉 is the pair's own coordinate, which the new terms take. J^h(V̂) is the current that the old terms take at P(𝐔) (*The 𝐉 coordinate*). Bałaban's T-step is M-factored only inside □₀: "in the interior averages M·(U_{k+1}) we replace the configuration U_{k+1} by 𝐔", 𝐉 replaces J_{k+1} "as usual", and in 𝐇_k and the Q_j the configuration U_{k+1}(□₀, M·(𝐔)) is used, "extended as equal to 𝐔 outside □₀" (RG1 p.275 l.2–5). The full M-factoring is the note's.
          - {#130} The bounds. Every member of the class is taken composed with P: on 𝒟^{λ,M}_k it is Y∘P, with Y its function of the configuration at the pair's 𝐉. For the old insertions' post-R functions this is their extension (*The scope*). For the members that are functions of 𝐔 itself (SW closure (b2)'s Y_W, the att, lw, and wrap members, and D2.6 closure 5.4's transports) it changes their values off 𝓜^c. On 𝓜^c, P is the identity (*At s = 1*), so the values at the real minimisers, where the effective density is evaluated at s = 1, are unchanged. The values at the real s-decoupled points do change. The cluster expansion uses the members at s = 1, at s = 0 through the factorisation over the decoupled components, and analytically in s in between. Locality. P is local: U^{post} = U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·), with 𝐁_k″(Y₁) = 𝐁(Y₁) ∪ 𝐁_k″ CRE's localized set (2.14) (#39), and a determining set's layer Γ₀ = Ω₁^c carries M⁰, the bonds themselves (CRE (2.2), (2.11), p.255 l.12–16, p.256 l.36–41). So for 𝐁(Y₁) every bond off Ω₁(Y₁) is pinned, Y₁^c included, and dist(Ω₁(Y₁), Y₁^c) ≥ ξM₁ (p.256 l.61). The free bonds and the plaquettes containing them lie in Y₁, and P(𝐔) depends on 𝐔 on Y₁ only. Each member takes the P of its own localization domain. An old insertion takes that of its R-step component Y₁ and step j (#39, #135). A member that is a function of 𝐔 itself, produced at step j with domain X, takes P_Ω := U(𝐁_j″(Ω), M·(·)), 𝐁_j″(Ω) := 𝐁(Ω) ∪ 𝐁_j″, for a neighbourhood Ω of X chosen as CRE p.257 l.8–11 and l.22–26 require (∂Ω in the layers, at distance ≥ 2M₁ from their boundaries). Its P, its determining set S := 𝐁_j″(Ω) with S's block points, axial conditions (1.19), and normalisation (1.29), are fixed at production and kept at every later step k ≥ j, as an old insertion keeps 𝐁_j″(Y₁) (#135). The later steps' sets are coarser than S (#135), so *The identity on minimisers* below covers them. This is how Bałaban's T-step evaluates the old terms, at local minimisers 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, exp iB̃_□M·(U_{k+1}))) (RG1 (3.23), p.274), with □₀ = □̃⁵ and X ⊂ □̃² (RG1 p.273 l.37–45). So every Y∘P is local, and at s = 0 it factorises over the decoupled components as a member localized in its Ω or Y₁, as Bałaban's terms localized in □₀ do. The identity on minimisers. A localized minimiser map U(S, ·), pinned off Ω₁ as above, is the identity on the minimisers of every determining set S′ that equals S or is coarser than S on S's support. For such a minimiser U, every W admissible for S with U's data equals U off Ω₁. W has U's S′-averages, since the averages are iterated (M_S = M^j on Γ_j, CRE (2.11), with M^j the j-fold average) and S′'s blocks meeting the support are there unions of aligned S-blocks, down to the pinned bonds (the layers are unions of L^nξM₁-cubes, CRE p.256 l.59–61). So W is admissible for S′, A(W) ≥ A(U), and VAR Theorem 1's uniqueness gives U(S, M_S(U)) = U up to a gauge transformation. This is the nesting argument of *At s = 1*, and Bałaban prints the instances RG1 p.273 l.45 and LFR II (1.61). It covers the 𝐁_k-minimisers, the 𝐁_k″-minimisers (𝐁_k″ is finer than 𝐁_k on Z, LFR II (1.33)), and the points of 𝓜 for a domain Y₁′ with Ω ⊂ Y₁′⁰, where 𝐁_k″(Y₁′) = 𝐁_k″ (LFR II p.373 l.26–27). The identity principle of *The scope* extends it to their complexifications. So the values at the real minimisers, where the effective density is evaluated at s = 1, are unchanged.
            {#130.1} At s ≠ 1, P_Ω of the point differs on Ω from the s = 1 point by at most 2K sup|𝐇|, by VAR Proposition 9 for the member's set 𝐁_j″(Ω). CRE takes VAR's theory for every determining set (p.256 l.48–53) and applies it to the localized minimisers U_{p,X} (p.261 l.10, (2.35)), and K is derived for 𝐁_j″(Ω) as for 𝐁_k″(Y₁), whose strip *S_L is finite* covers (#99). The fine bonds of the pinned layer differ by at most 2 sup|𝐇| as well. sup_{𝒟^{λ,M}_k}|Y∘P| is the sup of |Y| at the points P(𝐔) of 𝓜^c whose representative V̂ has (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) ∈ 𝒟^λ_k, for each member's own P, which is what the bullets of (iii) bound ((R-eval-cr)). These bounds hold up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), with the reduced representative in 𝒟♮_k and the words' continuation along V̂_t derived, not reviewed (#6.10–#6.14, #113).
            {#130.2} At step k+1 the members are Y′∘P_{k+1}, with P_{k+1} the step-(k+1) member's own localized projection, local as above. So the T-step needs Y′ only at 𝐕 := P_{k+1}(𝐔) ∈ 𝓜^c_{k+1} for that member's domain Y′, which is taken to contain each constituent's own domain (its Ω or Y₁) in Y′⁰. Both parts of this transfer are derived below, part (a) with the re-projected points' curvature conditions derived, not reviewed (#6.14), as at s ≠ 1, and part (b) with the conjugation factors of the covariant members (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2) and the outer fields' domain (#6.17) derived, not reviewed.
            {#130.3} (a) At a localized background (derived; the curvature conditions at the re-projected points by #6.14, derived, not reviewed). The real points of 𝓜_{k+1}(Y′) are localized minimisers U(𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′), M·(·)), pinned off Ω₁(Y′), and RG2's identity (map #MOMST.44 (ii)) holds at the global minimisers only, which are not a uniqueness set in 𝓜_{k+1}(Y′). So at a localized background 𝐕 the s = 1 point e^{iη𝐇_𝐕(1,tB′)}𝐕 is not shown to be a step-k minimiser, and the transfer does not use it as one. Bałaban's T-step does not either: it deforms the localized background U_{k+1}(□₀, M·(𝐔)) by 𝐇_k and re-projects with U_j(□₀, M·(·)) (RG1 (3.24)–(3.25), p.275). The comparison of *At s ≠ 1* (#137) is made instead against 𝐕 itself, at every s, relative to the real base U_{k+1}. Each member's P fixes 𝐕. The step-(k+1) set 𝐁_{k+1}″ is Bałaban's R-operation set at step k+1 (LFR I (1.12)–(1.13), p.179 l.32–45, at k+1). That R-operation starts from 𝐁^T_{k+1}, the T-step's coarsening on Ω_{k+1} of the post-R set 𝐁_k (CRE (2.2)) ((1.14), l.59–62). Its preliminary integrations replace parts of Γ_j by their versions at the scale j + 1 ((1.12)–(1.16); #75), so 𝐁_{k+1}″ is 𝐁^T_{k+1} or coarser, and it equals 𝐁^T_{k+1} if that R-operation has no region Z ((1.12) with Z = ∅). 𝐁_k is coarser than 𝐁_k″ (LFR II (1.33), p.365). So every average of 𝐁_{k+1}″ is an iterated average of those of 𝐁^T_{k+1}, hence of those of 𝐁_k″, and of an old insertion's 𝐁_j″ (#135).
            {#130.4} *The real base.* U_{k+1}, the real base of 𝐕, at a global and at a localized background, is a real point of 𝓜_{k+1}. Every real point of 𝓜_{k+1} is a 𝐁_{k+1}″-minimiser at its own averages, and 𝐁_{k+1}″ is 𝐁^T_{k+1} or coarser (above) and is every localized 𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′) or coarser. By the nesting of *The identity on minimisers* (above), every real point of 𝓜_{k+1}, U_{k+1} among them, is fixed by every P_{k+1}, and it is the 𝐁^T_{k+1}-minimiser at its own averages, a T-step background at its own data, whether or not the (k+1)-th R-operation has a region Z. Bałaban's post-R background at step k+1, with the post-R set 𝐁_{k+1} (LFR II (1.33)–(1.35), p.365, at k+1), is one of these points, since that set equals 𝐁_{k+1}″ off Z and is Z^{(k+1)} on Z, so it is coarser than 𝐁_{k+1}″. The nesting argument of *At s = 1* (#133) then applies as for the points of 𝓜 for a domain Y₁′ with Ω ⊂ Y₁′⁰ (above), with 𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′) in place of 𝐁_k″(Y₁′): each real 𝐕 is a minimiser of the member's problem with its own averages, and P(𝐕) = 𝐕 by VAR Theorem 1's uniqueness of the minimal orbit, on 𝓜^c_{k+1}(Y′) by the identity principle of *The scope*. M of the point differs from M(𝐕) by the data of 𝐇_𝐕(s,tB′), of sup at most sup|𝐇|, the pinned bonds included, since the layer Γ₀ carries the bonds themselves (CRE (2.11)). By Proposition 9 relative to the real base U_{k+1}, as in *The scope*, P of the point is the minimiser with the data d_𝐕 ⊕ δ, with d_𝐕 := M(𝐕) and δ this difference, and its Landau field relative to U_{k+1} is that at d_𝐕 plus 𝓗 := 𝓗_S(d_𝐕 ⊕ δ) − 𝓗_S(d_𝐕) = ∫₀¹κ(B_w)∂_wB_w dw, κ := δ𝓗_S/δB, with B_w the coordinates (1/i)log of d_𝐕 ⊕ wδ relative to the base's data, so |∂_wB_w| ≤ (1 + O(α_{1,k}))sup|δ| by BCH. (190) bounds κ uniformly on the ball, pointwise with its decay and with its gradient and D*D entries, as at #164–#165. So |𝓗| is at most (1 + O(α_{1,k}))K sup|𝐇| in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights, for phase, gradient, and current. This is below the response 2K sup|𝐇| of *At s ≠ 1*, and #137–#138's budget holds with the whole of 𝓗 counted as response. On every layer Kh_k^{(K)} ≤ 2Kh_k^{(K)} ≤ ¼(1−β)α_{1,k} (#138), inside SW (b3)'s uniform margin ½(1−β)α_{1,k}, which is the room that 𝐕's own data leave, since data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} use at most ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n of the step-k phase room (#54), and likewise for the gradient. 𝓗's current is at most the response's (1−β)²α_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k), which replaces both the response's and 𝐇_𝐕's shares of #137's current budget. Part (b)'s pair for the point has the representative e^{iη(a″+𝓗^h)}U_{k+1} exactly, with 𝓗^h the same integral at d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ δ^h, at most (1 + O(α_{1,k}))e^{2a_c}K sup|𝐇| ≤ 2K sup|𝐇| for g small, within the same budget. Its real base is 𝐕's, U_{k+1}.
            {#130.5} *The base near the support.* At a global and at a localized background the real base of the T-step's points is U_{k+1}, and part (b)'s pair is built relative to it. #159's bounds use the support of the R-step's indicators at the real base only through the bound on δ^{re} (#160). U_{k+1}, the T-step point at B′ = 0, is not shown to lie on that support, but its data lie within O(ε_k) of a datum that does. Let B′ be the T-step's real integration variable and V the T-step data of U_{k+1}. U^{B′} := U_k(e^{iB′}V) is Bałaban's post-R background at the datum e^{iB′}V (#132). It equals e^{iη𝐇(1,B′)}U_{k+1} up to a G-valued gauge transformation (RG1 (2.6), p.266; #131 at the real point U_{k+1}). The T-step's integrand carries the post-R function at e^{iB′}V with its indicators as factors (RG1 (2.1), (2.9), p.265 l.30–39). So wherever the integrand is nonzero, e^{iB′}V lies on their support, and there sup|B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_k (#138; map (6)(e)). At a real base U, δ^{re} is the difference, on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, between U's data and the averages of U_0^{(AL)} = U^{(L)}_{k,Z}(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}, V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c})), a function of U's data off Λ (#160). Along the real segment from the data e^{iB′}V to V, the first term changes by at most O(K)sup|B′|: on Z, U's 𝐁_k″(Y₁)-data on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} are averages at scales j ≤ k, namely h on the part outside Ω″_{h+1}, where Γ″_h = (Ω″_h∖Ω″_{h+1})^{(h)}, and h+1, …, k inside it (LFR I (1.10)–(1.13), p.179 l.44), of the minimiser at the T-step's 𝐁_k-data, which are scale-k on Z (LFR II (1.33)), and VAR Proposition 9 and (190) bound their change. The critical extension changes by at most C_{rsp}M⁵sup|B′|, by LFR II (1.38)–(1.42) (p.366 l.30–p.367 l.24), in the axial gauge of (1.38): (1.41) gives |B′_Λ(B̃′)| ≤ 4d(100M)⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃²|B̃′| for a small data change B̃′ at a regular real base (table #T4.30's C_{rsp}). The averages of the minimiser U_{k,Z} change by at most K(1 + C_{rsp}M⁵)sup|B′|, by VAR Proposition 9 and (190). So at U_{k+1}, |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k per block, with a constant fixed before g, far below r_B for g small. #159's bounds then hold at the real base U_{k+1}, with C_{mx} carrying this constant (#45, #160). The conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the point's Landau representative hold with more than a quarter of the margin left (#6.14, derived, not reviewed), as at s ≠ 1.
            {#130.6} (b) The step-k normalisation (derived, with the conjugation factors of the covariant members (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2) and the outer fields' domain (#6.17) derived, not reviewed, with λ_w := (1/i)log w for a gauge function w, and U^w the left action, (U^v)^w = U^{wv} (RG1 (1.10); REG p.78 l.14)). The step-k Landau representative V̂ of a point is generated from that point's axial element and normalised by (1.29) on the step-k layers (REG p.81; #102–#103), and the normalisation "depends on an element U chosen to generate" the set (p.80 l.29–30). The step-(k+1) representative X := e^{iηa″}U_{k+1} of 𝐕 is generated from 𝐕 and normalised on the step-(k+1) layers, and 𝒟^λ_k is G-invariant only. So at the evaluated point 𝐏, an axial-gauge minimiser (VAR Proposition 9; #156), V̂ need not be the point that SW (b3) computes. The step-(k+1) member therefore evaluates each step-k member at another pair of 𝐏's G^c-orbit, chosen from 𝐕 alone, and equal to the T-step's value at real parameters. The construction is made for each member's own P, with that P's determining set: its block points, its axial conditions (1.19), and its normalisation (1.29); the bound is read member by member, as #129 defines it for each member's own P. The normalised axial element. For a configuration U₁ near U₀, real or G^c-valued, CMP98's gauge conditions (72) for U₁^u fix u from its values at the top block points ((77) and p.30 l.13), and every such u has \overline{R₀u}^j(x_j) = u(x_j)\overline{R_{0,x_j}U₁}^{(j)} ((84), p.30). So exactly one of them has REG's normalisation, u(y) = (\overline{R_{0,y}U₁}^{(k)})^{−1} ((86)–(87), p.31): it "is uniquely determined by all the conditions" (p.31 l.20), as an explicit analytic function of U₁. REG takes this over layer by layer, with (1.29) at the top points of each Λ_j, for its u₁, "determined uniquely in terms of U₁" (p.88 l.36), and (1.70)–(1.72) bound it at every point of Ω₀. Let v_{k+1} and v_k be this transformation for U₁ = X, with 𝐕's step-(k+1) set and with the member's own set S, fixed at production (#130), and 𝐕_X := X^{v_k}, axial for S. Here and below, "the member's step-k set", its layers, and its normalisation are S's, the same at every step k ≥ j. 𝐕 = X^{v_{k+1}}, since 𝐕's own normalised transformation is such a u for U₁ = X. So 𝐕_X = 𝐕^c with c := v_kv_{k+1}^{−1}. By (1.70)–(1.71) at both steps, as #106 bounds λ̂ by A_L, |λ_c| ≤ a_c at every point, with a_c := 33d times the sup over the layers of L^jη|a″| on Ω_j, which is O(α_{1,k}) on 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. At real 𝐕, X is real and v_k, v_{k+1}, and c are G-valued, whatever s, t, and B′ are.
            {#130.7} The pair. Let h := c at the block points of the member's set, ĥ the gauge transformation with these values that keeps the conditions (1.19), and 𝐔″ := 𝐏^ĥ. A transformation keeps (1.19) if and only if u(x_n) = R(Ū₀ⁿ(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}))^{−1}u(x_{n+1}) (p.78 l.14, p.79 l.6–8), so ĥ is fixed by h and entire in h, and since R(Ū₀ⁿ) conjugates by the real base's averages, |λ_ĥ| and |Im λ_ĥ| are at most a_c at every point. In fact ĥ = c. At every point the member's layer index j is at most 𝐕's (the member's set is finer, part (a)), and the chains x_0, …, x_j of the member's conditions (1.19) are the first levels of 𝐕's chains at that point, with the same averages relative to the same base U_{k+1} (REG p.79 l.4–9). So 𝐕, axial for its step-(k+1) set, is axial for the member's set too, and so is 𝐕_X = 𝐕^c. So c keeps (1.19), and it is the transformation with the block values h that keeps (1.19). Plain averages transform with the values at the block points (REG p.78 l.14), and the axial gauge picks one element in each orbit of the group (1.14) (p.79 l.15). So 𝐔″ is axial and critical with the data M(𝐏)^h, within VAR's radius since a_c → 0 with g, and P(𝐔″) = 𝐔″ by VAR Theorem 1's unique critical orbit (#103) at real parameters, and on the complex parameters by the identity principle of *The scope*.
            {#130.8} Its representative. M(𝐏) = d_𝐕 ⊕ δ, with d_𝐕 := M(𝐕) and δ the data of the deformation (part (a)), so M(𝐏)^h = d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ δ^h, with d_{𝐕_X} = M(𝐕^c) = d_𝐕^h, since c = h at the block points, and δ^h(b) := Ad(h(b₋))δ(b), so |δ^h| ≤ e^{2a_c}|δ|. X is generated from 𝐕_X with REG's normalisation, so it is 𝐕_X's representative by the uniqueness of Theorem 4 (p.88 l.2–6), once X meets (1.37), (1.38), and (1.62) at step k. (1.38) is R_kD*a″ = 0 (the representatives, below). (1.62) for the member's set follows from (1.62) for 𝐕's step-(k+1) set, which Theorem 4 at step k+1 gives at real 𝐕: the member's set is finer, so at every point its layer index j is at most 𝐕's, and the bounds in powers of (L^jη)^{−1} weaken as j decreases (likewise (1.69) for (1.72)), with REG's α₀ and α₁ taken as the larger of the step-k and step-(k+1) values; Theorem 4 holds for any α₀ + α₁ ≤ c₁. (1.37) is Q_j(U₀, ηA) = B with B the data's. It holds for every representative generated with REG's normalisation from its axial element, since by CMP98 (88) (p.31) the axial element's averages are then the k-th order averages of the representative (p.82; #102), with |B| < 2dLα₁ by (1.66) at 𝐕_X. Theorem 4's hypotheses hold at 𝐕_X and 𝐔″ as at 𝐕 and 𝐏 (p.82 l.33–37, p.87 l.47–49): (1.33) concerns the base U₀ only; (1.34)'s axial condition holds by construction, and its regularity because c and ĥ are G-valued at real parameters; and (1.66) changes by at most 2a_c, so it holds with α₁ + 2a_c in place of α₁, and α₀ + α₁ + 2a_c ≤ c₁ for g small. The complex parameters follow by the identity principle. So VAR's Landau form at the data d_{𝐕_X} is X, 𝓗_S(d_{𝐕_X}) = a″ in part (a)'s notation, and 𝐔″'s representative is exactly V̂″ = e^{iη(a″+𝓗^h)}U_{k+1}, with 𝓗^h := 𝓗_S(d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ δ^h) − 𝓗_S(d_{𝐕_X}) = ∫₀¹κ(d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ wδ^h)δ^h dw, which (190) bounds uniformly on the ball as at #164–#165.
            {#130.9} At s = 1 at a global background 𝓗^h is 𝐇(s,tB′) up to second order (the representatives, below). At s ≠ 1, and at a localized background, it is #137's response, or part (a)'s 𝓗, with δ^h in place of δ. This multiplies its bound by (1 + O(α_{1,k}))e^{2a_c} = 1 + O(α_{1,k}), which #138's inequalities absorb for g small, since each holds with room: (1+2K)h_k^{(K)} is at most (3/16)(1−β)min(β,1)α_{1,k} + O(ε_k) against min(β,1)α_{1,k}/4, and 2Kh_k^{(K)} is at most ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k} + O(ε_k) against ¼(1−β)α_{1,k}. h depends on 𝐕 alone and is the same at every s; no implicit-function step and no bound on a transformation from 𝐏 enters.
            {#130.10} V̂″ meets the Landau condition of every member's set. The condition for a set follows from that for a coarser set, since the kernel of the averages shrinks as the set gets finer (below, as N(Q′_k) ⊂ N(Q′_{k+1})), and each member's set is finer than 𝐁_{k+1}″ (part (a)). The pair (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) has the Landau form (V̂″, R(t)𝐉) (RG1 (1.10)), with t := u″^{−1}ĥ and u″ REG's normalised transformation of 𝐔″, V̂″ = 𝐔″^{u″^{−1}}. These are the representative and Landau-frame current that #136 and *The 𝐉 coordinate* (#153) treat. (1.70)–(1.72) bound u″ at every point by V̂″'s field, so |λ_t| ≤ a_c + O(α_{1,k}) at every point. R(t)𝐉 = R(f)R(v_{k+1}^{−1})𝐉, where R(v_{k+1}^{−1})𝐉 is the data's current in 𝐕's step-(k+1) frame and f := tv_{k+1} is G-valued at real parameters, with |Im λ_f| = O(α_{1,k}) on 𝓜^c.
            {#130.11} 𝒟^λ_k's current condition is on 𝐉 − J_k (#51), with J_k the real base current, and R(t)𝐉 − J_k = R(f)(R(v_{k+1}^{−1})𝐉 − J_k) + (R(f) − 1)J_k. The first term is the step-(k+1) frame current's, as #54 transfers it, up to the factor e^{2|Im λ_f|} = 1 + O(α_{1,k}) in the pointwise norm. For the second, f = u″^{−1}ĥv_{k+1} = u″^{−1}v_k, since ĥ = c (the pair, above). Both u″ and v_k are REG-normalised for the member's set. At a point of its layer n, (1.70)–(1.71) bound each by Σ_{m<n}8dL^{m+1}φ ≤ 16dL^nφ (REG p.88 l.37–61, the factor 2 of (1.70) times (1.71)'s bound per term), with φ the phase per fine bond of its field (V̂″'s and X's), as at #106. That phase is O(λ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k}) on every layer, so |λ_f| ≤ C_fdλ_wα_{1,k}L^{n−k} on layer n, with C_f absolute. Let B_J := sup(L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, with n the bond's layer index. On the other bonds f = 1, since REG (1.70) at a layer-0 point is an empty sum (p.88 l.38–44), so the term vanishes there (#173, *The real base current*). Then (L^nξ)³|(R(f) − 1)J_k| ≤ 3C_fdλ_wα_{1,k}L^{n−k}B_J. Since α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k} (#6, #14) and f_nλ^{(k)}_n ≥ 1 − β (#48), this is at most 3C_fdλ_wλ_RB_J/(1−β) times the room f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{0,k}L^{n−k} of #51, uniformly in n and K.
            {#130.12} The step uses B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K. CRE (2.36) (p.261 l.19) at the real base pair (U_k, J_k), at which D(k)(iii) evaluates the factors (map #MD2.0.3.56), would give B_J ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k}, since α_{0,n} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k} for n ≤ k (g_n ≤ (1+β₀)g_k by CRE (2.6), and x(log x^{−2})^{q₀} is increasing for small x). Neither this bound nor that membership is among the real-U base-point conditions of map #MD2.0.3.55. B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, holds: *The real base current* (#173, reviewed as packet JB) derives B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ with ε₁′ := max(2L²ε_0, 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}) at D(k)(iii)'s real base points, by VAR Theorem 1 and its uniqueness clause for the problem with support Ω_1^∼, and B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ + O(KC₁^{RG2})ε_k at this part's real base U_{k+1}, with B_J there over the post-R term's Ω_1 = Ω_1 ∪ Z, from the post-R term's base-point clause (LFR II Theorem 1; D2.7 item 15.8, derived, not reviewed). A fixed factor L between a bond's and its endpoint's layer index at the layer boundaries is harmless, since B_J → 0 with L fixed. Both terms fit inside the current room of #54 for g small. At real parameters the factor is 1, and the second term is still present.
            {#130.13} So 𝒟^{λ,M}_k's bound for the member applies at (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) once V̂″ meets 𝒟^λ_k's conditions, which is SW (b3)'s computation below.
            {#130.14} The step-(k+1) member evaluates the step-k member there, with the outer fields at R(ĥ)ω (Cov(k), map #MD2.2.4.3). At real parameters (real 𝐕, s, t, and B′) 𝐏 is real, and h and ĥ are G-valued. There the old terms, their post-R extensions, and the words take at (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω) their values at (𝐏, 𝐉, ω), the T-step's: Bałaban's spaces are G-invariant (LFR I p.191 l.32–33), his effective action is gauge invariant (LFR II p.378), the post-R maps U_k^0 are G-covariant by the minimal orbit (CRE (2.12)), and the words are traces of holonomies. The T-step uses the complex parameters only through the analytic extension of its integrand, for the Cauchy bounds of the cluster expansion. That extension is unique by the identity principle, so the step-(k+1) member is this function. No continuation in the gauge variable enters, as at #111.
            {#130.15} A covariant member (D2.6 closure 5.4's transports, and every member with Cov(k)'s endpoint factors, map (4)(a)) satisfies Y(𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω) = π(ĥ(x))Y(𝐏, 𝐉, ω)π(ĥ(y))^{−1} at real parameters, and its step-(k+1) member is π(ĥ(x))^{−1}Y(𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω)π(ĥ(y)). Conjugation by the complex ĥ enlarges its bound by at most ‖π(ĥ(x))^{−1}‖‖π(ĥ(y))‖ ≤ e^{c_π(|Im λ_ĥ(x)|+|Im λ_ĥ(y)|)} ≤ e^{2c_πa_c}, with c_π the constant of (G, π). CRE's B-terms are invariant scalars (CRE (2.41)(iii)), so at real parameters their value at (𝐏, 𝐉, ω) equals that at (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω), and their step-(k+1) member is the latter, with no conjugation factor (#153). The outer fields' domain absorbs R(ĥ) by #6.17 (derived, not reviewed): at ω's bonds ĥ decays with the layer; on the layers i ≥ 1 each step's enlargement fits that step's slack of the radius schedule (#6.17.1), and on the layer 0 the rotations are charged to the budget of the fixed sub-polydisc ρ_ω, summed over the steps (#6.17.3 (2)). The conjugation factor is 1 + O(α_{1,k}), and 1 at real 𝐕. It is paid once, at the current step, and does not compound over the steps, by the direct transfer below (#130.15.1–#130.15.2, derived, not reviewed).
            {#130.15.1} *The direct transfer (derived, not reviewed).* Let a covariant member be produced at step j ≤ k, with its own P and determining set S (#129, #130), and let B_Y be its bound on 𝒟^{λ,M}_j, which the bullets of (iii) give at production. An untouched member is one function of the fine configuration at every step (Cov(k), map #MD2.2.4.3), and the later steps only coarsen S's averages (#135). Part (b) normalises to S itself at every step: v_k is REG's transformation for S (#130.6), and V̂″ is the representative of (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) for S, which lies in 𝒟^λ_k (#130.13, #136), with R(ĥ)ω in the step-k domain of the outer fields (#6.17). By the nesting (#130.15.2), 𝒟^λ_k ⊆ 𝒟^λ_j for every k ≥ j, and the outer fields' radii r_{ω,i}(1/2 + 2^{−(k−i)}), with r_{ω,i} = g_i^{1/2} for i ≥ 1 and r_{ω,0} = 1 (map #MD2.0.3.61, #MD2.2.6.168; #6.17.1, #6.17.3), decrease with k; on the layer 0 the bounds hold on the fixed sub-polydisc ρ_ω (#6.17.3 (2)), which does not depend on k. The member's P and S, with S's block points, (1.19), and (1.29), are fixed at production (#130, #130.6), so its representative V̂″ is the same function of the point at every step, and 𝒟^{λ,M}_k ⊆ 𝒟^{λ,M}_j for the member. (1) On the family. The member's sup on 𝒟^{λ,M}_k is at most B_Y at every k ≥ j, with no factor, so its family bound does not grow over the carry, as (R-eval-cr)'s *The carry*, #63, and the weights (#296) use. (2) At part (b)'s pairs. (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) ∈ 𝒟^{λ,M}_k ⊆ 𝒟^{λ,M}_j, so |Y(𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω)| ≤ B_Y. The member's value at the T-step's point is π(ĥ(x))^{−1}Y(𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω)π(ĥ(y)), as above, so it is at most e^{2c_πa_c}B_Y, with a_c = O(α_{1,k}) (#130.6). This factor arises only in the T-step's evaluation at step k, once per member, and it does not enter any family bound, so it does not compound: at step k+1 the member is again bounded by B_Y on the family by (1), not by e^{2c_πa_c}B_Y. In the step-(k+1) expansion it multiplies the member's activity once, through the touched members (map #MD2.4.12.15); an untouched W-vertex carries no factor (#MD2.4.12.16). There a_c ≤ C_aα_{1,k} with C_a fixed (#130.6), and α_{1,k} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{1,k+1}, since g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_{k+1} (CRE (2.6)) and x(log x^{−2})^{q₁} increases for small x. Every member's window has |win| ≥ 40 (map #MD2.4.12.19). So the factor is at most e^{c_π(C_a(1+β₀)/20)α_{1,k+1}|win|}, and it is covered by the share's transport term c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(v)| (map #MD2.4.12.21) with c_π replaced by (1 + C_a)c_π. That term's conditions (map #MD2.4.11.3, #MD2.4.11.5; #61) are smallness conditions on g, which hold for any fixed multiple of c_π. A touched member enters the activity of a new member produced at step k+1 (map #MD2.4.12.15), and from then on it is read from that member's bound.
            {#130.15.2} *The nesting (derived, not reviewed).* For j ≤ k, m := k − j ≥ 1, 𝒟^λ_k ⊆ 𝒟^λ_j at every real base point of 𝒟^λ_k. Let n be a point's layer index at step k and n_j its index at step j, on step j's own post-R layers, where 𝒟^λ_j is posed. Then n_j ≤ n, since the later steps only coarsen the layers (#130.3, #135); S's index n′ obeys n′ ≤ n_j (#130.7). (2) The gradient, in #54's normalisation. (3) The current. The weight (L^{n_j}ξ)³ is L^{−3(n−n_j)} times (L^nξ)³. With α_{0,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²√mα_{0,j} (map (6)(e), #53), the ratio is at most ((1−β)/(2(1+β₀)))^m(1+β₀)²√m. That is (1−β)(1+β₀)/2 ≤ ¾(1−β) at m = 1, as at #54, and below (1−β)√m2^{−m} ≤ 0.36(1−β) for m ≥ 2. With the weight μ^{(k)}_n of #52.1, on every layer, the ratio stays within these bounds (the contacts draft, §4a). (4) 𝒟♮'s conditions (2.34)–(2.37). These are the rooms. (2.35) and (2.37) constrain U_{p,X}(M·𝐔) and 𝐉_{p,X}, built on the step's own {Γ_i}_{i<p} (CRE p.261 l.10), which are the same configurations at steps j and k wherever the Γ_i on X are unchanged. Since p ≤ j (the map's reading R-p), these are Γ_i with i < j. The T-steps leave them unchanged: the T-step after step i changes only Γ_i, through the new Ω_{i+1} (Γ_i = Ω_i^{(i)}∖Ω_{i+1}^{(i)}, CRE (2.2), p.255 l.15–16; the domains Ω_m are "determined by the m-th renormalization transformation", CRE p.256 l.21–22). An R-step changes them only on its region Z (LFR II (1.33), p.365 l.5: Γ_j = Γ_j″ ∩ Z^c), and a member whose localization domain meets Z is integrated by that R-operation, while only the terms with domains disjoint from Z pass to the new action, read at the new background (LFR II p.369 l.28–31). That member is then touched, and it is read from the new member's bound (#130.15.1). So for an untouched member U_{p,X} and 𝐉_{p,X} are the same at steps j and k. (5) The real base.
