Over the N + 1 steps, γ_0 ≤ γ_Φ + O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²ε_Φ, and L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2} (#3.2.1). With ε_Φ ≤ 2λα_{1,k}, the deformations' part is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k} for g small, on every layer. No factor L^{k−i} appears, since (190)'s entries carry exactly the weights of the norms.
        - {#145} *The current of the deformations (derived on 𝓜^c).* The current is tracked covariantly, at the configurations themselves. Let C_n := J^h(𝕌̃^{(n)}) − J(U₀^{(n)}), the holomorphic current of the reduced stage-n configuration minus the current of the reduced real stage, and κ_n its (−2)-norm.
          - {#146} Covariance. J^h(V^G) = Ad_GJ^h(V), with Ad_G := Ad_{G(b_−)} at each bond b, which is the action R(u) of RG1 (1.10), (𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉) = (u_−𝐔u_+^{−1}, R(u_−)𝐉). So J^h(𝕌̃^{(n)}) − J^h(𝕌) = Ad_G ΔJ with ΔJ := J^h(𝕎_n) − J^h(𝕎), and at U, where G = 1, J(U₀^{(n)}) − J(U₀) = ΔJ^{re} := J^h(𝕎_n(U)) − J^h(U₀). Hence C_n − C_{n+1} = (Ad_G − 1)ΔJ + (ΔJ − ΔJ^{re}).
          - {#147} The expansion. For a field a, J^h(e^{iηa}U₀) = J(U₀) + D*_{U₀}D_{U₀}a + 𝒬_{U₀}(a). Here 𝒬 collects the rest: (D*_V − D*_{U₀})F_V, of order zero in the plaquette field F_V with coefficient O(|a|), and D*_{U₀} of the plaquette field's nonlinear part and its commutators with the real curvature. The nonlinear part is a local analytic function of a on each plaquette, vanishing to second order, and D* is a first-order difference operator. So D* of it is bounded by its first differences, which are bilinear in a and its first differences (with the real curvature as a coefficient). In the (−2)-norm this is (L^iη)²|a||∇a| ≤ εγ on layer i, uniformly in k. So 𝒬 and its differences are bounded by a, its first differences, and the real curvature ((2.34)), and no second difference of a enters.
          - {#148} The rotation term. Pointwise on layer i, |ΔJ| ≤ O(c_χS_L)δ′_j(L^iη)^{−3}, by (190)'s D*_UD_U entry, (L^iη)^{−3}, with |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, and the expansion. With |λ̂| ≤ B_GL^iηε_{n+1} pointwise (#120), |(Ad_G − 1)ΔJ| ≤ (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|ΔJ| ≤ O(c_χB_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}(L^iη)^{−2}, so |(Ad_G − 1)ΔJ|_{(−2)} ≤ O(c_χB_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}. Only |λ̂| enters.
          - {#149} The mixed difference. ΔJ − ΔJ^{re} is D*D of [F(d_{n+1}, s̃) − F(0, s^{re})]/η plus the mixed difference of 𝒬 at A_L, 𝓗(d_n), and their real values. The first is bounded by (190)'s D*D entry with *The Schwarz bound* for (a) and with (190) in s for (b), as for the phase. The second is first order, bounded by |A_L|, |∇A_L|, the values and gradients of 𝓗(d_n), and γ_{n+1}.
          - {#150} Hence κ_n ≤ κ_{n+1} + C_*L^NS_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n}(ε_m + ε_{m+1} + γ_{m+1}), L^N from (b) (#149, #119). No Hessian of λ̂, of 𝓗, or of A_L enters, and neither does a base change of the current. Over the N + 1 steps, κ_0 ≤ κ_Φ + O(L^NN^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²(ε_Φ + γ_Φ), and the deformations' part is below ⅛(1−β)α_{0,k} for g small, on every layer.
          - {#151} The starting value. κ_Φ is the current deviation of Φ(𝐔). In the decomposition of *The starting values*, 𝓗(Δd)'s current is 𝒟^λ_k's current condition at the representative V̂, T₁'s and T₂'s are bounded by their D*D there, and the 𝒬 terms are at most O(sup_nα_{0,n} + ε_Φ + γ_Φ)(ε_Φ + γ_Φ).
        - {#152} *The 𝐉 coordinate (derived).* At real data the old terms are evaluated at a configuration together with its current: RG1 substitutes (U_n(M·(𝐔)), J_n(M·(𝐔))) for (𝐔, 𝐉) (p.262 l.61–p.263 l.2, under (1.16)), with J = D*ξ^{−2}π Im ∂U and the real-data identity (1.8)–(1.9). The analytic continuation of this current is the holomorphic current J^h(V) := D*_Vξ^{−2}π(∂V − ∂V^{−1})/(2i) of the evaluation configuration. So the 𝐉 coordinate at the evaluation point is J^h of the representative. It follows the representative by RG1 (1.10), (𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉), and by the E-terms' G^c-invariance ((1.19)). The old terms also take the derived configurations' currents J_n(M·(𝐔)), which are CRE (2.37)'s 𝐉_{p,X}(M·𝐔). These are quoted at the u-free representative (*The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*), through (1.65) at (V̂, J^h(V̂)) (#6.10, #156, #158). The old terms' own space U^c_j is RG I's, a union of orbits of the G^c-valued gauge transformations (RG1 p.262 l.13; CRE p.259 l.40–45; table §11), and the old terms are G^c-invariant (RG1 (1.19)). So the passage from the u-free to the reduced representative needs no transfer for the old terms: they take the same values there. In that passage 𝕌̃ obeys (2.34)–(2.37) with the coefficients f♮_n, so it lies in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13, derived, not reviewed; #158), up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), #6.2), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2). *The current of the deformations* bounds a different object, the evaluation configuration's own current J^h, which is (2.36)'s. The data's own 𝐉 enters the new terms: "The variable 𝐉 replaces the function J_{k+1} as usual" (RG1 p.275 l.4), and LFR II introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) into the other expressions "in the usual way" (p.375 l.11–12).
          - {#153} The data's 𝐉 is not transported. 𝒟^{λ,M}_k is a set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉), and P acts on 𝐔 alone (*The M-factored domain*). The members are evaluated at (P(𝐔), 𝐉), whose Landau form is (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) by RG1 (1.10), with u_L the G^c gauge transformation from P(𝐔) to V̂. So R(u_L)𝐉, the pair's 𝐉 in the Landau frame, obeys 𝒟^λ_k's current condition and, by #53, 𝒟_k's (2.36) about 0, by the definition of 𝒟^{λ,M}_k. The new terms take this 𝐉 where J_k appeared, with no transport beyond the gauge transformation of the pair. CRE's B-terms are scalars, invariant under G-valued gauge transformations of (𝐔, 𝐉, A) (CRE (2.41)(iii), p.261 l.60–62). As for the words (#6.10), their extension is taken at the G^c transform (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉, R(u_L)A), which agrees with the T-step's value at real parameters, where u_L is G-valued. So their bounds at (P(𝐔), 𝐉) are (2.42) at that pair, with no conjugation factor, since R(u_L)A lies in their outer fields' domain (#6.17, derived, not reviewed).
          - {#154} At the T-step's points the new 𝐉 lands by the transfer bullet (#54: the 𝐇-part takes ¼(1−β) of the room, the rest ¾(1−β)) at s = 1, and by *At s ≠ 1* with T_Y's α₀-entry otherwise. The landing of R(u_L)𝐉 uses the step-k frame u_L, so at the next step's points it is taken in part (b)'s frame t, which differs from the data's step-(k+1) frame by a factor e^{2|Im λ_f|} = 1 + O(α_{1,k}) and by the term (R(f) − 1)J_k, at most 3C_fdλ_wλ_RB_J/(1−β) times the room, which is o(1) once B_J → 0 as g → 0 (derived at #173 and reviewed as packet JB, at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause, derived, not reviewed, #130.12; (iii), *The bounds*, part (b)), and at every s and at both kinds of background (part (a); #136), it also uses (2.36) at the point's Landau representative, derived, not reviewed (#6.9, #6.14; #136, #137).
        - {#155} *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points (quoted).* 𝒟_k, D(k)(iii)'s domain, is CRE's full complex part of Ũ_k^c about the base point U_k: (2.34)–(2.35), (2.36)–(2.37), and (2.39) (SW closure (b1)), and 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟♮_k, with 𝒟_k itself at the T-step's points (#53, #6.14).
          - {#156} LFR II (1.65) (p.375) maps (𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ Ũ_k^c(Y₄) to U_k^0(𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ Ũ_k″^c(Y₁), the space (1.64)–(1.69) of LFR I for n = N. (1.65) is stated for Bałaban's extension, which depends on 𝐉 (*The scope*). The note's U_k^0(M(𝐔)) equals Bałaban's U_k^0 at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))), by the identity principle on the graph over 𝓜^c (*The scope*). P(𝐔) is the axial-gauge minimiser (VAR Proposition 9), and its Landau form V̂ is a G^c gauge transform of it, complex and ≠ 1 at the block points (#106). Ũ_k^c is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (LFR I p.191 l.32–33; CRE p.261 l.42–43), and CRE (2.39) at the axial form (CRE p.261 l.35–40) changes additively under a G^c gauge transformation, by D(Im λ̂) at first order, so membership at V̂ does not give membership at P(𝐔) (#6.6). The words are invariant under the G^c block transformations near the G-valued ones (#6.10), so (1.65) is applied at (V̂, J^h(V̂)) instead. (V̂, J^h(V̂)) lies in 𝒟_k itself at the T-step's points (#6.14) and at the extension-domain points (#6.16), both derived, not reviewed, and there (1.65) applies, read as an inclusion of spaces as on #17.
            {#156.1} The chain's first step is the 𝐇_k″ step with its normalisation: from U_k^0 ∈ Ũ_k″^c(Y₁) to U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} ((1.51), p.370 l.11), where the chain starts (#86). That U_k″ lies in Ũ^{(N)c} is the reading *R-chain-start* (#17). The later steps follow (#157), with Bałaban's stages read under *R-chain-norm* (#88.1).
          - {#157} Along the chain, LFR I p.191 l.39–44: if 𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n+1)c}, then exp iηℍ_k^{(n)}(g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j))𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n)c}, "and the corresponding statement for 𝕁", with the proof "almost identical to the proof of a similar statement for the configurations U_k^{(n)}, given in (1.44)–(1.52)". For n = 0 the space coincides with Ũ_k^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.191 l.33–34). (1.68) prints c = 3 on Ω_k (p.191 l.13–14), which at n = 0 gives 3α_{0,k} there, so taking CRE's radii at n = 0 is the reading *R-coincides* (#17). LFR I (1.68) lists |∂𝕌 − 1|, |∂U_{p,X}(M·𝕌) − 1|, |𝕁|, and |𝕁_{p,X}(M·𝕌)|, which are (2.34)–(2.37).
          - {#158} So, where V̂ ∈ 𝒟_k (above), the u-free representatives of the evaluation points, built from (V̂, J^h(V̂)), obey the complex curvature conditions (2.34), (2.35), and (2.37), and (2.36) about 0, with Bałaban's radii under *R-coincides*, *R-chain-start*, and *R-chain-norm* (#86, #88.1, #156.1), and with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β) (#87, #156). These are Bałaban's presented-as-true claims, with the proofs omitted as "almost identical", and are accepted. The reduced representative 𝕌̃, at which the class's bounds are taken, is a G^c transform of the u-free one, and the spaces are G-invariant only, with no margin stated. So 𝕌̃ obeys these conditions with the coefficients f♮_n, that is, it lies in 𝒟♮_k, where the members are analytic and bounded (#6.12–#6.13, derived, not reviewed), up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), #6.2), with C_Λ's frame bound in ε_Φ derived, not reviewed (#41.1–#41.2).
        - {#159} *The starting values γ_Φ and κ_Φ (derived at the R-step's evaluation points).* The bounds at 𝐔 are those at P(𝐔) (*The scope*), so take 𝐔 ∈ 𝓜^c with 𝐔 = P(𝐔), whose representative V̂ lies in 𝒟^λ_k. The bounds below are derived where the real base is one of Bałaban's post-R backgrounds on the support of the R-step's indicators, and where the real base is the T-step's U_{k+1}, at a global and at a localized background, whose data lie within O(ε_k) of that support, with the larger constant of δ^{re} given there ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*). At every s and at both kinds of background, V̂'s conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the T-step's points are derived, not reviewed (#6.14, #136, #137). REG's step-k normalisation is arranged by the choice of pair (part (b), derived). γ_Φ and κ_Φ are bounds on configurations, and no member is evaluated in them, so neither the covariant members' conjugation factors (#6.8) nor the outer fields (#6.17) enter here. At a general real point of 𝓜 the data on Λ are free, and |δ^{re}| = O(ε_k) is not claimed there. On 𝓜^c, Φ(𝐔) = U_k^0(M(𝐔)), so the chain starts at a VAR minimiser, and no transport by E enters.
          - {#160} The two minimisers. 𝐔 = U^{post}(M(𝐔)) and U_k^0(M(𝐔)) are minimisers for 𝐁_k″(Y₁) (*The M-factored domain*, nesting) whose data agree off Λ (*The response is sourced on Λ*). On Λ^{old} = Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, U_k^0 takes T′_k(X)'s real integration variables V_h (LFR II p.370 l.4, p.378 l.31), and their difference from U's averages is not bounded ((R-eval-old) at the top, derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), through #5.8); the bounds below concern Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}. There U_k^0 takes the averages of U_0^{(AL)} = U^{(L)}_{k,Z}(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}, V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c})) (LFR II p.366 l.23), and the post-R configuration is the global minimiser U_k = U_{𝐁_k}(V) ((1.35)) for the data V_k′V_Λ of (1.34). U_{k,Z}(V_k′V_Λ) is LFR I (1.74)'s minimiser, defined in each component of Z separately (LFR I p.192 l.52–55). So the real data differ on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} in two ways. The first is through V_k′ = exp ig_kB′, and the R-step's integrand restricts |g_kB′| < M₀ε_k (LFR II (1.1), p.356, and the display below it, l.37). The second is the replacement of U_{k,Z} by U_k, which Bałaban makes "in the same way as above, i.e., with the configuration U_{k,Z} replaced by U_k, and with some new small terms" (LFR II p.365 l.35–36). "Above" is (1.36)'s representation, an 𝐇-function whose argument "has a support in the boundary layer of the width 2M₁ at the boundary ∂Z^{∼−3}, and it is bounded by 44d²ε_k" (p.365 l.29–30). Bałaban prints neither the representation nor that bound for this replacement. Reading it as the same representation with the same bound (Bałaban's "small terms", accepted) is the reading *R-small-Z* (#17), and under it the 𝐇-function's linear bound (VAR Proposition 9 and (190), with factor K, as in the next sub-bullet) puts this part at most O(Kd²ε_k) per block. Let δ^{re} be the real data difference there at the real base U, so |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd²)ε_k per block of 𝐁_k″(Y₁), and at the T-step's real base U_{k+1}, whose data lie within O(ε_k) of the support, |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*), far below r_B for g small.
          - {#161} The real base. Take U_k^0(M(U)) as its Landau representative relative to U, U_k^0(M(U)) = e^{iηR₀}U with R₀ := 𝓗(δ^{re}). This holds where the real data differ only by δ^{re}, that is, apart from the real data difference on Λ^{old}, which is (R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5). The words are gauge invariant (*The gauge representatives*), so this choice is free, and (1.51)'s gauge u_k″ plays no part. By (190), (L^iη)|R₀| ≤ O(S_L)|δ^{re}| on layer i, which is all that passing from ∇_U to ∇_{U_k^0(M(U))} uses: a base change of order zero for the gradient with coefficients O(S_L(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k), as in *The gradient of the deformations*. The current is compared at the configurations themselves (*The current of the deformations*), with no base change.
          - {#162} The complex part. Relative to its real base, Φ(𝐔)'s complex part is 𝓗(Δd^0 ⊕ δ^{re}) − 𝓗(δ^{re}), where Δd^0 is the complex data: Δd off Λ, 0 on Λ^{old}, where U_k^0 takes the real V_h, and the critical extension's complex part on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}. Write it as 𝓗(Δd) + T₁ + T₂, plus the part T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)).
            - {#163} 𝓗(Δd) is 𝐔's own complex part. Only its complex part enters, so its phase, gradient, and current are 𝒟^λ_k's conditions at the representative V̂, which hold by the definition of 𝒟^{λ,M}_k. Its current is the configuration's (*The 𝐉 coordinate*).
            - {#164} T₁ is the response to Δd^0 − Δd, which is supported on Λ: −Δd on Λ^{old}, at most 𝐔's own room per block (packet A's "+1", #43), and on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} at most O(1)L^j(C_{rsp}M⁵ + 1)s_Z per j-block, with C_{rsp} = O(d)(C⁰_{rsp} + B₃²B₅) of #42, by (1.41), (190), and the frame's bound (#41.2) (*The response is sourced on Λ*, *The fine field at the scale k* and *The averages*, #42–#43). Its phase is *The response is sourced on Λ*'s. By (190)'s gradient and D*D entries, uniform on the ball, its gradient and D*D are at most C_Λ′M⁵e^{−δ₀dist(x,Λ)/16}s_Z in the layer weights, with C_Λ′ = O(K)C_Λ. K is bounded uniformly in M once M meets *S_L is finite*'s condition, since a larger M only increases the distances.
            - {#165} T₂ := ∫₀¹[κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)]Δd dt, with κ := δ𝓗/δB. The map w ↦ κ(tΔd ⊕ wδ^{re}) is analytic for |w| ≤ r_B/(2|δ^{re}|), and (190) bounds it there, pointwise in (x, y′) with its decay. By the Cauchy estimate in w, |κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)| ≤ (4|δ^{re}|/r_B) times (190)'s bound. The same holds for the gradient and D*D entries. So T₂'s phase and gradient are at most O(K²(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k/r_B) times 𝐔's room on every layer, and its D*D at most O(λ_RK²(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k/r_B) times the current room, since Δd is bounded through the phase room and α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k}. This tends to 0 with g.
          - {#166} Landing. On a layer n ≤ k₀, λ^{(k)}_n = λ, since k − n ≥ N₀ and c′^{N₀} ≥ λ_w ≥ λ for g small (λ_w := λL, #52.1) (N₀ → ∞ as g → 0, *Where it lands*). There T₁'s gradient is at most ⅛(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n times the gradient room once λ ≥ 8C_Λ′M⁵/(1−β). Its D*D is at most ⅛(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n times the current room once λ ≥ 8C_Λ′M⁵(C₁/C₀)/(1−β), with C₁/C₀ = λ_R fixed after L and M (*The constants*). #72's λ meets both. On k₀ < n ≤ k, crossing n − k₀ layers of the Ω_m costs at least (n − k₀)M/M₁ in the scaled distance (*S_L is finite*), so T₁ is at most C_Λ′M⁵e^{−δ₀M/(16M₁)} times the gradient room and C_Λ′λ_RM⁵e^{−δ₀M/(16M₁)} times the current room. Both are ≤ ⅛(1−β) for M large given d, L, and M₁, since λ_R = K_RLM + 1 is linear in M (table #T2.24). T₂ on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} and the base change tend to 0 with g, and are below (1−β)/32 of the room for g small; On the layers n ≤ k₀, T₂^{old}'s phase lands in the weighted room λw of #52.1, derived and reviewed, given LF (#5), the passage at U₀ ≠ 1 of #5.8 (a) included ((R-eval-old)), and its gradient and current land in the rooms μ^{(k)}_nλ of #52.1, at most ⅛(1−β)μλ where μ^{(k)}_n = μ, Λ^{old} included (the contacts draft, §4a; derived and reviewed, packet CT). Its phase on the layers k₀ < n ≤ k, and its gradient and current where μ^{(k)}_n < μ, are at most (1−β)/64 times the units, at most (1−β)λ^{(k)}_n/64, by its decay away from Λ^{old} (#5.8 (c); the contacts draft, §4a; derived and reviewed, packet CT). 𝐔's own part at the T-step's points carries (b3)'s margin (*The R-step lands in the family*, #72–#75, with P of the point by *The M-factored domain*, and at s ≠ 1, and at s = 1 at a localized background (*The bounds*, part (a)), with (2.34)–(2.37) at the point derived, not reviewed, #6.14, #137, as at s = 1 at a global background, #136). The gradient budget is that of the phase (#75), with #137's response at s ≠ 1 inside SW (b3)'s margin (#138). On the older layers this uses λ^{(k)}_n ≥ c′ ≥ 2, which L ≥ 12d + 6 (table #T1.3) gives with β ≤ 1/4 and β₀ = 1/7. The current budget, in units of λ^{(k)}_n times the current room's unit, which the room f_nλ^{(k)}_n exceeds by f_n ≥ 1−β, runs as follows. The data's current transfers with ratio at most ½(1−β)(1+β₀) = (4/7)(1−β) at β₀ = 1/7 (#54). 𝐇_𝐕's current is at most (1−β)/64 (#14, with β ≤ 1/4), the quadratic terms are at most (1−β)/32 for g small (#136), and at s ≠ 1 the response of #137 adds at most (1−β)/64 + o(1). T₁ is at most ⅛(1−β), T₂ and the base change at most (1−β)/32, and the deformations at most ⅛(1−β) for g small (*The current of the deformations*). The total is at most (4/7 + 1/64 + 1/32 + 1/64 + 1/8 + 1/32 + 1/8)(1−β) + o(1) < 0.92(1−β), inside the room. Where μ^{(k)}_n = μ, T₂^{old} adds at most ⅛(1−β)μλ and the room is μλ (#52.1): the data's current lands in (4/7)(1−β)μλ, the other terms, ≤ 0.35(1−β)λ, are ≤ 0.175(1−β)μλ since μ ≥ 2, and the total is < 0.88(1−β)μλ; the gradient's total is ≤ (13/16)(1−β)μλ + (β/4)μλ. Elsewhere, by #5.8 (c), T₂^{old} adds at most (1−β)/64 to the budgets above, which are in units of λ^{(k)}_n times the unit (the contacts draft, §4a).
    - {#167} (iv) D2.7 15.6(c), (d), and (f)'s extension domain of radius r^{ext}_k (derived; the parts marked at its end derived, not reviewed). Its points are the post-R data V_k within 2r^{ext}_k per scale-k bond, in the gauge-fixed deviation, of a real datum V_k⁰ on the support of the R-step's indicators: the 2r^{ext}_k-neighbourhood of the real support. The insertions are evaluated at their transport form as above. r^{ext}_k := min(r^{an}_k, c_{ext}α_{0,k}), with c_{ext} := (1+β₀)/(2LK), K the factor of VAR Proposition 9 and (190) (#8), and r^{an}_k := g_kC₁p₁(g_k), the radius of CRE (3.43), |A| < C₁p₁(g_k), for the unscaled field g_kA (a scaling not stated at (3.43), as in D2.7 15.7). CRE does not define p₁(·), which occurs in CRE only in (3.43). It is either LFR I's p₁ < p₀ (p.183 l.2) or a slip for q₁ (READINGS R43; TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS, LFR II p.368). Under either reading r^{ext}_k ≤ c_{ext}α_{0,k} and r^{ext}_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}, which is all that (iv) and D2.7 15.7 use. The post-R top-layer radii are α_{0,k} = g_kC₀(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀} and α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k} per scale-k bond (CRE (2.28), table #T2.24). The Cauchy step of 15.6(d) at a real datum V_k⁰ on the support of the R-step's indicators, with deviation ε₀, evaluates D_g at the data of deviation tε₀/|ε₀|, |t| < r^{ext}_k (when |ε₀| ≥ r^{ext}_k, (c)'s bound at V_k⁰ itself suffices, and V_k⁰ is in the domain). Each of these points is within 2r^{ext}_k of V_k⁰ per scale-k bond, so it is in the domain. At every point of the domain, V_k⁰ is taken as the real base, so the base is on the support, as #159 and *The real base points* require. At such a point the configuration is U_k(V_k) = U_{𝐁_k}(V_k), the post-R data being at scale k on Z. It lies in 𝓜^c up to a gauge transformation by #133–#135: U^{post}(M(U_k(V))) = U_k(V) on Y₁ at real data, continued analytically. VAR Proposition 9 and (190) apply to the 𝐁_k-minimiser directly, which is VAR's own setting, with K taken as the largest of VAR's factors for 𝐁_k, 𝐁_k″(Y₁), the 𝐁_k″(Ω) of #130, and U_{k,Z}'s 𝐁_k(Z) (LFR II p.367 l.11), and c_{ext} uses this K. So the configuration's whole deviation from U_k(V_k⁰), real and imaginary directions together, is at most 2K r^{ext}_k in phase, gradient, and D*D in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights, up to a relative error O(r^{ext}_k/r_B). The deviation counts as the g^c-valued complex part, as in CRE (2.39). So per scale-k bond it is below ((1+β₀)/L)α_{0,k} ≤ ((1+β₀)/L)α_{1,k}, which is the bound used above at the T-step's points, for 𝒟^λ_k's phase and gradient conditions and for the current condition, whose room is α_{0,k}. The pair's 𝐉 is J^h of the point (*The 𝐉 coordinate*). So the bullets above apply to these points as at the T-step's points, and they obey 𝒟^λ_k's phase, gradient, and current conditions. The bullets carry their part T₂^{old}, derived and reviewed, given LF ((R-eval-old), #5). The slots' CRE B-terms take (2.42) at the Landau form with no conjugation factor, and their outer-field argument R(u_L)A lies in its domain, derived, not reviewed (#153, #6.17). These points are not part (b)'s pairs, so no conjugation factor arises in the evaluation at them. The class's bounds used there include the carried att bounds, cross weights, and transports, which hold on the family with no factor, #6.8's factors at the earlier T-steps' pairs not entering the family bounds (#130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #68, #78, #82, #83). 𝒟_k itself at the point's Landau representative V̂, where (1.65) is applied (#6.10), and along the segment V̂_t on which the words' equality at the reduced representative rests (#111, #113), is #6.16 (derived, not reviewed): the real base is ε-regular at each layer's scale, and the deviation takes less than half of each room. Then the reduced representative lies in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13). D2.7 15.6(d) and 15.7 take r^{ext}_k in their Cauchy factors.
    - {#168} *A_k (derived).* D2.7 15.6(c)'s e^{A_k} bounds the transports of the words on span(g); the slots' insertions are bounded separately, by e^{2ΣA} each.
      - {#169} For a set S of loop bonds, A_k(S) := c_π Σ_{b∈S} sup|Im ηA_b|, the sup over the evaluation points, where U_b = e^{iηA_b}u_b with u_b ∈ G, and c_π := sup_{|X|≤1}‖dπ(X)‖.
      - {#170} π is unitary on G, and the Hermitian part of iηdπ(Re A_b) − ηdπ(Im A_b) is −ηdπ(Im A_b). So ‖π(U_b)‖ ≤ e^{c_πη|Im A_b|}, the transports along S have norm at most e^{A_k(S)}, and the word is at most dim π·e^{A_k(S)} times its slots' bounds. This holds for every compact G and unitary π.
      - {#171} On 𝒟^λ_k the complex part is at most λ_wα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (λ_w := λL bounds λw, #52.1), and a scale-k bond has L^k fine bonds. So A_k(S) ≤ c_πλ_wα_{1,k}ℓ_k(S), with ℓ_k(S) the length of S in scale-k units. With α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} (CRE (2.28)), this is g_k times a polylog per unit length.
      - {#172} The evaluation points of 15.6(c) obey 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition: U^R at the T-step's points, the transport-form points ((i) and (iii)), and the extension-domain points of (iv), on which 15.6(d) uses B(g). This holds up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), and at the next step's points, at every s and at both kinds of background, with the curvature conditions at the point derived, not reviewed (#6.14; #136; (iii), *The bounds*, part (a); #137). The sup is taken at the representative at which 𝒟^λ_k's conditions hold, which the words' gauge invariance permits (*The gauge representatives*). At the transport-form points that representative is the reduced 𝕌̃, and the equality of the words there rests on #111's identity theorem, which holds as far as (V̂_t, J^h(V̂_t)) ∈ Ũ_k^c(Y₄), derived, not reviewed, at the T-step's points (#113, #6.10, #6.14) and at the extension-domain points (#6.16), with 𝕌̃ in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13). At U^R the slots' CRE B-terms take (2.42) at the Landau form with no conjugation factor (#153), with their outer-field argument R(u_L)A in its domain by #6.17, derived, not reviewed. The phase bound itself uses no curvature condition, and A_k uses no other condition of 𝒟^λ_k.
    - {#173} *The real base points (derived, given D(k)(iii)'s pre-R clause (map #MOMGD.11's on a term with the printed characteristic functions; after an earlier R-step, D2.7 item 15.8 (e)'s clause O3, derived, not reviewed; #175), for the transfer to U_{k+1} the post-R clause of D2.7 item 15.8 (derived, not reviewed), and Bałaban's printed implications).* At real external data, at every point of the R-step's integration support, the preliminary variables included, U_k^{(0)} is a G-valued gauge transform of one of D(k)(iii)'s real base points. So it satisfies their conditions, the real-U part of CRE (2.34) and (2.38) (map #MD2.0.3.53, #MD2.0.3.55).
      {#173.1} *The real base current (derived and reviewed as packet JB; the transfer to U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8).* At every such point B_J := sup(L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, with n the bond's layer index, satisfies B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′, with ε₁′ := max(2L²ε_0, 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}), which tends to 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K. This is the bound that #130's frame term (R(f) − 1)J_k needs. On the other bonds that term vanishes: every REG-normalised transformation is 1 at the layer-0 points, since REG (1.70) at a layer-0 point is an empty sum (p.88 l.38–44), so f = 1 there, and those bonds have both endpoints in Λ_0 = Ω₁^c. Let Ω := Ω_1^∼. The bonds off Ω_1 are first-step variables. Γ₀ = Ω₁^c carries the bonds themselves (CRE (2.2)), and they are the first step's V_0 = U⌈_{Ω₁^c} (CRE (1.12), p.248). An R-operation's region Z is a union of whole components of Λ_k^c (LFR I p.177 l.17–33), with the set Z^{(k)} on Z and unchanged off Z (LFR II (1.33), p.365 l.2–7; LFR I (1.12)). Since Ω₁^c ⊂ Λ_k^c, each component of Ω₁^c is removed whole or keeps its first-step boundary, and later large fields change only Ω_{j+1} ⊂ Ω_1.
      {#173.2} So every remaining part of ∂Ω_1 has its first-step margin: Ω_1 lies at distance at least 2LMR_1 from every large-field region (CRE p.248 l.8–9). The first-step bounds used below are statements about the step-1 integrand. There χ_0 localized in Ω_1^∼ is implied by the other step-1 functions and dropped (p.248 l.9–12), and p.246 l.21–22 bounds V on the support of χ_0(P_0^c). The variables V_0 and V⌈Γ_1 are not integrated at later steps, so later integrations only shrink the support in them, and every bound below that involves only V_0 and V⌈Γ_1 holds at every point of the support of the step-k integrand. D(k)(iii)'s base points are taken in that support (map #MD2.0.3.53). The restricted problem. The free bonds of 𝐁_k lie in Ω_1, and every plaquette that contains one lies in Ω. The bonds off Ω are at distance at least LMR_1 from Ω_1, so a configuration admissible for the problem with support Ω (VAR's Ω_0 := Ω, with 𝐁_k's layering on Ω, as for the localized sets of CRE p.256 l.58–61) extends by U_k off Ω, with the same action change and the same conditions (2) off Ω. The sequence Ω ⊃ Ω_1 ⊃ … ⊃ Ω_k meets VAR's geometric conditions (p.277 l.11–17). Ω_1 is a union of LMR_1-cubes (CRE (1.10), p.248 l.8), and Ω adds one layer of them (CRE p.245 l.40–43), so both are unions of M_1-cubes, the partitions being compatible (p.245 l.38–40), and η^{−1}dist(Ω^c, Ω_1) ≥ LMR_1 > R_1^VM_1, VAR's (1) at j = 0, for g small: CRE's scale R_1 ≥ (log g_1^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.36–39) with g_1 ≤ (1+β₀)g_k, while VAR's R_1^V and M_1 are fixed (map #MOMGD.11 distinguishes the two). For j ≥ 1 the conditions are those of 𝐁_k's own sequence {Ω_j}, to which VAR applies at the T-step, and the restriction leaves Ω_j unchanged for j ≥ 1.
      {#173.3} So U_k on Ω is a minimal configuration of that problem. Its data meet VAR (7) at ε₁′ (VAR p.278 l.34–45, mixed plaquettes included), as follows. Layer 0 (the bonds V_0 in Ω∖Ω_1): |V_0(∂p) − 1| < ε_0, since χ_0 (CRE p.246 l.4–6), localized in Ω_1^∼, equals 1 on the domain of integration (p.248 l.9–12). Layer 1: |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < 2L²ε_0 by CRE p.246 l.21–22, since (∂V)(p′) is the first step's V(∂p′) (V = Ū on Ω_1^{(1)} by CRE (1.11), and V̄ on the layer-0 bonds is the average of V_0), and p′ ⊂ Ω_1^∼ ⊂ (P_0^c)^{(1)}. Layer j ≥ 2: the four blocks of p′ lie in Ω_{j−1} ⊂ Ω_1, where the real-U part of (2.34) gives |∂U_k − 1| < α_{0,n}L^{−2n} ≤ (1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}L^{−2j} on layer n ≥ j − 1 (α_{0,n} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k} for n ≤ k, as at #130). V = Ū_k^j there by VAR (3), so CMP98 Proposition 2 in its local form (p.26 l.46–53, with its k := j and α₀ := (1+β₀)L²α_{0,k} ≤ c₂) gives |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}. For g small, ε₁′ ≤ ε₁, the fixed constant of #94. So VAR Theorem 1 gives a minimal orbit in 𝔘(B₃ε₁′) (VAR (8), p.279). U_k's orbit lies in the space with ε₀ := B₃ε₁ (#94), restricted to Ω. On U_k's own support, a small neighbourhood of Ω_1 including a layer of M_1-cubes (CRE p.255 l.21–23), the conditions (2) are those of #94. On the rest of Ω, in layer 0, the plaquettes satisfy |∂U_k − 1| < ε_0 < B₃ε₁ by the layer-0 bound above, and at each bond the problem's current is a sum of at most 2(d−1) such plaquette terms, so it is at most C(d)ε_0η^{−1} < B₃ε₁η^{−1} for g small.
      {#173.4} So by the uniqueness clause (p.279 l.15, with B₃ε₁′ ≤ B₃ε₁ ≤ B₃a₁ ≤ a₀) U_k's orbit is that minimal orbit. The second line of VAR (2) (p.278) and J = Im ξ^{−2}D*∂U (VAR (28), p.282 l.34), with the current gauge covariant, give (L^nξ)³|J_k| < B₃ε₁′ at every bond with an endpoint in Ω_1: every plaquette containing such a bond lies in Ω, so there the problem's current is J_k's. At part (b)'s base. Part (b) uses J_k at its real base U_{k+1}, which is not shown to lie on the support (#130 (a), *The base near the support*). There some U^{B′} = U_k(e^{iB′}V), with real B′ and sup|B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_k, lies on the support of the post-R function's indicators (#130 (a)). U^{B′} is the post-R background U_{𝐁_k}(V) of LFR II (1.35) at the datum e^{iB′}V (#132), not one of D(k)(iii)'s pre-R points, and part (b)'s layers at U_{k+1} are those of this post-R 𝐁_k (#132). The post-R density is of the CRE Sect. 2 form with new regions (LFR II Theorem 1, p.355 l.17–21, accepted). Off Z the preliminary steps leave the regions unchanged (Ω_j″ = Ω_j ∩ Z^c there, and Γ_0″ = Ω_1^c; LFR I (1.12)–(1.13), p.179 l.32–47), and LFR II (1.33) (p.365 l.2–7) sets Γ_j′ = Γ_j″ ∩ Z^c for j < k and Γ_k′ = Γ_k″ ∪ Z^{(k)}.
      {#173.5} So the new regions are Ω_j′ = Ω_j ∪ Z for 1 ≤ j ≤ k, and Γ_0′ = Ω_1^c ∖ Z. The real base points of the post-R term satisfy the real-U part of CRE (2.34) and (2.38) on these regions: map #MOMGD.11 derives that clause from the printed small-field functions of a term of the CRE Sect. 2 form, and D2.7 item 15.8 (`WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md`, derived, not reviewed) applies it to the post-R term, as #175 records.
      {#173.6} So the derivation above runs at U^{B′} with the post-R term's objects in place of the pre-R ones: Ω_1′ for Ω_1, Ω′ := Ω_1′^∼ for Ω, the post-R term's own support (CRE p.255 l.21–23 for that term) for U_k's, and B_J taken over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1′. Its inputs carry over as follows. f = 1 at the layer-0 points of the post-R 𝐁_k by the same empty sum. Z is a union of whole components of Λ_k^c ⊇ Ω_1^c, so Ω_1′^c = Ω_1^c ∖ Z is a union of whole components of Ω_1^c, and no component of Ω_1′^c is adjacent to Z. Its bonds are first-step V_0, not integrated by R, every part of ∂Ω_1′ is a remaining part of ∂Ω_1 with the first-step margin, and layers 0 and 1 of Ω′ are bounded as above. VAR's geometric conditions hold for Ω′ ⊃ Ω_1′ ⊃ … ⊃ Ω_k′: at j = 0, Ω′∖Ω_1′ is the layer of LMR_1-cubes along those remaining parts of ∂Ω_1, so η^{−1}dist(Ω′^c, Ω_1′) ≥ LMR_1 > R_1^VM_1 as above; for j ≥ 1 they are those of the post-R 𝐁_k's own sequence, to which LFR II (1.35) applies VAR. The R-operation acts only at k > N (D2.7 item 13: the 𝐑^{(j)} terms vanish for j ≤ N; N read as N_k, #609), so the data on Z lie in layer k ≥ 2, and the four blocks of a layer-k plaquette on Z lie in Ω_{k−1}′ ⊇ Z. There, and on every older layer, VAR (7) at ε₁′ comes from the real-U part of (2.34) by CMP98 Proposition 2 as for layers j ≥ 2 above, with the coupling g_k on Z (p.365 l.19).
      {#173.7} Hence B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ at U^{B′}. U^{B′} equals e^{iη𝐇(1,B′)}U_{k+1} up to a G-valued gauge transformation (RG1 (2.6), p.266), and the current is gauge covariant. VAR Proposition 9 and (190) bound the current of the field 𝐇(1,B′) by O(K)sup|B′| in the weights (L^nξ)³, as #130 (a) bounds 𝓗's, with K of #167. So at U_{k+1}, B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ + O(KC₁^{RG2})ε_k, which also tends to 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K. Bałaban's own instance is the first step: CRE p.248 l.37 applies VAR Theorem 1 ("By Theorem 1 [15] there exists exactly one critical point of (1.12)") to "U = V_0 on Ω_1^c, Ū = V on Ω_1^{(1)}", at the data level O(L²ε_0).
      - {#174} *The pre-R support.* Before R, D(k)(iii) is posed for real U_k in the support of the term's indicators (map #MD2.0.3.53).
        - {#175} Its base-point clause there is the T-step's. On a term of the form CRE (2.21) with the printed characteristic functions, map #MOMGD.11 derives it from the printed small-field functions (derived and reviewed there); map #MOMGD.11 leaves every other 𝐑-modified term to D2.7. Before the first R-step the term that reaches the R-step has that form, from the T-steps alone, so the clause is map #MOMGD.11's.
          {#175.1} An R-operation keeps the form (2.21) but changes the characteristic functions (CRE p.258 l.34–36). So after an earlier R-step the clause is D2.7's: the post-R density is of the CRE Sect. 2 form with the new {Ω_j, Λ_j} (LFR II Theorem 1, p.355, accepted), and the base points enter the next T-step as at k ≤ N (D2.7 15.8 (e), its base-point clause O3, derived, not reviewed; 15.8 has no OPEN items). This bullet uses the pre-R clause, and #173's transfer to U_{k+1} uses the post-R one from D2.7 item 15.8 (derived, not reviewed). The note derives neither.
        - {#176} The indicators of the term (1.2) of LFR I are χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}), the indicators among the seven groups (1.3)–(1.9) of the steps j = h, …, k−1 (p.178; (1.6) is a density), χ_h(Ω_h∖Ω^∼_{h+1}), the indicators of 𝐓_h(Z_h), and those of 𝕋_k(Z_k∩Z^c), which the R-step does not change (LFR I p.177 l.42). Let S be the set of original field variables on which every one of them equals 1.
      - {#177} *The operations keep the support.* Up to (1.75) Bałaban's transformations "preserve the k-th density" (LFR I p.193 l.36); after it they are equivalences, with equal integrals over V_k. They are of six kinds.
        - {#178} Decompositions of unity 1 = χ + (1 − χ), which multiply the integrand by an indicator.
        - {#179} Changes of variables, which are bijections at fixed external data:
          - {#180} (1.53), V′_j = V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1}, with the representation (1.44) of U_{k,Z}^{(n)};
          - {#181} (1.81), V″ = V′V_0 on Λ_0 (p.195), restated in the AL gauge as (1.92);
          - {#182} the change of variables (1.22) [III] with ℍ_Z^{(j)} on the components with 1 − χ′^{(j)} (p.188 l.35–37);
          - {#183} the scaling B′_j = g_jB_j (p.187 l.54);
          - {#184} the integration of the δ-functions of (1.72), which parametrises their constraint surface;
          - {#184.1} at each preliminary step, the removal of the linearised δ-functions with the operator C (LFR I p.187 l.53–54), which gives V_j = exp i[g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j)]V^{(j)} (p.188 l.53) and parametrises their constraint surface by B_j;
          - {#184.2} LFR II's V′ = exp ig_kB on 𝐁_0 (LFR II p.360 l.41–42), with the Haar density σ(g_kB) (p.369 l.12), under the restrictions (1.82) [IV] of χ′.
        - {#185} Gauge fixings: the Faddeev–Popov insertions (LFR I p.182 l.43, p.196 l.33), the reversed procedure on first-class components (LFR II p.378 l.17–19), the average over the 2^dd! graph families, and the compensating gauge transformations of V_h and V′ that accompany the AL gauge of U_0 (LFR I p.197 l.39–42). A forward insertion multiplies by a gauge-fixing factor, and the others move points along their gauge orbits.
        - {#186} The R′-replacement (1.100), (1.102): the integration of V_k over Λ, followed by a normalised fraction in dummy variables. The integration variables are V_h on Z_h, left unchanged (LFR I p.194 l.13–15), and V″ on 𝔹_k″ ∩ (Λ ∩ Z_h^c), with V″ = V_j on Γ_j″, j = 0, …, k (p.194 l.12). Through the changes of variables above and the integration of (1.72)'s δ-functions (#184), they parametrise the original V_k↾Λ together with the V_j of (1.72), on the δ-constraint surface. The insertions do not depend on the dummy variables.
        - {#187} Bałaban's exact integrations of the preliminary fluctuation fields, with ℂ_k^{(n+1)} the logarithm of the integral in (1.60) plus the renormalisation term of the Wilson action ((1.62)), and its expansion (1.63), and the localisation expansions of his action terms (LFR II p.372 l.16–20, p.377). They act on his own terms only.
          - {#188} In the integrand that carries the insertions, the preliminary variables (A_j, B_j) stay integration variables, with χ^{(j)}, χ′^{(j)}, and the densities (1.6) as factors. U_k^{(0)} depends on them ((6C), *The insertions*), and the insertions are evaluated at U_k^{(0)} of the original variables, B included, with nothing subtracted from them (D2.7 closure l.6).
          - {#189} Integrating that integrand over (A_j, B_j) gives Bałaban's integrand times the insertions' average over (A_j, B_j). The normalisation is the iterated integral of the integrands (1.60), one per preliminary step, each carrying the earlier ℂ_k^{(i)} through 𝔹_k^{(n)} ((1.61)–(1.62), p.189). No interpolated expansion acts on the insertions.
        - {#190} Removals of a factor that equals 1 on the support of the remaining ones (next bullet).
        - {#191} So every point of the final support corresponds to a point of S, up to a G-valued gauge transformation v of the variables. U(𝐁_k, V^v) is then a G-valued gauge transform of U(𝐁_k, V): the averages are gauge covariant (RG I p.265 l.14), the Wilson action is gauge invariant, and the minimiser is unique up to gauge (VAR Theorem 1 with map #MOMGD.11's uniqueness identification; on Λ, LFR I Proposition 1, p.194, gives the critical orbit from which U_0's V_Λ is taken). The words are traces of ordered products of insertions at one configuration, so they are gauge invariant, and the gauge-fixing identities hold with them.
      - {#192} *Every removal is an implication.*
        - {#193} p.181 l.20–22 and l.33–34: χ_k^{(0)} gives (1.3) for j = h and □ ⊂ (Ω^∼_{h+1})^c∖Z_h, and χ_k^{(1)} gives (1.4) for j = h. On Z_h the function stays as χ_h(Z_h∩Ω_h) until (1.89).
        - {#194} (1.29) (p.183–187), for every n = j − h: on the support of χ_k^{(n+1)}, the completed (1.9) product over Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}, and χ′_j, the functions (1.3), (1.4), (1.5), (1.7), (1.8), and χ_k^{(n)} equal 1 (p.183 l.22–24; (1.31), (1.42)–(1.43), (1.48)–(1.52)). So the left-hand side's χ(Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}), ζ(Ω^c_{j+1}), and χ_k^{(n)} are removed, and the densities (1.6) extend to the whole region. For j + 1 = k, (1.4) is kept as χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}).
        - {#195} *χ′_j and χ′^{(j)} (derived).* Bałaban introduces χ′^{(j)} of (1.55) in the B_j-integral and excludes from Z the components with 1 − χ′^{(j)} (p.188 l.4–10), where χ′_j stays in the form (1.59). (1.60) carries χ′^{(j)} and not χ′_j. B_j is the scaled field (p.187 l.54, p.188 l.2), and Bałaban's large-field estimates take the threshold on the unit scale, |g_jB_j| < δ′_j (LFR II p.383: "|B(b)| ≥ g_j^{−1}δ′_j = A₁p₁(g_j)"; `TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS.md`, LFR II p.380–383). χ′_j is not a pre-R indicator, so S does not need it. What is needed is that the removals of (1.29) also hold on supp χ′^{(j)}.
          - {#196} χ′_j = 1 on supp χ′^{(j)}, so its drop is an implication. Both (1.27) (p.182 l.53) and (1.55) (p.188 l.7) are taken on the * bonds (Ω^c_{j+1}∖Z″_{j+1})^{(j)*}, which omit the bonds b_0(c) (CRE (1.9), p.247 l.51–57). There CB_j = B_j, since C solves the δ-constraint for the variables at b_0(c) only (CRE p.249 l.21–24, RG I p.268 l.2–3), and hD̃ vanishes, since hB vanishes off {b_0(c)} (RG I p.267 l.19). So V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1} = exp ig_jB_j on the * bonds (p.188 l.53), and on supp χ′^{(j)} |exp ig_jB_j − 1| ≤ |g_jB_j| < δ′_j, under (1.55) as printed (g_j ≤ 1) and with the threshold on |g_jB_j| (LFR II p.383; `TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS.md`) alike. By (1.58) and its bound (p.188 l.29–34), |V^{(j)}(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ O(1)e^{−R_j}ε_j < δ′_j for g small. So |V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| < 2δ′_j on the * bonds, which is (1.27). CRE p.249 l.14–16 makes the same remark for χ′_0. (1.29) holds on supp χ′_j, hence on supp χ′^{(j)}.
          - {#197} On all the bonds, the b_0(c) included, V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1} = exp i[g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j)] (p.188 l.53), and D̃ "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms" (RG I p.267). C is linear and local: (CB)(b_0(c)) is a linear combination of the B(b), b ⊂ B(c_−) ∪ B(c_+), b ≠ b_0(c) (CRE p.249 l.21–22), and CB = B on the other bonds. Its coefficients depend on V^{(j)} through the whole linear part of Q̃ ((2.4), RG I p.266 l.6), and through h(c), the inverse of the coefficient of B′(b_0(c)) in (Q̃B′)(c), multiplied by L^{−1} (RG I p.267 l.21–23, p.268 l.2–3). Let S_θ be the closed set of configurations V = e^{iX}V_r on B(c_−) ∪ B(c_+), with V_r real, |∂V_r(p) − 1| ≤ θ on its plaquettes, and X ∈ 𝐠^c, |X| ≤ θ per bond. Let c_C be the sup of C's norm over S_{θ₀}, and c′_C the larger of 1 and the sup over S_{θ₀} of the norm of C's derivative in V, for one θ₀ = θ₀(d, L) fixed below. C's norm and that of its derivative are invariant under G-valued gauge transformations, since C is gauge covariant (as M is, RG I p.265 l.14) and Ad is isometric. In an axial gauge of the connected set B(c_−) ∪ B(c_+), every bond's V_r is a product of at most C(d, L) plaquette holonomies, so |V(b) − 1| ≤ (C(d, L) + 2)θ on S_θ for θ ≤ 1. RG I's averages are CMP98's (its [12], RG I p.299 l.12, applied at p.265 l.17), and (2.4)'s ratio M(V′V^{(j)})M(V^{(j)})^{−1} is CMP98's Ṽ_1 = (V_1V_0)‾(V̄_0)^{−1} ((113), p.34 l.32) with V_1 = V′ and V_0 = V^{(j)}. So Q̃'s linear part is CMP98's Q′(V_0) of (118)–(119) (p.35), up to RG I's constant normalisation. Q′(V_0) is written out (p.35 l.50–52) through the transports R_{0,c_±}, which are polynomial in V_0, R̄_{0,c} = R((V̄_0)_c) (p.35 l.43–44), which is analytic in V_0 near the pure gauges because the average V̄_0 of (113) is built from logarithms of products near 1, and the elements Y, Y_x of V_0, logarithms of products near 1, with g, g^{−1}, and e^{iz} analytic near 0, so it is analytic in V_0 near the pure gauges, and at V = 1 the coefficient of B′(b_0(c)) is invertible (h is defined there, RG I p.267 l.21). (CMP98's Q(V_0) of (121)–(124) linearises the gauge-changed average V̄̄_1 instead; since (122) (p.36 l.11) writes its linear part as L·Q(V_0), by (120) L·Q(V_0) differs from Q′(V_0) only by terms on bonds inside B(c_−) or B(c_+), so the coefficient of the joining bond b_0(c) is the same in Q(V_0) and in L^{−1}Q′(V_0).) So for θ₀(d, L) small the coefficient stays invertible on a neighbourhood of S_{θ₀}, C is analytic in V there (the inverse of an invertible analytic coefficient is analytic), and on the compact set of gauge-fixed configurations of S_{θ₀} (G is compact) c_C and c′_C are finite and depend only on d, L, and G, uniformly in g and j. D̃ is the unique solution of L Q̃B′ + C̃(B′) = L Q̃B with B′ = B − hD̃(B) (RG I p.267 l.24–26, l.28–29), and Q̃, C̃, and h are analytic in V on that neighbourhood. So by the analytic implicit function theorem hD̃(B) is jointly analytic in (V, B) for V near S_{θ₀} and |B| ≤ r₀(d, L), and it vanishes to second order at B = 0 for every V. Let c_D be the sup over V ∈ S_{θ₀} and 0 < |B| ≤ r₀ of |∂_V(hD̃(B))|/|B|². By Cauchy's estimates on the compact gauge-fixed set it is finite and depends only on d, L, and G. The configurations V^{(j)} on the support lie in S_{θ₀} for g small. At a complex point 𝐔, the reduced stage's average is V_{red}(𝐔) = e^{iX}V_{red}(U) with |X| ≤ C_GL^jηε_{n+1}, as for the block data (#97, #116), so it lies in S_{θ₀} as well for g small. #116 evaluates C, h, and D̃ there. χ_k^{(n+1)} bounds U_{k,Z}^{(n+1)}, whose average is V_Z^{(j)} ((1.26), p.182), so |∂V_Z^{(j)} − 1| tends to 0 with g by CMP98 Proposition 2, as at #173.3. And V^{(j)} = exp iℍ_Z^{(j)}V_Z^{(j)} with |ℍ_Z^{(j)}| ≤ O(1)e^{−R_j}ε_j ((1.58), p.188 l.29–34). So |V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ c_Cδ′_j(1 + O(δ′_j)) on supp χ′^{(j)}, and |V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ c_χδ′_j with c_χ := c_C + 2. (iii) uses this bound (#91, #144).
          - {#198} So the functions (1.3), (1.4), (1.5), (1.7), (1.8), and χ_k^{(n)} equal 1 on supp χ′^{(j)}, and the densities (1.6) extend, as in #194.
        - {#199} (1.89) (p.198–200): on the support of χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4})χ_{k,Λ}χ_{h,1/2}χ′, the functions χ_h(Ω_h∩Z_h) and χ_k″ equal 1. Bałaban sketches the proof ("we will sketch only main points", p.198 l.17), and (1.96), (1.98), and p.200 l.12–42 check each line of (1.24) at n = N. It assumes β ≤ 1/4 (p.198 l.16), and its proof needs (1.94)'s two conditions on N (p.198 l.54–57), M large and α ≤ 1/8 (p.200 l.20–21), L₀² ≥ 3 at p.200 l.22–23 (from L₀ ≥ 2, p.182 l.18, a standing assumption), and O(1)B₃B₅M⁵L₀^{−2(N₀−1)} ≤ 1/4 (p.200 l.30).
        - {#200} LFR II p.378 l.9–14: on first-class components χ_{k,Λ} and χ equal 1, on the support of the new small-field function χ_k. On second-class components χ_{k,Λ_i}χ_iδ_{G_i} are kept (l.20–22). So (1.89) applies on both classes.
        - {#201} A component excluded by a large-field function is "not changed by the subsequent operations" (LFR I p.181 l.18). The old functions not yet removed when it is excluded stay on it. Those already removed were removed by implications from factors that stay on it:
          - {#201.1} at step 0, (1.3) for j = h by χ_k^{(0)} (p.181 l.20–23), and (1.4) for j = h by χ_k^{(1)} (l.33–34), which applies to the components excluded later by 1 − χ′_h (p.183 l.3–4) or by (1.28) (p.183 l.11–14);
          - {#201.2} at a (1.29) step, for example before an exclusion by 1 − χ′^{(j)} (p.188 l.10), that step's functions (p.187 l.35–37), from χ^{(j)}, χ′^{(j)} or (1.59), and χ_k^{(n+1)}; where χ_k^{(n+1)} was itself removed at a later (1.29) step (p.183 l.23), the chain runs to the last χ_k^{(m)} still present.
        - {#202} The factors left only shrink the support. They are:
          - {#203} in 𝐓′_k(X) and (1.100): χ′(X), χ_{h,1/2}, σ(g_kB), δ_{rT}(B), χ(Λ_i), χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}), χ_k(X^{∼−6}), and 𝐓_h(Z_h∩X);
          - {#204} on second-class components: χ^c_k(Y_i^{∼−6}), χ_{k,Λ_i}, χ_i, and δ_{G_i};
          - {#205} for the preliminary variables: χ^{(j)}, χ′^{(j)}, and the densities (1.6).
        - {#206} Bałaban "combine[s]" χ_k(X_i^{∼−6}) with χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}) (LFR II p.378 l.15–16). The integrand's factors multiply, and χ_k(Ω) := Π_{□⊂Ω}χ({sup_{p⊂□^∼}|U_{k,□}(V_k, ∂p) − 1| < ε_kη²}) is a product over the cubes of one partition, each factor depending on its cube only (CRE (2.17), p.257 l.39–42). So for unions A, B of these cubes χ_k(A)χ_k(B) = χ_k(A ∪ B), since χ² = χ, and the combination is χ_k(X_i^{∼−6} ∪ Ω_k^{∼4}) (*R-combined*, #17, derived).
      - {#207} *The L₀-weighted layers.* On Z's layers i > k₀ the powers of L₀ weaken only the new functions χ_k^{(n)} ((1.24), (1.49), (1.51)), χ_k″, and the analyticity spaces (1.64)–(1.67), and no old function is replaced by them.
        - {#208} At step j, (1.3) and (1.4) follow from the last line of (1.24), on Ω_{j−1}∖Ω_{j+1}, which carries no power of L₀ ("with c = 1", p.183 l.38).
        - {#209} χ_k^{(n)} follows from χ_k^{(n+1)} by (1.48)–(1.52). (1.48) needs δM/M₁ ≥ 2 and γ or A₁/A₀ small (p.186 l.35–42), and (1.50)–(1.52) need β₀, β ≤ 1/2, L₀ < L/2, and α = 1/8 (p.187).
        - {#210} χ_k″ follows from (1.89), under its own conditions (#199): β ≤ 1/4 (p.198 l.16), (1.94)'s two conditions on N (p.198 l.54–57), M large and α ≤ 1/8 (p.200 l.20–21), L₀² ≥ 3 (p.200 l.22–23, from L₀ ≥ 2, p.182 l.18), and O(1)B₃B₅M⁵L₀^{−2(N₀−1)} ≤ 1/4 (p.200 l.30). The condition L₀² ≤ L/3 (p.191 l.35) makes the spaces Ũ_k^{(n)c} descending; it is not a condition of (1.89).
        - {#211} So the old functions on those layers equal 1 on the final support.
      - {#212} *Conclusion.* At real external data, at every point of the R-step's integration support, for every class component and with the preliminary variables, U_k^{(0)} is a G-valued gauge transform of U_k(V) for some V ∈ S. The term's indicators (1.5)–(1.9) are not gauge invariant (LFR I p.179 l.2–3), so this places U_k^{(0)} on a gauge orbit through S, not in S. CRE's spaces "are invariant with respect to G-valued gauge transformations" (p.261 l.43). So the real-U part of (2.34) and (2.38) hold there, by the pre-R clause (map #MOMGD.11's; after an earlier R-step, D2.7 item 15.8 (e)'s O3, derived, not reviewed; #175, #175.1). D2.7 item 5(a) and the real parts of the evaluation points in (i)–(iv) and *A_k* rest on this.
    - {#213} With the bullets of (iii) and the readings of #17, (R-eval) holds: the deformations' current, the Landau representatives, the data's 𝐉, (2.36) about 0 at the evaluation points, and the curvature conditions are derived or quoted there, except for T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), with the conjugation factors of the covariant members at part (b)'s pairs, the outer fields' domain there, and the B-terms' outer-field argument at (P(𝐔), 𝐉) derived, not reviewed (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2, #6.17, #153). The bound B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, for the real base current in part (b)'s frame term, B_J the sup of (L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, is derived at #173 and reviewed (packet JB), at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause (derived, not reviewed, #130.12).
  - {#214} *Why Bałaban's U_k^0 itself does not do.* Without the composition with P_{k+1} (*The bounds*), the next T-step's points have a″ a general element of 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}, and U_k^0, with (𝐔, 𝐉) taken in the usual way, re-minimises the data off Λ as well. Its response is then of the order of a″'s full room, which T_Y does not reduce.
    - {#215} The re-minimisation's constant is multi-scale, and every multi-scale constant in the prints depends on d and L: PRT II p.242–248, PBG p.406, p.416, p.423–424, and p.432, and VAR Theorem 1's B₃ (p.279). Only single-scale constants are "dependent on d only": PRT I's (p.28–29), and the decay rate of PRT II's single-scale H_j′ (p.246 l.28–29).
    - {#216} On Λ, (1.41) and (1.45) carry the factor M⁵ with M = L^m (RG I p.257 l.3). So on the uniform sub-domain, a response constant C with C(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/2 is not available.
    - {#217} The Schwarz lemma on (1.65)'s own spaces does not reach the older layers. On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k their phase radius is L₀^{2(k−m)}α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (LFR I (1.66), p.190 l.40–48, at n = N), so the bound is (4/π)((1+β₀)/L)L₀^{2(k−m)}α_{1,k}L^{−k}. With L₀² ≤ L/3 (p.191 l.35) it is at most (4/π)(1+β₀)/3·α_{1,k}L^{−k} < ½α_{1,k}L^{−k} on layer k − 1, inside 𝒟^{sub}_k, but on layer k − 2 it can reach (4/π)(1+β₀)(L/9)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. On a layer n ≤ k₀ the radius is α_{1,n}L^{−n} (p.190 l.24–29), so the bound there is (4/π)((1+β₀)/L)α_{1,n}L^{−n}, outside 𝒟^{sub}_k.
    - {#218} The transport form removes the re-minimisation, and the dilated sub-domains take the M⁵ where Bałaban's spaces take it: on the old layers. The composition with P keeps a″ on 𝓜^c_{k+1}, so at a global background a re-minimisation enters only at s ≠ 1 (at a localized one, also at s = 1: (iii), *The bounds*, part (a)), through P or the localized P_Ω, where it is of the order of sup|𝐇| and T_Y absorbs it (*T_Y reduced by 4K*).

{#220} **(R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed (rounds P1–P10), given (R-eval) where it uses (R-eval)'s points, with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed: the cross insertions' weights at the R-steps' evaluation points.**
- {#221} The issue: the points Φ(𝐔) leave 𝒟^{sub}_k near the components ((R-eval)(i)), and the bound of an R-made cross insertion is a product over its slots and members ((6B), D2.7 15.6(f)), whose number is not bounded uniformly in k. So a growth factor per insertion at Φ(𝐔) does not bound it.
- {#222} How it holds: the cross clause (ii^cr) is stated with sup bounds on the dilated sub-domains, as the dev clause and D2.6's att bounds are (*The dilated sub-domains*). After a T-step into step k, and before the R-step at k, ‖Y_W‖ in w(W) is the sup over 𝒟^λ_k (at k = 1 over 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟_1, #227). After the R-step at k it is the sup over 𝒟^{ev}_k, the set of the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause: the part (b) pairs (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) ((iii), *The bounds*, part (b); #130.6) of the s = 1 points of data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*, the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}), and the further pairs listed in #222.1. The class's domain is M-factored ((iii), *The M-factored domain*), and 𝒟^{ev}_k ⊆ 𝒟^λ_k holds in that sense: each member is evaluated at its own pair of the point (#130.6), which lands by #130.13. The R-step's own evaluation points Φ(𝐔), 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^{ev}_k, lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*; #6.13, derived, not reviewed, with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed) up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), where the pre-R clause holds. Every constituent of a product bound is then bounded at the evaluation points by its own clause, and no factor per constituent arises. The clause is derived at the three places where it is made or used.
  {#222.1} The further pairs of 𝒟^{ev}_k: those of the s ≠ 1 points, whose P lies within 2K sup|𝐇| of the s = 1 point in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights ((iii), *At s ≠ 1*, #137; *T_Y reduced by 4K*), and, at a localized background, those of the re-projected points, compared with 𝐕 itself at every s (#130.3). These points enter only through their pairs (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉), at which the members are evaluated (#130.6). The pairs land in 𝒟^λ_k by #130.13, with #136's normalisation. At K′ there is no next T-step: there 𝒟^{ev}_{K′} is the set of the real points at which (8.2)'s final integration evaluates the clause (#923–#924). Φ maps a real point to a real point of 𝓜, which lies in 𝒟^λ_{K′} given D(k)(iii)'s pre-R base-point clause ((R-eval), *The real base points*).
  - {#223} *Production at the T-steps ((6)).* A cross(k+1) group passes SW §1(a1)'s test (ii): its components and the traversals of its hull lie in Ω_k. At step k+1 these are the layers Ω_{k+1}, where 𝒟^λ_{k+1} and 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} coincide (λ^{(k+1)}_{k+1} = 1), and Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, where the phase room is λ^{(k+1)}_k = min(λ, c′) ≤ c′ times 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}'s. ‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ S_𝒢, the product of the vertex sups and the transports (map #MD2.4.12.45).
    - {#224} The vertex sups on 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. A group with a parent that is not dev takes the first species of its parents, with cross(j) after wrap(j) (*The species of a new cross group*). So a cross(k+1) group's old members are dev, and its vertices are fluctuation activities and dev insertions. The fluctuation activities are Bałaban's, bounded on 𝒟♮_{k+1} ⊃ 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*; #6.12, derived, not reviewed). The dev insertions are bounded by the dev clause on the family (*The masses and weights*), at evaluation points that (b3)–(b4) keep in the family (*The dilated sub-domains*, the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}). Their complex-data size there is O(λρ_kpl_k), λ times their size on 𝒟^{sub} ((b2)'s size, *The dilated sub-domains*; map #MD2.4.11.5).
    - {#225} The transports: map #MD2.4.8.1's bound e^{c_πα_{1,k+1}n_{k+1}(𝒢)} ≤ e^{νσ_{k+1}(𝒢)/64} holds with c′α_{1,k+1} in place of α_{1,k+1} once c_πc′α_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64. Map #MD2.4.11.3's condition 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1, and map #MD2.4.11.5's a′-cost e^{(ν_r/16)σ_k(W)} per touched W, hold once c_πc′α_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16 (map (12)(d)'s condition with c′α_{1,k+1}). Both hold for g small, since c′ depends on β, L, and β₀ only, fixed before g.
    - {#226} (S-w)'s right side is 2C_winε^{new} + C_win(ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k})²/ε_* (map #MD2.4.12.44), with ε^{new} ≤ C_Mρ_{k+1}pl + K_β(b_k/ᾱ_k)ε^{old} (map #MD2.4.12.47). The old sums enter linearly or quadratically and are at most λ times their values on 𝒟^{sub}. So for λ ≥ 1 the right side is at most λ² times its value on 𝒟^{sub}, and the KP condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map #MD2.4.12.41) holds for g small given λ. So A^cr_{k+1} obeys (6)'s bound on 𝒟^λ_{k+1} with C_cr^λ := λ²C_cr in place of C_cr, a constant fixed after L and M.
    - {#227} The first step: (6)'s first-step bound is stated on 𝒟_1 = 𝒟^{sub}_1 (SW §3(b1), one layer). 𝒟^λ_1 lies in 𝒟♮_1, not in 𝒟_1 (#48, #6.12), so the step-1 clause is posed on 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1, which is 𝒟_1, since 𝒟_1 = 𝒟^{sub}_1 ⊆ 𝒟^λ_1 (#53). Its consumers evaluate it at the T-step into step 2 and in the carry's 5.1 step at n = 1. There the points of data in 𝒟^λ_2 lie in 𝒟_1 itself (#6.14, derived, not reviewed) and in 𝒟^{ev}_1 ⊆ 𝒟^λ_1 (#222), and no R-step acts at step 1, since R acts only at k > N_B > N₀ ≥ 1. So the clause covers them.
  - {#228} *The carry ((6), the cross carry lemma).* The lemma is stated on 𝒟^λ_{n+1}. Its 5.1 step: a cross insertion carries no deviation, and its bound is a sup bound on 𝒟^λ_n (at n = 1 on 𝒟_1, #227, with the points in 𝒟_1 by #6.14), or on 𝒟^{ev}_n after an R-step at n. The step-(n+1) evaluation points of data in 𝒟^λ_{n+1} are the points of 𝒟^{ev}_n ⊆ 𝒟^λ_n, in the M-factored sense (#222): at s = 1 by the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*), and the others by #222.1. So its norm does not grow there. Its 5.4 step: the transports do not grow, under D2.6 §5.4's condition on the family, 4c_πMλα_{1,n+1} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*), up to their conjugation factor at part (b)'s pairs, paid once (#6.8, #130.15.1, derived, not reviewed; #68). 5.5's reserve budget spends (ν_r/16)σ on the a′-transports of a touched or merged fixed member (map #MD2.4.11.5). A carried cross(j) insertion can lie on a layer where the room is λ times (b1)'s, so this needs c_πλα_{1,n+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16, a smallness of g given λ, under which the budget is unchanged. The rest of the proof uses only the exponents. The constant c̄ is unchanged: c̄_* = max(2K_β, C″) (D2.6 closure 5.0) comes from the sum rooted at D, linear in D's weight, and the group's other vertices enter it only through the KP condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map #MD2.4.12.41), which holds on the family for g small given λ.
  - {#229} *The R-steps ((6B), (6C)).* For 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^{ev}_k, Φ(𝐔) ∈ 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*: the points of 𝓜^c where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause, through their part (b) pairs (#222, #222.1); #6.13, with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed). So a carried cross insertion W has ‖W(Φ(𝐔))‖ ≤ ‖W‖, its pre-R sup on 𝒟^λ_k, with no growth factor. This is the ‖W‖ that 15.6(c)'s bound B(g) uses for each cross member (*15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas*), and the factor w_{k−1}(W) of *Second case*. B(g)'s other factors are (R-eval)'s. So an R-made D_g^× obeys B(g) at every point of 𝒟^{ev}_k, which is its post-R clause, the one the next T-step's carry and production use. Neither uses a point of 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k outside 𝒟^{ev}_k.
  - {#230} The same holds for the cross insertions in the blocks of second-class components (*15.8*, the third class). Their factor is the sup over the evaluation points of 𝐓′_k(Y_i), which are points Φ(𝐔) of the same form ((1.71) and 𝐓′_k(X) for X "any component of the first, or of the second class", p.378 l.27). (R-eval)'s landing holds there: Y_i lies in the new Ω_k ((1.33); *Where it lands*), so s_Z ≤ α_{1,k}L^{−k} on its component of Z, and Λ_i = Λ ∩ Y_i is at least N₀ layers old before R. This is also the radius of Bałaban's own extension, U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}) (p.366 l.25–27), and of his first-order bound O(1)B₃²A₁²p₁²(g_k)|𝐁₀|(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k}) for both classes (p.379 l.27–31). So the factor is at most ‖W‖ ≤ w_{k−1}(W).
- {#231} Constants added: c_πc′α_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64, c_πc′α_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16, and c_πλα_{1,n+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16 (smallness of g given λ), and C_cr^λ = λ²C_cr, fixed after L and M. λ is (*The R-step lands in the family*)'s dilation, not the rate factor λ_n of (6B).
- {#232} Where it enters: (R-eval)(i), *The masses and weights*; the carried cross total at the R-steps, and through it (8.2).

{#234} **(R-round), derived, not reviewed: the rounding of the carried members.**
- {#235} The issue: the members in a first-class component's slots are not bounded in number uniformly in k, since members made at a step j < k can have L^{k−j} disjoint intervals in one step-k bond (D2.6 closure 5.2 (T0) *Positions*). So no per-member constant can be paid by the margin of (1.89).
- {#236} How it holds: the rounding is counted per cube ((6B), *The units of the members*, *Big and small members*, *The rounding*).
  - {#237} A big member (σ(D) ≥ 40 when j = k, σ(D) ≥ 22 when j < k) has cubes, traversals, and diameter at most (21/20)σ(D) in π_k-units, with no constant, paid from its own weight. For j = k the diameter is that of T_D, which holds the π_k-cubes of the base Y′₀ over D's intervals.
  - {#238} A small member (the others: fewer than 40 cubes, fewer than 40M interval bonds, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M) whose T_D meets X⁺ for a component X of its polymer, every arc-slot one included, has T_D inside X^{(44)}, the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. All these small members of X are counted through #π_k(B_P) and the constant 4R_k + 92 of D_P, once per cube and per bond, whatever their number. Any other small member is joined to its polymer by a tree of length at least 2MR_k or a piece with at least 2R_k − 1 cubes outside its base, and half of that tree's or piece's term of D_P covers its count, with no constant ((6B), *Big and small members*). #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (105R_k)^d for R_k ≥ 88, and the margin pays it as it pays X⁺ (*The volume*).
  - {#239} (6B)'s *The payment*, *Joins*, *Join count*, and *First case*, and (6C)'s *The single loop's R-made groups*, its extent, and its re-run of D2.7 15.7 use only these bounds for the slot members 𝔅(g). The span members 𝒲_g of a single-loop group keep a constant 17 each, paid through their masses, not by the margin ((6C), *The members' weights*).

{#241} **(R-member), derived and reviewed given (R-eval) with its points #3.3 (rounds P1 to P1-4): the merge rule for shared cross members.**
- {#242} The issue: a carried cross insertion W can have intervals in the spans of several groups of one loop (D2.7 15.6's members 𝒲_g), on C₁ and on C₂ or on one loop. It is then a member of several groups, the groups' insertions are not separate factors of the word, and the (H2) set and the payment of the resulting insertion are not those of one group.
- {#243} Which sharings occur.
  - {#244} A single-loop insertion is a member of at most one group: its arc is one arc holding no slot bond (D2.7 15.6, the definition of 𝒲_g; map #MD2.4.4.3's single arc).
  - {#245} A cross insertion is a slot member of at most one polymer (*Second case*: the polymers of its slots overlap), hence of at most one group. A polymer with a cross slot member has no span (*The groups of 15.4*). The groups of (6B) with slots on both loops contract only their slots' bonds (*The arc set*), so they have no span members.
  - {#245.1} A slot member's intervals are bonds of its slot, also those outside every X⁺ (#617, #620.1). So a polymer whose span holds one of them is in the slot's group, and a slot member of a cross group h lies in the span of no other group.
  - {#246} So the only sharing is W ∈ 𝒲_g ∩ 𝒲_{g′} for single-loop groups g ≠ g′, of a carried cross insertion W in no slot. A component of the links below that contains a cross group h is h with its cross slot members, and its D_G is D_h^× (#669–#670).
- {#247} *The rule.* On a term of 15.6's expansion with marked set S, link g ∈ S and a carried cross insertion W when W ∈ 𝒲_g ∪ 𝔅(g). Each connected component of the links that contains a cross insertion, with its groups G ⊆ S and its cross insertions 𝒲^×(G), is one new insertion D_G: the contraction in loop order of the groups' insertions D_g (D2.7 15.6, 15.6(f)) and D_h^× ((6B)), with every interval of each W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) in its place. The intervals of the W that lie in no span of G and no slot of G stay open: they lie in the word at U^R, possibly in the span of an unmarked group, whose term c_{g′}Π_{P∈g′}F(P)·𝒲^{sf}_{span(g′)}(U^R; 𝒲_{g′}) carries them as ordered factors. A group of S linked to no cross insertion keeps its D_g or D_h^×. A cross insertion linked to no group of S is untouched. Each cross insertion lies in at most one D_G.
- {#248} *The identity.* D2.7 15.6's expansion is unchanged as an identity. Pointwise in the integration variables, both terms of a group's expansion are linear in each interval factor that lies in the group's span or slots: each member is an ordered factor on its interval in both terms (D2.7 15.6(f)), and a slot's insertion is a factor of the slot's word. So the identity holds for elementary tensors ⊗_iW_i of interval factors, and by linearity for W. On each term it is the product of the D_G, the other groups' insertions, and the word at U^R.
- {#249} *The bound.* B(g) bounds the integrand pointwise: 15.6(f)'s bound ‖D_g‖ ≤ B(g)·Π_{W∈𝒲_g}‖W‖ holds at every point of 15.6(d)'s domain, the 2r^{ext}_k-neighbourhood of the real support, with no Cauchy step, and so does (6B)'s ‖D_h^×‖ ≤ B(h)·Π_{W∈𝔅^×(h)}‖W‖, #680's bound with the cross members' norms, which it puts in their slots' bounds, taken out as the product, so that B(h) is 15.6(c)'s bound. Contraction of adjacent factors does not raise the projective norm on ⊗End(V_π), since ‖AB‖ ≤ ‖A‖‖B‖ on elementary tensors (map #MD2.2.6.126 for the unitary transports), and at complex data the transports are in B(g)'s e^{A_k}. So ‖D_G‖ ≤ Π_{g∈G}B(g)·Π_W‖W‖, over the members of all groups of G, each cross insertion once.
- {#250} *Species and lineage.* D_G is a cross insertion: it carries no deviation bound and is never contracted. It is in the lineage of its first cross insertion W₁ in D2.0(3)'s order and then position, with the rate λ_k(D_G) ((6B), *The rates*) and θ(G) := Σ_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}θ(W), as in *Second case*: in M^cr, or, when W₁ is a two-loop att insertion, in W₁'s register of D2.6 5.3 ((6C), *The members' weights*). When a group of G is att(k, ·) by 15.6(e), D_G stays in W₁'s lineage, and that group's polymer part is paid in (6C), as for the cross groups (*15.6(e)'s scope rule*). A member att(·, Z) or lw keeps its factor in its species' total ((6C), *The members' weights*). A D_G with a wrap member is wrap(k), and its mass enters (ii)'s wrap sums through its first wrap member ((6C), *Wrap members*).
- {#251} *The arc set and the units.* ℐ(D_G) := ∪_{g∈G}ℐ(g) ∪ ∪_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}ℐ(W), with ℐ(g) := span(g) for a single-loop group and the slots' bonds for a cross group. X_{D_G} is the union of the groups' counted domains and the cross insertions' X_W, each cross insertion once. Put
  {#252} σ(D_G) := Σ_{g∈G}π(g) + Σ_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}σ(W) + Σ_{D}c_Dσ(D) + Σ_{W′}(½σ(W′) + 17),
  {#253} with π(g) := Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P), D over the single-loop slot members, c_D = ⅓ in a single-loop group (σ̃) and ½ in a cross group (σ_g), and W′ over the single-loop span members.
  - {#254} The pieces of *The units of the members* and *The count* are counted once each. A cross insertion contributes #_k(X_W) + n_k(W)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(W) + 17 ≤ 18.05σ(W), and diam_∞(T_W) ≤ 18.05Mσ(W), since σ(W) ≥ 1. A single-loop group's parts are at most 16 times its part of σ(D_G) (*Units* in (6C)), and a cross group's at most 6 times ((6B), *The count*). So D_G has the units of (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor* with u = 19.
  - {#255} (H0) and (H1). (H0): D_G depends on the configuration through its groups' insertions and its cross insertions. Their (H0) puts their Cov domains in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_{D_G} ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D_G)), since the groups' counted domains and the X_W lie in X_{D_G}, and the groups' arcs ℐ(g) and the ℐ(W) lie in ℐ(D_G). (H1): S_{D_G} is the union of the groups' sets S_{D_g}, S_g, and the T_W. It is closed-connected, since each T_W is closed-connected and meets the span or slot of each group linked to W, through ℐ(W).
  - {#256} (H2): E_{D_G} := X_{D_G} ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D_G) ∪ H₂(D_G)) ∪ ∪_{W′}E_{W′}, with W′ over the span members made at step k. For a group h ∈ G, the π_k-cubes meeting each dom P, P ∈ h, lie in h's counted domain ⊆ X_{D_G} or meet ℐ(h) ⊆ ℐ(D_G), and form a closed-connected set holding P's slot bonds and its slot members' X_W and intervals ((6A)(i), *R-made insertions*, for a cross group; (6B), *The count*, D_g's (H2), for a single-loop one, with ℐ(h) = span(h)). Each W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) has T_W closed-connected, containing ℐ(W), and inside X_W ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ⊆ E_{D_G} ((6B), *The units of the members*). Each E_{W′} is closed-connected by W′'s (H2), contains X_{W′}, and meets a cube meeting ℐ(W′) ⊆ ℐ(D_G). A span member made at a step j < k has T_{W′} = X_{W′} ⊇ ℐ(W′) ((6B), *The units of the members*). Each H_i(D_G) is an arc containing ℐ_i(D_G), with the members' intervals outside the spans. So E_{D_G} is closed-connected.
  - {#257} The count of E_{D_G}: X_{D_G} has ≤ 19σ(D_G) cubes. |H_i(D_G)| ≤ 4diam(S_{D_G}) ≤ 76Mσ(D_G) (*Filled hulls*), so the arc cubes number ≤ 1216σ(D_G) + 160 (*Arc cubes*). E_{W′} has ≤ 65σ(W′) + 160 ≤ 225σ(W′) ≤ 450(½σ(W′) + 17) cubes. In all ≤ 1685σ(D_G) + 160. So D_G's domain one step later has at most 16 + 20480/L + 215680σ(D_G)/L cubes, and the share needs (219024ν_f + 1672ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4. This holds for L ≥ L₀^× = 10⁶, since (219024·17/16 + 209)/10⁶ ≤ 0.233.
  - {#258} (T0)'s condition at u = 19 is (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, a smallness of g under (R-m′). The transport condition holds since 1408·19 ≤ 4096L.
- {#259} *The weight and its payment.* D_G carries w_k(D_G) := e^{λ_k(D_G)ν_rσ(D_G)}e^{θ(G)}‖D_G‖, with D_G's shift of (6B), *The rates*.
  - {#260} The polymer parts π(g) are paid group by group: at the rate 2ν_r or (5/2)ν_r for a cross group ((6B), *The payment*; (6C)), and as in (6C), *The payment*, for a single-loop group. These payments are per polymer, and λ_k ≤ 1.
  - {#261} e^{|Ψ|}: the clusters incompatible with a group of G are rooted at that group's cubes and bonds, so |Ψ| ≤ Σ_{g∈G}|Ψ_g|. Its member part is over the slot members (*The payment*), and a cross insertion is a slot member of at most one group, so it pays its part once.
  - {#262} A single-loop slot member pays as in (6B), *The payment*, or (6C), *D's budget*.
  - {#263} A single-loop span member pays ½σ(W′) from its reserve, and its 17 through e^{17ν_r} in x_g or in its species' normalisation ((6C), *The members' weights*). The dev ones are summed in 15.7's factor e^{x_g} of their group, with x_g ≤ n_g, absorbed by a third of e^{−κ(size)} as in D2.7 15.7. So the dev members' masses are counted inside D_G, in M^cr, and not in (ii).
  - {#264} A cross insertion W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) enters with e^{λ_k(D_G)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖W‖ ≤ w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W)}, since λ_k(D_G) ≤ λ_k(W) = λ_{k−1}(W) − ε_k − ϑ_k. Of the released (ε_k + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W), ε_k/2 pays its member part of Ψ, ε_k/4 pays its join positions and its link positions (below), and ϑ_k/4 pays its parts' factor 1 + η(W) ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*; η's bound is derived through #3.3, not reviewed). That leaves at least ε_k/4.
- {#265} *The link sum.* A group linked to W contains a π_k-cube of X_W (a slot, *Second case*) or holds a bond of ℐ(W) in its span. So W has at most #_k(X_W) + n_k(W) ≤ 19Mσ(W) link positions (M ≥ 100).
  - {#266} At a bond b, the groups whose span holds b contain a polymer whose span holds b. *Joins* roots these polymers at b, at total ≤ C_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2}, and the rest of the group adds at most a factor 2 for small g_k, and B(g)'s constant C_B (*The rooted sum*). At a cube, the groups are rooted by *The rooted sum*, at total ≤ C_gre^{−p₀(g_k)/2}.
  - {#267} So, with C_lk := 2max(C_gr, C_BC_jn), the link factor of W is Π_{positions}(1 + C_lkε_k²) ≤ e^{19MC_lkε_k²σ(W)}. The join positions cost e^{(21/4)MC_jnε_k²σ(W)} (*Join count*). Together these are ≤ e^{(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} under 100MC_lkε_k ≤ ν_r, a smallness of g_k. The other cross insertions of G pay their own link factors, so the sum over the structures of G factorises over the cross insertions.
- {#268} *The lineage growth.* For W₁ the first cross insertion of G, at least one group is linked to it. So Π(1 + C_lkε_k²) − 1 ≤ 19MC_lkε_k²σ(W₁)e^{19MC_lkε_k²σ(W₁)}, and with the remaining e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W₁)} and σe^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ} ≤ 4/(eε_kν_r),
  {#269} Σ_{G: W₁ first}w_k(D_G) ≤ y^R_k·w_{k−1}(W₁), y^R_k := C_yε_k, C_y := 77MC_lk/(eν_r).
  - {#270} The other cross insertions of G enter with w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W), as in *Second case*. This C_y contains *Second case*'s 73C_gr/(eν_r), and it is the constant of (H^R).
- {#271} Constants added: u = 19 for the merged insertions, L₀^× = 10⁶, 100MC_lkε_k ≤ ν_r, and C_y = 77MC_lk/(eν_r).
- {#272} Where it enters: (6B)'s *The arc set*, *Second case*, and *15.8*; (6C)'s *The single loop's R-made groups*; *The units of the members*' cross members of single-loop groups; and the cross carry lemma at the R-steps.

{#274} **(R-att-ext), derived, not reviewed: the extent of an attached D_g.**
- {#275} The issue: D_g can be att(k, ·) through its box, a polymer, or a non-dev member. A member's own collar distance is measured from the collar of its production step (D2.6 closure 5.0), which an intervening R-step can remove. And the non-dev members' factor e^{17ν_r} in σ̃ is not in D2.7 15.7's member sum x_g.
- {#276} How it holds ((6C), *The single loop's R-made groups*, *The attached extent*, *The members' weights*):
  - {#277} The members enter through their domains: a dev member through ext(W), a non-dev one through diam_∞(T_W) ≤ (21/20)Mσ(W) or < 42M. Any two points of D_g's Y-set are within Δ_g, the hull's diameter plus 2R_k + 1 plus twice the largest member term.
  - {#278} When D_g's Y-set meets the post-R collar Coll_k (a polymer in a component, or the box near Λ_kᶜ ∪ S_k ∪ Ω_kᶜ or outside the new small-field region), D_g is att(k, ·) and ext^{coll}_k(D_g) ≤ Δ_g. Otherwise D_g continues the lineage of its first non-dev member W₁, as D2.6 closure 5.5 does for T-step merges, with ext^{coll}(D_g) ≤ ext^{coll}(W₁) + Δ_g and growth at most e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} at the R-step. A D_g with a wrap member is wrap(k).
  - {#279} A member enters σ̃ at half its σ, and the other half of its reserve pays its extent term. The non-dev members' e^{17ν_r} goes into D2.6 closure 5.0's species normalisation, as e^{c_W} does, once per insertion. It at most doubles A_j(Z) and ε_A, and the masses e^{2ΣA} stay polylogs of order r < p₀.

{#281} The cross groups of the R-gas whose polymers meet a second-class or unchanged component are paid in (6C), and so are the single loop's R-made groups, at the factor u = 16 when a slot holds a carried insertion, and the groups that share a cross member are merged by (R-member), at u = 19. Nothing else is needed beyond the derived and not reviewed (R-eval) with its points #3.3, (R-round), and (R-att-ext), (R-member) and (R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed given (R-eval), and the inherited inputs below.

{#283} The bound also uses these conditions on constants:
- {#284} the smallness conditions (H^cr) ((6)) and (H^R) ((6B)) of the couplings;
- {#285} (T0)'s smallness of g for the multi-interval insertions, (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8 ((6A)(i), (6C), (R-member) at u = 19), of the kind the single loop imposes under (R-m′);
- {#286} the smallness of g_k at the R-steps in (6B) and (6C), among them 182C_Ψ′Mε_k ≤ ν_r, R_k ≥ 17/ν_r, R_k ≥ 46 ((6B), *Big and small members*), R_k ≥ 88 ((6B), *The volume*), e3^d(1 + C_αe^{δMt_k})e^{−δMR_k/2^{d+5}} ≤ ½ and 4c_r⁺e^{−31δMR_k/512} ≤ ν_r ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*), C_δc̄S_R + (1 + C_s + C_attC_y)S^R ≤ log 2 and y^s_k ≤ 1/32 ((6C), *The members' weights*), and the polylogs of order r < p₀ and the cost e^{5p₀(g_k)/64} of the sets X^{(44)}, the small members' rounding included, that the margin of (1.89) pays ((6B), *The volume*);
- {#287} δM ≥ 5·2^{d+3}ν_r ((6B), (6C)), that is ν_r ≤ δ_α/(5·2^{d+3}) since δM = δ_α (#728.1), with δ_α = 1 (#728.5), a bound on the map's rate (ν_r = ν, map #MD2.4.12.21) implied by its ν ≤ 1/1000 (#MD2.4.13.19) at d = 4, and M ≥ 100 ((6B), *The units of the members*) and M ≥ δ_α·max(32/δ₀, 1/δ_𝐇) (#728.1), lower bounds on M of the kind the map already imposes (#MD2.2.6.187);
- {#288} L ≥ L₀^× := 10⁶ ((6A)(i), (6C), (R-member)), a lower bound on L of the kind of the map's L₀ = 5000 (#MD2.4.12.23).

{#290} The conditions 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ 1 and (5/2)ν_r ≤ ¼ of (6B) and (6C) hold by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (#MD2.4.13.19), with ν_r = ν and ν_e = ν/(2C_box) ≤ ν/8 (#MD2.4.12.6, #MD2.4.12.21). The span bound of (6B) uses a torus period of at least twice each side, N_s ≥ 2max(ℓ₁, ℓ₂), as V4's tori have (L1565).

{#292} *Inherited, not new:* the inputs of the single-loop (6.3). These are:

- {#299} the evaluation at U^R of the insertions that R leaves unchanged (D2.7 15.8, as for one loop);
- {#300} the bounds |μ^R_n|, |ζ^R_n| ≤ C_R𝔞_n on the coefficients of the R-steps (map #MD2.3.5.5, D2.7 note 15.4), which the continuum note's (6.1) needs. They control κ, which (8.2) divides out.

{#302} The continuum note's (2), (5.2), and (6.3) bound E_const by C_Σ𝔞̄ alone, which omits the R-steps' remainders E°^R_k(C) of D2.7 15.4(ii). (7) adds them for the pair as C^{R°}_E, and the single-loop bound needs the same term. That note is not changed here.

## {#304} What the Objects Are

- {#306} The pair observable W_{χ₁}(C₁;U)W_{χ₂}(C₂;U) is the normalised trace of π₁(Hol_UC₁) ⊗ π₂(Hol_UC₂) on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} (V4 eq:two-loop-observable). It is a product of two closed words, not the trace of one holonomy.
