- {#307} The stopping step is common to both loops: K′ = K − j, with j the least j ≥ max(q₁, q₂, q₁₂) + q̄(j) (map #MOMGD.30; that j exists and does not depend on ε, #MD2.3.4.15(i)).
  - {#308} So the corners of both loops are period points, and every step is commensurate for both.
  - {#309} At K′, C_i is a fixed contour C_i^{(K′)} with side ℓ_i = p_iL^{j−q_i} bonds.
  - {#310} D ≥ 1 is the ℓ^∞ distance between C₁ and C₂ in step-K′ units.
  - {#311} In step-k units the sides are ℓ_iL^{K′−k}, and every traversal of C_i lies at distance ≥ DL^{K′−k} from C_{3−i}.

## {#313} What That Forces

- {#315} **The normalisation factorises exactly.** The coefficients δm_{k+1} and ζ_{k+1} are ℤ⁴ quantities of a straight line and of a bent path, for one character (D2.3(5) *Independence*). Perimeters add, P_k(C₁ ⊔ C₂) = P_k(C₁) + P_k(C₂), and the pair has eight corners. So the pair's κ^{(K′)} is κ^{(K′)}_{C₁,χ₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂,χ₂}, with no cross factor ((5)). The cross constant E^× of (5) is the only cross term that the step map extracts. The loops stay coupled through the clusters of Q_k, which may hit insertions on both loops, through the cross insertions of (6), and through the common measure.
- {#316} **A cross group has no scalar flat value.** A group with arcs on both loops has a flat value in End(V_{π₁}) ⊗ End(V_{π₂}) (more tensor factors when its arc set on one loop has several intervals). This value commutes with π₁(u) ⊗ π₂(u) for u ∈ G, but it is not a scalar in general. So D2.3(3)'s subtraction c_𝒢T is not available for it. Cross groups get the flat value 0, as in V4 (a_W := 0, V4 L3590), and they are not contracted.
- {#317} **Cross groups are made with a doubly exponential decay in the distance to the stopping step.**
  - {#318} A cross group made at step k spans at least DL^{K′−k} step-k units.
  - {#319} The extent weight of (S-w) has the same rate at every step.
  - {#320} So the cross mass made at step k is ≤ C𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−ν_×DL^{K′−k}/M}, on every labelled term.
- {#321} **So the cross groups are carried, not taken out.**
  - {#322} They form their own species cross(j), last in D2.0(3)'s order. They are never contracted, so no step needs their flat value.
  - {#323} The D2.6 closure's carry lemma carries them without a bridge. Every later step can touch them, since the loops lie in the small-field region on most terms. So each step multiplies their mass by at most 1 + C_δc̄ ((6)).
  - {#324} The doubly exponential decay pays this geometric growth: the carried cross total A^× of (7) is uniform in K.
  - {#325} So the class carries everything to the stopping step, and the large-field terms are paid there, by B^×_LF, as for one loop. No step below K′ needs a large-field bound for the cross species. The attached species are carried by D2.6 as for one loop.
  - {#326} Taking the cross words out at the step that makes them doesn't work: on the large-field terms that step has no bound uniform in K.

## {#328} Derivation

### {#330} (1) Setting (Quoted from V4 Section 10 and the Map, with Derived Bounds)

- {#332} C₁, C₂ are coarse translates of counterclockwise squares, each in a plane parallel to a coordinate plane of its own (the planes may differ; #332.1), with V(C₁) ∩ V(C₂) = ∅. χ_i = dim(π_i)^{−1}tr π_i, with π_i irreducible and unitary; the reversed orientation is χ̄_i (V4 L3556). C := C₁ ⊔ C₂. Its step-k traversals are those of C₁ and of C₂, 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k} in all. They are ordered as those of C₁, then those of C₂, each loop cyclically from its own base point ((6A)(iv)).
  - {#332.1} *The planes.* V4 Section 10 states the pair in the plane of e₁, e₂, and it is read here with each square in its own plane. The labelled terms, their carriers, and their weights contain no observable (V4 L3727; #386), so both squares share them whatever their planes. A square enters only through its own bonds: slots, contact sets, hulls, and V4's square/torus geometry. These are the same for sides along e_μ, e_ν as along e₁, e₂, since V4's period conditions hold for every period (V4 L1565). The cross arguments use only distances between V(C₁) and V(C₂) (#333–#335), q₁₂ (a displacement in L^{−q}uℤ⁴, map #MD2.3.4.15-11), and #749's per-loop torus distances, none of which uses a common plane.
- {#333} *The cross distance.* For a step-k traversal n of C_i, δ_n^{(k)} is the step-k ℓ^∞ distance on the torus (periodic, as at V4 L3560) from n to C_{3−i}.
  - {#334} Both squares are unions of step-K′ bonds with corners on the step-K′ lattice (the q₁₂ clause of the stopping rule). Two lattice bonds without a common site are at ℓ^∞ distance ≥ 1. So D ≥ 1, and δ_n^{(k)} ≥ DL^{K′−k}. On the torus of period N_s step-K′ units, D ≤ N_s/2.
  - {#335} Put Δ_×^{(k)} := Σ_ne^{−ν_×δ_n^{(k)}/M} over the step-k traversals of C, with ν_× of the constants below. At the first step V4's Δ_× (eq:two-loop-cross-sum) has the same form with the rate c_×/(LM) per fine unit, c_× = 2η/A′_E ((6)).
  - {#336} Then Δ_×^{(k)} ≤ 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−ν_×DL^{K′−k}/M}.
- {#337} *Constants.*
  - {#338} c_d = ν/(64ML) and C_S′ are those of the tail bound (T′) (map D2.3(5)).
  - {#339} ν_e = ν/(2C_box) is the extent rate of (S-w) (D2.4(12)(b)).
  - {#340} C_ζ is that of D2.3(5).
  - {#341} 𝔞̄ := max(τ̄², sup_kρ_k·pl_k, sup_kρ_{k+1}·pl_k), with pl_k := 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊} and R-m's exponents q₀, q₁ (not the loops' q₁, q₂). The first two entries are the amplitude of D2.3(7); the third is (S-w)'s amplitude at step k+1, which carries pl_k (map #MD2.4.12.11).
  - {#342} 𝔟̄ := max(𝔞̄, sup_kmax(ρ̂_k, b_k)·pl_k) is the amplitude of the wrap groups (map #MD2.0.3.34: max(C_S′ρ̂_k, C_S″b_k)), with b_k the collar amplitude of the D2.6 closure §2.2.
  - {#343} ν_× := min(ν_e, 2η/A′_E).
  - {#344} C_Σ(χ) is D2.3(7)'s constant for the character χ.
  - {#345} C_ν is the constant of ρ̃_kδ*_n = O(ρ_k) on the support, under R-m's entailments (map #MD2.0.3.30), with C_χ := max(C_{χ₁}, C_{χ₂}) inside it.
  - {#346} c̄ := e^{(3/8)ν_e}c̄_*sup_mb_m/ᾱ_m and C_δ := 2 are the D2.6 closure's (5.0), and c_W is map (12)(d)'s share constant (D2.6 closure 5.5), with c_sh^× of (6A)(i) in place of c_sh for every insertion (c_sh^× ≥ c_sh, and the insertions with several intervals and the touched ones of a two-loop M-vertex need it).
    - {#347} c̄_* contains C″ = 2(K_an+1)C_be^{(5/8)ν_e}/ε_* (D2.6 closure 5.5). K_an is the animal-sum constant of the closure's (T0) (5.2: ε^{new}_{re-run} ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + K_anb_n/ᾱ_n), written K′ there; it is not the stopping step K′. So c̄ does not depend on K.
  - {#348} r₀ := 2 is the range, in step-k units, within which a constituent carries a slot or a contact of a contour bond.
    - {#349} At step 1 the contact sets lie within 2L fine units, that is two step-1 units, of the domain (map #MOMGD.14, V4 L3696).
    - {#350} At k ≥ 1 the new slots of a component Y are the bonds of C in Y (map #MD2.1.4.10), and the old contact sets lie in the network (D2.3(4), map #MD2.3.4.5–#MD2.3.4.6), within 2L^{1−k} ≤ 2 step-k units of the step-1 domains.
    - {#351} r₀ enters only through e^{c_dr₀}.
- {#352} *Norm of a cross group.* Y_𝒢 is a sum of terms, each an ordered product of slot matrices, old insertions, and transports along each interval of ℐ(𝒢). ‖Y_𝒢‖ is the sum over these terms of the product of the factors' operator norms, a projective norm.
  - {#353} The rooted sums of D2.4(12) are sums of exactly this form ((6A)(i)).
  - {#354} A word containing Y_𝒢 is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of products of operators. So at real backgrounds its modulus is at most the product of the insertions' norms (V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-source, first sentence of the proof). At complex backgrounds the transports cost exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length) (map #MD2.2.6.126), as for one loop.

### {#356} (2) The Two-Loop Class (Definition)

{#358} For k ≥ 0, the pair's dressed loop of step k on a labelled term is

{#360} 𝒲^×_k = κ^{(k)}_{C₁}κ^{(k)}_{C₂}e^{𝓔^×_k}[f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)·w(𝔅)].

- {#362} f^{(i)}_k := dim(π_i)^{−1}tr π_i(Hol_{C_i}(U_k)).
- {#363} 𝔅 is a family of insertions. Each has arcs on one loop only (a C_i-insertion), or on both (a cross insertion, of species cross(j), or two-loop of a single-loop species by (6)'s rules).
- {#364} w(𝔅) is the normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of the two-contour word with the insertions of 𝔅 (D2.0(3)'s display, along each loop). Without cross insertions it is w₁(𝔅₁)w₂(𝔅₂), the product of the single-loop words, and w(∅) = f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k.
- {#365} Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), over the clusters of the pair's step-k reference gas ((5)).
- {#366} κ^{(k)}_{C_i} := ∏_{j≤k}e^{−δm^{χ_i}_jP_{j−1}(C_i)}(z^{χ_i}_j)⁴, with the single-loop coefficients of χ_i (D2.0(3), D2.3(5)).
- {#367} 𝓔^×_k is the sum of the local terms and the constant E^×_const,k := E_const,k(C₁,χ₁) + E_const,k(C₂,χ₂) + Σ_{j≤k}E^×_j + Σ_{N_B<j≤k}E^{R×}_j. Here E_const,k(C_i,χ_i) is the single-loop constant of C_i, with the R-steps' remainders E°^R_j(C_i) of D2.7 15.4 in it, E^×_j is the cross constant of (5), and E^{R×}_j is that of the R-steps ((6B)).
- {#368} *The other clauses.* The norms (ii) and (ii′), analyticity (iii), the constants (iv), the scalar parts (v), and torus consistency (vi) are D(k)'s clauses (map #MD2.0.3.16–#MD2.0.3.65) with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, per traversal of either loop, under the reading (6A).
- {#369} *The species.* D(k)'s species (map #MD2.0.3.21–#MD2.0.3.26) and one more, cross(j) for j ≤ k. Its insertions are of three kinds.
  - {#370} The cross groups made by the step map at step j that are not attached or wrap by the D2.6 closure §2.1's rule ((6)). These pass §2.1's tests (i)–(iv), so they lie in (S-w)'s scope.
  - {#371} The cross groups of the R-gas made by the R-operation at step j > N_B that 15.6(e)'s scope rule does not make att(j, ·), with no cross member ((6B), first case). Their units are (6B)'s count.
  - {#372} The descendants of both ((6), and (6B)'s second case). In D2.0(3)'s order (map #MD2.0.3.26) cross(j) comes after wrap(j). A cross insertion carries no deviation bound and is never contracted.
- {#373} *(ii^cr), the cross clause.* For each j ≤ k the cross(j) insertions present at step k satisfy M^cr_k(j) := Σe^{c_W}w(W) ≤ Γ_{j,k}((1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j), with Γ_{j,k} := Π_{j<k′≤k}(1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_{k′}).
  - {#374} w(W) := e^{λ_k(W)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ is the D2.6 closure 5.0's weight without its lineage extent, at the rate λ_k(W)ν_r of (6B), with σ(W) fixed at production. ‖Y_W‖ is the sup over the M-factored domain on 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k before the R-step at k (at k = 1 on 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟_1, #227), and over 𝒟^{ev}_k after it, with λ the dilation of *The R-step lands in the family* ((iii), *The M-factored domain*; (R-eval-cr)).
  - {#375} Each insertion is counted once, at the first traversal of its arc set. A^cr_j is the production bound of (6); y^R_j and A^R_j are (6B)'s at the R-steps and 0 at the other steps, so that Γ_{j,k} = (1 + C_δc̄)^{k−j} when no R-step lies in (j, k].
- {#376} *The base.* At k = 0 there are no insertions and κ^{(0)} := 1, so N^×_0 := W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)ρ₀.
- {#377} *D^×(1).* This is IT-1's step-1 class built on V4 Section 10's objects for the pair:
  - {#378} the groups of Definition def:two-loop-groups;
  - {#379} the reference scalars a_W, with a_W := 0 for cross groups;
  - {#380} the retreated carrier D(Λ) of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, with its cross exponent E^×_Λ (part (a)).
  {#381} V4's cross families W are the species cross(1). Their factors are bounded by M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{𝔞_W/8}, as in the proof of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(c) (L3709, by Lemma lem:two-loop-defect). D(1)'s weights read them as IT-1 reads V4's single-loop factors (map #MD2.4.5.10, an inherited reading). The production bound is (6)'s *first step*.
- {#382} *The identity (derived in (6)).* With N^×_k := Σ_ℓχT[𝒲^×_ke^{A_k}] the class's numerator density (map #MD2.0.3.66), Z·E_ε[W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)] = ∫N^×_k dV_k for every k ≤ K′.

### {#384} (3) The Step Map for the Pair, Ingredient by Ingredient

- {#386} *D2.0(1)–(2), units and background regularity:* D2.0(1)'s contour bullet (C is a union of block bonds at every scale, map #MD2.0.1.6) holds for C₁ ⊔ C₂ by the stopping rule with q₁₂. The rest does not involve the contour. Verbatim.
- {#387} *D2.1, the leading word (derived by the product rule):*
  - {#388} f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k = (f^{(1)}_{k+1} + s^{(1)})(f^{(2)}_{k+1} + s^{(2)}), with s^{(i)} := f^{(i)}_k − f^{(i)}_{k+1} the D2.1 slot expansion of C_i.
  - {#389} Expanded, this is the product of the two single-loop expansions: f^{(1)}_{k+1}f^{(2)}_{k+1}, words with slots on one loop, and words with slots on both. All are products of two single-loop words.
  - {#390} So the slots are C_i-insertions, and the words keep the two-trace form. At real fields the leading words have modulus ≤ 1; at complex fields D(k)(iii)'s bounds apply to each loop.
- {#391} *D2.2, the small-field map:* the observable enters only through the slots and contact sets of C₁ ⊔ C₂. The domains, probe measures, vacuum clusters, and reference data do not involve it, as at V4 L3566. Derived.
- {#392} *D2.2(6)(d), the groups (definition):*
  - {#393} The merging rules are applied with hulls and long-ness per loop, as V4 Definition def:two-loop-groups does at step 1.
  - {#394} The arc set is ℐ(𝒢) = ℐ₁(𝒢) ⊔ ℐ₂(𝒢). The hull on C_i is taken in the cyclic set of C_i's traversals. At k ≥ 1 the threshold P_k(C_i)/2 only names the shorter-arc convention: a gap arc is the shorter of the two arcs (map #MD2.2.6.119), and no hull is replaced by all of C_i. V4's replacement of a long hull by I_{C_i} (rule (A2), V4 L3571) is used at step 1 only, where V4 bounds the long groups by their total mass (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(c)–(d)).
  - {#395} The linked hull (map #MD2.2.6.119, "the shorter of the two arcs of C between them") is taken per loop. Joined pieces on different loops add no gap arc, and the group is then a cross group. When a joined pair has an insertion D with several pieces on C_i, its gap arc is defined as the shorter arc between the other's piece and the nearest piece of ℐ_i(D), since the shorter arc to another piece can run around the far side of C_i. Each loop's traversals are numbered cyclically from its own base point (V4 L3560).
  - {#396} A C_i-group has ℐ_{3−i}(𝒢) = ∅. A cross group has both parts nonempty. A group with a cross member is a cross group, since its arc set contains the member's.
- {#397} *D2.3(2), the flat value:*
  - {#398} For a C_i-group, Schur's lemma for π_i (as V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-schur(a)). Derived.
  - {#399} For a cross group, c_𝒢 := 0. Definition.
- {#400} *D2.3(3), the factorisation:* (6). Derived.
- {#401} *D2.3(4):*
  - {#402} Contour locality: (4), derived by D2.3(4)'s induction for objects with short arcs. Long groups are paid by (T′) in (5), as the map pays whole-contour groups (map #MD2.3.4.8).
  - {#403} Periodicity: the corners of both loops are period points under the stopping rule (map #MOMGD.30). Verbatim.
- {#404} *D2.3(5), the coefficients:* δm^{χ_i}_{k+1} and ζ^{χ_i}_{k+1}, one pair for each character. Verbatim.
- {#405} *D2.3(6)–(8), the remainder, the sum over the steps, and the assembly:* (5) and (7). Derived.
- {#406} *D2.4, the contraction count and (S-w):*
  - {#407} These are per position. A position meets the traversals of two contours, so the per-position counts at most double. The multi-interval arc sets and the long groups are (6A)(i)–(ii), with V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(a)–(d) at step 1.
  - {#408} The dev contraction is used for C_i-groups only. Cross groups need no deviation factor, since they are carried uncontracted ((6)).
- {#409} *D2.5, D2.6, and D2.7:* (6A) and (6B).

### {#411} (4) Locality for the Pair (Derived by the Induction of D2.3(4), for Short Arcs)

{#413} (L×) Take any step k and a non-cross object of the pair on C_i: an insertion, a group with its occurrences, a cluster of the reference gas, or its weight, all of whose groups have short arcs on C_i (fewer than P_k(C_i)/2 traversals). Suppose its network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
- {#414} Then it is the object of the single-loop class of C_i with the same constituents, and it has the same value.
- {#415} Conversely, a single-loop object of C_i whose network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i} is a non-cross object of the pair.
- {#416} The network of a group contains the domains of its old members and of the members of its merged occurrences (map #MD2.3.5.3: the supports, the arc hulls, and the merged occurrences' domains). Inductively, it therefore contains the domains of the group's whole lineage.

{#418} *Step 1.*
- {#419} V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(c): the C_i-groups of the pair are the single-square groups of C_i none of whose roots has a contact on C_{3−i}. They have the same interval, weight, footprint, and compatibility.
- {#420} a_W = a^{(i)}_W (V4 L3590).
- {#421} The paragraph *Cancellation* in the proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-cross: tuples of C_i-groups have the same Ursell coefficient and the same activities.
- {#422} A constituent with a contact on C_{3−i} lies within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
- {#423} At the retreated carrier D(Λ) these transfer: by the proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, part (i), the grouping lemma does not involve the carrier (V4 L3737).

{#425} *Step k → k+1.*
- {#426} A step-(k+1) object is built from its constituents by the composition, the joint cluster expansion, the merging rules, the flat value, and the hard core.
- {#427} For short arcs these use the contour only through the slots, arcs, and contact sets inside the object's network (D2.3(4) *Contour locality*, map #MD2.3.4.5; its proof is per object). A group with a short arc has only members with short arcs, since its arc contains theirs.
- {#428} The constituents are step-k objects whose networks lie inside the new network, so the hypothesis applies to them. The vacuum activities and occurrences do not involve the contour.
- {#429} Consider an old occurrence with arcs on both loops, that is, a cluster containing a C₁-group and a C₂-group. It puts arcs on both loops into the group it merges into, since the third merging rule takes the hull over J_Γ. That group is then a cross group.
- {#430} So no non-cross object contains such an occurrence or a cross insertion. Cross groups have activity 0 in the reference gas; in the words they are the cross insertions of (6).
- {#431} Groups long on C_i are not covered. D2.3(4)'s contour locality is for short arcs (map #MD2.3.4.5, #MD2.3.4.8), and the long-ness and the membership of a long group are properties of the whole term. The step-(k+1) analogue of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(a) and (c) for long groups is not derived. (5) pays the clusters with long groups by (T′) instead.

### {#433} (5) The Factorisation of the Reference Gas (Derived)

{#435} Work on ℓ₀ at the map k → k+1, k ≥ 1. The first step is V4's (*The first step* below).
- {#436} S°(ℓ₀) is the pair's reference group gas: C₁- and C₂-groups with their activities c_𝒢, cross activities 0, and D2.3(3)'s hard core.
- {#437} S°_{C_i}(ℓ₀) is the single-loop gas of C_i.

{#439} Put E^×_{k+1} := log S°(ℓ₀) − log S°_{C₁}(ℓ₀) − log S°_{C₂}(ℓ₀).

- {#441} *Allocation.* In each gas put a(b) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°(Γ)/|J_Γ| (D2.3(6)(a)).
  - {#442} Then log S° = Σ_ba(b) over the step-k traversals of C, and log S°_{C_i} = Σ_{b∈C_i}a_{C_i}(b).
  - {#443} So E^×_{k+1} = Σ_iΣ_{b∈C_i}(a(b) − a_{C_i}(b)). The rearrangement uses absolute convergence, from the pair gas's Kotecký–Preiss condition ((6A)(v)).
- {#444} *Cancellation.* Let b ∈ C_i, and let Γ ∋ b be a cluster of either gas whose network has diameter < δ_b^{(k)} − r₀ and which contains no group long on C_i.
  - {#445} Its network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
  - {#446} By (L×) it is a cluster of both gases, with the same weight and the same J_Γ; the Ursell function depends only on the incompatibility graph, which is the same in both.
  - {#447} So a(b) − a_{C_i}(b) contains only clusters of network diameter ≥ δ_b^{(k)} − r₀ and clusters with a group long on C_i.
  - {#448} The latter have network diameter ≥ ℓ_iL^{K′−k} = P_k(C_i)/4: the arc of a long group has at least 2ℓ_iL^{K′−k} consecutive traversals of C_i, so it contains a whole side, as for the whole-contour groups of map #MD2.3.4.8.
- {#449} *Bound.* By (T′) for each gas (for the pair gas by (6A)(v)), |a(b) − a_{C_i}(b)| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}(e^{−c_d(δ_b^{(k)} − r₀)} + e^{−c_dℓ_iL^{K′−k}}). Hence
  {#450} |E^×_{k+1}| ≤ 8C_S′e^{c_dr₀}𝔞_{k+1}(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−c_dDL^{K′−k}} + 8C_S′𝔞_{k+1}Σ_iℓ_iL^{K′−k}e^{−c_dℓ_iL^{K′−k}}.
  {#451} The second term does not decay in D. It would, given the long-group analogue of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(a) and (c) at the steps k ≥ 1; the bound of (8.2) does not need it.
- {#452} *The first step.* E^×_1 is V4's cross exponent at the retreated carrier, E^×_Λ of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated(a), with the bound of Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(b): |E^×_1| ≤ C_×ρ_VΔ_× ≤ 4C_×ρ_V(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′}e^{−c_×DL^{K′−1}/M}. There are 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′} fine traversals, each with δ_a ≥ DL^{K′} fine units, and V4's ρ_V (at g₀) is ≤ C_V(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ (map #MD2.0.3.38), with C_V depending on χ₁ and χ₂ through V4's constants (V4 L3614).
- {#453} *Assembly.*
  - {#454} By D2.3(8) for C_i alone, log S°_{C_i}(ℓ₀) = −δm^{χ_i}_{k+1}P_k(C_i) + 4ζ^{χ_i}_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}(C_i).
  - {#455} So log S°(ℓ₀) = −Σ_iδm^{χ_i}_{k+1}P_k(C_i) + 4Σ_iζ^{χ_i}_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}(C₁) + E°_{k+1}(C₂) + E^×_{k+1}.
  - {#456} Then D2.3(8)'s assembly holds for the pair, with κ_{k+1} := κ_{C₁,k+1}κ_{C₂,k+1} and E°_{k+1} := E°_{k+1}(C₁) + E°_{k+1}(C₂) + E^×_{k+1}.
  - {#457} E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) := log S°(ℓ) − log S°(ℓ₀) is D2.3(8)'s, for the pair gas.

### {#459} (6) The Cross Groups: Production and Carry (Derived Given the Inherited Inputs)

- {#461} *The step.* Apply the map k → k+1 to N^×_k. After the interactions are expanded (D2.3(3)), the family sum runs over the compatible families of step-(k+1) groups of the pair.
  - {#462} D2.3(3)'s multilinear expansion Y_𝒢 = c_𝒢T + Y^d_𝒢 and its resummation apply as for one loop. Each factor of the two-trace word is expanded along its own loop, as in V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-schur(b)–(c) at step 1.
  - {#463} For a cross group c_𝒢 = 0, so Y^d_𝒢 = Y_𝒢: the group enters the new word as an uncontracted insertion.
  - {#464} The result is 𝒲^×_{k+1} of (2), with insertions of every species, Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ), and the factor κ_{k+1} of (5).
  - {#465} Nothing is split off. So the numerator identity (map #MD2.0.3.66) gives the identity of (2) by induction on k. At the R-steps the R-operation preserves the integral (D2.7 item 3, for the pair by (6B)).
- {#466} *The species of a new cross group.*
  - {#467} The D2.6 closure §2.1's rule, which amends map #MD2.0.3.22's (its *Rule changes*), applies with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, as for any group. It makes the group att(k+1, Z) if only test (iv) fails (§2.1(a)), att(k+1, coll) if (ii) fails or (i) or (iii) fails (§2.1(b)–(c)), and wrap(k+1) if its set does not lift to the cover (§2.1(e)). These species carry no deviation bound, so they need no flat value. They are carried and paid as for one loop ((6A), D2.6).
  - {#468} A cross group made from parents that are not all dev takes the first species of its parents in D2.0(3)'s order (§2.1 *Merged groups*, map #MD2.0.3.26), with cross(j) after wrap(j).
  - {#469} The other new cross groups are cross(k+1). They pass §2.1's tests (i)–(iv) and lift, so they lie in (S-w)'s scope, the groups passing (i)–(iii) (SW §1(a1)).
  - {#470} Map #MD2.0.3.21's reclassification is a rule for dev insertions, so a cross insertion keeps its species.
  - {#471} At an R-step these rules are replaced by (6B)'s. The single-loop members' lineages end in the slots (D2.7 15.8(b)). A cross group of the R-gas with a cross member is a descendant of its first cross member. One without is att(k, ·) when a polymer of it meets a second-class or unchanged component (15.6(e)), and cross(k) otherwise.
- {#472} *Production.* Let 𝒢 be a cross(k+1) group, and m := K′ − k − 1.
  - {#473} (S-w) applies to 𝒢 on every labelled term (D2.4(12)(b), for the groups satisfying (i)–(iii), the scope that the D2.6 closure 5.0 states).
  - {#474} 𝒢 has a traversal n ∈ ℐ_i(𝒢) and a traversal of C_{3−i} in ℐ(𝒢) ⊆ X(𝒢). So its extent from n is ext_{k+1}(𝒢) ≥ δ_n^{(k+1)}/M, since π_{k+1}-cubes have side M step-(k+1) units.
  - {#475} Pin 𝒢 at the first traversal of its arc set and use (S-w) at the positions n. Here ‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ S_𝒢, with S_𝒢 read as the projective majorant of (1) (map #MD2.4.12.45: S_𝒢 is at most the product of the vertex sups and the transports), ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≥ 1, and the whole extent weight is spent on δ_n. (S-w)'s amplitude is ρ_{k+1}pl_k, with pl_k := 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}. Since ν_× ≤ ν_e and ρ_{k+1}pl_k ≤ 𝔞̄ ((1)),
    {#476} A^cr_{k+1} := Σ_{𝒢∈cross(k+1)}e^{c_W}w(𝒢) ≤ e^{c_W}C_S′ρ_{k+1}pl_kΔ_×^{(k+1)} ≤ 4C_cr𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^me^{−ν_×DL^m/M}.
  - {#477} *The first step.* The cross(1) insertions are V4's cross groups at the retreated carrier D(Λ), with ‖Y_W‖ ≤ M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{𝔞_W/8} ((2)) and 𝔞_W = η𝔰(W) (V4 L5249). At step 1, σ = 𝔰, ν_r = ν = η, λ₁ = 1, and e^{θ(W)} ≤ e^{η𝔰/16} (map #MD2.4.12.6, V3 L2806). So e^{c_W}w(W) ≤ e^{c_W}e^{(19/16)η𝔰(W)}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W).
    - {#478} The distance. V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(a) gives 𝔰(W) > δ_a/(A′_ELM) for every a ∈ ℋ(W) (its proof, L3677: the envelope is connected, has at most A′_E𝔰(W) cubes, and meets both loops). So e^{−2η𝔰(W)} ≤ e^{−c_×δ_a/(LM)}, with c_× = 2η/A′_E (L3658).
    - {#479} The pins. The pins (c4-6-5) hold for the groups of the pair (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(d), L3644), and at D(Λ) for M^{(ρ)}_Λ with the same constants (proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, (v)–(vi), L3737). The pin per fine traversal a carries e^{4η𝔰}.
    - {#480} Pin each cross group at a traversal of its hull, and spend 2η𝔰 on the distance and (19/16)η𝔰 ≤ 2η𝔰 on the reserve, θ, and e^{𝔞_W/8}. At complex backgrounds the group bounds carry e^{C′𝔰ρ₁} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8} more (map #MD2.4.12.4), and (21/16)η𝔰 ≤ 2η𝔰 still. So
      {#481} A^cr_1 ≤ e^{c_W}Σ_{a∈ℐ_C}e^{−c_×δ_a/(LM)}Σ_{W∋a}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} ≤ e^{c_W}C_pinρ_VΔ_×,
      {#482} with C_pin the pins' constant (C_pin ≤ C_×, L3711).
    - {#483} A fine traversal a inside a step-1 traversal n has δ_a ≥ Lδ_n^{(1)} fine units, so V4's Δ_× is at most L·Δ_×^{(1)}, since ν_× ≤ c_×. And ρ_V ≤ C_V(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ (map #MD2.0.3.38), where C_V depends on χ₁ and χ₂ through V4's constants (V4 L3614).
  - {#484} So the bound holds at every step with C_cr := e^{c_W}L·max(C_S′, C_V(1+β₀)⁶C_pin) on 𝒟^{sub}, and with C_cr^λ := λ²C_cr in place of C_cr on 𝒟^λ, the domain of (ii^cr) before an R-step (at step 1 on 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟_1, #227, whose evaluation points lie in 𝒟_1 by #6.14), and on 𝒟^{ev} after it ((R-eval-cr), *Production at the T-steps*, #222). Below, C_cr stands for C_cr^λ.
- {#485} *Lemma (cross carry).* Let j ≤ n < K′. On every labelled term, on 𝒟^λ_{n+1}, and on 𝒟^{ev}_{n+1} after an R-step at n+1 ((R-eval-cr), *How it holds*, #222–#222.1), with the weights w at the rates λ_{n+1}(D)ν_r of (6B):
  - {#486} M^cr_{n+1}(j) ≤ (1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_{n+1})M^cr_n(j), and M^cr_j(j) ≤ (1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j;
  - {#487} summed over the terms of the multilinear expansion at step n+1, the products of the cross(j) insertions are at most Π_D(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)Γ_{j,n+1}) ≤ exp(Γ_{j,n+1}((1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j)), with Γ_{j,n+1} := Π_{j<k≤n+1}(1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_k) and D over the cross(j) insertions present after the R-step at j;
  - {#488} y^R_k and A^R_k are (6B)'s at the R-steps k > N_B, and 0 at the other steps.
  {#489} The constants do not depend on j, n, K, or the labelled term.
- {#490} *Proof.* This is the D2.6 closure §5 at lineage extent zero, without (T1) and (T3).
  - {#491} (T0) holds as written. The cross(j) insertions present at step n are fixed data of a term, with at most 1020Mσ(D) positions each ((6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*, at u = 6 for those the R-steps make and their descendants, (6B)'s count), or 3230Mσ(D) for the merged insertions of (R-member), at u = 19, charged to b(D) = (ν_r/4)σ(D).
  - {#492} 5.1: a cross insertion carries no deviation, and its bound is a sup bound on 𝒟^λ_n (at n = 1 on 𝒟^λ_1 ∩ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟_1, #227, whose evaluation points lie in 𝒟_1 by #6.14), or on 𝒟^{ev}_n after an R-step at n. The step-(n+1) evaluation points of data in 𝒟^λ_{n+1} are points of 𝒟^{ev}_n ⊆ 𝒟^λ_n, in the M-factored sense: at s = 1 by the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*), and the others by #222.1 ((R-eval-cr), *The carry*, #228). So its norm does not grow at the new evaluation point.
  - {#493} 5.4: its transports do not grow, interval by interval, under D2.6 §5.4's condition on the family, 4c_πMλα_{1,n+1} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*).
  - {#494} (T2) and 5.5 without a bridge give 5.6's displays for the steps without a bridge, at lineage extent zero; those displays use only the exponents. A touched descendant has weighted mass ≤ c̄w(D), and a merged insertion adds ≤ c̄w(W).
  - {#495} So y_{n+1} ≤ C_δc̄M^cr_n(j) and M^cr_{n+1}(j) ≤ (1 + C_δc̄)M^cr_n(j), as in 5.3. Step 3 of (T0)'s product form gives the product bound.
  - {#496} 5.5's reserve budget is unchanged: for the insertions with several intervals its share is (6A)(i)'s, within (ν_r/4)σ + c_sh^× for L ≥ L₀^×. Its merge-extent charge (ν_r/16)σ is not used, so the budget needs (15/16)ν_rσ, within the rate λ_n(D)ν_r ≥ (15/16)ν_r, since λ_n ≥ 31/32 and s(D) ≤ 1 ((6B), *The rates*).
    {#496.1} A carried cross(j) insertion can lie on a layer where the room is λ times (b1)'s, and there the a′-transports of a touched or merged fixed member stay within the budget's (ν_r/16)σ (map #MD2.4.11.5) under c_πλα_{1,n+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16, a smallness of g given λ (#228, #231).
  - {#497} At an R-step the factor 1 + y^R_k and the new mass A^R_k are (6B)'s, and so is the count of the positions of the R-made insertions.
  - {#498} A group whose parents have several species is counted in its first parent's lineage, and the other parents are data of the term (5.5). So a cross insertion that becomes a non-first member of an attached lineage keeps its own factor in the product, and it stays in M^cr.
  - {#499} There is no bridge. A later step touches the insertion through supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{n+1}, which can contain the loops, so no distance is gained. The growth per step is the price, and (7) pays it.
- {#500} *The smallness (H^cr):* C_δc̄ ≤ 1. Like the D2.6 closure's C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2, this is a smallness of g under (R-m′) with q > p₀, since b_m/ᾱ_m ~ (log g_m^{−2})^{−(q−p₀)}. Then 1 + C_δc̄ ≤ 2 ≤ L.
- {#501} *The large-field terms.* Nothing is taken out below K′, so the cross species need no bound on the large-field terms of any step. The attached species still need the D2.6 closure's per-step bounds, which (6A) transfers to the pair. At K′ the words of the class are bounded as for one loop, with the cross insertions inside them. Summed over the expansion, the products of the cross insertions are ≤ e^{A^×} ((7)), uniformly over the labelled terms.

### {#503} (6A) The Two-Component Reading of the Map (Derived Given the Inherited Inputs)

{#505} Each item is the one-contour statement with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, checked where two components change it. At step 1 the model is V4: Lemmas lem:two-loop-pins(a)–(d), lem:two-loop-source, and lem:two-loop-retreated.
- {#506} (i) *Multi-interval arc sets.*
  - {#507} *Intervals.* A group's arc set on C_i is the union of the linked hulls of its classes of joined pairs on C_i ((3)). A class can have several intervals on one loop: two of its pieces on C₁ can be joined only through a piece on C₂, and then no gap arc joins them. The counts below use the number of intervals only at step 1 (*Base*).
  - {#508} *Connectedness.* For an insertion D, X_D is its counted domain, the cubes of its constituents' domains (map #MD2.4.12.1), and X^{Cov}_D is its Cov domain (map #MD2.2.4.3), the set of the configuration that D depends on, which contains ℐ(D). The proof is by induction on the step, with the following hypothesis for every old insertion D.
    - {#509} (H0) X^{Cov}_D lies in the closed ℓ∞-neighbourhood of radius 8M fine units of X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D)), k the current step. The cubes of a scale are unions of those of the finer scales, so (H0) at one step gives it at the later ones.
    - {#510} (H1) A closed-connected set S_D contains X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, with diam_ℓ∞(S_D) ≤ u_DMσ(D) in production units, u_D the factor of *Units with a factor*.
    - {#511} (H2) E_D := X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) is closed-connected, H_i(D) the arcs of *Filled hulls*.
    {#512} D's domain one step later is the set of π_{k+1}-cubes meeting the 8M-neighbourhood of E_D. By (H0) and (H2) it is closed-connected and contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D), since E_D contains the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D) ⊆ H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D), and *The share of D* counts it. For a new group 𝒢, X(𝒢) is the union of its constituents' domains, the old insertions' domains one step later, and S_𝒢 := X(𝒢) ∪ ℐ(𝒢). Map #MD2.4.4.3's argument needs I(𝒢) to be one arc, so it is replaced by the following.
    - {#513} The constituents of a class are connected through its joined pairs.
    - {#514} Each constituent's domain meets ℐ(𝒢); or it meets X^{Cov}_D for a touched old insertion D, whose domain one step later is connected and contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D), with ℐ(D) ⊆ ℐ(𝒢); or it is a vacuum-link cluster, which touches M (map #MD2.4.12.25).
    - {#515} The linked hulls of the classes are connected by overlaps on one loop or the other (map #MD2.4.12.20, part (3), per loop).
    - {#516} So S_𝒢 is closed-connected, and diam_ℓ∞(S_𝒢) ≤ M#_{k+1}(𝒢) + n_{k+1}(𝒢) = Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢). The pieces are the π_{k+1}-cubes of X(𝒢), each of diameter M, and the bonds of ℐ(𝒢), and a connected union of closed pieces has diameter at most the sum of theirs.
    - {#517} So 𝒢 has (H0)–(H2), with S_𝒢 and u = 1. Its Cov domain lies in S_𝒢, since its constituents' Cov domains lie in their domains one step later. E_𝒢 is closed-connected, since S_𝒢 is and each H_i(𝒢) is an arc meeting ℐ_i(𝒢).
    - {#518} *R-made insertions.* D_g^× of (6B) has X_D = hull_c(g), the counted cubes of hull(g) ((6B), 15.6(b)–(c)), which need not be connected, and ℐ(D) = ℐ(g), which holds the members' arc sets.
      - {#519} (H0): D_g^× depends on U^R on hull(g) and, through its members, on their Cov domains. The π_k-cubes meeting hull(g) lie in hull_c(g) or meet ℐ(g) ((6B), *Lengths*). The members' (H0) puts their Cov domains in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_W ∪ (the cubes meeting ℐ(W)), with X_W ⊆ hull(g) and ℐ(W) ⊆ ℐ(g). So X^{Cov}_D lies in the 8M-neighbourhood of hull_c(g) ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(g)).
      - {#520} (H1) holds with S_D := S_g of (6B), *The count*, and u = 6.
      - {#521} (H2): E_D := hull_c(g) ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g)), the form of (H2) with X_D = hull_c(g). E_D contains every π_k-cube meeting hull(g), since those outside hull_c(g) meet ℐ(g) ⊆ H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g).
        - {#522} The members need no cubes of their own. A member W lies in a slot of a polymer P ∈ g, and dom P contains Dep(W) = X_W ∪ X^{Cov}_W ((6B), *Lengths*), which contains ℐ(W) (*Connectedness*). So dom P holds X_W and all of W's intervals, on both loops.
        - {#523} T_W is closed-connected, contains X_W and ℐ(W), and lies in X_W ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ((6B), *The units of the members*), so in E_D, since X_W ⊆ dom P and ℐ(W) ⊆ ℐ(g).
      - {#524} E_D is closed-connected. Each dom P is connected (*Lengths*), so the π_k-cubes meeting it, all in E_D, form a closed-connected set, and it contains its slots' bonds, which lie in ℐ(g), and its slot members' sets X_W and intervals (above). Each H_i(g) is an arc containing ℐ_i(g). And g has a polymer with slot bonds on both loops, or a cross member, whose intervals on both loops lie in its polymer's dom P (*The groups of 15.4*).
      - {#525} The spans in S_g need not lie in H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g), and E_D does not use them.
    - {#526} *Base.* At step 1 the hypothesis holds by V4's derived connectivity.
      - {#527} *Connectivity* (V4 lem:two-loop-pins(a), its proof): for a component γ of W, X_γ is connected; a contact of γ on C_i lies in the hull H^i_γ, a cyclic interval or I_{C_i}, and within 2L fine units of X_γ (proof of lem:two-loop-pins(b)); overlapping components share a traversal; and in a group long on C_i every overlap class has a contact on C_i, or the group is long on both loops and a class has contacts on both. So X(W) ∪ π₁(ℋ(W)) is connected. X(W) itself need not be.
      - {#528} *Support.* Y_W depends on the configuration on X(W), on the coefficient stencil halo, which lies within 2R_h < 8M fine units of the constituent domains (V4 L5229; map #MOMGD.14: the hosts are rectangles of at most four M-cubes per direction), on the contour and free-input stencils of the traversals of ℋ(W), within 2L of their initial vertices (V4 L5229), and on the transports of ℐ(W).
      - {#529} Put S_W := the closed ℓ∞-neighbourhood of radius 8M fine units of X(W) ∪ ℐ(W), X(W) the union of its π₁-cubes. It contains the support and ℐ(W), so X^{Cov}_W ⊆ S_W, which gives (H0) and (H1). It is closed-connected by *Connectivity*, since 2L ≤ 8M. (H2) is *Connectivity* at the cube level, since π₁(ℋ(W)) lies in the cubes meeting H₁(W) ∪ H₂(W).
      - {#530} *Counts.* #_1(X_W) ≤ n(W), since n(W) counts the π₁-cubes of the roots and constituent domains (V4 L5197). ℐ(W) has at most 2n(W) intervals, at most |W| ≤ n(W) on each loop (proof of lem:two-loop-pins(a), *Count*), and each has at most two partial step-1 bonds. So n_1(W) ≤ |ℋ(W)|/L + 4n(W), and #_1(X_W) + n_1(W)/M ≤ (1 + 4/M)𝔰(W).
      - {#531} *Diameter.* S_W is the union of the 8M-neighbourhoods of the at most n(W) π₁-cubes of X(W), each of diameter at most M + 16M/L step-1 bonds, and of the at most 2n(W) intervals, an interval of t bonds giving at most t + 16M/L. So diam_ℓ∞(S_W) ≤ Mn(W) + n_1(W) + 48(M/L)n(W) ≤ (1 + 4/M + 48/L)M𝔰(W).
      - {#532} So the step-1 insertions have the units of *Units with a factor* with σ := 𝔰 and u ≤ u₁ := 1 + 4/M + 48/L. A single-loop step-1 insertion is the case of one loop.
  - {#533} *Vertices on two loops.* For a vertex with intervals on both loops, diam_v := max_i max_{y∈supp v} dist(y, int_i(v)), over the loops on which v has an interval.
    - {#534} For a W-vertex and a Γ-member with an interval on C_i, map (12)(d)'s bound holds on C_i. For an M-vertex it does not hold per loop as the map states it: a point of a touched X_W is within diam_W of W's interval, which can lie on the other loop. It is replaced by the following bound.
    - {#535} Put Q_v := M ∪ ∪_{W∈𝔅(M)}S_W ∪ ∪_{𝒱(M)}supp 𝒞. It is closed-connected: M is connected (map (10)), each touched W has X^{Cov}_W meeting M (map #MD2.2.4.53: the W with X_W meeting a component Y ⊆ M, X_W the Cov domain of map #MD2.2.4.3), X^{Cov}_W ⊆ S_W with S_W connected ((H1) of *Connectedness*), and each 𝒞 touches M.
    - {#536} Each int_i(v) contains a point of Q_v: a bond of C_i in a component of M, or a bond of a touched interval ℐ_i(W) ⊆ S_W. supp v ⊆ Q_v, so
      {#537} diam_v ≤ diam_ℓ∞(Q_v) ≤ M#(M) + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}(M#(𝒞) + 1),
      {#538} with diam_ℓ∞(S_W) ≤ u_W(M/L)σ_k(W), u_W the factor of *Units with a factor* (u_W ≤ 19). For a single-interval W made after step 1, S_W = X_W ∪ int(W) has diameter ≤ (M/L)#_k(W) + n_k(W)/L = (M/L)σ_k(W), so u_W = 1; at step 1, u_W ≤ u₁ (*Base*). The bound is the map's with diam_W replaced by diam_ℓ∞(S_W).
    - {#539} The window extends each int_i(v), so |win(v)| ≤ Σ_i|int_i(v)| + 80(diam_v + 1) ≤ |win_M| + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}80(M#(𝒞) + 1), with |win_M| of *M-vertices* below. A Γ-vertex's window is the union of its members' windows (map #MD2.4.12.17).
    - {#540} *Touched W.* A touched W with several intervals has window part 80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) ≤ 80u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 80, within its own |win(W)| ≤ 88u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 84 (*The share of D*, *Units with a factor*), so its share is as there. A touched single-interval W has |win(W)| ≤ |int(W)| + 80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) ≤ 81u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 80 in place of map sh(W)'s 41(M/L)σ_k(W) + 40. Map sh(W)'s computation, with ℓ(W)'s ν_f|win(W)|/M (map #MD2.4.12.26), gives
      {#541} sh(W) ≤ ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 160/M) + u_W(1186ν_f + 81ν_e)σ_k(W)/L + ν_e(1/8 + 1/L)ext_k(W) + ν_e(2 + 80/M) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|,
      {#542} so sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(W) + c_sh′ + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|, c_sh′ := 2ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 81/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 81/M) ≤ c_sh^×, when u_W(1186·17/16 + 81/8)/L ≤ 1/4, that is L ≥ 5081u_W, which L₀^× covers. Its transport condition u_Wc_πα_{1,k+1}·81M/L ≤ ν_r/16 follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν.
    - {#543} *Vacuum links.* |win_𝒞| := 80(M#(𝒞) + 1), so |win_𝒞|/M ≤ 81#(𝒞). Map sh(𝒞)'s computation, with ℓ(𝒞) and ext ≤ #(𝒞) + 1, gives sh(𝒞) ≤ (164.125ν_f + 85ν_e)#(𝒞) ≤ 2(83ν_f + 86ν_e)#(𝒞) ≤ #(𝒞)/4 for ν ≤ 1/1000, under c_πα_{1,k+1}·81M ≤ ν_f, which follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν. So map #MD2.4.12.25's bound stands.
  - {#544} *Filled hulls.* Let D be an old insertion (a W-vertex, a member of a Γ-vertex, or a fixed member of the D2.6 closure's (T0)) with intervals on both loops or several intervals on one.
    - {#545} H_i(D) := the smallest arc of C_i containing ℐ_i(D).
    - {#546} win(D) := the union over i of H_i(D), extended along C_i by 20(diam_D + 1) at each end, with diam_D := max_i max_{y∈supp D} dist(y, ℐ_i(D)).
    - {#547} The positions of D are unchanged: the traversals of ℐ(D) and the cubes of map (12)(e).
    - {#548} In D's production units, map *Arcs* (#MD2.4.12.22) and the connectedness give |H_i(D)| ≤ 4diam(ℐ_i(D)) ≤ 4diam(S_D) ≤ 4Mσ(D) and diam_D ≤ diam(S_D) ≤ Mσ(D). One step later these are at most 4(M/L)σ(D) + 2 and (M/L)σ(D) step bonds, as map (12)(d) has diam_W ≤ (M/L)#_k(W).
  - {#549} *Covering.* The covering lemma's part (2) holds piece by piece.
    - {#550} Let the supports of v and D meet or touch at x, and let v have an interval on C_i. Then x is within diam_v of int_i(v) and within diam_D of a traversal b ∈ ℐ_i(D).
    - {#551} By part (1) the shorter arc between them has length at most 2(diam_v + diam_D + 1). So it lies in win(v), or it starts at b ∈ H_i(D) and lies in win(D).
    - {#552} For a member with several pieces on C_i, the gap arc of the pair in the linked hull is, by (3)'s definition, the shorter arc between int_i(v) and the nearest piece of ℐ_i(D). It is no longer than the arc above and starts at the same interval, so it is covered too.
    - {#553} If D has no interval on v's loop, the pair has no arc to cover ((iii)).
    - {#554} Part (3) runs per loop. So ℐ(𝒢) ⊆ ∪_vwin(v), and map (12)(c)'s bounds of σ_{k+1}(𝒢) and ext_{k+1}(𝒢) by the shares hold.
  - {#555} *Positions.* In the D2.6 closure's units (5.2 (T0): n(D) ≤ Mσ(D), diam_D ≤ (M/L)σ(D), σ(D) ≥ 1), |win(D)| ≤ |H₁(D)| + |H₂(D)| + 80(diam_D + 1) ≤ 8Mσ(D) + 80((M/L)σ(D) + 1) ≤ 89Mσ(D).
    - {#556} So D has at most 81Mσ(D) + 89Mσ(D) = 170Mσ(D) positions, in place of (T0)'s 163Mσ(D).
    - {#557} A traversal of win(D) is within |H_i(D)| + 20(diam_D + 1) ≤ 24((M/L)σ(D) + 1) step bonds of ℐ_i(D). So map (12)(e)'s window sum holds with C_win^× := 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/24−1)₊} ≤ 196/c_w (four window ends, and the same spare (ν_r/8)σ(D) ≥ (ν_rL/(8M))(d/24 − 1)). C_win^× depends on L, M, and ν only.
    - {#558} In (T0)'s condition the window term counts the other vertices whose windows reach a traversal of D, at most n(D)C_win (D2.6 closure 5.2). A two-loop vertex has four window ends, so that constant is at most 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/20−1)₊} ≤ 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/24−1)₊} = C_win^×, term by term, and C_win^× serves for it.
    - {#559} For these insertions (T0)'s condition becomes (170 + C_win^×)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, again a smallness of g under (R-m′). *Units with a factor* below gives the condition for the whole class.
  - {#560} *The share of D.* E_D = X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) is closed-connected ((H2) of *Connectedness*).
    - {#561} By *Arc cubes* (map #MD2.4.12.19) it has at most #(X_D) + Σ_i(8|H_i(D)|/M + 80) ≤ 65σ_k(D) + 160 cubes, in place of map (12)(d)'s 8σ_k(W) + 80.
    - {#562} D's domain one step later, the π_{k+1}-cubes meeting the 8M-neighbourhood of E_D, has at most 16 + 128N/L cubes for E_D of N cubes, as in map #MD2.4.12.23. Its spanning walk cuts into pieces of at most ⌊L/2⌋ − 1 steps, and the 8M-neighbourhood of a piece has extent at most ⌊L/2⌋·(π_k-side) + 16M fine units < π_{k+1}-side per direction for L ≥ 6, so it meets at most 2⁴ π_{k+1}-cubes. The neighbourhood matters only for the halos of step-1 insertions (*Base*).
    - {#563} So #_{k+1}(X_D) ≤ 16 + 20480/L + 8320σ_k(D)/L, and |win(D)| ≤ 88(M/L)σ_k(D) + 84 step-(k+1) bonds.
    - {#564} Map (12)(d)'s computation then gives sh(D) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(D) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(D) + c_sh^× + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(D)|, with c_sh^× := 2ν_f(16 + 20480/L + 84/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 84/M). It needs (8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4, which holds for L ≥ 4·10⁴, since (8496·17/16 + 11)/(4·10⁴) ≤ 0.226.
    - {#565} The transport condition c_πα_{1,k+1}·88M/L ≤ ν_r/16 follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν.
  - {#566} *Units with a factor.* Let an insertion D have the units above with uσ(D) in place of σ(D), for a u ≥ 1: in production units n(D) ≤ uMσ(D), diam(S_D) ≤ uMσ(D), and #(X_D) + n(D)/M ≤ uσ(D). The counts above use only these bounds and (H0)–(H2) of *Connectedness*, so they hold with uσ(D) in place of σ(D).
    - {#567} D has at most 170uMσ(D) positions.
    - {#568} A traversal of win(D) is within 24u((M/L)σ(D) + 1) step bonds of ℐ_i(D), and the spare (ν_r/8)σ(D) is ≥ (ν_rL/(8M))(d/(24u) − 1). So the window sum holds with C_win^× ≤ 196u/c_w.
    - {#569} The share is within (ν_r/4)σ_k(D) + c_sh^× + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(D)| when u(8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4. The transport condition u·c_πα_{1,k+1}·88M/L ≤ ν_r/16 still follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν, since 1408u ≤ 4096L.
    - {#570} The factors: the step-1 insertions have u ≤ u₁ = 1 + 4/M + 48/L (*Base*), the ones made at the T-steps after step 1 have u = 1 (*Connectedness*), the single-loop ones made at the R-steps have u ≤ 21/10, or u = 16 when a slot holds a carried insertion ((6C)), the R-made cross insertions and their untouched continuations have u = 6 ((6B), *The count*, *The units of the members*), and the merged insertions of (R-member) have u = 19. An untouched insertion keeps its production σ and factor; (6B)'s *The units of the members* bounds its domain at every later step by (21/20)σ + 17, whatever the factor, so no factor grows along a lineage.
    - {#571} An R-made cross insertion has E_D = X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) with X_D = hull_c(g) (*Connectedness*), and the units with u = 6. So it has at most 390σ_g + 160 cubes, D's domain one step later has at most 16 + 20480/L + 49920σ_g/L, and the share needs 6(8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4, which holds for L ≥ 2.5·10⁵, since 6(8496·17/16 + 11)/(2.5·10⁵) ≤ 0.217.
    - {#572} So (T0)'s condition for these insertions is (2720 + 59584/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, a smallness of g under (R-m′): 170·16 positions, and the window term n(D)C_win with n(D) ≤ 16Mσ(D) and C_win ≤ 196·19/c_w, since the other vertices whose windows reach D have factors up to 19, the merged insertions of (R-member) included. (R-member)'s condition at u = 19, (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(…) ≤ ν_r/8, implies it. The shares hold for L ≥ L₀^× := 10⁶, since 16(8496·17/16 + 11)/L₀^× ≤ 0.145, the R-made cross ones need 6(8496·17/16 + 11)/L₀^× ≤ 0.055, and the merged insertions of (R-member), at u = 19, need (219024·17/16 + 209)/L₀^× ≤ 0.233.
  - {#573} *M-vertices.* An M-vertex has an interval on each loop where it has slots or touches an insertion with an interval.
    - {#574} *The enlargement.* D2.2(4)(e) enlarges I(Y) at each end to the end of any old interval that it cuts (map #MD2.2.4.53). For the pair an old interval is a piece of an old insertion's arc set on one loop, not its filled hull. The enlargement adds nothing. A cut piece J would contain an end bond b of the smallest arc containing S′(Y). The touched pieces lie inside that arc, and the pieces are pairwise disjoint, so b is a bond of C_i in Y. J's owner W has X^{Cov}_W ⊇ ℐ(W) ⊇ J (Cov(k), map #MD2.2.4.3; the touched relation of map #MD2.2.4.53 is read with this domain, which at step 1 lies in S_W, *Base*), so X^{Cov}_W meets Y, W is touched, and J lies inside the arc. The counts below use #(X_W), the constituents' count, and S_W ⊇ X^{Cov}_W, so they are unchanged.
    - {#575} Map (12)(d)'s count holds per loop with diam_ℓ∞(S_W) in place of diam_W + |int(W)|: diam_ℓ∞(S_M^{(i)}) ≤ M#(M) + 2max_Wdiam_ℓ∞(S_W), since a touched W's interval on C_i reaches S_M^{(i)} through S_W, and n^{(i)}_{k+1}(M) ≤ 4(5diam_ℓ∞(S_M^{(i)}) + 2max_W(piece of ℐ_i(W))) ≤ 20M#(M) + 48(M/L)Σ_Wu_Wσ_k(W), over the touched W with an interval on C_i. A piece has at most n_k(W)/L ≤ u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) step bonds, and the map's allowance for the enlargement is kept as slack.
    - {#576} So n_{k+1}(M) ≤ 40M#(M) + 96(M/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}u_Wσ_k(W) and |win_M| := n_{k+1}(M) + 80(M#(M) + 1), the M-part of the window bound in *Vertices on two loops*.
    - {#577} Map (12)(d)'s computation of sh(M), with #(X_M) ≤ #(M) + 8n_{k+1}(M)/M + 161 (arc cubes on two loops) and ext_M bounded by the same plus the touched boxes, gives
      {#578} sh(M) ≤ ν_f(282#(M) + 22) + ν_e(441#(M) + 164) + (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(96/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}u_Wσ_k(W) + ν_eΣ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(ext_k(W)/L + 1) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win_M|
      {#579} for M ≥ 100, in place of the map's ν_f(142#(M) + 11) + ν_e(221#(M) + 84) and 48/L.
    - {#580} A cube of M∖D′ then costs log 64 + log 2 + 2 + 1/8 + 24c + 283ν_f + 441ν_e ≤ 7.6 for ν ≤ 1/1000 and the vacuum constant c ≤ 1/100 (map #MD2.4.12.1), against r/648 ≥ 8. The bracket of a component cube stays within κ_s^{(12)}'s constant 9.
    - {#581} The constant part, 22ν_f + 164ν_e ≤ 0.045 for ν ≤ 1/1000, is charged to one π_k-cube of the components (#(G_M) ≥ 1). That cube's cost then has log 4 + 1 + 1/8 + 0.045 ≤ 2.6 outside the bracket, within the constant 4 of κ_s^{(12)}/4.
    - {#582} The window sums of a two-loop vertex have four ends, with C_win^×'s constant (*Positions*).
    - {#583} The part passed to a touched W is (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(96u_W/L)σ_k(W) + (ν_r/64)σ_k(W) ≤ (ν_r/16)σ_k(W) when 414u_Wν/L ≤ 3ν_r/64, that is L ≥ 8832u_W, which L₀^× covers at u_W ≤ 19. This stays within the reserve budget of an old W (map #MD2.4.12.27).
    - {#584} The transport conditions c_πα_{1,k+1}·120M ≤ ν_f and u_Wc_πα_{1,k+1}·96M/L ≤ ν_r/64 follow from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν, since 96·19·64 ≤ 4096L.
    - {#585} The per-piece constants 80c_π (the map's 40c_π) and 84c_π of the transports, ℓ_X's 16|win|/M + 320 for two window arcs (the map's + 160, #MD2.4.12.26), and map (12)(f)'s n⁴ arc choices for two arcs (the map's n²) change C_pc by a fixed factor, which the smallness of g absorbs.
- {#586} (ii) *Long groups.* At k ≥ 1 a long arc is a union of linked hulls with shorter gap arcs, and no hull is replaced by C_i ((3)). The covering lemma of (12)(c) is used for pairs of vertices with intervals on one loop, and its part (1) is that loop's square geometry. So a long arc is covered like any arc, and (S-w) and its counts hold for groups long on one or both loops, as for the single loop's whole-contour groups (map #MD2.3.2.14, #MD2.3.4.8). Long-ness enters only (4)–(5), where (T′) pays it. At step 1 V4 replaces a long hull by I_{C_i} and bounds the long groups by their total mass (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(c)–(d)); D^×(1) takes this as V4 states it.
- {#587} (iii) *The linked hull.* As in (3): the covering lemma's part (2) is applied to the joined pairs with intervals on one loop. A joined pair on different loops is linked by (ii) or (iii) of (12)(c), and it has no arc to cover.
- {#588} (iv) *Base points.* Each loop's traversals are numbered cyclically from its own base point (V4 L3560; the base point of map #MD2.3.6.6, per loop). The traversals of C are ordered as those of C₁, then those of C₂. "The first traversal" of an arc set refers to this order.
- {#589} (v) *The reference gas.* Its species are C₁- and C₂-groups only (cross activity 0), so their positions and networks lie on one loop, and map #MD2.3.5.3's network argument for (T′) transfers.
  - {#590} Its Kotecký–Preiss condition is the c-version of the pair's (S-w) (map (12)(f) and (13)(b)): the positions of 𝒢, at most 81Mσ, times C_S′ρ·pl, are at most ν/(64·81M).
  - {#591} The pair enters through the per-position counts of (12)(e)–(f): the traversals through a cube, the arc choices, and C_M. Each of them grows by a factor fixed before g when a position meets two loops. So the condition holds with the pair's C_S′, and S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and (T′) follow as for one loop.
- {#592} (vi) *The range r₀.* r₀ := 2 ((1)).
- {#593} *D2.5.* Covariance is a property of each insertion at the ends of each interval of its arc set.
  - {#594} For an insertion with intervals I ∈ ℐ(W), Cov(k) (map #MD2.2.4.3) is read with one factor per interval: Y_W(𝐔^u, …) = (⊗_Iπ_{i(I)}(u(x_I)))Y_W(𝐔, …)(⊗_Iπ_{i(I)}(u(x′_I)))^{−1}, with x_I, x′_I the ends of I and i(I) its loop.
  - {#595} The composition argument (map (4)(c)–(e), #MD2.2.4.13, #MD2.2.4.93) acts factor by factor, and the pair's word is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of two closed products. D2.5's proof is per insertion and per end, so it holds with two loops.
- {#596} *D2.6.* The attached species, their carry, and their payment are per large-field component and per position.
  - {#597} (T3) uses the square geometry of the loop that carries the gap arc, and gap arcs are per loop ((iii)).
  - {#598} A fixed member D″ with several intervals adds at most 12uσ(D″)/L π_{n+1}-sides to a group's reach, u ≤ 19 its factor of (i)'s *Units with a factor*, in place of 2σ(D″)/L (5.5, *The bridge of a merged W*). The reach is a crossing of S_{D″} ∪ H₁(D″) ∪ H₂(D″), whose diameter is at most 5diam(S_{D″}) and one cube side (each point of H_i lies within 2diam(ℐ_i) of ℐ_i, map *Arcs*), and a gap reached around one H_i(D″), of length at most 4diam(S_{D″}). The charge is 12uν_e/L ≤ ν_r/16 for L ≥ 456, since ν_e ≤ ν_r/8, inside D″'s merge-extent charge.
  - {#599} 5.5's share of a fixed member, sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ(W) + c_W, holds for the members with several intervals by (i), with c_sh^×, for L ≥ L₀^×. Their positions are (i)'s 170uMσ, with u ≤ 19.
  - {#600} §7.2's payment is per component. The contour-density constant c_C of §7.1–7.2 at most doubles, since a position meets the traversals of at most two loops.
  - {#601} So the D2.6 closure holds for C₁ ⊔ C₂. Attached cross groups need no flat value, since attached insertions carry no deviation bound (5.1).
- {#602} *B^×_LF.* B^×_LF is the continuum note's large-field payment (4.5) for C₁ ⊔ C₂, with the words' bound taken without the cross insertions: B^×_LF := sup_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}sup|e^{𝓔^×_{K′}(ℓ)}|·Π_{W not cross}(1 + e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖)/(κ^{(K′)}_{C₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂}), on the terms where the payment is made, with e^{E^×_const,K′} and e^{E^∂_{K′}(ℓ)} in e^{𝓔^×_{K′}(ℓ)}. The cross insertions enter (8.2) through e^{A^×} ((7)).
  - {#603} The far-component decay of (4.4) uses the 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂) traversals of the pair in place of 4ℓ_C.
  - {#604} (4.5) is derived given D2.7 (continuum note #ND.4.9) and rests on the D2.6 closure §7. So B^×_LF rests on (6B) as well as on §7 for the pair.
- {#605} *D2.7.* (6B). Items 2 (the class and its exclusions do not involve the observable) and 12 (D2.0(3)(b) at k > N_B, constants only) transfer verbatim.

### {#607} (6B) The R-Steps for the Pair (Derived, except (R-eval))

{#609} The R-operation is the identity at the steps k ≤ N_B (D2.7 item 1), with N_B Bałaban's N, written N_B here to keep it apart from the step index. N is read as N_k (D2.7 item 1(b); #3.2.1.1), so "k ≤ N_B" means k ≤ N_k at the step k, and "k > N_B" means k > N_k. N_B > N₀ ≥ 1 holds, since N_k ≥ N₀ + 1. Fix k > N_B and a term of LFR II (1.72), in the notation of D2.7 15.0 with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, and put m := K′ − k.
- {#610} *Item 3.* R′'s integral identity holds for every integrable integrand. The gauge-fixing equalities before it need the word to be invariant under the multi-scale gauge transformations. The pair's word is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of two closed products of link variables and insertions, and each insertion is covariant at the ends of each interval of its arc set ((6A), D2.5). So it is invariant, and ∫dV_k(R′N^×_k) = ∫dV_kN^×_k. Derived.
- {#611} *15.0, 15.2, and 15.3.* C ∩ X⁺ is a union of arcs on one or both loops, and the arc words are averaged per component as in 15.0. 15.2's covariance is per arc. 15.3's decomposition of the configurations does not use the observable, so Ξ^C = Σ_𝔉Ξ_{¬C}(𝔉)A^C(𝔉) with A^C(𝔉) the normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of the two path-ordered products. Derived as identities, with 15.3's bounds.
- {#612} *15.5.* The Möbius expansion is per bond and per insertion. So it holds for the words of both loops, and for the cross insertions, which are functions of U^{old} on their domains as the slot insertions are.
  - {#613} The sums of 15.5(ii) are run at the rate δ/4 in place of δ. Then D2.7 15.5(ii)'s sum over the attachment bonds gives a_k(X) ≤ 16C_a(100MR_k + 2Mw_k + 8M(δM)^{−1})(δM)^{−1}e^{−δMw_k/4} + 1200c_πα_{1,k}MR_k + log 2, with D2.7's arc part, w_k in M-units and C_a := 2O(1)e^{(κ₁−1)/4}(100R_k)^d the factors of (α) (D2.7's C_α in a_k), a polylog of order ≤ r(d+1) < p₀ (D2.7 l.175; D26 l.136), and the factor e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ(i,𝒦)} of each attached insertion is left for the lengths below. The carried insertions take the same expansion in their U-argument (D2.7 15.5(i)), about their home h(D): the first-class components whose X⁺ meets D's dependence set Dep(D) := X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, on which D is a function of U^{old} (Dep(D) ⊆ T_D, and Dep(D) = X_D when j < k, *The units of the members*), with Z′ for an att(j, Z′) or lw insertion of a first-class Z′. An insertion whose interval meets an arc of C ∩ X⁺ has X ∈ h(D), since X^{Cov}_D contains ℐ(D) (#508; map #MD2.2.4.3). The components of h(D) keep their old data in every configuration, as h(i) does for a bond, and 𝒦 runs over the other first-class components. The parts D^{𝒦} := Σ_{𝒥⊆𝒦}(−1)^{|𝒦∖𝒥|}D(U^{𝒥∪h(D)}) sum to D(U^{old}) exactly; D^{∅} is joined to the polymers of h(D) (none when h(D) = ∅: D is then untouched), and D^{𝒦} to those of h(D) and of the components in 𝒦. They obey |D^{𝒦}| ≤ ‖D‖(C_αe^{δMt_k})^{|𝒦|+1}e^{−δℓ(D,𝒦)}, with ℓ(D,𝒦) the length, in step-k units, of the shortest tree connecting Dep(D) and the components of 𝒦, C_α ≥ 1 the O(1) of (α′) (D2.7's c_{α′}, not its (β) constant), t_k := ⌈(32/(δ₀M))(2log α_{1,k}^{−1} + log(L⁴R_k²))⌉ in M-units (D2.7's segments of length t_k), δ = δ_α/M (#728.1), and ‖D‖ D's bound at the configurations U^{𝒥∪h(D)}, 𝒥 ⊆ 𝒦 (#619). This is the clause (α′) of R-far″ (D2.7 15.5(ii), derived, not reviewed, at c384e6a20d7a; its step (3) uses this note's (R-eval) points, #3.3): Bałaban's factor exp(−δd((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y_i′, X)) (p.376 l.10–13) is the decay of a term localised in a domain X in the scaled distance from X, and (α) is its case of a single bond. Each X ∈ 𝒦 has X⁺ ∩ Dep(D) = ∅, and X⁺ contains the MR_k-neighbourhood of X (*Lengths*), so ℓ(D,𝒦) ≥ MR_k for 𝒦 ≠ ∅. The parts' sums run at the rate δ/4, which leaves e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ(D,𝒦)} for the lengths below, and their total over 𝒦 ≠ ∅ is a factor of D's own, η(D) := Σ_{𝒦≠∅}(C_αe^{δMt_k})^{|𝒦|+1}e^{−(δ/4)ℓ(D,𝒦)} ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)e^{−63δMR_k/512}, c_r := 18.05·3^d(3^d + 2^dM) and c_r⁺ := C_αe^{δM}c_r, paid from D's weight (*The rates*, *The rooted sum*). Proof: the MR_k-cubes meeting the shortest tree of 𝒦 form a ∗-connected set S of m ≤ 2^{d+1}(ℓ/(MR_k) + 1) ≤ 2^{d+2}ℓ/(MR_k) cubes (*Cubes*' argument, with cubes of side MR_k, and ℓ ≥ MR_k), one of which meets Dep(D). The components are unions of MR_k-cubes (LFR I p.177 l.17–19), so each X ∈ 𝒦 contains a cube of S, distinct components distinct cubes, and the 𝒦 with a given S contribute at most C_αe^{δMt_k}(1 + C_αe^{δMt_k})^m. Half of the rate δ/4 gives e^{−(δ/8)ℓ} ≤ e^{−δMR_km/2^{d+5}}, the other half e^{−δMR_k/8}. The ∗-connected S of m cubes containing a given one number at most (e3^d)^m, and the given cube is one of at most c_rσ(D). Dep(D) ⊆ X_D ∪ ℐ(D) (*The units of the members*), so a cube meeting Dep(D) meets X_D ∪ ℐ(D). R_k is a power of L, so each π_k-cube of X_D lies in one MR_k-cube, and a closed MR_k-cube meeting X_D is that cube or a neighbour; a bond lies in at most 2^d closed cubes. So at most 3^d#_k(X_D) + 2^dn_k(D) ≤ 3^d(3^d#_k(X_D) + 2^dn_k(D)) ≤ 3^d(3^d + 2^dM)(#_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M) ≤ c_rσ(D) cubes meet Dep(D), by #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) + 17 ≤ 18.05σ(D) (*The units of the members*, σ(D) ≥ 1). Since δMt_k ≤ δM + (32δ/δ₀)(2log α_{1,k}^{−1} + log(L⁴R_k²)) and δ ≤ δ₀/32 (#728.1), e^{δMt_k} ≤ e^{δM}L⁴R_k²α_{1,k}^{−2}, at most a power of g_k^{−1}, while e^{−δMR_k/2^{d+5}} decays faster than every power, as R_k ≥ (log g_k^{−2})² (#776). With x := e3^d(1 + C_αe^{δMt_k})e^{−δMR_k/2^{d+5}} ≤ ½, a smallness of g_k, η(D) ≤ C_αe^{δMt_k}c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8}Σ_{m≥1}x^m ≤ C_αe^{δMt_k}c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8}. Finally δMt_k ≤ δM + (32δ/δ₀)(2log α_{1,k}^{−1} + log(L⁴R_k²)) ≤ δM + δMR_k/512 for g small, so η(D) ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)e^{−63δMR_k/512}.
  - {#614} The attachment cost a_k(X) counts the bonds of C near a component, per straight side. C₁ ⊔ C₂ has eight sides where C has four, so a_k at most doubles. 15.3's amplitude c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} is still ≤ e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} for small g_k.
  - {#615} R-far″ is inherited, with the pieces' domains Y′ of (ii)(β) in 𝐃_k, the connected unions of π_k-cubes (RG I p.257 l.7–11). This is where Bałaban's domains lie: Y_1 is "the smallest localization domain Y_1 ∈ 𝐃_k" containing the term's domain (p.373 l.15), and each component of Y_2′, Y_3′, and Y_4′ belongs to 𝐃_k (p.373 l.33, p.374 l.10, p.374 l.28).
  - {#616} This amends D2.7 15.5(ii)(β), whose text has "a connected union of MR_k-cubes". The volume factor is per π_k-cube under either class. The shapes of the Y′ in 𝐃_k are summed in the resummation over Y′, which Bałaban runs over his Y_i′ ∈ 𝐃_k (p.376 l.39), at the rate of *The free decay* below. The sizes that 15.4–15.7 use count MR_k-cubes, at most the number of π_k-cubes, so the finer class adds nothing to those sums.
  - {#617} For a slot holding a carried insertion D, of any species, the slot's bonds are read as those of D's domain X_D, the union of the π_k-cubes of its constituents' domains (map #MD2.0.3.50's #_k). So the base Y′₀ (the cubes of the slot's bonds, its components, and its tree, D2.7 15.5(ii)) contains X_D and, D being a function of U^{old} on its dependence set, Dep(D) = X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D (*15.5* above), and R-far″ claims no decay across Dep(D). This is the reading of 15.5(i), which joins D to the polymer. *The count* counts X_D by σ(D) for a big member, and for a small one in #π_k(B_P) or in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins it to P (*Big and small members*). Dep(D) ∖ X_D is empty when D was made before step k and lies in ℐ(D) when D was made at the T-step into step k (*The units of the members*). The π_k-cubes meeting it, the set Q(D), are cubes of Y′₀; they lie in T_D, which bounds their diameter, and they are left out of the counted domains, since each meets ℐ(D), whose bonds are counted as traversals (*Lengths*). The reading is for Y′₀ and the polymer's domain only: in the arc set ℐ(g) of *The arc set* below, a slot contributes the bonds of its tensor, its arcs of C ∩ ∪_{X∈P}X⁺ and the intervals of its carried insertions outside every X⁺ (*The slots of a cross member*).
  - {#618} A carried insertion of a single-loop species in a slot is part of that slot's word, bounded inside e^{2ΣA} as for one loop (15.1, 15.3, 15.8(b)). The cross insertions are not: on each term of (6)'s multilinear expansion they are fixed factors of the words, with their own norms.
  - {#619} A cross member W is evaluated where 15.5(i) evaluates every slot insertion: on the integration domain of 𝐓′_k(X), and at the configurations U^{𝒥∪h(W)}, 𝒥 ⊆ 𝒦, for its parts with 𝒦 ≠ ∅ (*15.5* above). 15.5(i) bounds a slot insertion there by its D(k) mass, and (6B) uses the same bound for W, ‖W‖ with its weight. That these bounds hold there, for W and for the single-loop slot insertions alike, is (R-eval), derived and not reviewed, with (R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed given (R-eval): W's weight and the D(k) masses are sup bounds on 𝒟^λ_k ((6), the cross clause; *The dilated sub-domains*). The D2.6 closure 5.1 places the T-steps' evaluation points in 𝒟^{sub}_n, which (b3)–(b4) extend to the family, and (6C) places the R-step's integration over the large-field old variables, at U_k^0, in 𝒟_k only.
- {#620} *The groups of 15.4.* For the pair, a span is taken only for a polymer whose slots lie on one loop and include no carried cross insertion: its span of 15.4 on that loop. A polymer with slots on both loops, or with a cross insertion among its slots, has no span. Two polymers are in one group when a bond of a slot of one lies in the span of the other.
  - {#620.1} A slot's bonds here are those it contributes to ℐ(g) (#617): the bonds of its tensor, its arcs of C ∩ ∪_{X∈P}X⁺, and the intervals of its carried insertions outside every X⁺.
  - {#621} For two polymers with spans this is 15.4's interleaving, so the groups all of whose polymers have spans are 15.4's groups, per loop.
  - {#622} Such a group's span is span(g) := ∪_{P∈g}span(P), as in 15.4. The spans of its polymers are chained by the links, so it is an arc of C_i or C_i itself. Its ends are slots, and no slot of another group lies in it, since a slot in span(P) joins its polymer to P. 15.1's Schur argument and 15.6(a)–(c) use only these properties, so 15.1's joint average applies to g.
  - {#623} A polymer without a span needs none: its tensor is contracted on its slots' bonds only (*The arc set* below). A cross insertion's arcs on one loop can be far apart, joined through the other loop, and the shortest arc containing them would hold the slots of unrelated polymers.
  - {#624} A link through a span puts a slot bond of one polymer in the span of the other, on that span's loop, so two polymers whose slots lie on one loop are linked only on that loop. So a group with slots on both loops contains a polymer whose slots lie on both loops: its domain meets both loops, or one of its slots is a carried cross insertion. Call such a group a cross group of the R-gas.
- {#625} *15.1 and 15.4(i).* For a group with slots on C_i only, the contraction over span(g) ⊂ C_i is covariant at its two ends, and Schur's lemma for π_i gives the scalar c_g (15.1, per loop). For a cross group of the R-gas, the average at the flat reference commutes with π₁(u) ⊗ π₂(u), and that is all. Put c_g := 0, as 15.4 does for the groups meeting a second-class component.
- {#626} *Lengths.* Let P be a polymer of 15.3, X′_1, …, X′_s the localisation domains of (1.90) in P, a its attached bond insertions Y_i^{𝒦}, with ℓ_a := ℓ(i,𝒦) the lengths of their trees of 15.5(ii)(α) in step-k units (#728.2), a′ the parts D^{𝒦}, 𝒦 ≠ ∅, of the carried insertions D in P's slots (the members of *The count*) with ℓ_{a′} := ℓ(D,𝒦), and Y′ its pieces' domains. Every part has ℓ_{a′} ≥ MR_k, since the components of 𝒦 lie outside D's home h(D), so their sets X⁺, which contain their MR_k-neighbourhoods (below), miss Dep(D) ⊇ X_D (*15.5* above). Put
  {#627} D_P := Σ_r(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92) + Σ_a(ℓ_a/M + 3) + Σ_{a′}(3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M + 2Σ_{Y′}#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀),
  {#628} with d_k the linear size in M-units (RG I p.257 l.14–16; it equals LFR II's d_{k,Z} of (1.67) when X′_r ∩ ∪_iY_i = ∅, the case of *The free decay*) and #π_k the number of π_k-cubes, of side M in step-k units. In (6A) and (6B) a π_k-cube is a block of the partition π_k of the step-k lattice's sites, and it meets a set of sites and bonds when it contains one of the set's sites (for a bond, one of its two end sites). So the π_k-cubes meeting a union of π_k-cubes are its own cubes, a bond meets at most two π_k-cubes, and a lattice path of at most M steps at most 2^d. Diameters and closed-connectedness are those of the cubes' closed hulls. The MR_k-cubes of *15.5* are closed continuum cubes and are named as such there.
  - {#629} dom P, the union of the X′_r, the sets X⁺ of P's first-class components, the trees of the attached insertions, the Y′, and the sets Dep(D) ⊇ X_D of the carried insertions D in P's slots (the trees end on Dep(D), and Y′₀ ⊇ Dep(D), *15.5*), is connected. Let 𝔅(P) be the set of these D. Its counted domain dom_c P is the set of the π_k-cubes meeting dom P, other than the cubes of the sets Q(D), D ∈ 𝔅(P), of *The units of the members*. Each cube of a Q(D) meets ℐ(D), so every π_k-cube meeting dom P is in dom_c P or meets ∪_{D∈𝔅(P)}ℐ(D). For the diameter, Dep(D) ∪ Q(D) ⊆ T_D (*The units of the members*). Then diam_∞(dom P) ≤ MD_P + Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}diam_∞(T_D) in step-k units, with diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D) for every big member, and the small members' T_D inside the sets X^{(44)} or, for the others, inside D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*The count*, *Big and small members*). When 𝔅^{big}(P) = ∅, diam_∞(dom P) ≤ MD_P. A connected union of finitely many closed pieces has diameter at most the sum of theirs, connected or not, and the bullets below bound the pieces.
  - {#630} A tree meeting every M-cube of X′_r gives diam_∞(X′_r) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 2).
  - {#631} The π_k-cubes of Y′₀ stick out by at most M. The cubes covering a tree of length ℓ have ℓ^∞-diameter at most ℓ + 2M, and a slot bond on ∂X⁺ lies in a cube outside X⁺. The constants 4R_k + 92 and ℓ_a/M + 3, and the half length ½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ 23 (*The count*), include this.
  - {#632} Each first-class component X of P lies in one X′_r, since X′_0 = ∪_{Y∈𝐃}Y ∪ ∪_jX_j and the X′_p are unions of the components of X′_0∖∪_iY_i (p.387 l.42, p.388 l.2–3).
  - {#633} X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes: X^∼ adds one, since ∼ means "adding one layer of cubes" of the family that defines the set (CRE p.245 l.40–43), and 15.0 adds one more. So diam_∞(X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X⁺) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 2), and ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 4) with the cubes of Y′₀. X^{(44)} widens X⁺ by 44 layers of π_k-cubes, so diam_∞(X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X^{(44)}) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92), and this set contains the sets T_D of the small members whose T_D meets such an X⁺ (*The units of the members*).
  - {#634} The scaled length is at least the length in step-k units, since (L^jη)^{−1} ≥ (L^kη)^{−1} for j ≤ k (PRT2 (2.46)).
  - {#635} Every cube of Y′∖Y′₀ is joined to Y′₀ by a chain of at most #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) cubes.
- {#636} *The free decay.* Put c_F := min(1, δM/2). For a polymer P that meets no second-class or unchanged component, each part of D_P has a factor that the uses of Bałaban's bounds leave free, at least c_F per unit:
  - {#637} d_k(X′_r): the activity carries e^{−(1+β/4)κd_k(P∖fixed)} (D2.7 15.3(b)), and D2.7's gases use e^{−κ(size)} of it, the size counted in MR_k-cubes (15.4(i), 15.7). A tree of length Md_k meets at most 2^{d+1}(d_k/R_k + 1) MR_k-cubes (*Cubes*' argument, with cubes of side MR_k), so the size of X′_r is at most d_k(X′_r) + 2^{d+1} for R_k ≥ 2^{d+1}, and e^{−κd_k} ≤ e^{2^{d+1}κ}e^{−κ(size)}. The rest, e^{−(β/4)κd_k}, is (β/4)κ ≥ 3·2^{d−1} ≥ 1 per M-unit, since 1 ≤ βκ/(6·2^d) (p.389 l.12).
  - {#638} ℓ_a/M and (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M: the factor e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ} left by 15.5 at the rate δ/4 (by (α) for a bond insertion and by (α′) for a part D^{𝒦}), which is 3δM/4 per M-unit of ℓ, so at least c_F per unit of ℓ_a/M and, since (3/2)c_F ≤ 3δM/4, of (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M.
    - {#638.1} The a′ terms are paid by (α′)'s factor, derived through #3.3, not reviewed (#613). So this mark applies to every polymer, cluster, or group sum of the note over activities whose polymers can hold carried insertions, and to every bound that uses one of the constants C_gr, C_jn, C_Ψ′, C_lk, or C_y such sums define, and to every bound that spends the free decay e^{−c_FD_P} of such a polymer, or a part of it. Among them are #699(a), #727, #729, *The rooted sum* (#742), #755, #758, #762, A^R_k at #764–#765, and y^R_k at #769–#770 and #772 in (6B); the cluster bound on |Ψ_g| (#681–#683), with its uses at #724, #730, #811, and #851; #801–#802, #806, #841–#842, #849–#850, and #854–#857 in (6C); and (R-member) (#241).
  - {#639} #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀): the pieces carry e^{−(κ₁−1)} per π_k-cube of Y′∖Y′₀ (15.5(ii)(β)), and the budget per cube is as follows.
    - {#640} Bałaban extracts the tree decay of the enlarged domain from half of his volume factor (p.376 l.34–35), and of the other half he writes "This we use now to control the resummations" (l.37–39).
    - {#641} D2.7 spends the pieces' factor on the resummation over Y′, at Bałaban's rate ½(κ₁−1), and on the pieces' part of the size decay e^{−κ(size)}: 15.3–15.4's Kotecký–Preiss weights, 15.4(i)'s group entropy, and 15.7's diameter. It extracts no tree decay over Y′. Since the size counts MR_k-cubes, at most the π_k-cubes, that part is at most κ per π_k-cube.
    - {#642} RG II assumes ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ (p.20 l.17). So ½(κ₁−1) − κ ≥ (2L−1)κ ≥ 2 ≥ 2c_F per cube, that is c_F per unit of D_P.
  - {#643} κ and κ₁ here are RG II's. LFR II's κ₁ is that of its localisation expansions (6.10) [I], which are RG II's (1.10), with the parameters |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁}, and "κ₁ is a sufficiently big positive number" (RG II p.5 l.6–8). The identification of [I] with RG II is by CRE's (I.6.34) and (I.6.41) (p.276 l.23–28), which are RG II's analyticity space (1.34) and expansion (1.41).
  - {#644} κ > 6·2^d. By p.389, the sixth line below (1.94), κ ≥ 6·2^d/β, and β < 1: p.376 l.5–6 bounds exp(−(1−β)κ(L^jη)^{−1}d_k(Y₀)) by exp(−(1+3β)κd_k(Y₀)), which fails for β ≥ 1 when d_k(Y₀) > 0.
  - {#645} The constants 4R_k + 92 and e^{2^{d+1}κ} per X′_r: the exponents of e^{c_F(4R_k+92)} and e^{2^{d+1}κ} are constants or polylogs of order r < p₀, so these factors are paid by the margin of (1.89) (*The volume*). Each X′_r has a first-class component to pay them: a localisation domain disjoint from ∪_iY_i has q ≥ 1 in p.389's sum (the three lines after (1.95)).
  - {#646} The constant 3 per attached bond insertion is not a per-component constant. 15.5(ii)'s sum over the attachment bonds of a component is the first term of a_k(X) (#613), so the factor e^{3c_F} of each insertion replaces a_k(X) by at most e³a_k(X), since c_F ≤ 1 and the arc part is ≥ 0. This is still a polylog of order ≤ r(d+1) < p₀ (#613), and 15.3's amplitude bound c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} ≤ e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} stands for small g_k.
  {#647} So 15.3's bounds and Kotecký–Preiss sums hold for the activities multiplied by e^{c_FD_P}, with the amplitude e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} for small g_k.
- {#648} *The volume.* (1.95)'s volume factor is βκ/(6·2^d) per π_k-cube of X′∖∪_iY_i, the Y_i the second-class components. X′_r is disjoint from them: P meets none, and a localisation domain X′ either contains a Y_i or is disjoint from ∪_iY_i (p.389, the three lines after (1.95)). (1.96) uses e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}100^dR_k^{−d} of it per cube (p.389, (1.96) and the three lines after it). The rest, a_V ≥ βκ/(12·2^d) ≥ ½ for small g_k, is unused, since (1.97) drops it ("the above bound is cancelled by the last exponential in (1.95)", the second line above (1.97)). The sets X⁺ are paid by the components instead.
  - {#649} *The factor of (1.89) per component.* Each first-class component carries e^{−2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_k)+1}. With its volume term e^{2κ(100R_k)^d} (p.389, the eighth line below (1.94)) this is ≤ e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_k)} (the display after "g_k is so small that").
    - {#650} (1.96)'s sum over the components uses e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} of it, as Bałaban's ((1.96)).
    - {#651} The rest, c₁ = e^{−p₀(g_k)}, is the component's factor in 15.3(b), and the component's loop factor e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} is counted with it, once. 15.3's amplitude takes e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} of c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k}, and 15.4(iii)'s rooted bound m^R_k is that amplitude.
    - {#652} The margin, e^{−p₀(g_k)/2+3+2ΣA+2a_k}, has −p₀(g_k)/2 plus polylogs of order ≤ r(d+1) < p₀ in its exponent (a_k, #613). (6B) takes from it e^{5p₀(g_k)/64} for X^{(44)} at the rate (5/2)ν_r (*The volume*) and the constants of *The free decay*, and the rest is ≤ 1 for small g_k.
    - {#653} 15.6(e)'s att charge is paid in D26 7.2's reserve Res(Z), not from (1.89).
  - {#654} A first-class component X lies in a cube of side 100MR_k (LFR I p.177 (i)), and X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes (*Lengths*). X^{(44)} adds 44 layers of π_k-cubes (*The count*). So #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (104R_k + 88)^d ≤ (105R_k)^d, for R_k ≥ 88.
  - {#655} p.389's assumption gives 2κ(100R_k)^d < p₀(g_k)/2. With 8(1.05)^dν_r ≤ κ (κ > 6·2^d, *The free decay*), 2ν_r#π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ p₀(g_k)/16. At the rate (5/2)ν_r of *The payment* and (6C) the cost is at most 5p₀(g_k)/64, which the margin e^{−p₀(g_k)/2 + (polylogs of order r)} pays for small g_k.
- {#656} *The cross constant of the step.* Let Ξ^c_{12}, Ξ^c_1, and Ξ^c_2 be 15.4's group gases of C₁ ⊔ C₂, C₁, and C₂ on the all-small-field term, and Ξ the gas without the loop. Put
  {#657} E^{R×}_k := E_k^R(C₁ ⊔ C₂;ℓ₀) − E_k^R(C₁;ℓ₀) − E_k^R(C₂;ℓ₀) = log Ξ^c_{12} − log Ξ^c_1 − log Ξ^c_2 + log Ξ.
  - {#658} These gases are 15.4's series at the flat reference: c_g and F(P)|_flat depend only on the shapes and relative positions of the polymers and of C's bonds (15.4(i)). No carried insertion enters them, so the domains that 15.5(i) joins do not either. Here a polymer meets C_i when its domain does, with dom P containing the X⁺ of its components and its trees.
  - {#659} (a) A cluster with no polymer meeting C₂ has the same polymers, activities, c_g, and compatibility in Ξ^c_{12} as in Ξ^c_1, and in Ξ^c_2 as in Ξ. (b) Among the rest, a cluster with no polymer meeting C₁ is the same in Ξ^c_{12} as in Ξ^c_2, and in Ξ^c_1 as in Ξ. So these clusters cancel in pairs, and E^{R×}_k is the signed sum, over the four gases, of the clusters with a polymer meeting C₁ and a polymer meeting C₂.
  - {#660} The rest contain a polymer meeting C₁ and one meeting C₂. Their objects are linked by overlaps and by interleaving, and an interleaving link joins two polymers with slots on one loop. So a chain of overlapping domains joins a domain meeting C₁ to one meeting C₂, and Σ_PD_P ≥ DL^m/M over the cluster's polymers ((1), *Lengths*; no polymer of these gases has a cross member, since c_g = 0 removes the cross groups).
  - {#661} With *The free decay*, the clusters of each gas through a π_k-cube with Σ_PD_P ≥ s sum to ≤ C_KPe^{−p₀(g_k)/2}e^{−c_Fs/2}, with 15.3's amplitude; the other half of c_F pays the interleaving entropy (*The rooted sum* below). At most 2^d·4ℓ_iL^m π_k-cubes meet C_i, and four gases enter. Hence
    {#662} |E^{R×}_k| ≤ C^R(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^me^{−p₀(g_k)/2}e^{−c_FDL^m/(2M)}, C^R := 2^{d+4}C_KP.
  - {#663} The rest of 15.4 holds per loop, with μ^R_k and ζ^R_k computed for each character: E_k^R(C₁ ⊔ C₂;ℓ₀) = Σ_i(P_{k−1}(C_i)μ^R_k(χ_i) + 4ζ^R_k(χ_i) + E°^R_k(C_i)) + E^{R×}_k. So the R-step's factor of κ factorises, and E^{R×}_k joins E^×_const,k.
  - {#664} The term-dependent part E^{R,∂}_k(ℓ) is charged per MR_k-cube to its component, with 15.4(iii)'s rooted bound m^R_k, as for one loop.
- {#665} *The sum over the R-steps.* p₀(g) decreases in g, so e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2} with ḡ_R := max_{N_B<k≤K′}g_k. By (7)'s sum lemma at p = 1 and a := c_FD/(2M),
  {#666} Σ_{N_B<k≤K′}|E^{R×}_k| ≤ C^{R×}_E := C^R(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}e^{−c_FD/(2M)}(1 + 12M/(c_FD)).
  {#667} It is bounded uniformly in K, it decays in D, and it is small when ḡ_R is.
- {#668} *15.6–15.7 for the groups on one loop.* As written, per loop: D_g is a dev insertion, contracted by D2.0(5)(c)'s recursion.
