- {#669} *15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas.* Since c_g = 0, every cross group of a family is in the marked set, with D_g^× := its whole contraction.
  - {#670} *The arc set.* D_g^× is the tensor of g's slots, contracted in loop order between adjacent slots, on ℐ(g) := the union of the slots' bonds. It has one pair of indices per interval of ℐ(g), and the words at U^R on the gaps of ℐ(g) stay in the loop's word. By the rule of *The groups of 15.4*, no bond of a slot of g lies in the span of another group, and distinct groups have distinct slots. So the spans of the other groups and the slots' bonds of the cross groups are disjoint, and 15.6's expansion over the marked sets runs with ℐ(g) in place of span(g). A cross member's intervals outside every X⁺ are bonds of its slot (#620.1), so a polymer whose span holds one of them is in g.
  - {#671} *The slots of a cross member.* Let a cross member W lie in a polymer P. Its slot is the contraction, in loop order, of W with all the arc pieces of the arcs of C ∩ ∪_{X∈P}X⁺ that contain intervals of W, and W's intervals outside every X⁺ stay open.
    - {#672} A single product (arc piece)·W·(arc piece) does not serve. W is covariant at the ends of each of its intervals ((6A), D2.5), so an interval of W with an end at a fixed site of another component of P is not telescoped by it, and two intervals of W on one arc need the arc piece between them contracted into W's index pairs.
    - {#673} Every interval of W inside a first-class X′⁺ lies in a component of P. The arc of C ∩ X′⁺ that contains it is a slot of the polymer of X′, whose base Y′₀ then contains X_W (*15.5*). So that polymer overlaps P and is P, since the polymers of a family are disjoint.
    - {#674} The pieces are not arc words of 15.2 on their own: an end of an interval of W inside X⁺ can be a site where 𝐓′_k(X) fixes a gauge, and 𝒯_X acts on the whole slot, since W depends on X's integration variables through U^{old}.
    - {#675} The slot is invariant under every transformation supported at the fixed sites of the components of P: each end of an interval of W inside some X⁺ is telescoped by the arc piece that contains it, as in 15.2(α), and a_X, b_X ∈ ∂X⁺ are not fixed sites.
    - {#676} It is covariant under the external transformations at the ends of the arcs and at the ends of W's intervals outside every X⁺, by 15.2(β)'s change of variables and D2.5.
    - {#677} So 15.2(α)–(β) apply to P's tensor T_P, the joint average over the components of P is 15.2's, and the contraction in loop order is unchanged.
  - {#678} 15.6(a)'s covariance holds at the ends of each interval of ℐ(g): 15.2 for the arc words, and the previous bullet for the slots that hold cross members. (b) and (c) hold with hull(g) := ∪_{P∈g}dom P, which contains the slots' arcs and the sets Dep(D) of the members. Its counted domain is hull_c(g) := ∪_{P∈g}dom_c P (*Lengths*); #π_k(hull_c g) below is the number of its cubes, and #π_k(hull g) that of all the π_k-cubes meeting hull(g). The others are the cubes of the members' sets Q(D), which hold the base Y′₀ over Dep(D) ∖ X_D ⊆ ℐ(D) (*The units of the members*). D_g^× depends on U^R on them, but they meet ℐ(g), whose bonds are counted as traversals, and the cubes meeting ℐ(g) enter D_g^×'s domain one step later through E_D ((6A)(i), *R-made insertions*). 15.6(b) states hull(g)⁺, and its argument gives hull(g).
  - {#679} D_g^× does not vanish at pure gauge, so (d)'s deviation bound does not apply. D_g^× depends on U^R on hull(g) only. Its box is hull(g)⁺, as in D2.7.
  - {#680} Its bound is (c)'s, ‖D_g^×‖ ≤ B(g), with ‖W‖ for each cross member W in its slot's bound. Its mass carries e^{|Ψ_g|}, by 15.6(d)'s convention, and the field-dependent part of e^{−Ψ(S)} is handled by 15.6(d)'s last sentence, as for one loop.
  - {#681} The clusters incompatible with g overlap hull(g) or are joined to g. A join is a slot of g in the span of one of the cluster's polymers, or a slot of one of its polymers in span(P) for a P ∈ g with a span (*The groups of 15.4*). So the clusters are rooted at a π_k-cube of hull(g), at a slot bond of g, or at a bond of such a span, and
    {#682} |Ψ_g| ≤ C_Ψ′e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}(#π_k(hull g) + |ℐ(g)| + Σ_{P∈g with a span}|span(P)|), C_Ψ′ := O(1 + MC_jn),
    {#683} with C_jn of *The rooted sum*, and |span(P)| ≤ 4M(D_P + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D)) (*The rooted sum*, *Spans*).
  - {#684} *15.6(e)'s scope rule.* Its first alternative, a box within d₀ = 6 layers of Λ_kᶜ ∪ S_k ∪ Ω_kᶜ, is SW closure §1(a3)'s rule for dev terms, whose deviation bound needs the box in the small-field region. D_g^× is not a dev term and carries no deviation bound, so this alternative does not apply to it, and the two cases below hold wherever hull(g)⁺ lies.
  - {#685} The second alternative, a polymer of g meeting a second-class or unchanged component, concerns the activities: c₁ = α^{1/3} there (p.390 l.1), and *The free decay* and *The volume* are derived above for the polymers that meet none. (6C) extends them to the others. Such a group is att(k, ·), and its activities are charged to the component as for one loop (D2.7 15.6(e)).
    - {#686} Its D_g^× has *The count*'s units, which do not use the activities.
    - {#687} A cross member W of it pays its part of |Ψ_g| and its join count from its weight, as in *The payment* and *Join count*, so its factor is at most w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W). D_g^× is then in the lineage of its first cross member, as in the second case, and stays in its register: M^cr, or D2.6 5.3's for a two-loop att first member ((6C), *The members' weights*).
    - {#688} The polymer part of its weight is paid in (6C).
  - {#689} The other cross groups have no polymer meeting a second-class or unchanged component, and their D_g^× is of one of the two cases below.
- {#690} *The count.* For every cross group g of the R-gas, att ones included, let 𝔅(g) be the carried insertions in g's slots, 𝔅^×(g) ⊆ 𝔅(g) the cross ones, 𝔅^s(g) := 𝔅(g)∖𝔅^×(g) the single-loop ones, and B_P := ∪_rX′_r ∪ ∪_{X∈P}X^{(44)} for P ∈ g, with X^{(44)} the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. The first layer holds the cubes of Y′₀ that contain a slot bond on ∂X⁺ (*Lengths*), and the 44 layers hold the small members (*The units of the members*). Put
  {#691} σ_g := Σ_{W∈𝔅^×(g)}σ(W) + ½Σ_{D∈𝔅^s(g)}σ(D) + Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P),
  {#692} with 𝔅(P) the carried insertions in P's slots, and σ(D) the production value of each member, the one its weight carries (map #MD2.4.12.6: a carried insertion's σ is in the units of its step of production). A single-loop member counts half: the other half of its weight pays the joins across its domain (*The payment*, *The rooted sum*, *First case*). The rounding of the members' domains is in #π_k(B_P) (*The rounding*).
  - {#693} *The units of the members.* Let D ∈ 𝔅(g) be produced at step j ≤ k, with the factor u_D ≤ 19 of (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*. Let Q(D) be the set of the π_k-cubes meeting Dep(D) ∖ X_D. Put T_D := S_D ∪ Q(D) when j = k, and T_D := X_D, D's step-k domain, when j < k. When j < k, X_D is D's domain one step later, which contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D) by (H0) and (H2), so Dep(D) = X_D and Q(D) = ∅. When j = k, D is made at the T-step into step k, since R acts only at k > N_B and N_B > N₀ ≥ 1 (D2.7 item 1(b); LFR I p.179 l.8–10). So u_D = 1, and X^{Cov}_D ⊆ S_D = X_D ∪ ℐ(D) (*Connectedness*): Dep(D) ∖ X_D ⊆ ℐ(D), and each cube of Q(D) meets ℐ(D) ⊆ S_D. In both cases Dep(D) ⊆ X_D ∪ ℐ(D), and T_D is closed-connected and contains X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D) and the cubes of Q(D), which are the cubes of the base Y′₀ over Dep(D) ∖ X_D (*15.5*). Then
    {#694} #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) + 17 and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M((21/20)σ(D) + 17).
    - {#695} When j = k, u_D = 1, so #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ σ(D) and diam_∞(S_D) ≤ Mσ(D) (*Units with a factor*, (H1)). Every point of a cube of Q(D) is within M of a point of ℐ(D) ⊆ S_D, so diam_∞(T_D) ≤ diam_∞(S_D) + 2M ≤ M(σ(D) + 2).
    - {#696} When j < k, they come from *The share of D*. At step j + 1, #_{j+1}(X_D) ≤ 16 + 20480/L + 8320u_Dσ(D)/L, with 215680σ(D)/L in place of the last term for a merged insertion of (R-member) (*Units with a factor*). At each later step E_D has at most #(X_D) + Σ_i(8|H_i(D)|/M + 80) cubes, with |H_i(D)| ≤ 4u_D(M/L)σ(D) + 2 (*Filled hulls*), so the count stays at most 16.1 + 0.23σ(D) for u_D ≤ 19 and L ≥ L₀^× (8320·16/L₀^× ≤ 0.23 and 215680/L₀^× ≤ 0.22). The intervals lie in the filled hulls, so n_k(D)/M ≤ 8u_Dσ(D)/L + 4/M. This gives #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ 17 + σ(D)/4. And diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M#_k(X_D), since a closed-connected union of N π_k-cubes has ℓ∞-diameter at most NM (the sum-of-diameters bound of *Lengths*).
    - {#697} The 17 is the rounding of a small domain to whole cubes: the 8M-neighbourhood of one π_j-cube can meet 2^d cubes one step later (*The share of D*). Production units alone do not bound a carried member's later domain.
    - {#698} *Big and small members.* Call D big if σ(D) ≥ 40 when j = k, or σ(D) ≥ 22 when j < k, and small otherwise. A big member has #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D), without the 17: for j = k by the first bullet, since σ(D) + 2 ≤ (21/20)σ(D) once σ(D) ≥ 40, and for j < k since 17 + σ(D)/4 ≤ (21/20)σ(D) and 16.1 + 0.23σ(D) ≤ (21/20)σ(D) once σ(D) ≥ 22. A small member has #_k(X_D) < 40, n_k(D) < 40M, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M: for j = k by the first bullet with σ(D) < 40, and for j < k since #_k(X_D) ≤ 16.1 + 0.23·22 < 22, n_k(D) ≤ M(17 + σ(D)/4) < 22.5M, and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ 21M. A small member whose T_D meets X⁺ for a first-class component X of its polymer has T_D within ℓ^∞-distance 42M of X⁺, so inside X^{(44)}, the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. Every small member in an arc slot does: T_D contains ℐ(D), and an interval of D meets C ∩ X⁺ for the component X in whose slot D lies. 𝔅^{big}(P) and 𝔅^{small}(P) are the big and the small members of 𝔅(P).
      - {#699} 𝔅(P) holds the carried insertions in P's slots (D2.7 15.5(i)): those in P's arc slots, whose interval meets an arc of C ∩ X⁺ (D2.7 15.0); the whole-joined ones, an att(j, Z′) or lw insertion of a first-class component Z′ of P, which 15.5(i) makes "a slot inside their activity", the activity of the polymers of its home, here P; and the attached ones, a carried insertion that 15.5(i) joins to the polymers of its home h(D), the first-class components whose X⁺ meets Dep(D) = X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, or, by a part with 𝒦 ≠ ∅, to those of the components in 𝒦 (*15.5*; "the attached insertions are slots of the polymers of 15.3", 15.5(ii)). Each X_D and each cube of Q(D) lies in dom P (#617), its intervals are in ℐ(g), and D_g^× depends on it. A big member is counted by (21/20)σ(D). A small member has #_k(X_D) < 40, n_k(D) < 40M, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M (above). If T_D meets X⁺ for a component X of P, T_D lies in X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P (above). Otherwise T_D is at ℓ^∞-distance at least 2MR_k from every component of P, since X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes (*Lengths*), and D is joined to P in one of two ways, each with a term of D_P whose half covers D's count: its fewer than 40 cubes of X_D, its fewer than 40M interval bonds, and the 43M by which T_D and an end cube lengthen the piece that joins it. R_k ≥ 46 holds for g_k small, since R_k ≥ (log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)). (a) Through a part D^{𝒦}, 𝒦 ≠ ∅. The components of 𝒦 are components of P, so the tree has ℓ_{a′} ≥ 2MR_k, scaled lengths being at least step-k lengths (*Lengths*). So ½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ R_k ≥ 46, and the half length in D_P's term (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M covers the count: 2^{d+1}·½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ 2^{d+1} + 40 with the tree's own 2^{d+1}(ℓ_{a′}/M + 1) cubes, 40M ≤ 16M·½ℓ_{a′}/M, and 43M ≤ ½ℓ_{a′}. (b) Through its part with 𝒦 = ∅, for a whole-joined member. Each piece of that part has a connected domain Y′ containing X_D and Z′ (15.5(ii)(β)), whose base Y′₀ is the cubes of X_D, of Q(D), and of Z′, all but Z′'s inside T_D. So #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) ≥ 2R_k − 1 ≥ 91, and of D_P's term 2#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) one half is the piece's own (*Lengths*, *Cubes*) and the other covers the count: 40 ≤ 2^{d+1}#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀), 40M ≤ 16M#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀), and 43M ≤ M#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀). No per-member constant of them enters, and their cost is paid by their trees' decay or their pieces' volume factors (*The free decay*).
    - {#700} The factors: u_D ≤ u₁ for the step-1 insertions, u_D = 1 for the ones made at the T-steps after step 1 (*Connectedness*), u_D ≤ 21/10 or u_D = 16 for the single-loop ones made at the R-steps ((6C)), and u_D = 6 for the R-made cross ones and their untouched continuations (the bullets below), with u_D = 19 for the merged insertions D_G of (R-member) and their untouched continuations. The T-step products of every species, lw and wrap included, have u = 1. The proof is by induction on the production step. A descendant made at a T-step takes its own σ at production (D2.6 closure 5.5), with u = 1. An untouched insertion keeps its production σ and factor (map #MD2.2.4.60, #MD2.2.4.93; D2.6 closure 5.3), and the bound for j < k covers it at every later step, whatever its factor.
    - {#701} *The rounding (derived).* The rounding is counted per cube, not per member. The number of members in X's slots is not bounded uniformly in k: members made at a step j < k can have L^{k−j} disjoint intervals in one step-k bond (D2.6 closure 5.2 (T0) *Positions*).
      - {#702} A big member carries no rounding constant. Its cubes, traversals, and diameter are at most (21/20)σ(D) in π_k-units, paid from its own weight at the rates below.
      - {#703} The small members of X's slots lie in X^{(44)}. Their cubes are cubes of X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P, their intervals lie on the arcs of C in X^{(44)}, and their sets T_D lie in the set X′_r ∪ X^{(44)} of *Lengths*, of ℓ^∞-diameter at most M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92). So they are counted in #π_k(B_P) and D_P, once per cube and per bond, whatever their number.
      - {#704} #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (105R_k)^d (*The volume*), so the cost at the rate (5/2)ν_r is paid by the margin of (1.89) of X (*The volume*), and the constant 4R_k + 92 of D_P is a polylog of order r < p₀, paid as in *The free decay*.
      - {#705} No per-member constant of the slot members 𝔅(g) remains in the uses below. The span members 𝒲_g of a single-loop group keep a constant 17 each, which (6C) pays through their masses, not by the margin ((6C), *The members' weights*).
    - {#706} A single-loop insertion D_g made at an R-step j (D2.7 15.6) is a function of U^R on hull(g) and of its members W ∈ 𝒲_g (D2.7 15.6(b), (f); 15.6(b) states hull(g)⁺, and its argument gives hull(g), as for D_g^× below). D2.7's hull(g) is the union of span(g) and the domains of g's polymers (D2.7 15.6). The counted domain X_{D_g} is the set of the π_j-cubes of ∪_{P∈g}dom_c P (*Lengths*), counted once as a set (the slot members 𝔅(g) included), together with the span members' domains X_W, W ∈ 𝒲_g, counted with multiplicity as in map #MD2.4.12.3. The arc of D_g is span(g), so σ(D_g) := #_j(X_{D_g}) + |span(g)|/M (map #MD2.0.3.50): the bonds of span(g) are counted as traversals, not as cubes. Put S_{D_g} := ∪_{P∈g}dom P ∪ span(g) ∪ ∪_{D∈𝔅(g)}T_D ∪ ∪_{W∈𝒲_g}T_W. Then (H0)–(H2) hold.
      - {#707} (H2): E_{D_g} = X_{D_g} ∪ (the π_j-cubes meeting span(g)) is closed-connected. The cubes meeting the arc span(g) form a closed-connected set. The π_j-cubes meeting a dom P form a closed-connected set, since dom P is connected (*Lengths*); it contains P's slot bonds, which lie in span(g); and those of its cubes outside dom_c P are cubes of the sets Q(D), which meet ℐ(D) ⊆ span(g). So all of them lie in E_{D_g}. Each member's T_W is closed-connected, contains ℐ(W) ⊆ span(g), and lies in X_W ∪ (the cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ⊆ E_{D_g}. X_{D_g} itself need not be connected.
      - {#708} (H0): X^{Cov}_{D_g} ⊆ hull(g) ∪ ∪_WX^{Cov}_W. The π_j-cubes meeting hull(g) lie in X_{D_g} or meet span(g) (above), and the members' (H0) puts X^{Cov}_W in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_W and the cubes meeting ℐ(W), which lie in X_{D_g} or meet span(g). So X^{Cov}_{D_g} lies in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_{D_g} and the π_j-cubes meeting span(g).
      - {#709} (H1): S_{D_g} is closed-connected for the same reasons and contains X_{D_g} ∪ X^{Cov}_{D_g}. Its pieces are *Lengths*' pieces of each dom P with each X_D replaced by T_D, the bonds of span(g), and the sets T_W. So, by *The units of the members* and the cube bound below, σ(D_g) and diam_∞(S_{D_g})/M are both at most
        {#710} σ̄(g) := Σ_{P∈g}(#π_j(B_P) + (2^{d+1} + ½)D_P) + |span(g)|/M + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(g)}σ(D) + Σ_{W∈𝒲_g}((21/20)σ(W) + 17).
        {#711} The small members of 𝔅(g) lie in the sets X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P, inside *Lengths*' pieces (*The rounding*), or are counted in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*Big and small members*).
        {#712} (6C) bounds σ̄(g) by a multiple of D_g's weight.
      - {#713} A member with intervals on both loops, a cross insertion whose interval on C_i lies in span(g), makes D_g a cross insertion, with ℐ(D_g) := span(g) ∪ ℐ(W) over these members. The bounds above are unchanged, since T_W contains ℐ(W) and n(W) is counted in (21/20)σ(W) + 17. Such a D_g is the merged insertion D_G of (R-member), with G = {g} when no other group is linked to its cross members. Its (H2), units (u = 19), lineage and register, rate λ_k(D_G), and θ are those of (R-member).
  - {#714} Cubes: #π_k(hull_c g) ≤ Σ_{D big}#_k(X_D) + Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + (2^{d+1} + ½)D_P). The counted cubes of Y′₀ lie in B_P, on the attached trees, or in the big members' X_D, the cubes of the sets Q(D) being left out (*Lengths*); the small members' X_D lie in B_P or are counted in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*Big and small members*). Each cube of a Q(D) meets a bond of ℐ(D) ⊆ ℐ(g), and a bond meets at most 2^d π_k-cubes, so #π_k(hull g) ≤ #π_k(hull_c g) + 2^d|ℐ(g)|. A lattice tree of length ℓ meets at most 2^{d+1}(ℓ/M + 1) π_k-cubes: its contour walk, of length 2ℓ, cuts into pieces of length ≤ M, each meeting at most 2^d cubes. The pieces' domains add at most D_P/2 cubes outside Y′₀.
  - {#715} Traversals: the bonds of ℐ(g) outside the big members' intervals lie in the arcs of C ∩ X⁺, X a first-class component of a P ∈ g (*The slots of a cross member*), or in the small members' intervals, which lie in X^{(44)} or, for the others, have fewer than 40M bonds each, counted in 16M times half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them. Each other such bond lies in one set X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X^{(44)}, of ℓ^∞-diameter ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92), or on one attached tree. Each of the eight sides of C₁ ⊔ C₂ meets a box of side s in at most s + 1 bonds. So n_k(D_g^×) := |ℐ(g)| ≤ Σ_{D big}n_k(D) + 16MΣ_PD_P, whatever the number of small members.

(6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*, at u = 6, then give it at most 1020Mσ_g positions in (T0), and its share is (6A)(i)'s, for L ≥ L₀^×.
- {#722} *The rates.* Put ε_k := e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} and ϑ_k := e^{−31δMR_k/512} at the R-steps, and λ_n := 1 − Σ_{N_B<k≤n}(ε_k + ϑ_k) (λ_n := 1 for n ≤ N_B). ϑ_k pays the members' parts: η(D) ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)e^{−63δMR_k/512} ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)ϑ_k² ≤ (ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D) (*15.5*, derived through #3.3, not reviewed), under 4c_r⁺ϑ_k ≤ ν_r, a smallness of g_k. The single-loop weights carry no decrement; their parts are booked in (6C), *The members' weights*. The weights of the cross insertions after step n carry the rate λ_n(D)ν_r: w_n(D) := e^{λ_n(D)ν_rσ(D)}e^{θ(D)}‖D‖, with λ_n(D) := λ_n − s(D)/32 and the shift s(D) ∈ {0, 1}. s(D) := 1 for an R-made insertion whose first cross member is a two-loop att insertion that D2.6 5.3 carries ((6C), *The members' weights*), for an R-made insertion with a cross member of shift 1, and for a touched or merged descendant of an insertion of shift 1; s(D) := 0 otherwise, the two-loop wrap and att insertions of #467 included. So λ_n(D) ≥ λ_n − 1/32 ≥ 15/16 under (H^R). An R-made insertion has w_k(D_g^×) := e^{λ_k(D_g^×)ν_rσ_g}e^{θ(g)}‖D_g^×‖, θ(g) := Σ_{W∈𝔅^×(g)}θ(W), and λ_k(D_g^×) ≤ λ_k(W) for each cross member W, since a member's shift passes to D_g^×. An untouched insertion has w_k ≤ w_{k−1}. The cross insertions here are all the insertions with intervals on both loops (#713), the two-loop wrap and att ones of #467 and the merged ones of (R-member) included. A two-loop wrap insertion keeps its (S-w) weight, with e^{ν_rσ_j} (#737), and a two-loop att one keeps D2.6 5.0's weight e^{ν_rσ_j}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}e^{θ}‖Y‖ in its species' register. Both dominate e^{λ_nν_rσ_j}e^{θ}‖Y‖, since λ_n ≤ 1, so the rate costs their registers nothing, and their R-step payments (#264) are made from w_n.
- {#723} *The payment.* For a cross group g none of whose polymers meets a second-class or unchanged component, the polymer part of e^{λ_kν_rσ_g}, together with the polymer part of e^{|Ψ_g|}, is paid by the activities, at the rate 2ν_r per unit of Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P). For the other cross groups it is paid in (6C), at the rate (5/2)ν_r. The member part below holds for all.

For small g_k this is at most ν_r per unit, and λ_k ≤ 1.

The first holds by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (#MD2.4.13.19) at d = 4. The second is ν_r ≤ δ_α/2^{d+5}, since δM = δ_α (#728.1), implied by #287's ν_r ≤ δ_α/(5·2^{d+3}).
    - {#728} δ is at most the decay rate of the 𝐇-functions in the scaled distance (p.376 l.11–13; #728.1). LFR II bounds them as B₃exp(−δd(·,·)) (p.368, (1.47)), CRE's form with the same B₃ (CRE p.250, the third and fourth lines below (1.19); p.251, the eighth line below (1.21); p.268, the fourth line below (3.17)). CRE fixes δ and then takes M large: below (1.21) it bounds |𝐇_{1,Ax}| < O(1)B₃exp(−δMR₁)ε₁, "much smaller than δ₀, for MR₁ large enough" (p.251, the eighth and ninth lines below (1.21)), and p.250, the third to fifth lines below (1.19), does the same for M₂R₁. M is chosen after L and M₂ (map #MD2.2.6.187, whose δ₀ is VAR's rate). That LFR II's δ is this δ is a reading of the shared form B₃exp(−δ·), listed under *Inherited*.
    - {#728.1} *Units.* D2.7 15.5(ii) measures (α)'s and (α′)'s lengths in M-units (ℓ(i,𝒦) := M^{−1}(the length of a shortest tree), ℓ(D,𝒦) ≥ R_k, segments of length t_k; D2.7 l.175), at the rate δ_α := min(1, (κ₁−1)/(4√d)). (6B) and (6C) measure lengths in step-k units and put δ := δ_α/M, so δM = δ_α, and (α) and (α′) hold at δ as stated (#613, #728.2). δ_α is fixed before M (κ₁ by map #MD2.2.6.163). The 𝐇-rate of #728 and the rate δ₀/32 of (R-eval)'s locality (#46), which fixes t_k (#613), are per step-k unit, and δ is at most both once M ≥ δ_α·max(32/δ₀, 1/δ_𝐇), δ_𝐇 the 𝐇-rate, a lower bound on M. The 𝐇-bounds B₃e^{−δ_𝐇·} hold at δ, since they only weaken as the rate decreases. So the conditions on δM (#287, #727, #776, #802) are conditions on ν_r and on g, not on M.
    - {#728.3} This δ is not #46's δ := δ₀/32, which (R-eval-old) and (R-eval) use (#5.7, #46, #74, #78) and which (6B) writes as δ₀/32 (#613). The note's decay rate δ in #3.3, #286, #287, (6B)–(6C) (with #728's first sentence), and (8)'s hypotheses (#917, #932) is #728.1's δ_α/M. The quoted rate of LFR II's and CRE's 𝐇-functions (#728 after its first sentence, #298, and #613's quotation of p.376) is δ_𝐇, and #750's δ is a diameter.
    - {#728.4} The δ of #47, #91, #99, and #209 is the rate printed in LFR I at the pages quoted there, and #93's δ is that of LFR I (1.45) (p.186, quoted at #91), not of LFR II's (1.45) (#46). δ₀ is VAR's rate (#46), except in #728's quotation of CRE p.251, where δ₀ is CRE's small-field threshold (A₁/A₀)ε₀ (CRE p.247 l.50). The δ = δ(d) of #5.10.2, #5.10.2.1, #5.10.3, and #5.10.5 is the decay rate of the kernels of H′ and P, a rate of d only, distinct from the rates above. The remaining δ's are symbols of their own, none of them a decay rate: the point mass δ_y (#5.10.1, #5.10.2); the change δ of the minimiser's complex part (#6.9, #6.14.1, #6.16.1); the data differences δ, δ^h, and δ^{re} (#45, #130.4, #130.5, #130.8, #130.9, #130.15.2, #130.19, #159–#162, #165); the functional derivatives δ𝓗/δB (#45, #94, #130.4, #165); (1.72)'s δ-functions and δ-constraints (#184, #184.1, #186, #196); the coefficients δm (#315, #366, #404, #454, #455); the diameters δ_P and δ_{P′} (#748, #751, #754, #832); Bałaban's threshold δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) (LFR I p.183 l.2, at #91; LFR II p.383, at #195); the symbols written with a star, bar, or subscript (δ_n^{(k)}, δ_b^{(k)}, δ_a, δ*_n, δ̄*, δ_m, δ_{k′}, δ_{rT}, δ_{G_i}); and C_δ := 2 (#346).

So #287's condition is ν_r ≤ 1/(5·2^{d+3}) and #727's is ν_r ≤ 1/2^{d+5}, that is ν_r ≤ 1/640 and ν_r ≤ 1/512 at d = 4, both implied by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (map #MD2.4.13.19). They need no order between ν and κ.

For 𝒦 = ∅ the part D^∅ = D(U^{h(D)}) has |D^∅| ≤ ‖D‖ (#613), within ‖D‖C_αe^{δMt_k} since C_α ≥ 1. The note's ℓ(i,𝒦) (#613, #626) is likewise the tree's length in step-k units, M times D2.7's ℓ(i,𝒦).
  - {#729} Of the rest of e^{−c_FD_P}, e^{−(c_F/4)D_P} pays the interleaving entropy (*The rooted sum*) and e^{−(c_F/4)D_P} is left for the join of the first case.

So the member part is ≤ 23C_Ψ′Me^{−p₀(g_k)/2}Σ_Dσ(D), since 17·21/20 + 4.2 ≤ 23 at d = 4. This is ≤ (ε_k/2)ν_rΣ_Dσ(D) for small g_k, under 182C_Ψ′Mε_k ≤ ν_r, since e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} = ε_k².
  - {#731} A cross member W enters D_g^×'s weight with at most e^{λ_k(D_g^×)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖W‖e^{(ε_k/2)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/2 + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W)}, since λ_k(D_g^×) ≤ λ_k(W) = λ_{k−1}(W) − ε_k − ϑ_k (*The rates*).

In all this is ≤ e^{(1/2 + 3ε_k/4 + ϑ_k/4 + 1/4)ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖ ≤ e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖, since ε_k, ϑ_k ≤ 1/32 under (H^R).
  - {#733} This is within its weight, species by species, since σ(D) is the production value that its weight carries (map #MD2.4.12.6).
    - {#734} dev: e^{ν_rσ + ν_e·ext} inside (ii)'s and (iii)'s per-position sums, in production units (map #MD2.0.3.32, #MD2.4.12.6).

    - {#736} lw: each piece of an lw slot is counted as a fixed member with its own σ, the one its weight carries (D2.6 closure §6.4, *(T0)'s count for lw slots*).
    - {#737} wrap(j), j ≥ 2: (S-w) carries e^{θ + ν_rσ_j + ν_e·ext_j} (map #MD2.0.3.32, D2.4(12)–(13)). The D2.6 closure 2.1(e) spends the extent weight on ext_j ≥ N^{(j)}_min/2 − 1 and drops e^{ν_rσ_j}, and the map states the weight for the dev species only (#MD2.0.3.32, #MD2.4.12.6). Keeping e^{ν_rσ_j} on the wrap(j) insertions is an amendment of 2.1(e), derived here: it gives the same bound, Σ_{𝒢∋n, wrap(j)}e^{θ(𝒢) + ν_rσ_j(𝒢)}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j)·pl·e^{ν_e}e^{−(ν_e/2)N^{(j)}_min}, merged groups with a wrap member included (2.1, *Merged groups*). The insertions are carried unchanged in (ii) with the growth L per step (map #MD2.0.5.19). The weight is a fixed factor of each insertion, so the weighted sums grow in the same way.
