With e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8}, ν_r = η, the factor e^{η𝔰/8} at complex backgrounds (IT-1 §3e(4), map #MD2.4.12.7), §3j(6)'s e^{2η𝔰}, which map #MD2.4.12.7 books on the same pin, and the pin Σ_{W∋n}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} ≤ Cρ (IT-1 note §3a(6); for the pair V4 lem:two-loop-pins(d)),
      {#739} Σ_{W∋n, wrap(1)}e^{θ°(W) + ν_r𝔰(W)}‖Υ⁰_W‖ ≤ 2K_χCρe^{−(3/4)ηN_min/(CLM)},
      {#740} since 4 − 1/8 − 1 − 1/8 − 2 = 3/4. This is map #MD2.0.3.18's bound of the wrap(1) insertions with σ₁ = 𝔰 in the weight and the rate (3/4)η/C in place of (31/8)η/C. The torus condition of map (5)(g) needs T_ph ≥ C′M with a constant C′ fixed by that rate, so only C′ changes, and (7)'s wrap rate c_w is taken at most this rate at j = 1.
    - {#741} Its mass stays inside e^{2ΣA} (15.3), as for one loop.
- {#742} *The rooted sum.* For a π_k-cube or a bond □, consider the groups g of the R-gas with a polymer containing □. In g, each polymer P carries its activity with the factors of *The payment* and e^{(c_F/4)D_P}, each member D carries e^{−((ε_k + ϑ_k)/4)ν_rσ(D)}, and a single-loop member also e^{−(ν_r/8)σ(D)}. The factor e^{−(ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D)} pays the sum over D's parts: their total is at most (1 + η(D))‖D‖ ≤ e^{(ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖ (*15.5*, *The rates*; η's bound derived through #3.3, not reviewed), the part D^{∅} included, so the members' number per bond does not enter. The sum over these g of the products of these factors is ≤ C_gre^{−p₀(g_k)/2}, with C_gr := 2^dC_KP·C_B. Here C_B collects the constant factors of B(g) (D2.7 15.6): its 2, D(k)(iii)'s e^{A_k} for the words at U^R on each loop, and dim V_{π₁}·dim V_{π₂} in place of dim π. D2.7 15.7 takes the same factors into its constant C′.
  - {#743} The members are data of the term, and their norms stay with their own weights (*The payment*).
    - {#744} A single-loop member's norm sits inside the activity's e^{2ΣA} (15.3). The parts of a dev member that 15.5(i) joins to other components through a 𝒦 ≠ ∅ keep their D(k) mass instead (D2.7 15.5(i)), with the factor (C_αe^{δMt_k})^{|𝒦|+1}e^{−δℓ(D,𝒦)} of R-far″ (α′); their sum over 𝒦 is the member's factor 1 + η(D), paid by its e^{−(ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D)} above (*15.5*; through #3.3, not reviewed).
    - {#745} A cross member's factor sits in its own slot of (T0)'s product form (*Second case*). Its bound at the evaluation points of R is (R-eval), derived and not reviewed, with (R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed given (R-eval).
    - {#746} The sums of D_g^×'s weights below are read with this convention. It persists at the later steps: a composite that contains D_g^× carries D_g^×'s weight as one factor, and the members' norms stay where they sit here.
  - {#747} The polymers through a fixed cube are summed by 15.3's Kotecký–Preiss bound, with its constant C_KP per cube. A bond meets at most 2^d π_k-cubes.
  - {#748} *Spans.* A polymer P with a span has no cross member (*The groups of 15.4*). Its slots lie in dom P, of ℓ^∞-diameter δ_P ≤ M(D_P + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D)) (*Lengths*, *The units of the members*). Bonds are taken as closed unit segments.
    - {#749} The torus period is at least twice each side, N_s ≥ 2max(ℓ₁, ℓ₂), as for V4's tori (periods LK_i with K_i ≥ 3(n+2) for a side nL, V4 L1565, and the pair's period condition, V4 L3560). Two points of a square loop differ by at most its side in each coordinate, so their torus ℓ^∞-distance is their planar one.
    - {#750} On a square of side s, points on opposite sides are at ℓ^∞-distance ≥ s. So a set of ℓ^∞-diameter δ < s lies on one side, or on two adjacent sides within δ of their common corner, and the shortest arc containing it lies on these sides. On one side that arc has at most δ bonds; on two adjacent sides it is an L of legs ≤ δ, with at most 2δ bonds and ℓ^∞-diameter ≤ δ. When δ ≥ s, the loop has 4s ≤ 4δ bonds and ℓ^∞-diameter s ≤ δ.
    - {#751} So span(P) has at most 4δ_P bonds, and diam_∞(span P) ≤ δ_P = diam_∞(dom P).
  - {#752} *Joins.* A polymer P′ joins g at a bond b that is a slot of g in span(P′), or a bond of a span in g that holds a slot of P′.
    - {#753} In the second case P′ meets a π_k-cube meeting b, one of at most 2^d.
    - {#754} In the first case span(P′) is an arc of at most 4δ_{P′} bonds through b, and its first bond in the loop's orientation is a slot of P′. So P′ meets a π_k-cube meeting the bond at offset j behind b, for some j ≤ 4δ_{P′}. P′ has a span, so its members are single-loop. Put S_{P′} := Σ_{D∈𝔅(P′)}σ(D) and c_j := min(c_F, ν_r)/(34M). Since 4δ_{P′} ≤ 4M(D_{P′} + (21/20)S_{P′}), the indicator of j ≤ 4δ_{P′} is at most e^{−c_jj}e^{4c_jM(D_{P′} + (21/20)S_{P′})} ≤ e^{−c_jj}e^{(c_F/8)D_{P′}}e^{(ν_r/8)S_{P′}}, since 4/34 ≤ 4.2/34 ≤ 1/8. The factors e^{−(c_F/8)D_{P′}}, half of the activity's remaining e^{−(c_F/4)D_{P′}}, and e^{−(ν_r/8)S_{P′}}, the single-loop members' factor in the summand above, pay it. Summed over j, the root costs at most 2^dΣ_{j≥0}e^{−c_jj} ≤ 2^d(1 + 34M/min(c_F, ν_r)) times 15.3's sum per cube.
    - {#755} So the polymers joining g at b, with their own joins, sum to ≤ C_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2}, with C_jn := 2^{d+1}C_KP(1 + 34M/min(c_F, ν_r)). 15.3's sum per cube holds for the activities with the factors of *The payment* and *Join count*, by *The free decay*.
  - {#756} *Join count.* A polymer P of g has at most 16MD_P slot bonds outside its big members, the small members' included (*The count*), and a span of at most 4MD_P + 4M(21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D) bonds (*Spans*).
    - {#757} Several joins can share one position, so a position contributes Σ_{m≥0}(C_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2})^m/m! = e^{C_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2}}: the joins at a position form a set, and the sum over the sets of m joins gives the factor 1/m!.
    - {#758} The factor e^{20MC_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2}D_P} of the polymer's part is ≤ e^{(c_F/8)D_P} under 160MC_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ c_F, a smallness of g_k. It is paid by the other half of the activity's remaining e^{−(c_F/4)D_P}.
    - {#759} A big member D has n_k(D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D) slot bonds, and a single-loop one adds at most 4M(21/20)σ(D) span bonds; a cross member lies in a polymer without a span (*The units of the members*). So a big member's factor is at most e^{(21/4)MC_jnε_k²σ(D)} ≤ e^{(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(D)}, since e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} = ε_k² and 24MC_jnε_k ≤ ν_r for small g_k. For a cross member this share also pays its link positions ((R-member), *The link sum*). A small member has no factor of its own.
  - {#760} C_gr and C_jn depend on d, M, c_F, ν_r, 15.3's C_KP, D(k)(iii)'s bound on A_k as in D2.7 15.7, and the representations χ₁, χ₂ through dim V_{π₁}, dim V_{π₂}, and not on k, K, or the term.
- {#761} *First case: no member of g is a carried cross insertion.* A polymer P ∈ g has slots on both loops (*The groups of 15.4*), all in dom P, so diam_∞(dom P) ≥ DL^m by (1). Its members are single-loop, so *Lengths* and *The units of the members* give D_P + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D) ≥ DL^m/M. The join between the loops runs through the sets X′_r and X^{(44)}, the attached trees, the pieces' domains, and the small members joined by a tree or a piece, which D_P counts, and through the big members' domains X_D, which their σ(D) count.
  - {#762} P carries e^{−(c_F/4)D_P}, held back in *The payment*, and each D ∈ 𝔅(P) carries e^{−(ν_r/8)σ(D)} from its weight (*The payment*). Since c_F/4 ≥ 2^{d+2}ν_r ≥ ν_r/8, their product is ≤ e^{−(ν_r/8)(D_P + Σ_{D big}σ(D))} ≤ e^{−(20/21)(ν_r/8)(D_P + (21/20)Σ_{D big}σ(D))} ≤ e^{−ν_rDL^m/(9M)}, since 20/(21·8) ≥ 1/9.
  - {#763} The single-loop insertions in g's slots end inside them (15.5 above). The polymers of g meet no second-class or unchanged component (*15.6(e)'s scope rule*), so D_g^× is a new insertion of species cross(k). The precedence rule of *The species of a new cross group* is for the T-steps, and (6B)'s rules replace it at the R-steps (#471).
  - {#764} Put A^R_k := Σ_{g first case}e^{c_W}w_k(D_g^×), with (6)'s normalisation e^{c_W}. The groups are rooted at the first traversal of ℐ(g), one of the 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^m bonds of the loops, and summed by *The rooted sum*. The decay e^{−ν_rDL^m/(9M)} is the product of the previous bullet, taken from the factor e^{−(c_F/4)D_P} that *The payment* holds back for the polymer with slots on both loops and from its members' e^{−(ν_r/8)σ(D)}. So
    {#765} A^R_k ≤ 4e^{c_W}C_gr(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^me^{−p₀(g_k)/2}e^{−ν_rDL^m/(9M)}.
- {#766} *Second case: g has a carried cross member.* D_g^× is a descendant of g's first cross member W, in D2.0(3)'s order and then position, and it is counted in W's lineage. The other members are data of the term.
  - {#767} Each member is a slot of one polymer, and each polymer is in one group. A cross member whose intervals lie in two first-class homes is still in one polymer: Y′₀ ⊇ X_W holds in the polymer of each of its slots, so these polymers overlap, and distinct polymers of a family have disjoint domains. So in a term of the expansion every carried cross insertion is untouched, or in exactly one group's slot. With (R-member) it is untouched, or in exactly one new insertion D_g^× or D_G: the hard core of (T0).
  - {#768} The groups with a cross member that 15.6(e)'s scope rule makes att(k, ·) are in the lineage of their first cross member as well (*15.6(e)'s scope rule*). Their polymer parts are charged to the component, and the polymer part of their weight is paid in (6C). The sum below is over all the groups assigned to W, these included.
  - {#769} The polymer of W's slot meets one of the π_k-cubes of X_W, since Y′₀ ⊇ X_W, and #_k(X_W) ≤ (21/20)σ(W) + 17 ≤ 18.05σ(W) (*The units of the members*, σ(W) ≥ 1). With the rooted sum (η's bound through #3.3, not reviewed), W's factor is w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)}. With 18.05σ(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ 72.2/(eε_kν_r), the groups assigned to W have
    {#770} Σ_gw_k(D_g^×) ≤ y^R_k·w_{k−1}(W), y^R_k := C_ye^{−p₀(g_k)/4}, C_y := 77MC_lk/(eν_r).
    - {#771} This is (R-member)'s *The lineage growth* with G = {g} (#246): the sum runs over at most W's ≤ 19Mσ(W) link positions, cubes of X_W included, with C_lk := 2max(C_gr, C_BC_jn) ≥ C_gr. So the constant 73C_gr/(eν_r) of the cube count alone is replaced by C_y.
  - {#772} A non-first member W′ enters with w_{k−1}(W′)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W′)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W′), the factor 1 + η(W′) of its parts paid (*The rooted sum*; η's bound derived through #3.3, not reviewed), so the R-step needs no factor e^{c_W}. An insertion with h(W′) = ∅ is untouched on its part W′^{∅} and a member on its other parts, and the two together are ≤ (1 + η(W′))e^{−ϑ_kν_rσ(W′)}w_{k−1}(W′) ≤ w_{k−1}(W′).
  - {#773} Steps 1–3 of (T0)'s product form, with the members' factors ≤ w_{k−1}, give, summed over the terms of 15.6's expansion, Π_D(1 + e^{c_W}w_{k−1}(D)(1 + y^R_k)) over the cross insertions present before the R-step. So the cross mass of each lineage is multiplied by at most 1 + y^R_k, and the new lineage cross(k) gains A^R_k. This is the cross carry lemma's step at an R-step ((6)).
- {#774} *The smallness (H^R):* S^R := Σ_{N_B<k≤K′}(e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} + e^{−31δMR_k/512}) ≤ 1/32 and C_yS^R ≤ log 2.
  - {#775} Then λ_n ≥ 31/32 for every n, and Π_{N_B<k≤K′}(1 + y^R_k) ≤ e^{C_yS^R} ≤ 2.
  - {#776} It is a smallness of g_{K′}, as in 15.7: by Cor. 8.2 of BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md, u_k := g_k^{−2} ≥ u + b(K′−k) with u := g_{K′}^{−2} − C, and e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} ≤ u_k^{−2} once A₀(log u)^{p₀−1} ≥ 8 (p₀ > 1). R_k ≥ (log u_k)^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.35–39) with r ≥ 2 (CRE p.245 l.33–34), so e^{−31δMR_k/512} ≤ u_k^{−2} once δM log u ≥ 34. So S^R ≤ 2u^{−2} + 2(bu)^{−1}.
- {#777} *The R-made total.* Under (H^cr), (1 + C_δc̄)^m ≤ 2^m ≤ L^m. By (7)'s sum lemma at p = 2 and a := ν_rD/(9M),
  {#778} Σ_{N_B<k≤K′}(1 + C_δc̄)^{K′−k}A^R_k ≤ A^{cr,R} := 4e^{c_W}C_gr(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}e^{−ν_rD/(9M)}(1 + 972M²/(ν_rD)²).
- {#779} *15.8.* The post-R term carries D^×(k)'s species and the cross clause, with the R-made cross insertions added. The cross insertions fall into five classes. One in a slot of a group is in the second or the fourth class, also when it lies in a block of a second-class component; the third class holds the other block insertions, and the fifth the span members of marked groups.
  - {#780} One in no slot of a polymer, in no span of a marked group, and in no block of a second-class component is untouched by R and re-evaluated at U^R, with its weight at the rate λ_k(W)ν_r. That is, its home h(W) is empty, so its dependence set Dep(W) ⊇ ℐ(W) meets no X⁺ of a first-class component X ((6B), *15.5*), and the term takes its part W^{∅} = W(U^R) of 15.5(i).
  - {#781} One in a slot of a group that 15.6(e)'s scope rule makes att(k, ·) is a member of that group's D_g^×. D_g^× continues the lineage of the group's first cross member and stays in its register (M^cr, or D2.6 5.3's for a two-loop att first member, (6C), *The members' weights*), the other cross members keep their own factors in the cross product, and the polymer part of D_g^×'s weight is paid in (6C).
  - {#782} One in the block inside a second-class component Y_i^∼ is a fixed factor of that block's word, which item 7 of D2.7 makes att(k, Y_i).
    - {#783} Item 5(a) of D2.7 bounds the block's average by the sup of its word over the integration domain of 𝐓′_k(Y_i) (D2.7 item 5(a) with its *Corrections* (a), l.59, l.63), and item 5(b) does so at complex data with the factor e^{3 sup|σ|} ≤ e³, kept in 5(b)'s mass (D2.7 item 5(b) with its *Corrections* (b), l.59, l.65). So W's factor comes out of the average as sup‖W‖ over the evaluation points of 𝐓′_k(Y_i).
    - {#784} It is then treated as the previous class: its factor stays in the cross product, and it stays in its register (M^cr, or its species' register for a two-loop att or wrap insertion).
    - {#785} That this sup is ≤ w_{k−1}(W) is (R-eval) with (R-eval-cr) ((R-eval-cr) itself derived and reviewed, rounds P1–P10), derived with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed, its part (R-eval-old) reviewed, given LF (#5), with the conjugation factors derived, not reviewed (#6.8, #130.15.1–#130.15.2), and not reviewed apart from the transfer to step k+1 (packet TB) and the real base current (packet JB; at U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8's post-R base-point clause, derived, not reviewed, #130.12). W's weight bounds W on 𝒟^λ_k ((R-eval-cr)). The evaluation points of 𝐓′_k(Y_i) over its integration domain, the large-field old variables included, are points Φ(𝐔) with 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^{ev}_k, and they lie in 𝒟^λ_k up to T₂^{old} ((R-eval-old), derived and reviewed, given LF (#5)), with the reduced representative in 𝒟♮_k (#6.13, derived, not reviewed), since Y_i lies in the new Ω_k ((R-eval), *Where it lands*; (R-eval-cr), *The R-steps*). The single-loop factors of the block's word, and its mass e^{3+2ΣA} in D2.7 15.8(c), are bounded by (R-eval) in the same way.
  - {#786} The others are slots of the small-field cross groups, and their descendants are those of the second case.
  - {#787} One in the span of a marked group on one loop is a member of that group's merged insertion D_G, and it continues as (R-member) states.

### {#789} (6C) The Attached R-Groups and the R-Step's Evaluation Points (Derived, Except (R-eval))

{#791} *The attached groups.* Let g be a group of the R-gas, a cross group of (6B) or a group of D2.7 15.6 on one loop, one of whose polymers meets a second-class or unchanged component. 15.6(e)'s scope rule makes it att(k, ·), and its amplitude is charged to the component as for one loop. Its weight is paid here.

- {#793} *The weights.* A cross group with a cross member carries e^{λ_k(D_g^×)ν_rσ_g} (*The rates*). Any other such group carries the production weight of the D2.6 closure 5.0, e^{ν_rσ}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}, with σ := σ_g for a cross group (*The count*) and σ := σ̃(D_g) for a group on one loop (*The single loop's R-made groups*).
  - {#794} ext^{coll} is measured from the collar Coll_k, which contains the component's cubes, in π_k-sides (D2.6 closure 5.0), and the group's domain meets the component.
  - {#795} For a cross group, hull(g)⁺ adds one layer of MR_k-cubes to a set of ℓ^∞-diameter at most 6Mσ_g (*The count*), so ext^{coll} ≤ 6σ_g + 2R_k + 1. With ν_e ≤ ν_r/8 (header), (3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll} ≤ (9/32)ν_rσ_g + ν_r(R_k + 1)/8.
  - {#796} For a group on one loop, ext^{coll} has the bound of *The single loop's R-made groups*, *The extent*, and costs at most what the extent costs there, since 3/8 ≤ 1.
  - {#797} The constants are polylogs of order r < p₀, paid by the margin of (1.89) (*The volume*). Every group has such a margin: each of its slots is an arc word of a first-class component or an insertion whose 𝒦 consists of first-class components (D2.7 15.3, 15.5(i)).
  - {#798} So, with the polymer part of e^{|Ψ_g|} at ν_r per unit (*The payment*), the polymer part costs at most (5/2)ν_r per unit of Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P), in place of *The payment*'s 2ν_r.
  - {#799} A single-loop member D of a cross group gains (9/32)ν_r·½σ(D) = (9/64)ν_rσ(D) of extent, and its budget in *The payment* becomes 1/2 + 3ε_k/4 + ϑ_k/4 + 1/4 + 9/64 ≤ 0.922, the ϑ_k/4 paying its parts' factor 1 + η(D) ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*; through #3.3, not reviewed). A cross member enters with its own weight, which carries no extent.
- {#800} *First case: no localisation domain of a polymer of g contains a second-class component.* By p.389 l.27–28 every localisation domain then is disjoint from ∪_iY_i, even when the polymer meets a Y_i through some X⁺, an attached tree, a piece, or a carried X_D. For a localisation domain X′ disjoint from ∪_iY_i, (1.95)'s activity carries the factor e^{−p₀(g_k)} of (1.97)'s c₁ (p.390 l.1), the decay e^{−(1+β)κd_{k,∪Y_i}(X′)} with d_{k,∪Y_i}(X′) = d_k(X′), and the volume factor on all of X′. The pieces' factor of (1.66) is over all of Y′ (p.376 l.10–12). None of these sees an unchanged component.
  - {#801} *The free decay* and *The volume* use only these factors and the factor (1.89) of the first-class components, so they hold for the polymers of g verbatim. *The payment* holds with them, at the rate (5/2)ν_r.
  - {#802} Its conditions become 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ c_F, that is 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ 1 and δM ≥ 5·2^{d+3}ν_r (header). Further, (5/2)ν_r ≤ a_V/2, and (5/2)ν_r#π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ p₀(g_k)/16, which holds under 10(1.05)^dν_r ≤ κ as in *The volume*.
- {#803} *Second case: a localisation domain X′ of a polymer of g contains a second-class component Y_i.* Its other localisation domains each contain some Y_j or are disjoint from ∪_jY_j (p.389 l.27–28).
  - {#804} *Size.* Y_i satisfies (i), so it lies in a cube of side 100MR_k (LFR I p.177 (i)), and #π_k(Y_i) ≤ (101R_k)^d.
  - {#805} *Distance.* d_k(X′) ≤ d_{k,∪Y}(X′) + dΣ_{Y_i⊆X′}(#π_k(Y_i) + 1). A shortest tree for d_{k,∪Y}(X′) meets every M-cube of X′∖∪_iY_i. X′ is connected, so each Y_i ⊆ X′ is joined to a cube of X′∖∪_iY_i, or to a Y_j already joined, by a chain of adjacent cubes of X′. Add, for each Y_i, the segments of length M joining the centres of adjacent M-cubes along a spanning tree of its cubes, and a path of length at most dM inside two adjacent cubes to the tree or to a Y_j already joined. The result is a connected graph in X′ meeting every M-cube of X′, and a spanning tree of it is no longer. If X′∖∪_iY_i is empty, X′ is one Y_i, and the spanning tree of its cubes alone gives the bound.
  - {#806} *The cost.* Off ∪_iY_i, the polymer part of the weight and the uses of the free decay (c_F per unit of D_P: *The payment*, *The rooted sum*, *Join count*) are paid as in the first case, with d_{k,∪Y} in place of d_k and the volume factor on X′∖∪_iY_i. What is left, per Y_i ⊆ X′, is (5/2)ν_r#π_k(Y_i) for the cubes of Y_i in B_P and c_Fd(#π_k(Y_i) + 1) for the excess of d_k over d_{k,∪Y} in D_P. Since (5/2)ν_r ≤ 1 and c_F ≤ 1, this is at most (d + 1)(#π_k(Y_i) + 1) ≤ (d + 1)(102R_k)^d.
  - {#807} *The roots.* A rooted sum of (6B) through a π_k-cube of Y_i runs over two kinds of polymers.
    - {#808} Those with a localisation domain containing Y_i. Each contains a first-class component, which is disjoint from Y_i, and hence a cube of the layer Y_i⁺∖Y_i, Y_i⁺ being Y_i widened by one layer of π_k-cubes. Their sum is rooted at these at most 3^d(101R_k)^d cubes.
    - {#809} The others, which have the first case's factors and are summed by the ordinary rooted bound at that cube.
    - {#810} So the root count at Y_i is at most N(Y_i) := 3^d(101R_k)^d + 1. On a term, a polymer with a localisation domain containing Y_i carries the weight N(Y_i)·N(Y_i)^{−1}. The factor N(Y_i)^{−1} makes its rooted sum root-free, and the factor N(Y_i) is a weight of the term, at most once per Y_i (*Per term*).
    - {#811} In Ψ's per-cube bound at the cubes of Y_i the same multiplicity enters, so *The payment*'s ν_r per unit there needs C_Ψ′(1 + 20M)N(Y_i)e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ ν_r, which holds for small g_k, R_k being a polylog of order r < p₀.
  - {#812} *The clusters with q = 0.* The clusters of Ξ^c that make up Ψ(S) (D2.7 15.6) can contain a localisation domain X′ of the sum over q at q = 0, whose amplitude is α^{1/3}, not e^{−p₀(g_k)} (p.389 l.27; (1.97), p.390 l.1). Such an X′ contains some Y_i, since a domain disjoint from ∪_iY_i has q ≥ 1 (p.389 l.27–28).
    - {#813} Its activity carries no word of Y_i, the blocks inside Y_i^∼ being fixed factors att(k, Y_i) (D2.7 item 7), and no insertion joined through a 𝒦, whose components are first-class (D2.7 15.5(i)).
    - {#814} A domain through a π_k-cube of Y_i contains Y_i, so the amplitudes of the domains through it are at most α^{1/3}. Rooted at a fixed cube of the first Y_i that they contain, these clusters sum to at most C_Ψ″α^{1/3} ≤ C_Ψ″, C_Ψ″ the per-cube constant of D2.7 15.3's Kotecký–Preiss bound with its joins, times e³.
    - {#815} So their part of |Ψ(S)| is at most C_Ψ″ per second-class component, uniformly in S. The other clusters keep the amplitude e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} of *The payment*.
  - {#816} *The size decay.* d_{k,∪Y} gives no decay on the cubes of Y_i, and D2.7's gases use the decay e^{−κ(size)} on the MR_k-cubes of a domain. Y_i has at most 101^d of them, so the missing decay costs at most e^{κ101^d}.
  - {#817} *The factor per component.* E(Y_i) := N(Y_i)e^{(d+1)(102R_k)^d + C_Ψ″ + κ101^d} ≤ e^{p₀(g_k)/16}. p.389's assumption gives (100R_k)^d < p₀(g_k)/(4κ), so (d + 1)(102R_k)^d ≤ p₀(g_k)/32 when 8(d + 1)(1.02)^d ≤ κ, which holds at d = 4 since κ > 6·2^d (*The free decay*). And log N(Y_i) + C_Ψ″ + κ101^d ≤ p₀(g_k)/32 for small g_k, R_k being a polylog of order r < p₀ and κ a constant.
  - {#818} *Per term.* A localisation domain contains Y_i or is disjoint from it, and the polymers of a family are disjoint, so at most one polymer, and one group, cross or on one loop, has a localisation domain containing Y_i. On every term of 15.6's expansion the parts at the Y_i of all groups, with the clusters with q = 0, are at most Π_iE(Y_i), uniformly in the family and the marked set, and the product leaves the sums over 𝔉 and S.
  - {#819} *The payment of Π_iE(Y_i).* Y_i satisfies (i), (ii), and the R-step introduces its large-field function χ_k^c(Y_i^{∼−6}) (p.378 l.3–8). Each term that contains Y_i carries χ_k^c(Y_i^{∼−6}) ((1.72), p.379 l.17), whose bound is taken as the new factor exp(−p₀(g_k)) of Bałaban's small-domain branch at j = k (p.385 l.45–p.386 l.2; (1.79)'s Π′, p.383 l.40 and l.44). This is *R-Yi-branch*, derived, not reviewed (#819.1). The print attaches that branch and Π′ to components "for which some large fields are created during the preparatory steps" (p.383 l.44; "introduced in the preparatory operations", p.385 l.45), while χ_k^c(Y_i^{∼−6}) comes from "the last operations defining the complete R-operation" (p.378 l.22–24).
    {#819.1} *R-Yi-branch (derived, not reviewed; D2.7 15.8(c)).* On the support of χ_k^c(Y_i^{∼−6}) = 1 − χ_k(Y_i^{∼−6}) (p.378 l.6), some cube □ ⊂ Y_i^{∼−6} has a plaquette p ⊂ □̃ with |U_{k,□}(V_k, ∂p) − 1| ≥ ε_kη² (CRE (2.17), p.257 l.39). If every plaquette of V_k on the neighbourhood of □ that determines U_{k,□}, inside Y_i by the margin ∼−6, were < (O(1)B₃M²)^{−1}ε_k, VAR Theorem 1 would give |∂U_{k,□} − 1| < ε_kη², as Bałaban uses it for U_{k,Z} at LFR I p.193 l.20 and l.28–29. So some p′ ⊂ Y_i has |V_k(∂p′) − 1| > (O(1)B₃M²)^{−1}ε_k. From this condition Bałaban obtains, by the Wilson action, the factor of 1 − χ_{k,Λ} (LFR I p.193 l.30–31): exp(−¼g_k^{−2}(O(1)B₃M²)^{−1}ε_k²) ≤ exp(−A₁²p₀²(g_k)) (p.381 l.29–31), which is ≤ exp(−p₀(g_k)) for small g_k (p.383 l.27–28). The estimate uses only that condition on V_k in Y_i and the Wilson action of Y_i's term, not the operation that introduced the function. So Y_i carries one factor of Π′, which is a product over components (p.383 l.40 and l.44), and the Wilson action of Y_i's term pays that factor only. The factor is part of 𝐓′_k(Y_i)1, which after R is a function of V_k on Y_i multiplying the action density (p.391 l.32–p.392 l.3).
    - {#820} D2.7 15.8(c) divides this factor into quarters as D2.6 l.143 does. The quarter that pays the new region's costs (D2.6 7.3) also pays Y_i's own costs at the R-step: the e³ of item 7's att(k, Y_i) insertion with item 5(b)'s mass e^{3+2ΣA}, and E(Y_i). The e^{2ΣA} is the masses of the attached insertions of the components inside Y_i, already paid by their κ (D2.7 15.8(b)). The quarter also pays the factor e^{A_k} ≤ e of the leading word's transports on the loop's bonds in Y_i's 100MR_k-cube at the transport-form points ((R-eval) (i), *The transports*). Since 3 + p₀(g_k)/16 + 1 + 7.3's costs at Y_i ≤ ¼p₀(g_k) for small g_k, the quarter pays them.
    - {#821} Y_i's persistent factor exp(−κ_k(Y_i) − 2p₀(g_{j(Y_i)})) (p.384 l.5–6), bounded by (𝐓′_k(Y_i)1) ≤ e^{−2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_k)} ((1.89)), is not used here. It stays with Y_i for the later merges and the later (1.89).
    - {#822} So Π_iE(Y_i) is paid on every term of step k by factors of that term. The per-term statements that follow hold with this pairing: (6B)'s Σ_gw_k ≤ y^R_kw_{k−1}(W), the cross carry lemma, and (8.2).
  - {#823} *The charge.* The rooted mass of D2.7 15.4(iii) through an MR_k-cube of Y_i is rooted at the layer as above, so there it is at most O(1)N(Y_i)e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ O(1)e^{−(7/16)p₀(g_k)}. This is still at most the bound C_νc_CMR_kτ̂_k² on m_k for small g_*, so D2.7 15.4(iii)'s re-run of the clause stands.

{#825} *The single loop's R-made groups.* Let g be a group of D2.7 15.6 on one loop, with the carried insertions 𝔅(g) in the slots of its polymers (D2.7 15.5(i); possibly none) and the members 𝒲_g (D2.7 15.6(f)). D_g carries the weight e^{ν_rσ̃(D_g)}, with
  {#826} σ̃(D_g) := Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P) + ⅓Σ_{D∈𝔅(g)}σ(D) + Σ_{W∈𝒲_g}(½σ(W) + 17),
  {#827} the extent e^{ν_e·ext_k(D_g)} when it is dev (map (ii-w)), and the weight of *The weights* above when it is att(k, ·).
- {#828} *Units.* By *Spans*, |span(g)|/M ≤ Σ_P(4D_P + (21/5)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D)). So σ̄(g) of *The units of the members* is at most Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P) + (21/4)Σ_{D∈𝔅(g) big}σ(D) + Σ_{W∈𝒲_g}((21/20)σ(W) + 17), since 2^{d+1} + ½ + 4 ≤ 2^{d+2}.
  - {#829} Hence σ(D_g), diam_∞(S_{D_g})/M and n_k(D_g)/M = |span(g)|/M are at most 16σ̃(D_g), since 21/4 ≤ 16/3 and (21/20)σ(W) + 17 ≤ 16(½σ(W) + 17). When 𝔅(g) = ∅ they are at most (21/10)σ̃(D_g), since (21/20)σ(W) + 17 ≤ (21/10)(½σ(W) + 17).
  - {#830} So D_g has the units of (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor* with u = 16, or u = 21/10 when no slot holds a carried insertion. (H0)–(H2) are those of *The units of the members*.
- {#831} *The factor along a lineage.* An untouched D_g keeps σ̃(D_g) and its factor at the later steps (map #MD2.2.4.60, #MD2.2.4.93; D2.6 closure 5.3). *The units of the members* bounds its domain there by (21/20)σ̃(D_g) + 17 whatever the factor, so a group of a later R-step that holds D_g, in a slot or as a member, counts it as any other member. The factor 16 enters only (6A)(i)'s counts at the T-step after step k, where *Units with a factor* covers it (the conditions below).
- {#832} *The extent.* hull(g) = ∪_{P∈g}(dom P ∪ span P) is connected, since interleaving polymers have intersecting spans (D2.7 15.4) and each dom P contains its slots. By *Spans* and *Lengths*, diam_∞(hull g) ≤ 2Σ_Pδ_P ≤ 2MΣ_PD_P + (21/10)MΣ_{D∈𝔅(g) big}σ(D).
  - {#833} D_g's domain and box lie in X(D_g) ∪ hull_𝒲(g)⁺, with hull_𝒲(g) := hull(g) ∪ ∪_{W∈𝒲_g}Q^#_W (D2.7 15.6(f)). A point of hull(g)⁺ is within diam_∞(hull g)/M + 2R_k + 1 π_k-sides of every traversal of span(g). A point of a member's X(W) ∪ Q^#_W is within ext(W) of every traversal of ℐ(W) ⊆ span(g) (map #MD2.4.12.6; W's production units only help). So
    {#834} ext_k(D_g) ≤ 2Σ_PD_P + (21/10)Σ_{D∈𝔅(g) big}σ(D) + 2R_k + 1 + max_{W∈𝒲_g}ext(W).
  - {#835} With ν_e ≤ ν_r/8, the polymer part costs (ν_r/4)D_P, the part of a big D costs (21/80)ν_rσ(D), and the constant (ν_r/8)(2R_k + 1) is paid by the margin of (1.89) (*The volume*) of a first-class component of g, which every group has, since each of its slots is an arc word of a first-class component or an insertion whose 𝒦 consists of first-class components (*The weights* above; D2.7 15.3, 15.5(i)). A dev member's part is paid by its own extent weight e^{ν_e·ext(W)}, since D_g's members leave the term's word and are counted only inside D_g (D2.7 15.6(f)).
  - {#836} *The attached extent (derived).* When D_g is att(k, ·) (D2.7 15.6(e)–(f)), its lineage extent is measured from a collar fixed below. The members enter through their domains, not through their own collars, so no collar of another step or of a removed component is used.
    - {#837} *The member terms.* For W ∈ 𝒲_g let e_W := ext(W) if W is dev, and otherwise e_W := (21/20)σ(W) + 1 if W is big and e_W := 43 if W is small ((6B), *Big and small members*). A point of a dev member's X(W) ∪ Q^#_W is within ext(W) of every traversal of ℐ(W) (map #MD2.4.12.6). A non-dev member depends on U^R on its Cov domain, and X_W ∪ X^{Cov}_W ⊆ T_W ((6B), *The units of the members*), with T_W ∋ ℐ(W) and diam_∞(T_W) ≤ (21/20)Mσ(W) or < 42M. So its Y_W, the π_k-cubes meeting X_W or X^{Cov}_W (D2.6 closure 5.0), lies within e_W π_k-sides of a traversal of ℐ(W) ⊆ span(g).
    - {#838} Every point of D_g's Y-set is in hull(g)⁺, within R_k π_k-sides of hull(g), or within e_W of a traversal of span(g) ⊆ hull(g). So any two of its points are within Δ_g := diam_∞(hull g)/M + 2R_k + 1 + 2max_{W∈𝒲_g}e_W π_k-sides, with diam_∞(hull g)/M ≤ 2Σ_PD_P + (21/10)Σ_{D∈𝔅(g) big}σ(D).
    - {#839} *Case (a): D_g's Y-set meets Coll_k*, the post-R collar of step k (D2.6 closure §1). This holds when a polymer of g meets a component, whose cubes lie in Coll_k (D2.6 closure 5.0), and when D_g's box lies within d₀ = 6 layers of Λ_kᶜ ∪ S_k ∪ Ω_kᶜ or outside the new small-field region (15.6(e)–(f)). Then D_g is att(k, ·), produced at step k and charged as 15.6(e) states, and ext^{coll}_k(D_g) ≤ Δ_g, measured from Coll_k.
    - {#840} *Case (b): otherwise.* Then D_g is att only through a non-dev member. Let W₁ be its first non-dev member in D2.0(3)'s order of species, then of position, of species att(j₁, Z₁), att(j₁, coll), or lw(Z₁). D_g continues W₁'s lineage: it is counted in W₁'s species, as D2.6 closure 5.5 counts a group with several old members in its first attached member's species, and its lineage extent is measured from W₁'s collar Coll_{j₁}, as 5.0 measures every insertion of that species. A cube y₀ of Y_{W₁} has dist(y₀, Coll_{j₁}) ≤ ext^{coll}(W₁), so ext^{coll}(D_g) ≤ ext^{coll}(W₁) + Δ_g.
      - {#841} The lineage's growth at the R-step: D_g's mass is at most B(g)Π_{W∈𝒲_g}‖W‖ (15.6(f)), the members other than W₁ being paid in their own registers (*The members' weights*). B(g) carries at least one polymer amplitude e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} (15.3), and the increase e^{(3/8)ν_eΔ_g} of the lineage weight is paid as below. So D_g adds at most e^{−p₀(g_k)/4}w(W₁) = ε_kw(W₁) to W₁'s species total for small g_k ((6B), *The rates*). This is a growth 1 + ε_k of the register at the R-step k, booked in the recursion of *The members' weights*, and 5.3's lifetime sum stands.
    - {#842} *Wrap members.* A D_g with a wrap member depends on U^R on that member's box, which does not lift to the cover, so D_g does not lift: it is wrap(k) by map #MD2.0.3.25's rule, its extent weight is not used, and its mass enters (ii)'s wrap sums through its first wrap member W₁. Each insertion is a member of at most one group or merged insertion ((R-member)). B(g) carries at least one polymer amplitude e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} (15.3) and the other members' factors are at most 1, so, when W₁ is a single-loop insertion, these D_g add at most e^{−p₀(g_k)/4}w(W₁) at the R-step k for small g_k, as in the lineage growth above: W₁'s interval is one arc in span(g), and the groups holding it are summed from one traversal. When W₁ has intervals on both loops (#467), the D_G holding it add at most y^R_k·w(W₁), by (R-member)'s link sum over its ≤ 19Mσ(W₁) positions with the weight left to it (#264–#269), whether or not W₁ is their first cross insertion. Over the R-steps the wrap masses gain at most Π_k(1 + e^{−p₀(g_k)/4})(1 + y^R_k) ≤ e^{(1 + C_y)S^R} ≤ 4 ((6B), (H^R)), which *The wrap total* carries.
    - {#843} *The payment.* The parts of (3/8)ν_eΔ_g from diam_∞(hull g)/M and from 2R_k + 1 cost at most what the dev extent's parts cost above, since 3/8 ≤ 1, and are paid in the same way. The term 2max_We_W is taken at one member W. For a dev W it costs (3/4)ν_e·ext(W) ≤ ν_e·ext(W), from W's extent weight. For a big non-dev W it costs (3/4)ν_e((21/20)σ(W) + 1) ≤ (ν_r/10)σ(W) + ν_r/10, from the half of W's reserve e^{ν_rσ(W)} that σ̃ does not take. For a small non-dev W it is the constant (3/4)ν_e·43 ≤ 4.1ν_r per group, paid by the margin with (ν_r/8)(2R_k + 1). In case (b), ext^{coll}(W₁) is paid by W₁'s own lineage weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(W₁)}, which D_g continues.
- {#844} *The members' weights.* A member W enters σ̃ with ½σ(W) + 17. Its reserve e^{ν_rσ(W)} pays the half that σ̃ takes, its part of the attached extent above, and its parts' factor 1 + η(W) ≤ e^{(ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*), since ½ + 1/10 + ϑ_k/4 ≤ 1, and a dev member's extent weight pays its part of the extent. So D2.7 15.7's member sum x_g := Σ_{W⊂span(g)}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} runs with the members' weighted masses times e^{17ν_r}, at most |span(g)|·e^{17ν_r}C_χρ̃_kδ̄* by (ii-w). Its condition becomes 4MR_ke^{17ν_r}C_χρ̃_kδ̄* ≤ 1, and its constant C″ gains the factor e^{17ν_r} ≤ 2, both for small g_k. Each insertion is a member of at most one group or merged insertion ((R-member)), and a member made at an R-step carries its own members inside its (ii-w) bound, so the factor does not compound. A single-loop insertion D with h(D) = ∅ ((6B), *15.5*) is untouched on its part D^{∅}, with its full weight (D2.6 closure 5.3), and a member on its other parts, which use at most 0.922 of its reserve in every group besides η(D) (#732, #799, this bullet, *D's budget*). So its options together carry at most e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖(1 + η(D)e^{−0.078ν_rσ(D)}) ≤ (1 + y^s_k)e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖, with y^s_k := C_sϑ_k² and C_s := 13c_r⁺/(eν_r), by η(D) ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)ϑ_k² (#722, derived through #3.3, not reviewed) and σe^{−aσ} ≤ (ea)^{−1}. Each single-loop mass is thus multiplied by at most 1 + y^s_k at the R-step k, and y^s_k ≤ C_sϑ_k² ≤ C_sϑ_k. A two-loop wrap or att insertion D keeps its full weight (*The rates* in (6B)), and its parts as members carry at most e^{ν_rσ(D)}e^{−(3/4)ϑ_kν_rσ(D)} times their norms after the payments of #264 ((6B), *Second case*), so with h(D) = ∅ its options together carry at most e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖(1 + η(D)e^{−(3/4)ϑ_kν_rσ(D)}) ≤ (1 + C_sϑ_k)e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖, by η(D) ≤ c_r⁺σ(D)ϑ_k² (#722, derived through #3.3, not reviewed), σe^{−aσ} ≤ (ea)^{−1}, and 4c_r⁺/(3eν_r) ≤ C_s: its mass is multiplied by at most 1 + C_sϑ_k at the R-step k. In D2.6 closure 5.3 the T-step and the R-step act in turn, and a two-loop att(j, ·) insertion W₁ that 5.3 carries keeps in its lineage the R-made insertions of which it is the first cross member (D_g^×, D_G; #250, #766). These and their own descendants carry no extent weight and have the shift 1, the rate (λ_n − 1/32)ν_r ((6B), *The rates*): they are carried by the cross carry lemma's step, without a bridge ((6)), whose budget (15/16)ν_rσ is within (λ_n − 1/32)ν_rσ since λ_n ≥ 1 − S^R ≥ 31/32 (#496), and as members at later R-steps they pay from their own weights at this rate, which they pass to the insertions they enter (#264, #731), so over the m := K′ − k later steps they grow by at most 2(1 + C_δc̄)^m ≤ 2L^m ((7), *The carried cross total*). At the R-step their mass at the rate λ_k is at most y^R_k·w_{k−1}(W₁) ≤ y^R_kw(W₁) ((6B), *Second case*), w(W₁) W₁'s weight of 5.0. Each of them, D, has a closed-connected S_D meeting both loops, which are DL^m step-k units apart ((6), *Production*), with diam(S_D) ≤ 19Mσ(D) (u ≤ 19, (6A)(i)), so σ(D) ≥ DL^m/(19M), and the shift gives the factor e^{−(ν_r/32)σ(D)} ≤ e^{−aL^m}, a := ν_rD/(608M). By xe^{−ax} ≤ e^{−a}(1 + 1/a) for x ≥ 1 ((7), *The cross constant*) they add at most C_atty^R_k·w(W₁) over their lifetime, C_att := 2(1 + 608M/ν_r) ≥ 2e^{−a}(1 + 1/a) by D ≥ 1. They are booked once, at the R-step k, at this lifetime cap: with P_k the register's mass without them and their descendants, M̂_k := P_k + Σ_{N_B<k′≤k}C_atty^R_{k′}(1 + δ_{k′−1})P_{k′−1} bounds the register's mass at every step, since the W₁ present before the R-step k′, after the T-step into k′, have Σw(W₁) ≤ (1 + δ_{k′−1})P_{k′−1} (5.3). At the R-step k, P grows by the factor 1 + C_sϑ_k of the h(D) = ∅ options above and by the lineage growth 1 + ε_k of case (b) (#841), so P_k ≤ (1 + δ_{k−1})(1 + C_sϑ_k)(1 + ε_k)P_{k−1}. Hence M̂_k ≤ (1 + δ_{k−1})(1 + C_sϑ_k)(1 + ε_k)(1 + C_atty^R_k)M̂_{k−1}, and 5.3's lemma holds for M̂ with e^{C_δc̄S_R + (1 + C_s + C_attC_y)S^R} under C_δc̄S_R + (1 + C_s + C_attC_y)S^R ≤ log 2, by Σ_kε_k ≤ S^R and y^R_k = C_yε_k ((6B), *Second case*), a smallness of g after M. In the dev recursion (map #MD2.4.6.14) the carried terms gain the factor 1 + y^s_k ≤ 33/32, so it holds at the R-steps with (33/32)(L^{−1} + C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²)(1+β₀)⁸ and 2C_d″, still bounded uniformly in k, since (33/32)·1.5⁸ < L₀ = 5000. The wrap(j) insertions are in neither recursion: they are carried in (ii) with the growth L per step ((6B), *The payment*, wrap(j)), so at the R-steps after their production their masses gain Π_k(1 + C_sϑ_k) ≤ e^{C_sS^R} ≤ 2, single-loop or two-loop, by y^s_k ≤ C_sϑ_k ≤ C_se^{−31δMR_k/512} and C_sS^R ≤ log 2, and *The wrap total* carries this factor 2.
  - {#845} An att or lw member is not in x_g: its mass is in its species' total through D2.6 closure 5.0's normalisation Σe^{c_W}w(W) ≤ A_j(Z), which 5.0 states for att(j, Z), att(j, coll), and lw(Z) alike. Its factor e^{17ν_r} is put into that normalisation, as e^{c_W} is: the factors of the carry lemma become 1 + e^{c_W+17ν_r}w(D)Π(1+δ_m), taken once per insertion, since an insertion is a member of at most one group or merged insertion ((R-member)). The normalisation is applied to every insertion of these species, so the totals are fixed before the groups are formed. This replaces A_j(Z) by A′_j(Z) := e^{17ν_r}A_j(Z) ≤ 2A_j(Z), since 17ν_r ≤ log 2 under 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ 1. From here on ΣA denotes the sum of the A′_j(Z).
    - {#846} The masses e^{2ΣA} stay polylogs of order r < p₀, and 15.3's amplitude bound c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} ≤ e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} stands for small g_k.
    - {#847} D2.6 7.1–7.3 use A_j(Z) through E_j(Z) := 2A_j(Z) + … ≤ ε_A(d_j′(Z)+1) and the comparisons ε_A(j+1) ≤ K_{j+1}/8 and ε_A(1) ≤ K₁/(8C_X) (D2.6 closure l.136–138). ε_A at most doubles, and it remains g_* times powers of LR_j for j ≥ 2 and a polylog of the same degree at j = 1, so the comparisons hold for small g_* as before.
    - {#848} A wrap member is not in x_g or in these totals: D_g is then wrap(k) (*The attached extent*), and its mass is in (ii)'s wrap sums.
- {#849} *The payment.* The polymer part of e^{ν_rσ̃(D_g)}, with the polymer parts of the extent and of e^{|Ψ_g|} (|Ψ_g| ≤ O(1)c₁e^{3+2ΣA+a_k}#cubes_k(hull g), D2.7 15.6), costs at most (5/2)ν_r per unit of Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P): ν_r for the weight, ν_r for Ψ, ν_r/256 for the extent, and at most 3ν_r/80 for the small members in D2.7 15.7's sums (below).
  - {#850} It is paid as in the first case above, at the rate (5/2)ν_r and under its conditions, when no polymer of g meets a second-class component, and as in the second case otherwise. The small members are in #π_k(B_P) and D_P (*The rounding*).
  - {#851} The member part of |Ψ_g| is that of (6B), *The payment*: at most (ε_k/2)ν_rσ(D) per D ∈ 𝔅(g), under 182C_Ψ′Mε_k ≤ ν_r.
  - {#852} This is the argument that map (ii-w) and D2.7 15.7 need for these groups.
- {#853} *D2.7 15.4(i) and 15.7 over the carried insertions.* D2.7 pays the single loop's group entropy (15.4(i)), and 15.7's diameter factor diam_k(hull(g)⁺) and member sum x_g, with thirds of e^{−κ(size)}. That decay does not reach across X_D (*15.5* of (6B): R-far″ claims no decay across X_D). So for 𝔅(g) ≠ ∅ these sums are re-run, with D's own factors for D's part.
  - {#854} Joins through a span that runs through D's slots are summed as in (6B)'s *Joins* and *Join count*. The indicator of the offset is paid by e^{(ν_r/8)σ(D)} per big member, and the joins' positions by e^{(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(D)}.
  - {#855} The diameter: diam_k(hull(g)⁺) is at most 15.7's polymer part, which is ≥ 2MR_k, plus 88M per localisation domain for the small members' sets X^{(44)}, plus (21/10)MΣ_{D big}σ(D) (*The extent*). So it is at most the polymer part times e^{44n/R_k}Π_{D big}(1 + (21/20)σ(D)/R_k) ≤ e^{(ν_r/80)Σ_PD_P}Π_{D big}e^{(ν_r/16)σ(D)}, with n the number of localisation domains of g, since n ≤ Σ_PD_P/(4R_k + 92), R_k² ≥ 880/ν_r, and R_k ≥ 17/ν_r for small g_k.
  - {#856} The members: |span(g)| gains at most 352M per localisation domain for the small members and (21/5)MΣ_{D big}σ(D) for the big ones. So the exponent x_g gains at most (ν_r/80)Σ_PD_P + (ν_r/80)Σ_{D big}σ(D) for small g_k, and the linear factor of 15.7's bound e^x − 1 ≤ xe^x, whose base is at least 4MR_k, gains at most e^{88n/R_k}Π_{D big}(1 + (21/20)σ(D)/R_k) ≤ e^{(ν_r/80)Σ_PD_P}Π_{D big}e^{(ν_r/16)σ(D)}, under R_k² ≥ 1760/ν_r for small g_k.
  - {#857} The polymer parts, 3ν_r/80 per unit of D_P in all, are paid within the polymer rate (5/2)ν_r (*The payment* above). No per-member constant remains (*The rounding*).
- {#858} *D's budget.* D ∈ 𝔅(g) pays from its weight e^{ν_rσ(D)}, released at the R-step (D2.7 15.8(b)): ⅓ for σ̃, ε_k/2 for the member part of Ψ, 1/8 + ε_k/4 for the joins, 21/80 for the extent, 1/16 + 1/16 for the two linear factors, 1/80 for the exponent x_g, and ϑ_k/4 for its parts' factor 1 + η(D) ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*; through #3.3, not reviewed). In all this is (1/3 + 1/8 + 21/80 + 1/8 + 1/80 + 3ε_k/4 + ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D) ≤ 0.89ν_rσ(D), since ε_k, ϑ_k ≤ 1/32 under (H^R).
- {#859} *The conditions at u = 16.* The share needs 16(8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4, which holds for L ≥ L₀^× = 10⁶, since 16(8496·17/16 + 11)/10⁶ ≤ 0.145. (T0)'s condition is (2720 + 59584/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8 ((6A)(i), *Units with a factor*, with C_win ≤ 196·19/c_w), implied by (R-member)'s condition at u = 19. The transport condition holds since 1408·16 ≤ 4096L.

{#861} *The evaluation points (what is derived towards (R-eval)).*
- {#862} *The integration variables are real.* In (1.71), "only the quadratic form in B and the term V(X^∼)" depend on (𝐔, 𝐉) (p.379 l.29). 𝐓′_k(X) is represented "as the operation for a regular G-valued configuration U", with a small and possibly complex term in the exponential (p.379 l.37), and (1.73)–(1.75) take the sup over the integration variables of (1.71).
  - {#863} These run over the supports of the R-step's χ′(X), χ_{h,1/2}((Ω″^∼_{h+1})ᶜ ∩ Ω_h ∩ X), σ(g_kB) and δ_{rT}(B), and of 𝐓_h(Z_h ∩ X) (p.378). They are real, for every class component, since X "may be any component of the first, or of the second class" (p.378).
  - {#864} On every class component the pre-R term's indicators are either kept as factors (on the excluded components and the unchanged ones) or equal 1 on the support of the factors that remain. These are (1.71)'s factors, the factors of (1.72) (χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}) and χ_k(X^{∼−6}) on first class; χ^c_k(Y^{∼−6}), χ_{k,Λ}, and χ on second class), and the preliminary variables' χ^{(j)} and χ′^{(j)}. So at real external data U_k^{(0)} is a G-valued gauge transform of one of D(k)(iii)'s real base points, and it satisfies the real-U part of CRE (2.34) and (2.38) ((R-eval), *The real base points*, derived).
- {#865} *The configuration.* In (1.49) the old terms are evaluated at U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} ((1.51), p.370), with the real fluctuation field B and a gauge transformation u_k″. Bałaban separates the dependence on B by (1.59)–(1.60), from the old terms' own analyticity on □₀ or X (p.372), and u_k″ drops out of the 𝐄- and 𝐑-terms by gauge invariance; the 𝐁-terms are taken at U_k″ (#86). Only the configuration U_k^0 at B = 0, of (1.61)–(1.63), enters (1.65).
  - {#866} U_k^0's data: off Λ, 𝐁_k″ and V″ coincide with the determining set and the field of the post-R configuration (p.370 l.7–8); on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} it takes the averages of U_0^{(AL)}, built from the external data on Z ∖ Λ and their critical extension into Λ (p.366 l.22–25); on (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c ∩ X it takes the real integration variables V″ of 𝐓′_k(X) (p.378); and on the boundary layers it takes the 𝐇-function data of (1.61)–(1.63).
  - {#867} By (1.65), U_k^0 maps Ũ_k^c(Y_4, α̃_0, α̃_1) into Ũ_k″^c(Y_1, α̃_0, α̃_1), and this space is "contained in the analyticity domain of the term 𝐄(X)" (p.375 l.29–30). For the old terms Bałaban's reason is that "they are connected with the old determining set" (p.375), whose conditions are weaker "on the crucial domain Ω″_{k₀+1}∖Ω_{k₀+1}".
- {#868} *The insertions.* Each insertion of the term is a function of 15.0's U^{old} = U_k^{(0)} (CRE (2.12)). Bałaban's old terms are evaluated at U_k^{(N)} = U_k″, after the preliminary differences are moved into 𝐁_k″ (LFR I p.181 (1.21)). D(k)(iii)'s domain 𝒟_k is the old determining set's space, layer by layer (SW closure (b1); LFR I p.190–191's spaces have this form). Bałaban's inclusion, read as an inclusion of these spaces, is a property of them. With each insertion localised in a domain Y₁ ∈ 𝐃_k by R-far″(β) (D2.7 15.5(ii)), it places U_k^0 in 𝒟_k, where the insertions are analytic.
  - {#869} D(k)(iii) asserts no bound at the points of 𝒟_k outside 𝒟^{sub}_k (SW closure (b2), (b6); D2.6 closure 5.0 *Amendment*, 5.4). The class of this note asserts its bounds on 𝒟^λ_k ((R-eval-cr)), and none outside it.
  - {#870} The insertions' own evaluation point U_k^{(0)} is Bałaban's chain of the stages (1.44) applied to U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} (#86), as a function of the preliminary variables. This is the note's u-free chain (#88.1), so no gauge transformation is removed. That configuration lies in 𝒟_k ((R-eval), (ii), under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces, given R-far″(β), #87, #88).
- {#871} *What this gives.* The analyticity of every insertion and word at the u-free evaluation point, on the integration domains of 𝐓′_k(X) and 𝐓′_k(Y_i), on the whole post-R 𝒟_k, given R-far″(β), and under *R-chain-start*, *R-chain-norm*, and *R-coincides*, with p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces ((R-eval), (ii), #870).
- {#872} *What it does not give.* The bounds: e^{2ΣA}, the dev masses, the cross weights, and the transports. They are taken at the transport form Φ on the T-step's points ((R-eval), (i)), and they rest on (R-eval), which is derived and not reviewed, with T_Y's α₀-entry for the current part of K. The transports use only the phase condition ((R-eval), *A_k*). A_k is defined as a bound in (R-eval), *The remaining parts*.

### {#874} (7) The Count Check over the Steps (Derived)

- {#876} *A sum lemma.* For L ≥ 2 and a > 0: Σ_{m≥0}L^me^{−aL^m} ≤ e^{−a}(1 + 6/a), and Σ_{m≥0}L^{2m}e^{−aL^m} ≤ e^{−a}(1 + 12/a²).
  - {#877} For m ≥ 1, L^m − 1 ≥ L^m/2. So the terms with m ≥ 1 are ≤ e^{−a}x^pe^{−ax/2} at x = L^m, with p = 1, 2.
  - {#878} sup_{y>0}Σ_{m∈ℤ}(yL^m)^pe^{−yL^m} ≤ 3. The terms with yL^m ≤ 1 form a geometric sum ≤ 1/(1 − L^{−p}), which is ≤ 2 for p = 1 and ≤ 4/3 for p = 2. The others are values of f(t) := t^pe^{−t} at points t₀ > 1 and t_i ≥ 2^i for i ≥ 1. The first is ≤ max f = (p/e)^p, and f decreases on [p, ∞), so the rest sum to ≤ Σ_{i≥1}f(2^i). In all this is < 0.72 for p = 1 and < 1.40 for p = 2.
  - {#879} Hence Σ_{m≥1} ≤ e^{−a}·3(2/a)^p.
- {#880} *The cross constant.* For x ≥ 1 and a > 0, xe^{−ax} ≤ e^{−a}(1 + 1/a). By (5), with ρ_V ≤ C_V(1+β₀)⁶𝔞̄ at the first step ((6)) and the sum lemma at the others, Σ_{k=0}^{K′−1}|E^×_{k+1}| ≤ C^×_E, with
  {#881} C^×_E := 4LC_V(1+β₀)⁶C_×𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−c_×D/M}(1 + M/(c_×D)) + 8C_S′e^{c_dr₀}𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−c_dD}(1 + 6/(c_dD)) + 8C_S′𝔞̄Σ_iℓ_ie^{−c_dℓ_i}(1 + 6/(c_dℓ_i)).
  - {#882} The first term is the first step, with x = L^{K′−1}. The second and third are the steps k ≥ 1, with a = c_dD and a = c_dℓ_i.
  - {#883} The first two terms decay in D. The third, from the groups long on one loop, is bounded but does not decay in D ((5)).
- {#884} *The R-steps' remainders.* Each R-step adds to E_const,k(C_i, χ_i) the corner and torus remainder E°^R_k(C_i), which stays in the exponent as D2.3(8)'s E°₂ does (D2.7 15.4). D2.7 15.4(ii) bounds it by Ce^{−p₀(g_k)/2}[(s_k+1)e^{−cs_k} + s_ke^{−cι_{T,k}}], with s_k = ℓ_iL^{K′−k} and ι_{T,k} = N_sL^{K′−k}.
  - {#885} With m := K′ − k, Σ_m s_ke^{−cs_k} = c^{−1}Σ_m(cℓ_iL^m)e^{−cℓ_iL^m} ≤ 3/c, by the sup bound in the sum lemma.
  - {#886} Σ_{m≥0}e^{−cℓ_iL^m} ≤ Σ_{m≥0}L^me^{−cL^m} ≤ e^{−c}(1 + 6/c), since ℓ_i ≥ 1.
  - {#887} Σ_m s_ke^{−cι_{T,k}} = ℓ_iΣ_mL^me^{−cN_sL^m} ≤ ℓ_ie^{−cN_s}(1 + 6/(cN_s)).
  - {#888} With e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}, the R-steps add at most
    {#889} C^{R°}_E := Ce^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}Σ_i[3/c + e^{−c}(1 + 6/c) + ℓ_ie^{−cN_s}(1 + 6/(cN_s))].
  - {#890} D2.3(7)'s C_Σ𝔞̄ is computed from the reference-gas remainders E°_{k+1} only (map #MD2.3.7.2–#MD2.3.7.8), so it does not include these.
- {#891} So |E^×_const,k| ≤ E* := (C_Σ(χ₁) + C_Σ(χ₂))𝔞̄ + C^{R°}_E + C^×_E + C^{R×}_E for every k ≤ K′ (D2.3(7) for each loop, at K′ by map #MD2.3.4.16; C^{R×}_E from (6B)). The sums are of absolute values, so they bound every partial sum.
- {#892} *The wrap total.*
  - {#893} D(k)(ii)'s wrap terms are ≤ C_wL^{k−j}𝔟̄e^{−c_wN^{(j)}_min/M} per traversal, with N^{(j)}_min = N_sL^{K′−j}. Their amplitude is max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j) (map #MD2.0.3.34), and C_w absorbs C_S′, C_S″, and the R-steps' factors: Π_k(1 + C_sϑ_k) ≤ 2 for the parts of the wrap insertions with h(D) = ∅ ((6C), *The members' weights*), Π_k(1 + y^R_k) ≤ e^{C_yS^R} ≤ 2 for the two-loop ones, which are cross lineages ((6B), *Second case*, (H^R)), and e^{(1 + C_y)S^R} ≤ 4 for the groups with a wrap member ((6C), *Wrap members*), at most 16 in all. At j = 1 the wrap(1) insertions carry the weight e^{ν_r𝔰} ((6B), *The payment*), and c_w is at most their rate (3/4)η/C.
  - {#894} Summed over j ≤ k and over the 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k} traversals of step k, they give at most w̄ := 4C_w(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)𝔟̄e^{−c_wN_s/M}(1 + 6M/(c_wN_s)), for every k.
  - {#895} This is small under the torus condition (map #MD2.3.4.16), as in the continuum note's (5.3).
- {#896} *The carried cross total.* Under (H^cr), (1 + C_δc̄)^m ≤ 2^m ≤ L^m. Under (H^R), (1 + y^R_j)Γ_{j,n} ≤ (1 + C_δc̄)^{K′−j}Π_{N_B<k≤K′}(1 + y^R_k) ≤ 2(1 + C_δc̄)^{K′−j}. With (6)'s production bound at m := K′ − j, and the sum lemma at p = 2 and a := ν_×D/M, for every n ≤ K′
  {#897} Σ_{j≤n}M^cr_n(j) ≤ 2Σ_{j=1}^{K′}(1 + C_δc̄)^{K′−j}(A^cr_j + A^R_j) ≤ A^× := 2(A^cr + A^{cr,R}),
  {#898} A^cr := 4C_cr𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−ν_×D/M}(1 + 12M²/(ν_×D)²) ≥ 4C_cr𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)Σ_{m≥0}L^{2m}e^{−aL^m},
  {#899} with A^{cr,R} of (6B).
  - {#900} The first inequality uses n − j ≤ K′ − j.
  - {#901} A^× is uniform in K, and it decays in D.
- {#902} Each bound is uniform in K.
  - {#903} A^cr is linear in 𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂). C^×_E is linear in 𝔞̄ and at most a constant times 𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂), since ℓ_ie^{−c_dℓ_i}(1 + 6/(c_dℓ_i)) ≤ (1 + 6/c_d)ℓ_i.
  - {#904} C^{R×}_E and A^{cr,R} are linear in e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}(ℓ₁+ℓ₂). C^{R°}_E is at most a constant times e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}, since ℓ_i ≤ N_s/2.
  - {#905} w̄ is linear in 𝔟̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂).

### {#907} (8) The Pair Identity at the Stopping Scale and the K-Uniform Bound

{#909} *(8.1) The identity (derived from (2) and the continuum note's (2) with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂).*
- {#910} On ℓ₀ at K′, f^{(i)}_{K′} = W_{χ_i}(C_i^{(K′)};V_{K′}) + β^{(i)}, with |β^{(i)}| ≤ Lip_{χ_i}(e^{8a_dc_hB₃ℓ_iε_{K′}} − 1) (the background closure §3.4, for each loop).
- {#911} So f^{(1)}f^{(2)} = W_{χ₁}(C₁^{(K′)})W_{χ₂}(C₂^{(K′)}) + β^×, with |β^×| ≤ |β^{(1)}| + |β^{(2)}|, since |f^{(2)}|, |W_{χ₁}| ≤ 1.
- {#912} Exactly,
  {#913} W^×_ren(ε) = e^{E^×_const,K′}μ^{[K]}_{ℓ₀}(W_{χ₁}(C₁^{(K′)};·)W_{χ₂}(C₂^{(K′)};·)) + r^×_K,
  {#914} r^×_K := e^{E^×_const,K′}μ^{[K]}_{ℓ₀}(β^× + I^×_{K′}) + Σ_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}w_ℓ⟨e^{E^∂_{K′}(ℓ)}[f^{(1)}_{K′}f^{(2)}_{K′} + I^×_{K′}(ℓ)]⟩_ℓe^{E^×_const,K′}.
- {#915} Here I^× is the insertion sum of the class at K′, cross insertions included. This is the continuum note's (2), with κ^{(K′)}_{C₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂} for κ^{(K′)} and the two-trace words.

{#917} *(8.2) The K-Uniform Bound (derived given D^×(K′) on every labelled term, whose step map is §3's with the reading (6A), (6B) at the R-steps, and the inputs listed under *Inherited*, the R-step inputs (R-eval), (R-round), and (R-att-ext), which are derived and not reviewed, and (R-member) and (R-eval-cr), derived and reviewed given (R-eval), (R-eval) and (R-eval-cr) with the deep-layer bound of #3.2, derived and reviewed, and (6B)'s *15.5* derived through #3.3, not reviewed).* Assume (H^cr), (H^R), δM ≥ 5·2^{d+3}ν_r, L ≥ L₀^×, N_s ≥ 2max(ℓ₁, ℓ₂), the smallness of the couplings that (6) and (6B) use, and (C2)–(C3) of the continuum note's (5.5) for each loop. Then the right side of
{#918} |W^×_ren(ε)| ≤ e^{E*}[1 + Σ_i8.48a_dc_hB₃ℓ_iε_{K′}Lip_{χ_i} + (e^{4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)O(C_χρ_{K′}) + w̄ + A^×} − 1)] + (1 − w_{ℓ₀})e^{A^×}B^×_LF
{#919} is bounded independently of K: ε_{K′}, ρ_{K′}, 𝔞̄, and ḡ_R depend on K only through the couplings, which the windows bound (map #MD2.3.4.15–#MD2.3.4.16, g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K), as in (6.3).
- {#920} C_χ := max(C_{χ₁}, C_{χ₂}). B^×_LF is (6A)'s. E*, w̄, and A^× are those of (7). The conditions 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ 1 and (5/2)ν_r ≤ ¼ hold by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (#MD2.4.13.19).
- {#921} Proof:
  - {#922} |μ_{ℓ₀}(W_{χ₁}W_{χ₂})| ≤ w_{ℓ₀} ≤ 1.
  - {#923} |I^×_{K′}| ≤ exp(Σ_nν_n + A^×) − 1 over the 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂) step-K′ traversals: the continuum note's (5.3) for the other insertions, and the cross carry lemma with (7) for the cross insertions, those made at the R-steps included. The per-traversal bound (ii) of the other species and the product bound of the cross carry lemma, which is summed over the multilinear expansion, combine, since (T0)'s product form factorises the expansion over lineages (step 3 of (T0)).
  - {#924} On the large-field terms the cross insertions multiply the words' bound by at most e^{A^×}, on every labelled term ((7)), and the rest of the words' bound is B^×_LF by its definition in (6A). Its K-uniformity is (4.5)'s, transferred to the pair in (6A).
  - {#925} The constants are (7).
- {#926} Compared with (6.3) for one loop:
  - {#927} ℓ_C becomes ℓ₁ + ℓ₂ in the insertion term;
  - {#928} there are two background terms, and C_χ is the larger of the two characters' constants;
  - {#929} E* has C_Σ(χ₁) + C_Σ(χ₂) in place of C_Σ(χ), and gains C^×_E and C^{R×}_E. Its term C^{R°}_E, the R-steps' remainders, is one that (6.3) needs too and omits;
  - {#930} w̄ carries the pair's 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂) traversals and the amplitude 𝔟̄ of map #MD2.0.3.34. (6.3)'s wrap term has the amplitude ρ̂_j, so this is also a correction of the single-loop form;
  - {#931} the carried cross total A^× is added to the exponent, and e^{A^×} multiplies the large-field term, with B^×_LF the pair's payment without the cross insertions;
  - {#932} the hypotheses gain (H^cr), (H^R), δM ≥ 5·2^{d+3}ν_r, L ≥ L₀^×, and N_s ≥ 2max(ℓ₁, ℓ₂).

{#934} *(8.3) Subsequences (as the continuum note's (8.2)).*
- {#935} μ^{[K]}_{ℓ₀}, E^×_const,K′, and r^×_K are bounded uniformly in K.
- {#936} So every sequence of lattice spacings has a subsequence along which they converge. Along it, W^×_ren converges to e^{Ē}μ̄(W_{χ₁}(C₁^{(K′)})W_{χ₂}(C₂^{(K′)})) + r̄^×.

### {#938} (9) What the Bound Does Not Give

- {#940} Convergence along the full sequence. It is not derived here. `WILSON_IT1_TWO_LOOPS_CAUCHY_2026_11_05.md` derives it, not reviewed, given this bound and the Cauchy note `WILSON_IT1_CAUCHY_ESTIMATE_2026_10_27.md` (the continuum note's (3.5) and (9.3) for the pair).
- {#941} Factorisation. That W^×_ren − W_ren(C₁)W_ren(C₂) is small for large D, with both single-loop expectations normalised at the common stopping step, is not derived.
  - {#942} A^×, C^{R×}_E, and the first two terms of C^×_E decay in D. The long-group term of C^×_E does not ((5)).
  - {#943} μ^{[K]}_{ℓ₀} couples the two coarse loops, and its decay of correlations at the stopping scale is not addressed.
- {#944} Smallness of the cross terms at fixed coupling. A^cr and C^×_E are bounded for every D ≥ 1. They tend to 0 in the limit γ → 0 with γ/g_min bounded:
  - {#945} with q := max(q₁, q₂, q₁₂), ℓ_i = p_iL^{j−q_i} ≤ p_iL^{q−q_i}L^{q̄(j)+1};
  - {#946} 𝔞̄ ≤ 𝔟̄, and 𝔟̄ is O(g_k(1+2r_k)⁴pl_k) maximised over k (ρ_k, ρ_{ph,k}, and ρ_{t,k} at map #MD2.0.3.35; b_k ≤ Cρ_{t,k+1}, D2.6 closure §2.1);
  - {#947} so 𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂) → 0 there, as ℓ_Cε_{K′} → 0 does in the continuum note's (5.5).
  - {#948} C^{R×}_E and A^{cr,R} carry the factor e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2} ((6B)), so they are small when ḡ_R is.
